# Śrī Vyāsa-pūjā 1999

## Introduction by Badrinārāyaṇ dāsa

In September 1965, His Divine Grace Abhay Charan Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda walked down the gangplank of the *Jaladuta* and stepped onto the dock in New York Harbor. He had experienced prolonged seasickness and two heart attacks during the thirty-five-day journey. When he later related the experience of disembarking and standing on that pier, Śrīla Prabhupāda commented, “I did not know whether to turn left or right.” He had come with only forty rupees and no support or assistants, and he was not in strong health. He was elderly and used to the warm weather and respectful culture of his native India. All he was armed with was the order of his spiritual master to preach the message of Kṛṣṇa to the English-speaking world, and a number of three-volume sets of his translation and commentary on the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*.

Some months later, with his first half-dozen fledgling followers in tow, Śrīla Prabhupāda sat down on a patch of grass in Washington Square Park to begin the public chanting of the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mantra,* accompanying himself with a pair of hand cymbals. Even that modest approach had its difficulties: the local police told him to get off the grass. So Śrīla Prabhupāda and his small band obligingly moved to the park’s cement sidewalk.

From such a humble beginning flowed the amazing success of Śrīla Prabhupāda’s mission. In twelve short years he opened well over one hundred temples, schools, farms, and *prasādam* restaurants, inaugurated the worship of dozens of sets of beautiful Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa Deities, wrote more than sixty books, started his own publishing house, marched down New York’s Fifth Avenue and through London’s Trafalgar Square in magnificent Jagannātha Ratha-yātrā festivals, and initiated more than five thousand disciples from every corner of the world.

In the West, people love Horatio Alger “rags to riches” stories, and in India people are impressed by tales of yogis who can manifest some shiny trinkets. But Śrīla Prabhupāda’s success is the most amazing and mystical event of them all. He turned hearts from stone to gold and manifested temples in what had previously been a spiritual wasteland. From the most humble of beginnings he went on to make “Hare Kṛṣṇa” a household word in cities, towns, and villages around the globe. Such accomplishments are unprecedented in religious history.

Śrīla Prabhupāda often said that the reason for his phenomenal achievements was an open secret. Although apparently without any assistance, Śrīla Prabhupāda’s statement was that “I never felt alone. I knew that my spiritual master and the previous *ācāryas* were always with me.” We know from the *śāstra* that Kṛṣṇa is the cause of all causes and that not a blade of grass moves without the will of the Supreme. The history of the world has always been one of the Lord coming Himself and working via His empowered representatives to deliver the wayward souls. This process of deliverance plays itself out over the stage of time in progressive steps, its ultimate success being assured by the omnipotence of the Lord.

So Śrīla Prabhupāda’s success was the fruition and perfection of the efforts of a long chain of empowered personalities, each of whom built on the previous *ācāryas’* accomplishments. Lord Buddha revived dharmic principles and the value of renunciation. Śaṅkarācārya re-established the *Vedas* and the existence of a transcendent reality. One after another, the great Vedic teachers Madhvācārya, Rāmānujācārya, Viṣṇu Swami, and Nimbārka Swami wove into the Vedic path the profound truths that the *jīva* and the Supreme Lord each have an eternal individual identity, that this allows for the sweet exchange of devotional service even in liberation, and that the perfection of such eternal loving relationships is found in the mood and pastimes of the residents of Vṛndāvana and their unexcelled service and surrender to Lord Govinda, the all-attractive cowherd boy.

Like a lotus gradually opening in all its glory under the full-day sun, or like rock candy that has been gradually condensed from sugarcane juice, the complete manifestation of all these teachings, the resting place of all Vaiṣṇava *sampradāyas,* was manifested by Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, the Supreme Lord Himself. He came in the form of a devotee to teach by His example, to relish the taste of pure devotion, and to simultaneously deliver the path of dharma for this Age of Kali, the chanting of the holy name.

Next, Lord Caitanya’s most intimate associates, the six Gosvāmīs, wrote volumes of literature weaving together all these threads and clearly proving that the teachings and practices of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu were the culmination of all Vedic understanding. The philosophy was established, the books to support it were written—now how to deliver it all over the world?

Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura pushed for an organized preaching mission via his personal example and according to his outline given in *Śrī Śrī Godruma-kalpatavi* for his program of Nāma-haṭṭa (“marketplace of the holy name”). His son, Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura, inherited the mantle and carried it to glory via his all-India Gauḍīya Maṭha, even sponsoring forays into Europe. But it was saved for Śrīla Prabhupāda to carry this work all over the world via his ISKCON. Śrīla Bhaktivinoda, in his *Navadvīpa-dhāma-mahātmyā,* tells how Lord Nityānanda revealed to Jīva Gosvāmī that a *sena-pati bhakta,* a great general of Lord Caitanya’s army, would someday come to take the secrets of *bhakti* everywhere. Later, Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta counseled his disciples about Śrīla Prabhupāda: “Someday he will do everything himself.”

Given the incomparable accomplishments of Śrīla Prabhupāda, who can doubt that he is in fact that great general predicted by Lord Nityānanda? Can anyone honestly doubt that Śrīla Prabhupāda inherited the mantle of the previous *ācāryas* and the full mercy of Lord Caitanya?

It is in light of this historical progression that his followers see the unique position of Śrīla Prabhupāda, read his Bhaktivedanta purports, and work to maintain the standards, practices, and goals he established.

Śrīla Prabhupāda designed his society as a great transcendental boat. The planks, the sails, the rigging, and the charts are his morning program, his standard of sixteen rounds daily, his order to follow the four regulative principles, his stress on preaching and book distribution, and his organizational system. All these elements are held together by his books, lectures, letters, and personal instructions. Śrīla Prabhupāda once said, “I have given you the structure, all you have to do is decorate it. And I have even given you the decorations as well; just go and hang them.” Simply by sincerely serving and staying onboard Śrīla Prabhupāda’s boat, the faithful souls will reach safe harbor, crossing over the ocean of the material world and arriving at the lotus feet of the Supreme Lord.

Yet Śrīla Prabhupāda, in his humility, often said that his only qualification was that he had one hundred percent faith in the words of his spiritual master. He would quote the first line of *Bhagavad-gītā* 2.41—*vyavasāyātmikā buddhir ekeha kuru-nandana—*and then explain that Śrīla Viśvanātha Cakravartī’s commentary defines fixed intelligence (*vyavasāyātmikā buddhir*) as the unshakable determination to execute the order of the spiritual master, and that this is the real secret of spiritual success. He gave the example that different materials have varying degrees of ability to conduct electricity: wood and rubber have practically none, copper is better, and platinum is one of the best. He said that the reason for this difference was that each material provided increasingly less resistance to the electrical current. In the same way, to the degree the disciple is surrendered to the order of the spiritual master, to that degree the guru’s mercy will be able to flow through.

The offerings in this book celebrate the glories of Śrīla Prabhupāda and his continued impact on the world. In their own way, they also give us insight into the process, flavor, and rewards of mature surrender to the service of such an exalted person. The readers of this book are blessed to have this opportunity, and that also is yet one more gift from Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Badrinārāyaṇ dāsa  
Governing Body Commissioner

## The Meaning of Vyasa Puja by Hari-śauri dāsa

MEANING: *n.* 1. Something that is conveyed or signified; sense or significance. 2. Something that one wishes to convey, especially by language. 3. An interpreted goal, intent, or end. 4. Inner significance. (American Heritage Dictionary)

Is there any meaning to a body without a soul?  
Is there any meaning to a life without a goal?

Is there any meaning to chance philosophy,  
Chaos theory, unified field, existential mimicry,  
So artfully propounded by those we consider wise?  
Is there any meaning if they win the Nobel Prize?

Is there any meaning to celebrating our offspring’s birth,  
to consoling them in their sorrow and sharing their temporary mirth,  
If by our education they live but then only expire,  
With absolutely nothing known of who they really are?

Is there any meaning if from disease we provide relief,  
When repeatedly it recurs and always gives us grief?  
If we provide the unfortunate with some shelter and a bed,  
Is there any meaning, when we’ll all just end up dead?

Is there any meaning to a longer and longer life,  
If when we’re old and “experienced,” all we know is strife?  
Is there any meaning to mourning a beloved one passing on,  
If they and we have no idea where they might have gone?

Is there any meaning to a life of economic gain,  
If everyone that spends it is totally insane?  
Is there any meaning to consuming everything in sight,  
Turning mother earth into a wasteland; do we think that this is right?

Is there any meaning to the politician’s song—  
“A vote for me means good times ahead”—when all we see is wrong?  
Is there any meaning to breaking down a Berlin Wall,  
If those that become “liberated” still follow Māyā’s call?

Is there any meaning to counting minutes, days, and years,  
If all of our endeavor only ends in countless tears?  
Is there any meaning to the year 2000 and beyond,  
If no one has the means to break out of Māyā’s bond?

We found no meaning in anything, our life a mystery,  
Our ignorance, our covered hearts—existential misery.

Before meeting Śrīla Prabhupāda there was no meaning, no success. We knew only defeat. Life did not fit our aspirations. Life was not life, it was living death. Life had no luster, no brilliance, no shine.

The word *varṇam* refers to the luster of one’s original identity. The original luster of gold or silver is brilliant. Similarly, the original luster of the living being, who is part of the *sac-cid-ānanda-vigraha,* is the luster of *ānanda,* or pleasure. *Ānanda-mayo ’bhyāsāt*. Every living entity has the right to become *ānanda-maya,* joyful, because he is part of the *sac-cid-ānanda-vigraha,* Kṛṣṇa. Why should the living being be put into tribulation because of dirty contamination by the material modes of nature? The living entity should become purified and regain his *svarūpa,* his original identity. This he can do only by devotional service. Therefore, one should adopt the instructions of the Supreme Personality of Godhead, who is described here as *guror guruḥ,* the spiritual master of all other spiritual masters. \[*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 8.24.48, purport\]

With words like these Śrīla Prabhupāda saved us. Depicting the beauty of the soul, he painted vivid pictures of ourselves with words in clear, colorful strokes and revealed a spiritual realm that had remained unconceived for uncounted lifetimes. Though it lay hidden deep within us, with his words Śrīla Prabhupāda brought forth and revived our true propensity to love by informing us of our relationship with the only person with whom we can ever really enjoy.

This is the business of the guru, the words from the mouth of whom fall like drops of nectar into the ears and hearts of the fortunate. By hearing from the true guru, we feel restored and we regain our luster. Millions of years of crying and dying—the burning in our hearts symptomized by restless wandering, the loneliness of abandonment, the fruitless searching, and the frustration of thwarted endeavor—are all at once mitigated by the bold verbal strokes of Śrī Gurudeva.

What is worse than not knowing oneself? This madness is terrible indeed. Surely there is no greater suffering than that caused by ignorance of the self.

Conversely, there can be no greater gain than knowledge—real knowledge, knowledge of the real, knowledge undefeated by time.

REAL: *adj.* 1.a. Being or occurring in fact or actuality; having verifiable existence. b. True and actual; not imaginary, alleged, or ideal. c. Of or founded on practical matters and concerns. 2. Genuine and authentic; not artificial or spurious. 3. Being no less than what is stated; worthy of the name. 4. Free of pretense, falsehood, or affectation. 5. Not to be taken lightly; serious. 6. *Philosophy:* Existing objectively in the world regardless of subjectivity or conventions of thought or language. \[American Heritage Dictionary\]

Situated in reality, the guru, from his position of inviolability, delivers his audience from profanity simply by his words. Like Ali Baba opening up with some special words the rock-hewn door of the home of thieves, Śrīla Prabhupāda vibrated the mantras necessary to open our hearts; and he entered deep within.

I once purchased a book by a popular so-called guru with a chapter called “Is there a God?” Without reading any other part, I went straight to that section, hoping to have my last great question answered. I received only disappointment in his words: “If you think there is a God, then there is God. If you don’t think there is a God, then there’s no God.”

This “guru” is cheating—that much I knew. “What does it matter what I think?” I thought. “Either God exists or He doesn’t; it doesn’t depend on my thinking. When my mind is so confused, when my heart is so troubled, when I have not exorcised the haunting ghost of ignorance, what is the value of my thinking?” I threw the book in the trash bin.

A week later I met a disciple of Śrīla Prabhupāda in the city square and asked, “Do you have anything there that tells me what God is? Or if He even exists?” Immediately this representative of the real guru, Śrīla Prabhupāda, opened up a BTG and read to me his words: “God is omniscient; God is omnipresent; God is omnipotent; God is a person. And His name is Kṛṣṇa.”

I took that magazine and went down to the riverside to read. I studied those words. They were like lightbulbs going off in my head one by one—“Omniscient”—“Omnipresent”—“Omnipotent.” Those words struck home, penetrated and illuminated my soul.

“God is a person”—A thousand lights went on!—Of course! He has to be a person!—I’d never thought of that.

“And His name is Kṛṣṇa”—This is it! Far out, He has an identity!

I knew then that I had nowhere else to go. If this is true, I knew, then wherever I go, He’s already there. And whatever I think about, He already knows. And whatever I try to do, He’s already done it. And He knows me more than I know myself. Everything is His, including me. He’s not “God”—that terribly impenetrable mystery of a label that conveyed no meaning or understanding to me—He’s a person with a name that I can know and call out to.

I was so happy I felt like crying; not the tears of a million wasted and anguished lives, but those of relief and happiness for the first time in my life.

That was my first contact with a real guru, Śrīla Prabhupāda. A few words and I was out of it, out of ignorance, out of suffering, out of this material world. I gained real knowledge, real understanding, reality. And the truth shall set you free.

Śrīla Prabhupāda set me free and I can’t go back. Though still shadowed by my past many lives, still I can’t go back. I can’t give up reality. It doesn’t go away—He doesn’t go away, always calling, calling, calling.

The words Vyāsa uses to describe *bhakti* are *pūjā* and *anurāga*. These refer to worship of the Lord performed with sincere love and great attachment....A sincere devotee’s *pūjā* is never merely mechanical but is offered with *anurāga,* strong feelings of attachment for the Lord. True worship is performed with the mind, the senses, and all the bodily limbs. Therefore the meaning of worship is not limited; it includes the engagement of all one’s sensory and mental functions in service to the Supreme. \[*Nārada-bhakti-sūtra* 16, purport by Satsvarūpa dāsa Goswami\]

This for me is the meaning of Vyāsa-pūjā.

Your humble servant,  
Hari-śauri dāsa

## Vyāsa-Pūjā Homages from the GBC

## Anuttama dāsa

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

In the *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is* you write,

Men who are ignorant cannot appreciate activities in Kṛṣṇa consciousness, and therefore Lord Kṛṣṇa advises us not to disturb them and simply waste valuable time. But the devotees of the Lord are more kind than the Lord because they understand the purpose of the Lord. Consequently they undertake all kinds of risks, even to the point of approaching ignorant men to try to engage them in the acts of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, which are absolutely necessary for the human being. \[*Bhagavad-gītā*, 3.29\]

Thank you for the risks you took for us, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Each year your admirers publish new books that are full of additional details of the hardships you accepted, impelled by your intimate understanding of “the purpose of the Lord.”

As a junior and late-arriving member of your family, I was not fortunate enough to share in your private *darśana* or to struggle to keep up with one of your fast-paced walks, receive a personal letter, or enjoy an intimate moment direct glance. So I am unable to share remembrances of those experiences in glorification of you. But you have given me many gifts, and I am grateful.

You blessed me with acceptance into the great tradition of Gauḍīya Vaiṣṇavism, a glimpse through your personal example of what can be accomplished in life if one surrenders as an instrument to the Supreme Lord, and faith.

You blessed me with the faith that in the midst of the doubts, horrors, and suffering of this world, the Supreme Friend remains in our heart, directs our wanderings, and waits within for us to call out to Him.

You blessed me with faith that all living beings are sacred and should be respected as parts and parcels of the Lord, and that we all can return to Him—not just the few, the well-born, or the chosen.

You blessed me with a glimpse of the Personality of Godhead. Beyond God as the creator, judge, or omniscient controller, you revealed my most intimate friend. At my first visit to a temple twenty-five years ago you told me through one of your disciples, “Kṛṣṇa is the person we’re all looking for.” From that day forward I have had hope.

You gave me faith that if I am to follow you and try to “repay my debt” to you, I must develop your mood of kindness and “undertake all kinds of risks” to help the less fortunate understand the urgency of human life.

You gave me friends and a family who share this faith, who nurture it within me, and without whom I could not go on. And you bless me with reminders that despite my insignificance I can render service to you and Śrī Kṛṣṇa, and, more importantly, that my service can bring hope and faith to others, that I can become kind, as you are kind to me.

Today we face many challenges. Like an awkward teenager, your young ISKCON society is in the midst of growing pains. We burst very tall, but we lack grace. We were confident of our adulthood and our ability to manage our father’s business, but setbacks and obstacles have humbled us. Now we are again forced to see our personal shortcomings and to cry out more intently for your guidance and wisdom. This is your mercy upon us. This will make us strong.

Like a long-legged, big-eared adolescent, we may have thought we knew it all. Being your disciples and followers, we were special. We would make a difference, we were the few, the chosen, the well-born. We especially, the first-born generation, would fulfill your mission and make the world Kṛṣṇa conscious.

Now, like a chastened young adult, we understand more clearly our dependence upon our forefathers and you, our father, by whose gifts we are maintained.

Like the fabled blind men who groped in darkness to understand the elephant and in their proud foolishness ruled out the realizations and insights of their fellow explorers, we are learning that you highly value all who serve you and love you. Yours is not a society of renunciants, temple devotees, baby boomers, or those born in India. It is meant to be a refuge for all. And, more profoundly, we need one another to go forward.

Just as you compassionately engaged the myriad talents of young followers that Śrī Kṛṣṇa laid at your feet, we must also become *mahātmās*, broad-minded enough to teach and spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness in the time, place, and circumstances you have given us.

We must pray more sincerely, assess our personal motives more precisely, listen to you and the lessons around us more carefully, and examine our plans and strategies more intelligently. We must look closely at ourselves—as the world looks at us—to assure that we represent you not just in dress, markings of clay, and modes of music but in the spirit of humble and honest servants of the Lord.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, although we are immature, please bless us with wisdom and humility. Let us not imitate you, unaware of our personal frailties and the changing nature of the world around us. Rather, let us enter into a deeper understanding of your mood and your mission. Let us follow you not like parrots but as wise and humble disciples begging to better comprehend the essential principles of your teachings and the spirit of your kindness.

Your aspiring servant,  
Anuttama dāsa

## Bhakti-Bhṛṅga Govinda Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances to your lotus feet.

I miss everything about you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Passing life not seeing you is very difficult. I miss your words, your *kīrtanas,* your charming smiles, your overflowing compassion. I miss seeing your lotus feet and I miss touching them.

I think that I mostly miss your sublime example of Vaiṣṇava behavior. You are so full of heart. These days it is rare to see that type of example. I hanker for that in the way a man in the desert thirsts for water.

I am hankering for you and I am trying to please you. Bestow your divine grace upon me and allow me to become a small reflection of your noble self. Bless me to chant, bless me to preach, and bless me to have the good heart to care for Vaiṣṇavas as you have shown.

Prabhupāda, you are Kṛṣṇa’s eternal associate. You are with Him and are serving Him in ecstasy and love.

And, Śrīla Prabhupāda, I am your servant. How long will it be until I become qualified to receive your full mercy and thus join you on the plane of Kṛṣṇa consciousness? That day I most hanker for.

Your humble servant,  
Bhakti-Bhṛṅga Govinda Swami

## Bhakti Caitanya Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances at your divine lotus feet. All glories to you and to your ISKCON movement.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, recently I have been wondering why it is sometimes so hard. There are so many obstacles facing your movement now. I had expected that as time went on it would become easier, but this doesn’t seem to be the case. There are many problems, and sometimes it is hard to understand why these things are happening. What should we do? Are we doing some things wrong?

Of course, it is only natural that we do some things wrong, as we are relatively new to the process of Kṛṣṇa consciousness and are still deeply conditioned by the modes of material nature. But still, sometimes one wonders.

We have not yet come to the platform of Queen Kuntī, who in *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* prays:

*vipadaḥ santu tāḥ śaśvat tatra tatra jagad-guro*  
*bhavato darśanaṁ yat syād apunar bhava-darśanam*

**“I wish that all those calamities would happen again and again so that we could see You again and again, for seeing You means that we will no longer see repeated births and deaths.” (*****Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*** **1.8.25)**

Śrīla Jīva Gosvāmī explains in his *Prīti-sandarbha* that this verse manifests Queen Kuntī’s advanced Kṛṣṇa consciousness, on the level of *kṛṣṇa-prema* called *rāga*. He says that in that stage the devotee cannot tolerate even a moment’s separation from Kṛṣṇa. When the devotee associates with Lord Kṛṣṇa, the devotee thinks the greatest sufferings are all happinesses, and when the devotee cannot associate with Kṛṣṇa, the devotee thinks all happinesses are the greatest sufferings. Indeed, if a suffering causes her to take shelter of Kṛṣṇa more, and she feels His blessed association in that tribulation, then that tribulation becomes a merciful cause of her highest, and only real, happiness.

Queen Kuntī is speaking this verse immediately after the Battle of Kurukṣetra, when, on the material platform, all her problems are finished. Her sons have regained their kingdom, all their enemies have been vanquished, and the way seems clear for a peaceful and contented life. But she is not so optimistic. As far as she is concerned, the complacency of her new way of life is going to be a serious obstacle to her real happiness, which is in the shelter of Kṛṣṇa, and she is rebelling against that and appealing for more difficulty.

It seems contradictory, but actually it is a manifestation of a very exalted soul’s realization in pure love of God.

Are you trying to train us for that realization, Śrīla Prabhupāda? I think you are. You are not one to encourage the self-satisfaction of quietly chanting Hare Kṛṣṇa in a solitary place. You are the person who embraced difficulty by carrying the movement of Lord Caitanya and Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī to the Western world, and in doing so you found your greatest pleasure. You are the person who said you wanted to die fighting, like Arjuna, with “your boots on” on the battlefield of the preaching mission. You are the one who said, when the infamous Mr. Nair of Juhu fame died, that you had enjoyed dealing with him, despite his nonsense, because you “liked a good fight.” You strained and strained to push on this Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement despite sometimes being treated with disdain by your contemporaries, and despite having to face enormous difficulties in dealing with the materialistic temperament of the Western world.

If we are to be successful in Kṛṣṇa consciousness, we also have to learn to practice Kṛṣṇa consciousness in this way—to be ready to do anything to push on your movement, despite any apparent personal inconveniences. We will find your satisfaction in those difficulties undertaken for your pleasure, and there we will find the shelter of Lord Kṛṣṇa.

You have said, “If a disciple is very serious to execute the mission of the spiritual master, he immediately associates with the Supreme Personality of Godhead by *vāṇī* or *vapuḥ*. This is the only secret of success in seeing the Supreme Personality of Godhead. Instead of being eager to see the Lord in some bush of Vṛndāvana while at the same time engaging in sense gratification, if one instead sticks to the principle of following the words of the spiritual master, he will see the Supreme Lord without difficulty.”

Please bless us, Śrīla Prabhupāda, with more opportunities to advance in your service. We are not worthy candidates for your mercy, but we are realizing that it is only through that mercy that we have any hope for success in life, and we are willing to try to do whatever is necessary to attain it.

Your fallen servant,  
Bhakti Caitanya Swami

## Bhakti Chāru Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please allow me to remain at your lotus feet as a speck of dust forever, and never let me drift away from this most wonderful shelter.

Now that you have withdrawn your pastimes from this material nature, we are facing so many crises and calamities. Although it is painful to go through these difficulties, we welcome them because they remind us of the importance of your presence in our lives. During your presence we were protected in all respects and there was no anxiety or fear. But now, with the passing of time and the cruel arrangements of nature, we are afflicted with greater and greater calamities. Once you told us, “When an *ācārya* leaves the planet there is bound to be crisis.” At that time I thought that this might have been the case in the past but that since you gave us ISKCON it would not happen to us. I was confident that we would continue to safely sail across this ocean of material nature without any difficulty, no matter how dangerous it might appear to be. But today that misconception has disappeared and I have realized that when an *ācārya* like Your Divine Grace disappears from this planet, it is nañve to think there will not be any crisis.

You came and formed Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s army to fight against the influence of Kali and establish dharma. By your able leadership you recruited soldiers from all over the world who fought most valiantly to establish the glory of the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Śrī Kṛṣṇa. But today, in the absence of your leadership your army is being devastated by the repeated onslaughts of Kali.

Why are we facing this defeat? Did we think that in your absence we had to replace you? Your words ring in my ears: “I was never separated from my spiritual master even for a moment. My spiritual master is always with me.”

It is essential for us that we also feel your constant presence in our midst. In reality you never actually left us. Although you have disappeared from our vision, you are always in our hearts. If we become fully convinced about this fact, then you will empower us with your guidance and protection. Then we will be able to properly defend ISKCON from all the onslaughts, no matter how severe they may be. Thus being protected by your spiritual potency, ISKCON will continue to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness all over the world once again.

You are “Prabhupāda,” the *ācārya* at whose feet all other *ācāryas* sit. This title has been exclusively reserved for Your Divine Grace in ISKCON. It indicates that in ISKCON you will always remain the supreme leader, that everyone in this movement, no matter how great he or she may be, must act as your loyal servant. Today, while ISKCON is afflicted with the onslaughts of our enemies, I wonder whether we have failed to keep you in your exclusive position as the preeminent *śikṣā-guru* of all the members of ISKCON. Have we started to develop the dangerous misconception that you are not present here anymore? Or have we become so deluded to think that you are now dead and gone? Do we think your pre-eminent seat in ISKCON has become vacant and someone else has to occupy that seat to fill the void? Have we consciously or unconsciously become your competitors? If that is the case, then naturally everything will fall apart, and over and above everything, our spiritual life will be destroyed.

Therefore, Śrīla Prabhupāda, my eternal lord and master, I fervently pray unto you: Please never allow me to become deluded with thoughts that you are not with us anymore. Please never allow me to become tempted to try to occupy your seat of absolute glory. Please never allow me to claim anything as mine. Please bless me that I can always feel that you are eternally present in my heart as my lord and master. Please let me constantly hear your divine instructions from the depth of my heart. Please let me always remain your humble servant, to be used in whichever way you see fit. Please allow me to surrender everything, including myself, at your divine lotus feet. Please never allow me to lose the most precious spiritual gift you have given me.

Aspiring to remain your servant eternally,  
Bhakti Chāru Swami

## Bhaktimārga Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Last night (March 25th) I had a dream. I woke up feeling very satisfied. The dream revealed that all your followers worldwide were harmoniously immersed in song, dance, and devotional work. Those devotees whom we had not seen for years were situated in resolve and contentment, working under the big umbrella of your ISKCON. It is not my place to say that this is a prophetic picture, but it is a wishful thought.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, please reveal to us what we can do to make this dream real.

Your servant,  
Bhaktimārga Swami

## Bhakti Rāghava Swami

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

*śrī-caitanya-mano-’bhīṣṭaṁ sthāpitaṁ yena bhū-tale*  
*svayaṁ rūpaḥ kadā mahyaṁ dadāti sva-padāntikam*

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*mūkaṁ karoti vācālaṁ paṅguṁ laṅghayate girim*  
*yat-kṛpā tam ahaṁ vande śrī-guruṁ dīna-tāraṇam*

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace, who continue to inspire and attract more and more conditioned souls to the process of pure devotional service.

I am most pleased when I hear some of our congregational members praise and glorify your transcendental name, qualities, and pastimes. They are simply awed and overwhelmed to see how practically single-handedly you were able to establish Kṛṣṇa consciousness all over the world in those few short years between 1965 and 1977. When asked once by one Indian gentleman whether you had performed any miracles during your preaching tours, you replied simply that yes, by the mercy of Lord Caitanya, you have been able to transform thousands of young Westerners who had strayed so far away from the Vedic culture (*mlecchas*) into real practicing Vaiṣṇavas. This is truly your miracle, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and that work, by your kind mercy, continues even today.

It is said that the pure and empowered Vaiṣṇava is he who at first sight can attract others to Kṛṣṇa consciousness and inspire them to chant Hare Kṛṣṇa. One can also understand the pure preacher Vaiṣṇava by the number of conditioned souls he can bring into Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you were and you are such a pure empowered Vaiṣṇava. Your Guru Mahārāja, Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura, used to say that one who has life, he preaches. By your untiring preaching efforts while physically present in your *vapu* form, you were able to attract and enthuse so many conditioned souls to take up the path of devotional service, thus completely changing their lives. And now this same preaching work continues through your *vāṇī,* your transcendental instructions coming in the form of your books, tapes, letters, conversations, and, last but not least, your many and increasing dedicated followers, both disciples and grand-disciples.

Still, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we are very immature children, unable to understand even some of your most basic instructions. You wanted us to work together, cooperate with one another, and help you push on this great mission of Lord Caitanya’s through the specific spiritual family tree of ISKCON, your established International Society for Krishna Consciousness. The spiritual family of ISKCON is meant to be very much like the extended family concept one finds in India. Although the father remains the head of the family—the eldest member and thus the most respected, the wisest and thus the most consulted, and the purest and thus the most worshiped—he still allows, encourages, and also helps his own children become fathers like him, fathers who will take on responsibilities very similar to his own.

In India, within an extended family situation, the sons of such a father live happily and peacefully under the same roof. As brothers they also learn to become good fathers, beget their own children, and, as members of one large extended family, cooperate with one another, all the time endeavoring to fulfill the desires of their respected father. The brothers, although fathers themselves, continue to work and assist one another. They are always cautious to carefully focus their attention on pleasing their father by carrying out his instructions. Similarly, their children, although each having a separate father, all see one another as brothers and sisters, respecting and seeing each and every elder of the family as their own father, with special attention and recognition to the grandfather.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, out of immaturity we have not been able to follow this most basic instruction to cooperate with one another. Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu has summarized the philosophy of Kṛṣṇa consciousness in three words: *sambandha* (relationships), *abhidheya* (activities), and *prayojana* (goal). Without a proper understanding of *sambandha-jñāna* (how we are meant to interact within the large extended family of ISKCON), the other levels will simply not be achieved. We have in so many ways deviated from this desire of yours that we work together cooperatively within the society.

It is my fervent prayer that we become (and we can) more mature in understanding how this can be accomplished. We need your kind blessings, however, for we remain foolish children who quarrel over petty issues. While the world remains ablaze with the ever-increasing attacks of Māyā, we fail in our duty to fight our real enemies. Please help us resolve our basic differences so we may again focus our energy on helping you attract more conditioned souls to Kṛṣṇa's lotus feet. Without your continued blessings, we remain lost children in the darkness of Kali-yuga.

Your servant,  
Bhakti Rāghava Swami

## Bhaktisvarūpa Dāmodara Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Most worshipable Śrīla Prabhupāda,

On this most auspicious day of the Śrī Vyāsa-pūjā celebration of Your Divine Grace, I humbly beg for a drop of your boundless sense of humility, your total sense of surrender and dedication to your divine master, Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Prabhupāda, and your complete sense of dependence on the mercy of Lord Caitanya and Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa so that this insignificant servant can gather strength to execute your divine order. All glories to your divine mission1

Your insignificant servant,  
Bhaktisvarūpa Dāmodara Swami

## Bhakti-Tīrtha Swami

*yasya prasādād bhagavat-prasādo*  
*yasyāprasādān na gatiḥ kuto ’pi*  
*dhyāyan stuvaṁs tasya yaśas tri-sandhyaṁ*  
*vande guroḥ śrī-caraṇāravindam*

**“By the mercy of the spiritual master one receives the benediction of Kṛṣṇa. Without the grace of the spiritual master, one cannot make any advancement. Therefore, I should always remember and praise the spiritual master. At least three times a day I should offer my respectful obeisances unto the lotus feet of my spiritual master.” (*****Śrī Gurv-aṣṭaka,*** **Verse 8)**

Vyāsa-pūjā is not a day that we remember the spiritual master but rather a special day for the disciple to reflect on the quality of his remembrances of the spiritual master every day. As Viśvanātha Cakravartī explains in this *Gurv-aṣṭaka* verse, at least three times a day one should offer one’s respectful obeisances to the lotus feet of Śrīla Gurudeva. With the recent passing of my mother and father, I find myself thinking much more of you as my eternal father. What a sweetness this is, especially since my material father, due to his own lack of feeling loved, never once told me that he loved me, but I can reflect on you as my father who showered so much love on all of your children, grandchildren, and great-grandchildren.

For about ten years when you were on the planet, I used to write you a letter in my diary almost every day. In this way, as I performed my services, I knew that at the end of the day I would have to come before you to present my case. I have piles and piles of books of diary notes where I have written to you begging for your mercy so I could be a better devotee, begging for forgiveness for offenses and dullness, and always begging to offer better service to your real servant. During the last year before you left the planet, instead of putting such emphasis on the diary notes, I directly sent you regular reports on the special preaching results with the scholars and universities in the communist countries who were accepting standing orders of your books. Recently, in a similar mood of trying to remember you more every day, I started writing short prayers, which some of my disciples that you have sent me have published in the form of books called *The Beggar*.

In my quarters at The Institute for Applied Spiritual Technology in Washington, D.C., I have framed a very short prayer (included in the preface of my first *Beggar* book), which I try to reflect on almost every day. So on this special day of your Vyāsa-pūjā, when I have a chance to try to reflect on the quality of my daily remembrances of you, I want to reflect on this daily prayer, but hopefully with more profundity. As I try to take more seriously my relationship with you, reminding myself that once I took initiation from you I was no longer a free agent, I realize that now I have a duty to try like anything to become simply a puppet for you. I am begging you every day to force me to do the right thing in the right way for you, even though every day I am praying to you to allow me somehow to be a carrier of deep love, especially to help those generals and soldiers of yours who have become wounded. I normally act more like a puppet who is trying to have a life of its own rather than work fully under the strings and care of the puppeteer. I beg that you please not give up on me, and that you empower me to be a genuinely caring devotee whose life becomes fully one with his message. So at this time I resubmit this daily prayer, but this year I promise you that I will serve and glorify you more than I have done with my little attempts in the past, and that I will intensify my daily remembrances of you by going deeper into this prayer:

“Dear Lord Kṛṣṇa, You have sent Your pure devotee, Śrīla Prabhupāda, to teach and give us real knowledge of how to serve You. You are the source of everything. To those who constantly worship You with love, You give the understanding, blessings, and protection by which they can attain You. Dear Lord, you have given me such valuable facility to reach You, but I don’t seem to know the worth. I keep demanding, without giving You what is expected of me.

“Dear Sweet Lord, please allow me to take full shelter at Your feet. Empower me to carry out Your desires, and give me the strength and understanding with which to come to You. I am Your property; do with me as You please. Never allow me to be a slave of my senses. Guide me from within, and let me continue to experience the love of Śrīla Prabhupāda and Your divine transcendental blessings.”

Your eternal son, who is begging to remember you every day and praying to become a genuine puppet who is operating under your perfect direction and care,

With Eternal Love,  
Bhakti-Tīrtha Swami

## Bhaktivaibhava Swami

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble and prostrated obeisances at your lotus feet!

**Lamentation**

In his *Navadvīpa-bhāva-taraṅga,* Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura describes the following episode:

“Jīva Gosvāmī was agitated with love as he remembered the Lord’s pastimes, and he rolled on the ground in Śrīvāsa’s courtyard. Then, just as Jīva Gosvāmī was getting up, he saw Lord Gaurāṅga dancing wonderfully amidst His intimate associates in *saṅkīrtana*. Advaita Ācārya, Nityānanda Prabhu, Gadādhara Paṇḍita, and Haridāsa Ṭhākura were dancing and singing. And they were joined by Śuklambara Brahmacārī and hundreds of others. Seeing this, Jīva Gosvāmī became unconscious with ecstatic love. Regaining his consciousness, he could no longer see that pastime. Jīva Gosvāmī began to cry and lament, ‘Why couldn’t I have taken birth a little earlier? I have not been fortunate enough to enjoy the bliss of such *kīrtana*! Nityānanda’s mercy is unlimited, however, for by His grace I have been fortunate for a moment’s time. My wish is to stay here forever so that the troubles of material existence will go far away. But the Lord’s order is stronger than the servant’s wish! My heart is beating wildly at the thought of leaving Māyāpur!’”

Nityānanda Prabhu mercifully took Jīva Gosvāmī to the various places where Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu had performed His transcendental pastimes in the association of His eternal associates, and while remembering Lord Gaurāṅga’s pastimes, Jīva Gosvāmī was overwhelmed with transcendental lamentation. Similarly, as this insignificant servant of Śrīla Prabhupāda’s remembers the various places of his transcendental pastimes, he often thinks, “Why couldn’t I have joined his divine association a little earlier in a part of the world where he was performing his pastime of inaugurating the world-wide *saṅkīrtana* movement? Providence has brought me so close to his lotus feet, but unfortunate as I am, I did not take proper advantage of this opportunity.” As the Age of Kali progresses, there is a danger that Śrīla Prabhupāda’s *tīrthas* will become somewhat over-shadowed by the evil qualities of quarrel and hypocrisy. There is nothing more lamentable in his world.

**Hope**

Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura further describes that in Māyāpur there is a mysterious and cardinal place known as Īśodyāna, or “The Lord’s Garden.” Materially it is not possible to find any historical or geographical reference to such a spot. The contrast between material and spiritual vision is wonderfully illustrated by Ṭhākura Bhaktivinoda: “One whose eyes are covered by material illusion sees only a small house situated here, plus some ordinary earth, water, and a few articles. But if Māyā becomes merciful and lifts her covering, then one sees a grand spiritual dwelling.”

Those who have material eyes cannot perceive the grand spiritual dwelling Śrīla Prabhupāda created in his International Society for Krishna Consciousness. They will be deprived of viewing that wonderful garden of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, fully equipped with everything needed to serve Śrī Śrī Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa and to benefit the entire world. Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura, who knew the inner intentions of his father, Ṭhākura Bhaktivinoda, and who was very expert in fulfilling his wishes, duly excavated this place for the benefit of everyone. On the bank of Pṛthu-kuṇḍa he manifested the lake known as Rādhā-kuṇḍa, and upon its bank he reconstructed the Īśodyāna described by Śrīla Bhaktivinoda. Similarly, by the mercy of those who know the innermost desire of Śrīla Prabhupāda, the wonderful garden of Kṛṣṇa consciousness he so mercifully created will always be fully manifest for everyone’s eyes to see. I am hopeful that by sincerely following Śrīla Prabhupāda’s instructions and by always endeavoring to fulfill his innermost desire to benefit the whole world with Kṛṣṇa consciousness, the brilliant reality of the eternal garden of ISKCON will always be visible to everyone’s eyes. My only hope is that Śrīla Prabhupāda will bestow his causeless mercy upon this insignificant servant so that I will always remain under the protection and shelter of his lotus feet in this wonderful and mysterious garden.

Your insignificant servant,  
Dāsa A.C. Bhaktivaibhava Swami

## Bhūrijana dāsa

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Please accept my most humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace, whose preeminence is beyond dispute. Even amongst Gauḍīya *ācāryas* your position is “svayaṁ prakāśa,” self-illuminated. Practically speaking, true and pure spiritual leadership is personified in you, for you masterminded an organization, established effective and systematic preaching strategies, and then did the impossible: inspired conditioned souls to participate, work tirelessly, and cooperatively preach. All along it was you that was the breath of life within ISKCON, your society, for it was you who had breathed life into countless dead bodies.

Wonderfully, amazingly, almost ironically, regardless of the difficulties that your society endures, as time passes, recognition and glorification of your own unique and empowered position seem only to increase. Yet, Śrīla Prabhupāda, I know that the “offering” of your spreading fame is not ours, nor is it to our credit. Rather, it occurs because of who you are and what you did, and it occurs despite the failures of us, your servants.

Personally, I feel miserable due to my own mistakes, my own limitations, my own weaknesses, and my own lack of sincerity. Yet I nevertheless—almost contradictorily—desire to serve you in full Kṛṣṇa consciousness, constantly chant Kṛṣṇa’s names, and remember Kṛṣṇa in great happiness. Is following your personal order to “take this Kṛṣṇa consciousness and be happy” even possible for me, a fallen offender?

My own personal capacity seems to have lessened as I’ve aged. My intelligence has all along assisted my false ego in creating and maintaining my pride, but I now find myself dumber and understanding less. Please, stay always near this fully grown yet fully dependent son, who without you remains unable to step forward even once. Please always stay ever-so-near as I continue my clumsy attempts to serve you. Please come quickly, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and please always stay near. How I actually need you, Prabhupāda!

Always your servant,  
Bhūrijana dāsa

## Bīr Krishna dās Goswami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

Your ISKCON is going through many changes at this time, and the rate of these changes seems to increase exponentially each year. Some devotees may perceive these changes in a negative way, longing for the “good old days” in ISKCON.

Caitanya Mahāprabhu has ordered us to not be satisfied until all the living entities in this world are back with Him in His abode. For this order to be carried out under your guidance, changes are essential; paradigms must be shifted.

Of course, these changes should never touch the Kṛṣṇa conscious *siddhānta* as taught by you. If they did, we would quickly become *asāra,* or useless. The foundation that you laid must never change.

Changes should reflect the need to present the *siddhānta* in changing times in this mundane world, and at different stages in the progress of your movement. These changes can be made only after deliberation by your dedicated servants who are pure in their thoughts and actions.

I have always kept in my heart the guidelines you gave us for this movement: purity is the force, books are the basis, utility is the principle, and preaching is the essence. We see in your pastimes the perfection of the expression of these four principles. Hence your incomparable success in preaching.

While you were here on this planet you were never satisfied to rest on your achievements and retire happily in Kṛṣṇa consciousness. In the mood of spiritual greed and anxiety, you pushed us to bring more and more souls to Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

You stated this in a wonderful way in your poem written aboard the *Jaladuta:*

“As the vast mercy of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu conquers all directions, a flood of transcendental ecstasy will certainly cover the land. When all the sinful, miserable living entities become happy, the Vaiṣṇava’s desire is then fulfilled.”

You are the quintessential devotee in the mood of Vāsudeva Datta. You are only concerned for the happiness of others. To fully satisfy you, we must adopt this same selfless mood.

It was interesting that you called ISKCON the Hare Kṛṣṇa Movement. You could have called it the Hare Kṛṣṇa Society or Hare Kṛṣṇa Organization or used some other static nomenclature. You deliberately used the word “movement,” indicating that we should always be moving towards the goal of engulfing the whole world with Lord Caitanya’s mercy.

Movement means change. Growth means change. Structures that are applicable for small organizations are not applicable for large ones. We learn from our mistakes in management and correct those mistakes. We learn to imbibe your balanced compassionate mood.

It is now obvious that the *āśrama-*based paradigm, which was the best strategy for starting this movement and impelling its dramatic growth in the 60s and 70s, can no longer serve us in the next stage of the movement’s growth. Seeing that *varṇāśrama* had not been instituted in your Society, you stated that only half your work was done.

*Varṇāśrama* is the perfect strategy for enabling us to expand unlimitedly, because the individual’s service is custom-tailored to his /her nature. To jump-start the movement, it was necessary to ask everyone to “do the needful” and put aside their nature for some time. In the next stage, *varṇāśrama* allows us to expand far and wide rather than being compact in our temples. It also enables individuals to remain steady in their service, and above and beyond that they feel renewed enthusiasm to serve you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Spiritually, psychologically, managerially, and materially, it works.

Instead of your Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement being limited by the living facilities in the temples, the whole world potentially becomes an *āśrama* as devotees become properly situated in their *varṇas* and *āśramas*. Then your vision of making the whole world Kṛṣṇa conscious becomes a practical reality.

Instead of a few gurus serving in our temples, we will have an army of spiritual masters preaching out of their homes and making every town and village Kṛṣṇa conscious.

Instead of temples being seen as residential facilities, they will be seen as spiritual educational institutions that will teach the curriculum to train up the army of spiritual masters that will be needed.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I pray that you give us the vision and purity to carry this movement to this next stage. Let us see the present difficulties as growing pains directly sent by Kṛṣṇa, difficulties that are meant to push us to fulfill your desire to give everyone the opportunity to taste the highest nectar, the ocean of devotional service as taught by Your Divine Grace.

Hoping to be your instrument,  
The servant of your servants,  
Bīr Krishna dās Goswami

## Giridhārī Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

The past year has been a test of faith for your sincere followers. Your ISKCON body has sustained attacks from inside and out. Leading *sannyāsīs* have fallen. The poison theorists and the ritvik proponents have continued to recycle their worn-out rhetoric and convert your followers to their missions. Conflicts with the Gauḍīya Maṭha have escalated. Lawsuits loom in the background. The GBC has failed to lead the Society. Kali must certainly be laughing with delight.

Since *kali* literally means “quarrel,” we can surmise that the personality of Kali has entered our movement and gotten a strong foothold. At the slightest provocation, Godbrothers and Godsisters attack and try to destroy one another. Kali even has us going to court to decide who is guru and who is not. Manifesting right before our eyes are some of the same acts that crippled the Gauḍīya Maṭha.

Will we destroy ISKCON as well? Maybe I am naively optimistic, but I think not. I believe that you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and Lord Kṛṣṇa are purging our material desires and creating a purer and stronger ISKCON. Perhaps this purge will forcibly take away some of our properties, monies, and positions if they have become obstacles to purely chanting the holy name, spreading your movement, and taking care of your devotees. Removing these impurities may indeed be painful, and there are likely to be casualties, but once the disease has been removed, the body will grow stronger than ever before. Of this, I am certain.

In the face of such calamities, even your most dedicated followers could easily become disheartened. Some of them have.

But many have not. These stalwarts remain fixed in service to your lotus feet. They see these rough times as an increased impetus to take shelter of the *sādhana,* service, and instructions you have given them. It would seem that these exalted Vaiṣṇavas have understood what it means to remain fixed on your order no matter what.

These true followers of yours have imbibed the mood you had in serving your Guru Mahārāja. I see them having the same fixedness in serving your instructions that Śrīla Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura speaks of in his commentary on *Bhagavad-gītā* 2.41:

The best kind of intelligence I can have is intelligence used in the service of Kṛṣṇa. Intelligence is defined as “fixed” when it is intent upon my spiritual master’s instructions, such as chanting the name of Kṛṣṇa, remembering His activities, and performing service to His lotus feet. My spiritual master’s instructions are my *sādhana* and my life, both in the beginning stages of *bhakti* as well as in *bhakti’s* perfectional stage. I desire only to follow his instructions; I accept nothing else as my life’s work, even in dreams. Whether I am happy or distressed, whether the material world remains or is destroyed, I don’t care. There is no loss for me. I simply must carry out the orders of my spiritual master. Fixedness upon his orders is determined intelligence and devotional service, and only by being fixed on his orders can such determined intelligence prosper.

This is the same theme that you yourself have stated again and again throughout your teachings: If the disciple simply remains fixed on following the instructions of the spiritual master, he will overcome all obstacles and attain perfection.

When one becomes serious to follow the mission of the spiritual master, his resolution is tantamount to seeing the Supreme Personality of Godhead. As explained before, this means meeting the Supreme Personality of Godhead in the instruction of the spiritual master. This is technically called *vāṇī-sevā*. Śrīla Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura states in his *Bhagavad-gītā* commentary on the verse *vyavasāyātmikā buddhir ekeha kuru-nandana* (Bg. 2.41) that one should serve the words of the spiritual master. The disciple must stick to whatever the spiritual master orders. Simply by following on that line, one sees the Supreme Personality of Godhead. \[*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 4.28.51, purport\]

This is the secret of success. After being initiated and receiving the orders of the spiritual master, the disciple should unhesitatingly think about the instructions or orders of the spiritual master and should not allow himself to be disturbed by anything else. This is also the verdict of Śrīla Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura, who, while explaining a verse of *Bhagavad-gītā* (*vyavasāyātmikā buddhir ekeha kuru-nandana,* Bg. 2.41), points out that the order of the spiritual master is the life substance of the disciple. The disciple should not consider whether he is going back home, back to Godhead; his first business should be to execute the order of his spiritual master. Thus a disciple should always meditate on the order of the spiritual master, and that is perfectional meditation. Not only should he meditate upon that order, but he should find out the means by which he can perfectly worship and execute it. (*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 4.24.15 Purport)

This is called *vyavasāyātmikā buddhiḥ*. I may be quite incomplete. It doesn’t matter. But if I try to follow the words of my Guru Mahārāja, then I become complete. This is the secret. *Yasya deve parā bhaktir yathā deve tathā gurau*. If one has strong faith in the Supreme Personality of Godhead and as much faith in the guru, *yathā deve tathā gurau,* then the revealed scriptures become manifest. It is not the education. It is not the scholarship. It is faith in Kṛṣṇa and guru. Therefore *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* says *guru-kṛṣṇa-kṛpāya pāya bhakti-latā-bīja*. Not by education, not by scholarship, never says. Caitanya Mahāprabhu says, *guru-kṛṣṇa-kṛpāya,* by the mercy of guru, by the mercy of Kṛṣṇa. It is a question of mercy. It is not a question of scholarship or opulence or richness. No. The whole *bhakti-mārga* depends on the mercy of the Lord. So we have to seek the mercy. \[Lecture on *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 1.15.30\]

This is the panacea for ISKCON. We must simply meditate on, worship, and execute your instructions with collective and discriminative intelligence. And for those of us like me, who possess little intelligence, we should simply assist your truly intelligent followers in any small way possible.

The servant of your true followers,  
Giridhārī Swami

## Girirāj Swami

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my prostrated obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

During the past year I discovered I had a heart condition called angina. At first I was shocked. How could someone my age have angina? Old people get angina.

I did not know much about the condition. I had one old friend in India who had angina and died of a heart attack within two years. I also felt I could die at any moment. I considered, What should I do with the rest of my life?

Obviously I had to do what is most important to you and me. I thought of your instruction to me to write. Yes, I must write. And I must take shelter of the holy name of Kṛṣṇa and develop love for Him. I must prepare myself for the time of death.

Then I came across a letter you wrote to me in which you seemed to confirm the same conclusions: take care of your health, chant the holy name—and write.

If you are feeling tired, you may take rest. Your body is very valuable. It is dedicated to Krishna, so you must take care of the body very carefully. The best medicine is to rest and chant Hare Krishna mantra, along with the doctor’s prescription. Hare Krishna mantra is bhavausadhi, the panacea for all material disease.

In the same letter, you continued:

Keep on with the practice of writing articles; in the midst of your heavy duties go on writing something glorifying the Lord and put our philosophy into words. Writing articles means to express oneself how he is understanding the whole philosophy. So this writing is necessary for everyone. \[Śrīla Prabhupāda letter, 12 August 1971\]

Still, I felt bad I would not be able to travel and preach and serve as before. Then a Godbrother phoned and quoted you: “The duty of a *sannyāsī* is to write transcendental literature for the benefit of others.” At least I could offer you some service.

And you have been so kind. A nice couple south of Los Angeles have given me complete use of their town house, which also accommodates their beautiful Deities of Śrī Śrī Kṛṣṇa-Balarāma, Śrī Giri-Govardhana, and Your Divine Grace, while they stay nearby. They cook exactly according to my requirements, accompany me on my walk, and facilitate my service in every way.

You have given me the support and love of sincere and competent devotees. Even my former family, who were never very favorable to Kṛṣṇa consciousness, have come forward with all kinds of help. And my dear Godbrothers have given me their sublime association and sagacious advice even from afar. In fact, you have manifested yourself in all of them, as your Guru Mahārāja explains:

My Gurudeva is residing in many different personalities. If he does not reside in many different personalities, then who will protect me? Those whom my Gurudeva considered close to him are my saviors. But I hope I never have to see the face of such evil-doing rascals as those who have criticized the lotus feet of my Gurudeva, or those who have somehow supported such criticism. \[Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura\]

My health program includes strict diet, regular exercise, and the loving shelter of Giri-Govardhana and the holy name. The same program is also most conducive to spiritual life and to what may be my best contribution to you and your mission—writing about you and developing my relationship with Kṛṣṇa and Your Divine Grace.

**Forever**

“I will never die,” you said. “I will live forever in my books, and you will utilize.”

Over the past year you have given me many important instructions through your books and recorded talks. Today I wish to share some of the significant passages that appeared to guide me, in the hope that they may serve to encourage and enlighten others as they did me.

In Bombay you told me, “If you sincerely try your best to please Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa will be satisfied—even if others are not.”

Still, we do want to please others. Yet they may not be happy. Still, you give us hope:

Just like there was a clique. There was a *brāhmaṇa*. His name was Bhagavān. So he had some enemies. So the enemies, ten enemies, they sat down in ten places, and wherever he was passing, “Oh, you have become ghost! You have become ghost!” “Oh, how I have become? I am living. How I have become ghost?” Next post he goes, he also says, “Oh, you have become ghost! You have become ghost!” In this way, when he passes ten posts, he thinks, “I might be, I have become ghost. Otherwise everyone says I have become ghost.” So *daśa cakre bhagavān bhūta*.  
So if everyone says I am lowborn, I am lowborn, so I also think I am lowborn. But I am not lowborn. *Ahaṁ brahmāsmi*. I am *brahmāsmi*. I am spirit soul. I am born of Kṛṣṇa. Kṛṣṇa is my father. Take like that. Think like that. That is twice-born. That is twice-born. So Kṛṣṇa says, *māṁ hi pārtha vyapāśritya:* “Anyone who takes shelter of Me, never mind what he is...Never mind he is. He may be lowborn, he may be a *śūdra,* he may be woman, he may be whatever he may be.” *Te ’pi yānti parāṁ gatim:* “He can also go to My kingdom.” \[Śrīla Prabhupāda lecture, 29 July 1966\]

When people speak of *varṇāśrama-dharma,* you tell us the conclusion is to serve you:

The Aryans \[follow\] these Vedic principles, *varṇāśrama-dharma. Varnāśramācāravatā puruṣeṇa paraḥ pumān, viṣṇur ārādhyate.* What is the purpose of *varṇāśrama?* Now, *viṣṇu ārādhana.* Why Viṣṇu *ārādhana*?...That is also answered by Lord Śiva. When he was asked by Pārvatī, “What is the best form of worship?” he recommended, *ārādhanānāṁ sarveṣām:* “There are many different types of worship.” *Sarveṣām. Viṣṇur ārādhanaṁ param.*...Here Lord Śiva says, *viṣṇur ārādhanaṁ param.* Then again he stresses, *tasmāt parataraṁ devī tadīyānāṁ, tadīyānām ārādhanam:* “Viṣṇu *ārādhanam* is the best, but better than this best there is another *ārādhanam*.” What is that? *Tadīyānām ārādhanānām:* “To worship the Vaiṣṇava.” Viṣṇu *ārādhana* is the topmost, but Vaiṣṇava *ārādhana* is still more. Narottama dāsa Ṭhākura therefore says, *chāḍiyā vaiṣṇava-sevā, nistāra pāyeche kebā.* The Vaiṣṇava *ārādhana* is so important.

Therefore Kṛṣṇa says \[*Bhagavad-gītā* 4.34\],

*tad viddhi praṇipātena paripraśnena sevayā*  
*upadekṣyanti tad jñānaṁ jñāninās tattva-darśinaḥ*

\[Śrīla Prabhupāda lecture, 18 September1975\]

When darkness seems to engulf us, you explain why, and indicate that light may come:

Lord Kṛṣṇa said to King Yudhiṣṭhira, “My devotee is not deterred by any adverse conditions of life; he always remains firm and steady. Therefore I give Myself to him, and I favor him so that he can achieve the highest success of life.” The mercy bestowed upon the tried devotee by the Supreme Personality is described as *brahma,* which indicates that the greatness of that mercy can be compared only to the all-pervasive greatness. *Brahma* means unlimitedly great and unlimitedly expanding. That mercy is also described as *paramam,* for it has no comparison within this material world, and it is also called *sūkṣmam,* very fine. Not only is the Lord’s mercy upon the tried devotee great and unlimitedly expansive, but it is of the finest quality of transcendental love between the devotee and the Lord. Such mercy is further described as *cin-mātram,* completely spiritual. The use of the word *mātram* indicates absolute spirituality, with no tinge of material qualities. That mercy is also called *sat* (eternal) and *anantakam* (unlimited). Since the devotee of the Lord is awarded such unlimited spiritual benefit, why should he worship the demigods? A devotee of Kṛṣṇa does not worship Lord Śiva or Brahmā or any other, subordinate demigod. He completely devotes himself to the transcendental loving service of the Supreme Personality of Godhead....  
When after the dense, dark night there is finally sunrise in the morning, it is very pleasant; when there is scorching heat, cold water is very pleasant; and when there is freezing winter, hot water is very pleasant. Similarly, when a devotee, after experiencing the distress of the material world, relishes the spiritual happiness awarded by the Lord, his position is still more pleasant and enjoyable. \[*Kṛṣṇa, the Supreme Personality of Godhead,* Chapter 88\]

And when separation breaks our hearts, you assure us that if we follow your instructions we will serve you and Kṛṣṇa eternally:

There is a *rasa,* mellow—every *ānanda*. Just like if you take a nice fruit, mango, the taste is pleasing. That is called *rasa,* that taste....So this picture \[of Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa with the *gopīs*\] is *ānanda-cinmaya-rasa*. Here we have got that taste of *rasa* in a perverted manner. But *cinmaya-rasa* means it continues. This Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa with the *gopīs,* they are enjoying, dancing, chanting. That is eternally; that is never stopped. It is not that they become old and there is no more enjoyment or they are separated or somebody, Kṛṣṇa goes somewhere and the Rādhārāṇī goes to somewhere. No. Everything is eternal....  
So when you enter into the Kṛṣṇa science you get so much volumes of transcendental knowledge, and we are trying to present before you this knowledge in so many ways, by cleansing your heart from this material contamination....When one is not contaminated, he’s relishing this Hare Kṛṣṇa mantra by associating with Kṛṣṇa. Just like Rūpa Gosvāmī, he’s also chanting. Caitanya Mahāprabhu is also chanting, but His chanting and my chanting, there is little difference. That chanting is on the transcendental platform, actually relishing with Kṛṣṇa. My chanting...In the beginning there are three stages: offensive stages, liberated stage, and relishing stage....So we have to defend ourselves from the offenses....Then our chanting will be purified. And when the chanting is purified, immediately you are liberated. And after liberation? *Brahma-bhūtaḥ prasannātmā:* your life of devotional service actually begins. Then you relish reciprocation of love with Kṛṣṇa....  
So keep on chanting, you keep yourself purified, *māyā* cannot touch you....So this mantra, *apavitraḥ pavitro vā sarvāvasthām,* any condition of life, you can simply remember Puṇḍarīkākṣam, Kṛṣṇa. As soon as you chant “Kṛṣṇa,” you remember everything.  
So this remembering is the whole thing. If you have got always within your heart Kṛṣṇa, by sound or by mental thoughts, by work, some way or other, by reading books, any way, if simply you keep your consciousness in Kṛṣṇa, then your life is successful. That’s all. \[Śrīla Prabhupāda lecture, 16 July 1969\]

Thus we take shelter of the holy name in any condition.

You have left so many instructions for us all. But how do we know which apply to us? On the auspicious occasion of your appearance day, we pray you will appear in our hearts and give us the intelligence to know which instructions are meant for us, and how to apply them in pure devotional service.

“To those who are constantly devoted to serving Me with love, I give the understanding by which they can come to Me.”

So, you teach us to love, you and Kṛṣṇa:

We have a propensity to love, but we do not know how to make our love perfect. That perfection is possible when we love Kṛṣṇa. Now, in my old age, I am wandering all over the world teaching that everyone can become happy by practicing Kṛṣṇa consciousness. It is not that I love only my countrymen, only Hindus or Bengalis. I love everyone, even the animals. \[*The Quest for Enlightenment,* 7.2\]

We learned to open our heart to you and Kṛṣṇa:

*Guhyam* means “very confidential things,” and *ākhyāti* means “disclosing.” You must disclose your innermost thoughts to your beloved, and he’ll disclose his innermost thoughts to you....These are the six ways of increasing love. If you act in these ways with Kṛṣṇa, you will develop love for Him. \[*The Quest for Enlightenment,* 7.1\]

Thus you teach us *bhāgavata-dharma,* or *prema-dharma,* following Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu:

The mission of our Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement is to teach people how to love Kṛṣṇa. Only then will they be fully satisfied. As it is said in the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* \[1.2.6\]:

*sa vai puṁsāṁ paro dharmo yato bhaktir adhokṣaje*  
*ahaituky apratihatā yayātmā suprasīdati*

**“The supreme occupation for all humanity is that which awakens loving devotional service to Kṛṣṇa. When such devotional service is uninterrupted and free of selfish motives, it completely satisfies the self.”**

Teaching this truth is the mission of the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement. If people will accept it, all their problems will be solved, and they will be happy.” \[*The Quest for Enlightenment,* 7.2\]

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we are yours.

Forever yours,  
Your eternal, dependent servant,  
Girirāj Swami

## Gopāl Krishna Goswami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances at the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to your tireless efforts to bless the fallen conditioned souls of Kali-yuga, especially myself!

Every year as I sit down to write your Vyāsa-pūjā offering, I realize how unqualified I am to describe your transcendental qualities and pastimes. You possess *acintya-śakti*. Therefore, I cannot measure your greatness. You distributed Kṛṣṇa consciousness in its pure form to all parts of the world, to all classes of men, thus making the holy name of Kṛṣṇa heard in almost every town and village. This had never been done before in Vaiṣṇava history. You have opened up the treasure house of love of God stored in the *Bhagavad-gītā, Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* and *Caitanya-caritāmṛta*. Without you, these literatures would never have been revealed in virtually every major language of the world. The brilliance of your transcendental purports will continue to guide humanity for the next 10,000 years.

Without you, the world would never have seen so many hundreds of beautiful temples of Gaura-Nitāi, Jagannātha, Baladeva, and Subhadrā, and Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa. These temples serve as embassies of the spiritual world, where anyone can get trained in the process of devotional service. You personally built major temples in Bombay, Hyderabad, Māyāpur, and Vṛndāvana and guided and blessed your followers to construct major projects in Ahmedabad, Bangalore, Bhubaneswar, and Delhi. Without you we would never have known the value of *kṛṣṇa-prasādam,* which purifies the mind, senses, and intelligence. Without you we would never have understood the significance of the holy *dhāmas*. The list of what you have given us is inexhaustible. Your devotion to Lord Kṛṣṇa is unparalleled in history. Your scholarship has been appreciated the world over. Your purity is untainted.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, when you were physically present on the planet it was easy to solve problems. Everyone had full faith in your authority because you manifested all the qualities of a pure devotee. Hence it was easier to settle differences and solve problems. Presently your ISKCON Society is going through a severe test. Some senior leaders deviated spiritually and thereby created a disturbance. Some are attempting to push a concocted philosophy of the *guru-tattva* with the help of material agencies. Time and again you said that our love for you would be shown by our cooperating with one another. All problems can be solved if we surrender to your instructions on all issues—spiritual and managerial. We have to put you even more in the center. We need to work together to make your ISKCON strong: free from sectarianism, egotism and strife. We should stick exactly to the principles of purity and practice humility and simplicity. Your ISKCON movement is a manifestation of your mercy upon the fallen souls of Kali-yuga and the only real hope for humanity for the next 10,000 years. Therefore everyone who calls himself a Prabhupādānuga must endeavor to follow your instructions on purity and humility. This will be the most perfect Vyāsa-pūjā offering at your lotus feet. Also, I want to beg for your continued mercy so that I can go on doing some insignificant service in your Society in the association of devotees till the end of my life.

Your insignificant servant,  
Gopāl Krishna Goswami

## Guru Prasād Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to your undaunted and ever-increasing *saṅkīrtana* effort, which continues to inspire the whole world, regardless of Kali’s travail to divide the servants of the Lord.

In this past year I have seen many difficult situations arise, both personally and collectively. Sometimes it is bewildering, and throughout the year I have tried to take shelter of the instructions of Queen Kuntī, especially where she states that Kṛṣṇa’s pastimes are *viḍambanam,* bewildering. His purposes are known to Him. Similarly, your mission is simple to understand, but your personality and the degree of purity you possess are difficult for those of us engrossed in ignorance and self-love to fathom. Thus it becomes bewildering to only rely on the simple approach to Kṛṣṇa consciousness that you taught and demonstrated by your own example. I see these situations before us as the gentle but firm prodding by Kṛṣṇa to help us understand how to serve you in the same uncomplicated manner that you served the *paramparā*. You were never bewildered by constant waves of opposition, by senseless antagonism to your unceasing efforts to spread Kṛṣṇa’s glories in a way never imagined previously. Of course, the resistance I am facing is due to my own actions and lack of determination, not my virtuousness, as in your case. But the effect is an opportunity to better myself and become a more apt and capable servant of Your Divine Grace. Your example has served to increase my enthusiasm and to welcome such situations as steps toward taking more shelter of your lotus feet.
