I beg that you bestow on me sufficient purity, vision, and intelligence, allowing me to glorify you in the proper way, which means giving every ounce of my existence and hoping to gain one drop of your mercy. I pray that, being the loving father you are, you impart to me countless opportunities, compensating for my rebellious spirit to defy and resist that which is most beneficial for me—your causeless mercy and service, birth after birth.

Your begging servant,  
Guru Prasād Swami

## Hridayānanda dāsa Goswami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Śrīla Prabhupāda,

In the stormy sea of this world, your lotus feet are the safe and tranquil harbor. In the quicksand of all doubting all, your faith in us is the spiritual engine of our progress. In an age of sanctimony, your sacred leadership is the gold standard of purity. In the pain of illusion, the balm of your image, voice, example, and order bring back reality.

Kindly keep me on your caravan as it boldly wends through the gloom of Kali and crosses the risky river bordering the worlds, until at last its blessed passengers alight in perfect peace at the lotus feet of Śrī Śrī Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa.

Your hopeful servant,  
Hridayānanda dās Goswami

## Kavicandra Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

As I try to examine the past year, there are so many things that have passed that I don’t know where to start. There have been apparent great successes and apparent great problems, which seem to be ever-increasing. We have surely seen that Kṛṣṇa is the supreme controller and that it is very difficult to understand or predict what He will do next. As Śrīla Kṛṣṇadāsa Kavirāja says in his *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* (*Antya* 9.115)

*ke kahite pāre gaurera āścarya svabhāva?*  
*brahmā-śiva ādi yāṅra nā pāya antarbhāva*

**“No one can estimate the wonderful characteristics of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. Even Lord Brahmā and Lord Śiva cannot understand the intentions of the Lord.”**

I am trying to understand whether I have actually done any devotional service, and I can only depend on your causeless mercy to see that my efforts have been accepted. On my own merit I cannot hope for any progress.

I fervently pray that I can become free from *vaiṣṇava-aparādha*. From that point I can begin to make progress toward actually pleasing you. Our scriptures stress that *vaiṣṇava-aparādha* should be avoided, The warnings are very powerful:

*yadi vaiṣṇava-aparādha uṭhe hātī mātā*  
*upāḍe vā chiṇḍe, tāra śukhi’ yāya pātā*

**“As a mad elephant may trample all the plants in a garden, so by committing one offense at the lotus feet of a Vaiṣṇava one may spoil all the devotional service he has accumulated in his life.” (*****Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Madhya*** **19.156).**

And: “If a personality even of the stature of Lord Śiva commits an offense against a devotee of the Lord, then according to the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* he soon meets his end.” (*Caitanya-Bhāgavata, Madhya* 13.388)

Although we daily witness the truth of these scriptural injunctions and see devotees fall down, somehow or other we cannot give up the propensity to criticize devotees. Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu transferred all the sinful reactions of Jagāi and Mādhāi to those who criticize Vaiṣṇavas. Until we can seriously take up the practice of glorifying devotees, we will never taste the nectar of the holy name.

*nāmnām akāri bahudhā nija-sarva-śaktis*  
*tatrārpitā niyamitaḥ smaraṇe na kālaḥ*  
*etādṛśī tava kṛpā bhagavan mamāpi*  
*durdaivam īdṛśam ihājani nānurāgaḥ*

**“My Lord, O Supreme Personality of Godhead, in Your holy name there is all good fortune for the living entity, and therefore You have many names, such as Kṛṣṇa and Govinda, by which You expand Yourself. You have invested all Your potencies in those names, and there are no hard and fast rules for remembering them. My dear Lord, although You bestow such mercy upon the fallen, conditioned souls by liberally teaching Your holy names, I am so unfortunate that I commit offenses while chanting the holy name, and therefore I do not achieve attachment for chanting.” (*****Śikṣāṣṭaka*** **2, Antya 20.16)**

Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu took great pleasure in glorifying the devotees, and I pray that He will bless all of us with a taste for that sublime activity. By that simple process we can properly utilize our time for your pleasure.

Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu instructed that by two activities we can get the lotus feet of Kṛṣṇa: *vaiṣṇava-sevā* and *nāma-saṅkīrtana,* service to the Vaiṣṇavas and chanting the holy name with them.

Begging to be a humble servant at your lotus feet,  
Kavicandra Swami

## Madhusevita dāsa

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my prostrated obeisances at your lotus feet.

I am writing to you from a tiny town on a tiny planet in a tiny universe. You once visited and blessed this place in the course of your glorious journey for the upliftment of the conditioned souls. As you know, this planet is now populated by a race of ferocious cannibals that kill their mothers for the purpose of eating them, rape their children or sometimes kill them with the help of white-dressed witches and sorcerers who later prepare potions called beauty creams from the small dead bodies. They also steal each other’s spouses and start cruel wars on every silly pretext. Immensely proud of their so-called culture, they make so much effort to defend it by any means.

I am reminded of your words in the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam:*

Unfortunately, society is in such an uncivilized state that there are so-called *mahātmās* who are prepared to kill cows and children and stop the Hare Kṛṣṇa movement. Such uncivilized activities were actually demonstrated in opposition to the Hare Kṛṣṇa movement’s Bombay center, Hare Kṛṣṇa Land. As Kaṁsa was not expected to kill the beautiful child of Devakī and Vasudeva, the uncivilized society, although unhappy about the advancement of the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement, cannot be expected to stop it. Yet we must face many difficulties in many different ways. Although Kṛṣṇa cannot be killed, Vasudeva, as the father of Kṛṣṇa, was trembling because in affection he thought that Kaṁsa would immediately come and kill his son. Similarly, although the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement and Kṛṣṇa are not different and no asuras can check it, we are afraid that at any moment the asuras can stop this movement in any part of the world. \[*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 10.3.22\]

The asuric mentality permeates practically everything in society, and we have by now historical evidence that it also penetrated our movement, carried by the uncontrolled minds and senses of some of our members. The illusion of being “almost” on your level, prompted by envy, and immediately followed by lust, anger, greed, and madness; the cruel-heartedness against dependents; or the vision of ISKCON as a society to exploit for some economic purpose, are certainly not the symptoms of divine inspiration. In addition to these internal problems, the external *asuras* are relentlessly there with those who watch us and would like to take advantage of our weak condition.

So we are under attack both externally and internally, and I have to confess to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, that for the first time in twenty-six years I am at times afraid that ISKCON could be severely damaged by the attacks of some envious elements or brought to impotency by so-called reforms born out of frustration. A case in point is this *ṛtvik* ideology, which, by twisting the role of *dīkṣā* in the devotee’s life, serves the same purpose as that nearly accomplished by those who sought to usurp your position. If only your role as the permanent, fundamental *śikṣā-guru* in everyone’s life were properly understood, so many problems could be avoided.

I am also afraid for myself, because I am not like Vasudeva or you, whose fear is on the transcendental platform. I need this movement for the continuation of my spiritual life, and I need it the way you delivered it to us. My humble request to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, is that the devotees of this movement, including this lowly self, may be allowed to simply continue their *sādhana* and service with undeviated attention.

*Kṛṣṇa se tomāra, kṛṣṇa dite pāra, tomāra śakati āche.* If you pray to Kṛṣṇa, He won’t refuse you anything.

For our part, we know what you have instructed us to do. Each honest devotee that has come to be part of this movement knows the way he or she should think, speak, and act. In the same portion of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* you elucidate the above point clearly:

Men who are like Kaṁsa are very much afraid of the movement’s progress and acceptance by the younger generation, but as Kṛṣṇa could not be killed by Kaṁsa, this movement cannot be checked by men of Kaṁsa’s class. The movement will go on increasing more and more, provided the leaders of the movement remain firmly Kṛṣṇa conscious by following the regulative principles and the primary activities of chanting the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mantra* regularly. \[*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 10.2.20\]

I beg forgiveness if this year’s offering is more of a lamenting plea than a due glorification. The realization of my inconsistency and mediocrity weighs like a stone in my heart. Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, continue engaging me in service and revive my heart with your life-giving compassion. If, even rightly so, I was to be deprived of it, I would disintegrate into dust. I beg to remain,

*Your humble servant,*  
Madhusevita dāsa

## Mālatī-devī dāsī

Dearly beloved Śrīla Prabhupāda, eternal messenger of great hope and peace, and our most blessed savior, please accept my humbly offered obeisances at your most beautiful lotus feet, the sight of which wounds my heart with joy.

I am wondering, Śrīla Prabhupāda, if we (your disciples and followers) have disappointed you this past year with our fumbles and errors? Have we, despite all good intentions, hurt you with our middle-aged immaturities? Somehow or other, I cannot apply lofty thoughts and platitudes of scripture to the current situations in the movement...the answers are there, and will never change...but will we? Or are we somehow still those “fifth-class men and women” that you lamented? Is it our eternal folly to remain such?

Individually, there were many wonderful activities performed and words spoken, but collectively, we seemed to betray our best intentions with curious errors. I wonder what is meant by all this? (Ritviks, falling gurus, *sannyāsīs,* and householders; topics of poison against yourself ((horror of horrors!)); children abused by their very protectors; and so on...) And, I wonder, will we ever grow up?

I wonder if you are crying. I am. Yet, it is not my desire to lament, so my “forward-face” to you is of optimism...but, still, I wonder, what is going on? And, how to keep your mission spirited, alive, and healthy?

For a drowning person, chapter and verse of the *Bhagavad-gītā* or *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* will not appear to save him as much as a rope thrown by a caring hand....You even told us that hungry persons must be fed before they can chant....Still, our rope in hand remains the interwoven syllables of the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mahā-mantra:* Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa, Hare Hare / Hare Rāma, Hare Rāma, Rāma Rāma, Hare Hare...which alone can save an entire universe, what to speak of an ISKCON. Therefore, I am praying for greater use of this rope of the *mahā-mantra,* for I believe it can and will save us and please you...for nothing else is as effective in this age.

Reflecting always on your lotus feet,  
*Your fallible servant,*  
Mālatī-devī dāsī (aspiring servant of the GBC)

## Mukunda Goswami

**“That’s Gratitude”**

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

“*I had a dream that Vaikuṇṭha men came to take me. They were all white men with shaven heads.”—*  
*A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami, Śrīla Prabhupāda Līlāmṛta, Vol. 6, Chapter 8, “I Have Done My Part,” p. 356:*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to you!

Gratitude has been poetically referred to as “thy marble-hearted friend.” It is indeed hard to come by these days. You showed me what this meant during my first *mṛdaṅga* lesson, which I will tell about shortly.

You have proven to be the ultimate in streaming reciprocation. You act as Lord Kṛṣṇa guarantees: “As all surrender unto Me, I reward them accordingly.” (*Bhagavad-gītā* 4.11) Kṛṣṇa consciousness has always been, in your words “a system of recreation,” a fun-loving adventure, because you are always the satisfied captain of our team. You make us feel important, useful, and successful.

In over a hundred letters you thanked your disciples, saying that they were all “helping” you “in pushing forward this mission of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu.” Nowadays there is much talk about *sambandha,* or relationships, and people-centered management, and no doubt in this regard we have a lot to learn, a long road to travel.

These days we sometimes work hard but don’t feel effective because it seems we’re not getting any feedback. We might think we’re not pleasing the Supreme Lord or His representatives. We tend to forget how we always needed to feel your pleasure for even seemingly insignificant services.

When we feel empty, it can usually be traced to our own lack of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, but it seems that we still don’t recognize one another’s achievements enough. Are we afraid to offer praise and to compliment one another? Too often our leaders don’t seem to “catch them doing something right,” to borrow a phrase form a modern-day management guru. Although such heedlessness is only human, devotees are meant to perfect their ability to show love and gratitude, to encourage, inspire, and enlighten others on the path of devotional service.

We’re expected to come to the point of saintliness, part of which means being friendly to everyone. A *sādhu,* Lord Kapila instructs us in *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 3.25.21, is *suhṛdaḥ sarva-dehinām,* or “friendly to all living entities,” just as Lord Kṛṣṇa is (*suhṛdāṁ sarva-bhūtānām*—*Gītā* 5.29). Friendship in devotional circles includes constant and vocal appreciation of others’ service. It’s all too easy to take devotees’ activities and accomplishments for granted. Rūpa Gosvāmī recommends that we constantly engage in exchanges of love with devotees. This incorporates the giving of gifts (*dadāti pratigṛhṇāti*) and revealing our minds (*guhyam ākhyāti pṛcchati*).

In a letter to Yamunā and Pālikā you said, “It is only because Kṛṣṇa has somehow or other given me such assistants as yourselves that I have got any success. I appreciate your helping me in this way, you are more dear to me than my own daughters.”

Writing to Hridayānanda Mahārāja, you once expressed your gratitude in this way: “So I am very much indebted to all of you who are helping me to carry out the order of Srila Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati Goswami.” In letters, you repeatedly referred to one of your disciples as “heroine Govinda dasi.”

My first *mṛdaṅga* lesson was an experience I will never forget. I sat on the floor in your San Francisco room in early 1967. It was your first visit to California. You were showing me how to strike the drum. It was a science. A specific portion of the hand had to come into contact with a particular part of the drum head. It was most exacting, a precision operation. You showed me and I tried it. I knew I had to get it right. At the very beginning of the lesson, I had to seek and gain your approval because I didn’t know what I was doing.

You insisted that I pronounce each beat as I carefully slapped the drum. *Kee-ta-ta, kee-ta-ta, kee-ta-ta, kee-ta; ghee-ta-ta, ghee-ta-ta, ghee-ta-ta, ghee-ta; kee-ta-ta, kee-ta-ta, kee-ta-ta, kee-ta; ghee-ta-ta, ghee-ta-ta, ghee-ta-ta, ghee-ta...*over and over again. But I kept speeding up, and you kept slowing me down. “Slow,” you would say, “slow.” I knew I had to get it together. I closed my eyes and focused all my attention on doing it again, correctly, without speeding up. *Kee-ta-ta, kee-ta-ta, kee-ta-ta, kee-ta; ghee-ta-ta, ghee-ta-ta, ghee-ta-ta, ghee-ta...*over and over again, steady as a clock, no hurry. Like waves lapping the shore—timeless.

I got lost in the sound and kept playing for a long time—a very long time. It seemed an eternity. I realized I had lost track of time. But that was OK. I was feeling good about the whole thing. But I couldn’t help wondering what you were doing. Were you still standing next to me as I sat on the floor cross-legged? Were you still in the room? Had you gone off to work on your translating or letters and left me to practice on my own? Strange how the mind can imagine so many things in an instant. My main thought, however, was to keep focused on what I was doing. I was learning something. It was a unique and treasured opportunity.

Nonetheless, I thought I must at least look to see if you were still in the room. Of course I didn’t mind practicing on my own, but curiosity overtook me. I keep playing and reciting the *tālas,* but even though I was a little apprehensive, I decided to open my eyes for a moment. When I did, I saw you standing right next to me in exactly the same spot. But there was a big difference. Now your eyes were closed and, with a slight smile and an intense expression on your face, you were swaying your head to and fro with the beat. I was surprised that you were still there. Amazingly, you seemed to enjoy what I was doing—attempting to play the *mṛdaṅga,* an instrument you had mastered in childhood.

At that point I realized that you were a perfect teacher. I knew what you meant when you distinguished teachers from cheaters. You were a teacher, not a cheater. You actually enjoyed the crude strugglings of a neophyte trying to play the *mṛdaṅga*. I was so encouraged by that one sight of you standing there that in my heart I determined that I had to at least perfect that one beat, and I thought I would have to seriously consider learning the entire culture of rhythms in the Gauḍīya tradition.

All this was your mercy and gratitude, expressed not in words but in action. You showed your gratitude in your patience and happiness at seeing a rookie fumbling forward. You were pleased because you were transferring your knowledge of this musical instrument to an eager student.

As devotees, our reciprocation with Kṛṣṇa is said to be the act of service itself. Nonetheless, we all want the ones we look up to, to be pleased. We need their praise. Without it, there may be no meaning for us; absent the support of those we see as our spiritual superiors, we can easily become downcast, confused, and unsure of what to do.

You startled us once in Mumbai, speaking on the *Bhagavad-gītā* in a large assembly. It was March, 1971. You referred to your Western disciples and a few newly-minted Indian devotees. “This Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement is expanding by their help. I am alone, but they are helping me. They are my gurus—I am not their guru (audience applauds)—because they are helping me in executing my Guru Mahārāja’s order.”

On August 22, 1973, in London, on the occasion of your appearance day, you announced to the congregation that “*Caitanya-caritāmṛta* is now published.” You went on to say, “Our Paṇḍitji, Pradyumna, has worked for it, although I have translated. But I am very much indebted to him that he very carefully edits and makes the thing very perfect...So, my beloved disciple, Pradyumna, I call him Paṇḍit Mahāśaya, because he is actually doing the *paṇḍita’s* work. So he edits and he works very hard. And not only that, his wife also helps in this connection.” Could we believe it? You were almost saying that Pradyumna was greater than yourself.

On another occasion, this time in Los Angeles, it was the anniversary disappearance day of your beloved Guru Mahārāja, 31 December 1973. You concluded your public speech not only by saying you were grateful, but by showing your gratitude: “So this is a memorable day. What he \[Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura\] desired, I am trying little bit, and you are all helping me. So I have to thank you more. You are actually representative of my Guru Mahārāja \[starts to cry\] because you are helping me in executing the order of my Guru Mahārāja. Thank you very much. Chant Hare Kṛṣṇa.” (lecture ends)

After almost every lecture, well over a thousand times, you concluded with the words “Thank you very much.”

Rūpa Gosvāmī explains the meaning of the word “grateful” in his *Bhakti-rasāmṛta-sindhu:* “Any person who is conscious of his friend’s beneficent activities and never forgets his service is called grateful. In the *Mahābhārata,* Kṛṣṇa says, ‘When I was away from Draupadī, she cried with the words “*He govinda!*” This call for Me has put Me in her debt, and that indebtedness is gradually increasing in My heart!’”

Although the spiritual master is eternally dominant in relation to the disciple, sometimes, out of humility, you would characterize a disciple as being “greater,” at least in some way, than yourself. This is obviously a characteristic of a *sādhu,* who always thinks others are greater. Surely, for a disciple, this has to be the highest compliment and the most advanced form of reciprocity. No amount of money or exhibition of power, intelligence, beauty, or fame can purchase the depth of devotion or determine how hard we will work for our spiritual master. But when the spiritual master puts us in a “greater” position, one that is apparently superior to himself, we are both humbled and enlightened in the knowledge that our devotion is not a one-way street. This mutuality makes for lasting and increasing devotion, as opposed to a commitment that is time-bound and diminishing. Did not Lord Kṛṣṇa apparently subordinate Himself to Arjuna by becoming his driver in the Kurukṣetra War? In the summer of 1966 in New York, you said, “Sometimes the servant of God becomes the master of God. The real position of the living entity is a servant of God, but in *Bhagavad-gītā* we see that the master, Kṛṣṇa, has become the servant of Arjuna. Arjuna is sitting on the chariot, and Kṛṣṇa has accepted his drivership.”

You underscore this concept with your words in the purport to the thirty-fourth text of the twelfth chapter of the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* Canto Four: “The conclusion is that a disciple or an offspring who is a very strong devotee can carry with him to Vaikuṇṭhaloka either his father, mother or *śikṣā-* or *dīkṣā-guru*. Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura used to say, ‘If I could perfectly deliver even one soul back home, back to Godhead, I would think my mission—propagating Kṛṣṇa consciousness—to be successful.’ The Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement is spreading now all over the world, and sometimes I think that even though I am crippled in many ways, if one of my disciples becomes as strong as Dhruva Mahārāja, then he will be able to carry me with him to Vaikuṇṭhaloka.”

Your eternal servant,  
Mukunda Goswami

## Nirañjana Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my prostrated obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace.

In *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* we find the following verse spoken by Pṛthu Mahārāja to the Lord:

*sa uttamaśloka mahan-mukha-cyuto*  
*bhavat-padāmbhoja-sudhā kaṇānilaḥ*  
*smṛtiṁ punar vismṛta-tattva-vartmanāṁ*  
*kuyogināṁ no vitaraty alaṁ varaiḥ*

**“My dear Lord, You are glorified by the selected verses uttered by great personalities. Such glorification of Your lotus feet is just like saffron particles. When the transcendental vibration from the mouths of great devotees carries the aroma of the saffron dust of Your lotus feet, the forgetful living entity gradually remembers his eternal relationship with You. Devotees thus gradually come to the right conclusion about the value of life. My dear Lord, I therefore do not need any other benediction but the opportunity to hear from the mouth of Your pure devotee.” (*****Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*** **4.20.25)**

On this most auspicious day of your divine appearance in this material world, I would like to thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for the unlimited “saffron particles” you’ve sprinkled into the air, and especially for those that have entered into the air of my life. I therefore beg you to allow me to go on smelling these particles coming from you. They are the only means by which I can remember my eternal relationship with Kṛṣṇa.

Just as Sārvabhauma Bhaṭṭācārya describes in his *Śacī-sutāṣṭakam* how Lord Caitanya awakens a sweet flavor to life throughout the universe, *kurute su-rasaṁ jagataḥ jīvanam,* similarly, by your mercy I can see how carefully you have translated, selecting just the right words and verses for our enlightenment, and how you have perfectly woven these verses into your purports and lectures in order to awaken within us, your followers, this sweet aroma of life filled with these saffron particles from the Lord’s lotus feet. Any sincere follower who smells such a fragrance can gradually awaken from his sleeping condition, and with each incoming breath this fragrance enters deeper and deeper, thus gradually bringing him to his fully awakened state—Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

Lord Caitanya distributed this sweet flavor while tasting it Himself: *ānandāśru-kalā-pūrṇaḥ pulakāvali-vihvalaḥ/ bhakti-yogaṁ pradāsyāmi hari-kīrtana-tat-paraḥ:* “Devoted to chanting the holy names of Lord Hari, My eyes filled with tears, and the hairs of My body erect, I will give to the people the gift of devotional service.” (*Vāyu Purāṇa,* Text 46) Similarly, you are always tasting the sweetness of pure love of Kṛṣṇa, and while relishing this taste, you are making this taste available for all of us.

In *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* (1.1.3) we find this famous verse:

*nigama-kalpa-taror galitaṁ phalaṁ*  
*śuka-mukhād amṛta-drava-saṁyutam*  
*pibata bhāgavataṁ rasam ālayaṁ*  
*muhur aho rasikā bhuvi bhāvukāḥ*

Śrīla Kṛṣṇadāsa Kavirāja quotes this verse in his *Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Madhya* 25.151, where you have translated it has follows:

“*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* is the essence of all Vedic literatures, and it is considered the ripened fruit of the wish-fulfilling tree of Vedic knowledge. It has been sweetened by emanating from the mouth of Śukadeva Gosvāmī. You who are thoughtful and who relish mellows should always try to taste this ripened fruit. O thoughtful devotees, as long as you are not absorbed in transcendental bliss, you should continue tasting this *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* and when you are fully absorbed in bliss, you should go on tasting its mellows forever.”

Śrīla Prabhupāda, although Śukadeva Gosvāmī has made *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* sweeter by his recitation, you have made it even sweeter, especially for those who have dedicated their lives to serving your order. Only those who have actually tasted the sweetness of your translations and purports, and who “go on tasting *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam’s* mellows forever” in exactly this same way, can actually say that they have fully tasted the nectar of your association. Those who say that they have, but who do not speak from your books, simply cannot satisfy the internal hankerings of my heart to fully relish the aroma of the saffron dust that you are eternally carrying to us from the Lord’s lotus feet.

Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, although I fall short, I would like to follow the same mood Pṛthu Maharaja expressed in his prayer: “I need no other benediction but the opportunity to hear from pure devotees.” Until my last breath in this body, please grant me the boon to both serve with and hear from those pure devotees who are genuinely relishing all you have given us. If I can go on breathing this way, smelling the aroma of saffron that you are mercifully continuing to carry to us from Kṛṣṇa’s lotus feet, I know that my life will become perfect.

Your eternally indebted servant,  
Nirañjana Swami

## Paraṁ Gacit Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, please accept my most humble obeisances.

On this sacred anniversary of your appearance, O divine master, we have the opportunity to increase our realizations about the unique importance of your appearance. The Age of Kali is becoming more and more difficult in every aspect of life. People are suffering terribly, misled by so-called spiritual and material leaders.

Unbecoming behavior and habits predominate, as lack of truthfulness, cleanliness, mercy, and austerity increase very quickly. In such terrible conditions, your practical instructions are the most valuable treasure.

However, purity is required to really understand your instructions, for Māyā is very powerful. In order to protect the followers of this *saṅkīrtana* movement, you stressed the importance of regular *sādhana,* including the chanting of sixteen rounds daily, avoiding the ten offenses. By this process the heart, mind, and senses will become purified. Then the devotee can see you and the Supreme Lord clearly as doubts and fear vanish.

You showed the importance of detachment in order to preach the truth. Detachment helps the devotee become humble, for he sees the Supreme Personality of Godhead as the supreme enjoyer, controller, and friend. In this way he will be able to help others, as your personal example demonstrated.

This *saṅkīrtana* movement is essential and precious for the concretization of your most intimate desire: that the masses of people become pure devotees of the Lord, happy and capable of stopping the cycle of birth and death.

Please help this most unqualified servant of yours to satisfy you by developing these wonderful qualities, so that I can be a useful instrument in your preaching mission.

Your servant,  
Paraṁ Gacit Swami

## Prabhaviṣṇu Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances! All glories to Your Divine Grace!

Yet another year has passed since your departure from this planet. As the effects of Kali-yuga increase, the ocean of material existence is becoming more and more turbulent. Wars are raging in different parts of the planet. Governments are rising and falling like dominoes. Some persons are becoming rich and decadent, while others are poverty-stricken and struggling just to exist. A few persons are somewhat religious or pious, many are very sinful, while others are outright demoniac. It is, however, extremely rare to find a person deeply committed to genuine spiritual practice. You were such a person, and you came to shine the light of transcendental knowledge into this ever-increasing darkness of Kali-yuga; to teach us who we really are, who is Kṛṣṇa, and what is our relationship with Him; to show us the pathway back to our eternal home in the spiritual world; and to give us a means to traverse that path—the process of *bhakti-yoga*. Thus you kindled a genuine interest in real spiritual life in the hearts of thousands of sincere souls.

Nevertheless, even with correct instruction, it is difficult to advance in the contaminating atmosphere of Kali-yuga. You therefore stressed the importance of proper association amongst devotees, and for this purpose you established ISKCON. During your presence ISKCON had strong and clear leadership from Your Divine Grace. It was also a fresh, new movement with many young enthusiasts. In your absence, however, and with the passage of time, many of those young enthusiasts have run into difficulty and lost the determination to continue with spiritual life. The allurements of Māyā in the form of material enjoyment have captured some. Others have been influenced by Kali in such a way that they spend their time vehemently quarreling with one another or with serious devotees about so-called philosophical issues, or about the practical application of your teachings. Sometimes, also, nondevotees, in their ignorance, try to disturb this movement in various ways. Thus, the ship of your ISKCON is being buffeted in the ocean of Kali-yuga.

Fortunately, there are still many serious devotees who adhere strictly to your instructions and who are very conscious of the importance you placed on co-operation. By their enthusiasm and potency they are always attracting new persons to take to the path of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Thus there is always an influx of sincere souls giving a boost to the *saṅkīrtana* movement, and many wonderful things are being accomplished. In this way you remain ever present in your ISKCON movement, and your followers live with you.

On this auspicious day, therefore, I pray to be able to continue following the process of Kṛṣṇa consciousness in your ISKCON by co-operating with your sincere followers and avoiding the pitfalls of false arguments and other allurements of Māyā. I pray that your ISKCON may withstand all kinds of attacks and tribulations and be ever-increasingly successful in distributing the message of Lord Caitanya by expanding the chanting of Kṛṣṇa’s holy names all over the world.

Your lowly servant,  
Prabhaviṣṇu Swami

## Pṛthu dāsa Adhikārī

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to you!

In the short history of ISKCON, whenever there has been the slightest possibility of misinterpreting your instructions, conflicts have broken out and deviant sects have parted from us. Fortunately, however, through your books, lectures, *darśanas,* and letters you have given us thousands of instructions covering every field of life, and there is very little room for disagreement. You have even given us a mechanism to deal with the rare cases of apparent ambiguity in your instructions. As for managing the Society, you left us a perfect system. And for our personal lives you have given millions of instructions on how to practice Kṛṣṇa consciousness in any condition. You have given everything we need, Śrīla Prabhupāda. The problem is that devotees often don’t care about your instructions or think they know better than you.

If not well organized, any gathering of people will deteriorate. Even the human body requires a structure: if we were to extract the skeleton, it would slump to the ground like a jellyfish, unable to function. That’s why in your wisdom you left us not only philosophy and personal instructions but also a structure, a society that can continue your work into the future.

Naturally there are imperfections—not in what you have given us but in the way we have implemented what you wanted us to do. You wanted us to work together, guided by Kṛṣṇa’s way of doing things, namely to “carry what is lacking and preserve what we have.” (*Bhagavad-gītā* 9.22) In other words, you wanted us to build and develop rather than tear down and destroy. Unfortunately, both in the past and at present many have made it their business to do the latter rather than the former.

You have modeled ISKCON according to the desire of Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura. He wanted a united preaching mission rather than a loose confederation of “Guru states” under the banner of “rugged individualism,” with people taking off in every conceivable direction in search of countless will-o’-the-wisps. Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura was a form-maker, not a form-breaker, and so were you. I pray that as time goes on we will mature until we find the perfect balance between the needs of the institution and those of the individual devotees. When that happens, the body of ISKCON will walk with perfect grace and balance and go from strength to strength, leaving in the dust all those who are not willing to subject themselves to the concept of a united preaching mission.

Your servant,  
Pṛthu dāsa Adhikārī

## Rādhānātha Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

The beautiful forest of Śrī Vraja-dhāma is your eternal home. Śrī Vṛndāvana is the supreme abode of divine love. The immortal realm of selfless surrender. The infinitely sweet abode wherein all walking is dancing, all speaking is singing, and the ambrosial song of Kṛṣṇa’s flute is everyone’s constant companion. The Vṛndāvana forests are filled with desire trees, cows, peacocks, and sweet-singing birds. The supremely fortunate *gopas* exchange intimate pastimes of spontaneous love for the pleasure of Śrī Śrī Kṛṣṇa-Balarāma. Upon the treasured banks of Śrī Yamunā, the supremely worshipable *gopīs* forever immerse their pure hearts in endless reciprocations of ecstatic love for the pleasure of Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Śyāmasundara. The intimate pastimes of Śrī Goloka reign supreme among all of the Vaikuṇṭha planets in the spiritual sky.

Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Gopīnātha have extended their most magnanimous mercy upon all souls by descending into this world as Lord Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. Lord Caitanya enacted His all-attractive pastimes with His eternal, confidential associates. They blessed all living beings by giving the opportunity to achieve freedom from the sufferings of birth and death. They plundered the storehouse of love of Godhead and profusely distributed the contents. By their grace all spiritual perfections are achieved. They invite everyone to enter into the divine *līlā* of Śrī Vṛndāvana through the congregational chanting of the holy names.

The six Gosvāmīs resided in the forests Śrī Vraja. For the benefit of all the world, they established the sacred life and teachings of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. That supremely merciful Lord Caitanya is Śrī Rādhā and Śrī Vṛndāvanacandra combined in an infinitely beautiful golden form. He personally empowered the heart of Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī with the most intimate realizations of His divine mission. Thus, through the *paramparā* flowed the nectarean current of the purest truth.

The heart of Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura wept upon seeing the all-pervading ignorance of Kali-yuga. The world was cast into darkness. His compassionate prayers invoked Lord Caitanya to bless this world with the descent of His beloved associate, Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura. This divinely empowered *ācārya* declared war against all misconceptions. He established pure love of Godhead throughout India by the tireless preaching efforts of the Gauḍīya Maṭha. The heart of Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura is the abode of Lord Caitanya’s love. That heart intolerably burned with the compassionate desire to inundate the entire world with the *saṅkīrtana* of the holy name.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, it was you who fulfilled this most benevolent prayer of your beloved Guru Mahārāja. You accepted the blessed order of Śrī Guru and Gaurāṅga as your very life and soul. At the sacred *samādhi mandira* of Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī, you made your residence. Immersed in the spirit of being the selfless instrument in the hands of the previous *ācāryas,* you worshiped Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Dāmodara. There you prepared to fulfill a prophecy never before achieved in the history of mankind. Armed with the unconquerable love of Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī, you departed from your dear home in Vṛndāvana.

In Bombay, as the ambassador to the spiritual world, you stood as a penniless beggar. Begging for the opportunity to sacrifice your life in the service of your beloved Guru Mahārāja.

Alone, on the *Jaladuta,* you gratefully endured repeated heart attacks as this small cargo ship, surrounded by a vast sea of darkness, was abusively smashed again and again by the mighty Atlantic storms. The humble prayers you offered upon the *Jaladuta* have transformed the consciousness of the world.

In New York City you struggled. We will never know how you struggled. There was not another Vaiṣṇava within ten thousand miles. Far, far from your dear home, you graciously accepted abuse after abuse from a demoniac civilization. Your only capital was your ever-present faith in the words of your beloved Guru Mahārāja. Your infallible determination sprung from the infinite compassion that burned in your heart. In the wealthiest city on earth you were by far the wealthiest of all, for you possessed the priceless treasure of pure devotional service. You came to humbly beg everyone, without discrimination, to accept this matchless gift.

As the most loving and caring spiritual father, you patiently nurtured your new-born spiritual children. Your compassionate love has no boundaries. You thus established the International Society for Krishna Consciousness. Your devotional ecstasies are manifested through your illustrious books. Each word of your glorious books, lectures, and other instructions are illuminated by the unfathomable mercy of Śrī Śrī Nitāi-Gauracandra. Unprecedented genius was revealed as you brilliantly balanced the needs of a worldwide preaching mission with the ultra-personal nature of Vaiṣṇava culture. As Lord Kṛṣṇa’s empowered spiritual warrior, you fearlessly challenged all the illusions of the Age of Kali. You have virtually constructed a canal to carry the nectarean flow of the pastimes of Lord Caitanya’s *saṅkīrtana* movement to every town and village on earth. You have openly revealed to the world the hidden treasures of your eternal home, Śrī Vṛndāvana-dhāma.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, today we are celebrating the glorious event of your most auspicious appearance in this world. I pray that I may worship your lotus feet with my heart and soul for all of eternity.

Aspiring to be the servant of your servants,  
Rādhānātha Swami

## Ramāi Swami

*To His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Śrīla Prabhupāda, my spiritual master, in the 103rd year from his appearance in this world. He lives forever by his divine instructions, and the follower lives with him.*

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

In one *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* purport you say,

“As a man cannot separate his life from his body, so a disciple cannot separate the order of the spiritual master from his life. If a disciple follows the instruction of the spiritual master in that way, he is sure to become perfect.”

In a dream I saw a ship being pounded by monstrous angry waves, and coming down were torrents of lashing rain. Kali-yuga is surely ever increasing; nevertheless your mercy is never ceasing. I was a little afraid, but for some reason there was a peaceful calm inside the cabin. I looked around and saw some leaks at the windows, but you were at the wheel and your face was radiating a tranquil smile. There were others outside on the deck waving and shouting angrily, trying to break down the door, but the next moment a huge wave put them on the floor and washed them out to sea. We seemed to rise and float on that ocean, although the waves kept crashing down. Steadily the ship sailed out toward the gap in between two heads of land. There was still some rough water out in front, but beyond that lay placid calm. Somehow I knew we would be all right as long as we stayed by your side. Although you didn’t speak, you appeared to say, “Just keep by me.” Suddenly I awoke, and I realized that your ISKCON would still go on nicely. If all together we sincerely try to follow your instructions, we will be safe. Sailing in the ship of your movement won’t always be calm and smooth; we have to be cautious and vigilant. However, our saving grace is that we have you as our eternal guide.

*Karṇadhāra ivāpāre bhagavān pāra-darśakaḥ.* You are like a captain of a ship in a great ocean, and you can direct us to our destination. In a lecture you gave in 1971 you said, “If you have got a good captain of the ship and the ship is well built, then you’ll have no danger. So the captain, he is the spiritual master....We have to cross over this material ocean, so we must have a good captain and a good ship.”

Who could be a better captain than you, Śrīla Prabhupāda? You are continuously blessing us with your eternally divine instructions. Recently I read where one Indian gentleman commented, “I think Lord Kṛṣṇa must have been standing next to Śrīla Prabhupāda when he was writing his purports.” How true! How incredible! I think the disciple can never fully understand the spiritual master, especially in the conditioned stage. Your position is unique, as is your contribution to the suffering humanity of Kali-yuga. You knew how to perfectly administer the proper spiritual medicine, and the results were miraculous. Responding to one lady who asked at a *pandal* program in Hyderabad, “Can you do any miracles like Sai Baba?”, you said “They are miracle” and pointed to all of us, your disciples. “Before, they were engaging in so many sinful activities, now they are saintly Vaiṣṇavas.”

In a letter to Professor Staal you wrote,

The scriptural quotations are sometimes contradictory, and every philosopher has a different opinion....It is therefore difficult to arrive at the right conclusion. The conclusion is...to accept the judgment of authority. We follow the authority of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu, who is nondifferent from Kṛṣṇa....In a drugstore there may be many medicines, and all may be genuine, but what is required is that an experienced physician prescribe medicine for a particular patient. We cannot say in this case, “This is also medicine, and this is also medicine.” No. The medicine which is effective for a particular person is the medicine for him—*phalena paricīyate*.

This is the principal of *śikṣā* in a nutshell—how to pass on the conclusions of the *śāstras* just rightly for each person, no matter who they are and no matter from which part of the world they are from. Scores of gurus and swamis came to the West before you did, presenting their versions of spiritual truth, but no one can compare to you. Being empowered by Lord Caitanya and acting like Lord Caitanya, you were able to completely transform the lives of so many, from mundane and sinful to spiritual and blissful. Over the years I have met numerous devotees, some of whom have temporarily gone away from devotional service to Kṛṣṇa, but practically all of them repeatedly say, “I can never forget how Śrīla Prabhupāda changed my life.” Like a touchstone, which can convert unlimited quantities of iron into gold and still remain as it is, you made and you are still making tens of thousands fit to render transcendental loving devotional service to the Supreme Lord. All glories unto you, Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Your insignificant servant,  
Ramāi Swami

## Ravīndra Svarūpa dāsa

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my fallen obeisances at your lotus feet. You are my savior, and I know well the difficulty you underwent to save me:

**Guest (1):** How many disciples do you now have, sir, in the U.S.?

**Śrīla Prabhupāda:** You cannot expect many disciples, but still, there are two thousand. Because I have got so many conditions and the fact is so difficult to understand, Kṛṣṇa consciousness. They have forgotten Kṛṣṇa, they have forgotten God, and I am trying to make them Kṛṣṇa conscious. It is a very difficult job. I have to shed my blood three tons before I make one convinced in Kṛṣṇa consciousness. That is my experience. \[Room Conversation—April 18, 1972, Hong Kong\]

I saw personally how hard and how tirelessly you labored to create the unified preaching mission, “a worldwide organization under the name and style of the International Society for Krishna Consciousness” (*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 2.4.18, purport). Once in the mid-seventies a devotee showed me something I was probably not supposed to see: your correspondence files for a few years, filled with all the letters you had received. We had all read the letters you had sent; few knew what you had received.

I was shocked by the volume of problems within our movement, by the *māyā* bedeviling your followers, not excluding many leaders like temple presidents, *sannyāsīs,* and GBC. It seemed not a day went by without your mail delivering to you setbacks, perplexities, quarrels, and failures. The combination of the world’s resistance and the movement’s weaknesses seemed to present an overwhelmingly fatal obstacle. It was a disillusioning and discouraging few hours of reading for me.

Then during a fretful night, I came to realize I had no right to be discouraged. For you, my leader and master, never showed yourself disheartened or discouraged. You had the quality of a great general who, in the thick of the mortal challenges and painful reverses of a pitched battle, remains calm, clear-headed, and fixed single-mindedly upon victory. His very confidence inspires the troops and thereby carries the battle.

I understood then more of your greatness. I realized that I had been foolishly idealistic about ISKCON; for years I had stupidly ignored or disregarded many anomalies that undercut our prevailing ideology that we in ISKCON alone were pure, holy, and could do no wrong. In truth, to a great extent we had replaced righteousness with self-righteousness and sanctity with sanctimoniousness.

You knew better; you knew with chilling clarity of vision all the shortcomings and failures of your followers and your institution. You corrected as much as possible and kept on advancing Lord Caitanya’s mission with whatever flawed and imperfect instruments came to your hands. You never quit trying to distribute Lord Caitanya’s mercy just because your means and instruments were defective. The fact is, that as fire is covered by smoke, even a transcendental endeavor must have faults. It will produce good and, inevitably, bad. What we need to know is this: in the calculus of this endeavor the good will be eternal; the bad, temporary.

Nowadays we encounter many disillusioned and discouraged devotees, who feel their idealism has been betrayed. Some indulge an intense nostalgia for “the good old days,” for the golden past as opposed to the leaden present. Nostalgia, however, is a species of illusion, a yearning for a past that never existed. They fondly think there was a time when, simply because of your magical presence, everyone was Kṛṣṇa conscious and enthusiastic and cooperative. I know better. This rosy picture does not acknowledge what you were actually dealing with from the very beginning, your ceaseless daily labor to hold the movement together and keep it moving forward.

Others, forced at last to acknowledge the truth when the nostalgia is dispelled, go from being idealists to cynics. It is an unfortunate tendency in human history that when we realize we have been engaged in something foolish, we rectify it by embracing the exact opposite thing, which is another foolishness. In this way, foolish idealists convert into foolish cynics, managing to bypass altogether actual wisdom.

Certainly we may be tempted to become discouraged and disheartened. However, it is not allowed to us. It is another form of *māyā*. Indeed, I have discovered that whenever we pass a blanket judgment on ISKCON—either in hope or in despair—we are inevitably reporting on ourselves. It seems that here the “I” in ISKCON indicates “I” the speaker. Whenever I find myself becoming disheartened about your mission, overwhelmed by the problems and difficulties, I have learned to recognize this state as a warning sign of personal weakness, a sign that I need to mend the fabric of my own spiritual life. When the remedial actions are done on myself, ISKCON at once looks brighter.

Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura, Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura, and you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, all have had a vision of a world-wide unified preaching movement, and each has pushed it toward further and further realization, and it is our job to continue. Many have given up on this effort. Some have formed their own enterprises to work separately. Still others spurn “the institution” to cultivate a “higher” practice. For myself, Śrīla Prabhupāda, I confess to being not so bold or enterprising. I admit that I know of no higher practice than to follow your order. Prabhupāda, I want only to be true to you and to your mission. Nothing more. Please grant me this request by your mercy.

Hoping to be your everlasting servant,  
Ravīndra Svarūpa dāsa

## Rohiṇī-sūta dāsa

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

Today we all remember and celebrate the most auspicious day of your appearance. As the bona fide representative of Śrīla Vyāsadeva and the founder-*ācārya* of ISKCON, you should always be glorified by all the three worlds. You are *sākṣād-dharitvena,* a direct manifestation of Lord Caitanya’s mercy, and therefore you are nondifferent from Him. The devotee of the Lord is, by His mercy, more merciful than the Lord Himself. You are also a direct representative of Lord Nityānanda, and that means you are a *śaktyāveśa-avatāra,* one especially empowered by Lord Nityānanda to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness all over the world. And at the same time you are also a very intimate associate of Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī because you are always engaged in assisting Her in Her intimate pastimes and service to Lord Kṛṣṇa. You really understood Her mission: how to inspire others in devotional service and bring them to the lotus feet of Kṛṣṇa. The *saṅkīrtana* mission is very confidential service. This is real *yukta-vairāgya,* not meditating in a secluded place.

You showed us how to engage the three allurements of the conditioned souls—*kanaka* (money), *kāminī* (women), and *pratiṣṭhā-bāghinī* (the tigress of fame)—in the service of the *saṅkīrtana* mission. You were expert at engaging money for book production and building temples, at engaging women equally with men in your mission, without exploiting them, and at making Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa and Their pure devotees most famous.

In *Kalyāna Kalpataru* Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura writes:

*na mānile su-bhajana sādhu-saṅge saṅkīrtana*  
*na korile nirjane smaraṇa*  
*na uṭhiyā bṛkhopari ṭānāṭāni phala dhori'*  
*duṣṭa-phala korile arjana*

**“But your idea, dear mind, is to neglect the best and most auspicious process for worshiping Kṛṣṇa, namely the congregational chanting and spreading of the holy names in the association of pure devotees. And you should not even bother to try to remember Him in a lonely place. Your attitude is just like trying to pick fruits forcibly from a tree by jumping at them from the ground. Instead of climbing the tree to properly pick the sweet, ripened fruits from the top of the tree, you will simply get the sour, unripe fruits by such a jumping process.”**

In *Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Ādi-līlā* 16.11, it is stated, “If one becomes a bookworm, reading many books and scriptures and hearing many commentaries and the instructions of many men, this will produce doubt within his heart. One cannot in this way ascertain the real goal of life.”

Let my mind be absorbed in your books. They are mines of transcendental gems.

The mission coming down from the *guru-paramparā* is:

To establish *daiva-varṇāśrama-dharma*. To crush anti-devotional concepts. To preach pure devotional service by setting an example through your personal conduct (*āpani ācari’ bhakti śikhāinu sabāre*). To establish Navadvīpa-dhāma Parikrama and to preach the glories of the Dhāma all over the world. In this way everyone in the world will receive Kṛṣṇa Bhakti. The real service to Śrī Māyāpur is by printing and distributing devotional books and *saṅkīrtana*—preaching. To preach the significance of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*. To establish an educational institution there; in this way Māyāpur will prosper. Never make any effort to collect knowledge or money for your own enjoyment. These things should only be collected for the service of the Lord. Never to engage in bad association (*asat-saṅga*), either for money or for some self-interest. \[Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura\]

Let this mission become our life and soul.

Your insignificant servant in this *saṅkīrtana* mission, who is trying to satisfy you with his little attempt, begs for some drops of mercy.  
Rohiṇī-sūta dāsa

## Romapāda Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

My beloved spiritual master, lord of my life, dearmost friend and ever well-wisher, Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances. I am bowing before you at your lotus feet, begging for you to shower your favorable glance upon me, that I may have all required strength, courage, and purity to carry forward with your divine mission.

The times we are passing through are turbulent, Śrīla Prabhupāda, just like your ocean crossing to America, undertaken to fulfill the order of your spiritual master, Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura. Despite the turmoil of your sea journey, you felt the constant presence of your Guru Mahārāja, and you took solace in reading *Caitanya-caritāmṛta*. In the same way, I feel your constant presence, for you are like an expert captain constantly guiding me through all of life’s trying experiences, through thick and thin—my ever-present preceptor and dearmost friend. Your teachings are my solace, as I ponder as deeply as possible the rich meaning to life which you are imparting to your broad listening audience. Most important of all, striving to live my life in accordance with your instructions I find to be the best part of being your disciple; it is my sustenance, in fact. Just as one cannot carry on one’s life without sustenance, without food or liquids, so in the same way I cannot think of living other than in a life of striving to live up to your highest expectations. I aspire to become a living example of your teachings, and efforts focused on implementing this aspiration give me great joy and renewed enthusiasm. However, errors and shortcomings abound. “Like washing a lump of coal,” you would say.

Despite my shortcomings, your instruction to come to this standard is my hope and the meaning of my life. Your love impels me to continue striving and gives me the confidence that such an awesome goal is attainable.

Like so many idealistic persons of this world, in my youth I would often question, “What is the real purpose of life?” In my search, I found many persons asking the same question, and an equal number who were eager to supply self-styled and unsatisfactory answers. But you came into my life, by your great kindness and compassion, to give the perfect understanding:

“Human life is meant for God realization, and the human being is given higher intelligence for this purpose. Those who believe that this higher intelligence is meant to attain a higher state should follow the instructions of the Vedic literatures. By taking such instructions from higher authorities, one can actually become situated in perfect knowledge and give real meaning to life.” (*The Nectar of Instruction,* Text 2, purport)

I like this phrase very much, that “one can actually become situated in perfect knowledge and give real meaning to life.” Beyond theoretical acceptance, you are assuring us that one can attain this superb stage of life, in which our very existence is a manifestation of your transcendental wisdom. I pray for your mercy, Śrīla Prabhupāda, to continue to strive tirelessly for continued progress toward that perfection.

The *saṅkīrtana* mission of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu supplies us with the life of transcendental knowledge. Side by side with our internal Kṛṣṇa conscious development, we must carry on with the great work of giving others the same opportunity you gave us. Maintenance of what you have given us includes giving careful training to others, so that they can effectively pursue spiritual attainment and in turn gradually transform the lives of many others.

Preaching and systematically training others how to live their lives in keeping with your divine instructions is so joyful! Today’s preaching field is ever ripe, Śrīla Prabhupāda. The public is as eager to receive your teachings as ever! Let us get on with this real work! Caring for each of the *āśramas* of devotees according to their needs; training, educating, and caring very lovingly for our devotee children; developing communication in our Vaiṣṇava relationships that instills a spirit of co-operation, love and trust...We have so much work to do for you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Please keep us focused on your words, on your instructions, on your loving reciprocations with us. Please empower us to continue pushing on this vital mission of Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for all your kindness. I am forever indebted to you, and I therefore beg you to maintain me forever in your service.

Your servant,  
Romapāda Swami

## Śeṣa dāsa Adhikārī

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances.

Today we celebrate your Vyāsa-pūjā ceremony, a function you taught us the meaning of and personally instructed us how to perform.

“Actually there is no difference between hearing directly from Kṛṣṇa and hearing directly from Kṛṣṇa via a bona fide spiritual master like Vyāsa. The spiritual master is the representative of Vyāsadeva also. Therefore, according to the Vedic system, on the birthday of the spiritual master the disciples conduct the ceremony called Vyāsa-pūjā.” (*Bhagavad-gītā* 18.75 purport)

It is particularly appropriate that we offer Vyāsa-pūjā to you. As Sahadeva conclusively established that Lord Kṛṣṇa was the most exalted personality in the assembly at Mahārāja Yudhiṣṭhira’s Rājasūya sacrifice, and as such should be offered the first worship, *agra-pūjā,* similarly you can be established as the most exalted representative of Śrīla Vyāsadeva.

In order to be someone’s representative, one must share the same faith, experience, and realization. “Śrī Vyāsadeva is the original spiritual preceptor for all men. And all other preceptors are considered to be his representatives. A representative is one who can exactly present the viewpoint of Śrī Vyāsadeva.” (*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 1.1.5 purport)

Out of his compassion for the fallen living entities, Śrīla Vyāsadeva compiled the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*:

*anarthopaśamaṁ sākṣād bhakti-yogam adhokṣaje*  
*lokasyājānato vidvāṁś cakre sātvata-saṁhitām*

**“The material miseries of the living entity, which are superfluous to him, can be directly mitigated by the linking process of devotional service. But the mass of people do not know this, and therefore the learned Vyāsadeva compiled this Vedic literature, which is in relation to the Supreme Truth.” (*****Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*** **1.7.6)**

The faith and experience that inspired Śrīla Vyāsadeva to give the world *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* are rooted in his spiritual vision, described in *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 1.7.3–8. You have described his meditative trance as follows:

Śrīla Vyāsadeva saw the all-perfect Personality of Godhead. This statement suggests that the complete unit of the Personality of Godhead includes His parts and parcels also. He saw, therefore, His different energies, namely the internal energy, the marginal energy and the external energy. He also saw His different plenary portions and parts of the plenary portions, namely His different incarnations also, and he specifically observed the unwanted miseries of the conditioned souls, who are bewildered by the external energy. And at last he saw the remedial measure for the conditioned souls, namely, the process of devotional service. \[*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 1.7.6 purport\]

You too have acted out of intense compassion for the fallen living entities. Traveling to America at such an advanced age and tirelessly translating the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* provide ample evidence of your compassion.

Similarly, the faith and experience that inspired you are rooted in your spiritual vision, a meditative trance remarkably similar to that of Śrīla Vyāsadeva.

After the struggle to get passage to America, and after weeks at sea, three events occurred during your journey that reveal your spiritual vision. Collectively they described your spiritual trance. First, you saw the all-perfect Personality of Godhead in the dream in which Kṛṣṇa appeared to you in His many forms, rowing a boat, urging you on in your mission. Second, your poem “Prayers to the Lotus Feet of Kṛṣṇa” describes the manifestation of the internal potency of the Lord within the external energy of the Lord, taking the form of the desire of your spiritual master, the mercy of Lord Caitanya, and a glimpse of the spiritual world. Third, your poem *Mārkine Bhāgavata-dharma,* written upon getting your first look at America. You describe how you saw the living entities covered by the illusory energy of the Lord, how the Lord is in control of this illusory energy, how you are the servant of the Lord, and how by acting to deliver the Lord’s message in *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* the covered living entities may be freed by the process of devotional service.

*taba kṛpā ha’le mor kathā śuddha habe*  
*śuniyā sabāra śoka duḥkha je ghucibe*

**“Only by Your causeless mercy will my words become pure. I am sure that when this transcendental message penetrates their hearts they will certainly feel engladdened and thus become liberated from all unhappy conditions of life.” (*****Mārkine Bhāgavata-dharma*** **14)**

It is because you share the same faith, experience, and realizations as Śrīla Vyāsadeva that you are his true representative.

Therefore it is with appropriate sentiment that we offer you Vyāsa-pūjā. Please accept my offering, presented in the manner taught to me by you, “with all the humility at my command.”

Your servant,  
Śeṣa dāsa

## Śivarāma Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace. I have made repeated attempts at this offering—the words that come are best left unwritten, those things that are within best remain unsaid.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are like the sun—self-effulgent, luminous, and unique. You cannot be eclipsed by any Rāhu, moon, or cloud. Those who would swallow you will see you emerge once again, those who impersonate you only reflect your glory, those who would cover you must shed their assets in time.

Your glory requires no artificial illumination. May your renown shine most wonderfully through the selfless and pristine service of your followers. As the monochrome sunlight manifests its inherent splendor within many-faceted gems, your greatness will be perpetuated through your pure-hearted followers—by your grace alone.

Please bless me to be the fortunate servant of such distinguished followers of your lotus feet.

Śivarāma Swami

## Śrīdhar Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

We are celebrating the most auspicious occasion of the 103rd appearance anniversary of His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, my eternal divine master and the founder-*ācārya* of the International Society for Krishna Consciousness .

In June of 1970 Śrīla Prabhupāda accepted me as his initiated disciple. He has been the only focus of my life ever since, and I want to offer a few humble words of appreciation for all that he has done for me. I realize that I can never fully glorify him enough. Nor can I communicate my feelings adequately to others, because we all have unique perceptions of and experiences with Śrīla Prabhupāda.

I will try to describe what I believe to be the greatest gift that most compassionate *paramahaṁsa* (or swanlike devotee) has given me and all of us, if we are inclined to accept it.

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

**“Everyone is blind, in the darkness of ignorance. So it is the guru’s business to open their eyes, to help them see things as they are. That is the guru’s business.”**

Śrīla Prabhupāda is the guru of all of us because he brought the torchlight of knowledge into our dark lives. Before coming in contact with Śrīla Prabhupāda, either directly or indirectly, we were all following blind men leading us to hell. Whether they were parents or political leaders, music gurus or scientific supermen, movie idols or sports heroes, they could not understand the ultimate goal of human life.

*pravṛttiṁ ca nivṛttiṁ ca janā na vidur āsurāḥ*  
*na śaucaṁ nāpi cācāro na satyaṁ teṣu vidyate*

**“Those who are demoniac do not know what is to be done and what is not to be done. Neither cleanliness nor proper behavior nor truth is found in them.” (*****Bhagavad-gītā*** **16.7)**

At the height of the hippie movement in San Francisco I found myself bewildered and searching for truth on the one side, and strongly attached to my false ego, false conceptions, and conditioned way of life on the other. How would I ever become free of ignorance? How would I be able to dislodge myself from the rut of ignorance that blocked any true progress to spiritual realization beyond the innumerable speculative theories of the day?

Then I met one of Śrīla Prabhupāda’s serious students. He was selling incense and some small books from a table on Telegraph Avenue, at the Berkeley campus of the University of California. He had also displayed a beautiful framed photo of Mahā-Viṣṇu.

After a few inquiries about LSD and God, I asked him who was depicted in the photo. He answered with a straight face, in all seriousness, that this was indeed the Supreme Godhead. I was shocked. How could this be? God with a form? “Oh yeah? What if I smashed the photo?” I challenged.

“I guess I’d have to kill you,” came the reply.

Some might have thought this man to be funny, dangerous, or crazy, but I took it in a different way. I thought, These Hare Kṛṣṇa people really believe what they preach. “Take it or leave it, but here is the truth,” they seemed to be saying.

I went to the Sunday Feast, discussed philosophy, joined the temple, read the literature available, and met Śrīla Prabhupāda. I perceived the same thread of gravity everywhere. Śrīla Prabhupāda was not giving me another idea or opinion to consider. Everything he said was based on the impeccable authority of the Vedic scriptures. He was a self-realized soul, and the words emanating from his lotus mouth were ambrosial nectar absolute.

His followers were not fanatics, like members of some religious sects I had run into, but they devoutly emulated Śrīla Prabhupāda. He had instilled in them a rational faith in Kṛṣṇa that attracted me.

From 1970, when I first met Śrīla Prabhupāda, till his passing in November 1977, I had ample opportunity to observe him in his preaching. He was the most insightful person and could exactly articulate the perfect arguments to defeat and attract anyone who had the good fortune to come into his association. I discovered that I very much wanted to please him and to be like him.

Nothing else mattered in my life.

I also realized that I could never hope, even after lifetimes, to approach the level of perfection attained by Śrīla Prabhupāda. He was aristocratic, sinless, in constant touch with Kṛṣṇa, humble beyond compare, bold and confident, empowered by Kṛṣṇa, compassionate, and a visionary who transcended all mundane considerations. He was enthusiastic, determined, and patient. He had a real desire and a plan to send everyone in this world back to Kṛṣṇa, back to Godhead.

I have tried to understand why I feel so much indebted to him. Why year after year my faith has not wane but has increased. Gradually it has become crystal clear: Śrīla Prabhupāda has shown me the path of perfection. He has put me on the path of perfection. He has given me the technology to achieve perfection.

That technology involves a process that includes rules and regulations. Śrīla Prabhupāda was so kind that he never compromised. He wrote so many books, and on practically every page he emphasized discipline. He equated discipleship with discipline.

“Disciple means who accepts the discipline. *Śiṣya. Śiṣya.*” (*Gītā* lecture 7.8, 1968)

He set the perfect example of a follower on the path, and he outlined the details in his books and letters and tapes. He stressed the morning program and the regulative principles :

You must see that they strictly follow the regulative principles, the four prohibitions, as well as the devotional practices of arising early, morning bath, putting on tilak, attending mangala aarti, chanting japa 16 rounds, and attending *Srimad Bhagavatam* class. This is the duty of all my initiated disciples, whether they are big or they are small....

Everyone must rise early, take bath, attend mangala aarti, chant at least 16 rounds, attend class, and follow the four regulative principles. If these things are lax, then there is no question of spiritual life. Any one who does not accept these things staunchly will have to fall down. You must teach them by your own personal example, otherwise how will they learn?...  
I am requesting again and again that all of my disciples simply follow all of the rules and regulations very strictly. Rising early, chanting 16 rounds, attending mangala aarti and class, etc. are all essential for spiritual development....  
Please institute these most important points of attending mangala aarti and chanting 16 rounds, these are the most important points of Kṛṣṇa Consciousness process....  
Everyone should join the mangal aarti. Gradually, we should become more regulated and strict for following the rules and regulations. Otherwise, we shall fall down....  
And never neglect to chant your 16 rounds of beads daily, rise without fail, attend mangala aarti, take bath, and follow the other regulative principles; and everything will come out very successfully, you can rest assured of that.  
Yes, reading my books will help you, philosophy must be there, but if you do not also apply the philosophy by participating in all temple affairs like rising early, attending mangala aarti, performing devotional service, then simply learning the philosophy will have no effect.

He gave me the institution and association of devotees to help me mature in the simple basic things. Śrīla Prabhupāda was simple, down to earth, and practical. He really cared for my spiritual advancement. He didn’t compromise. That was his beauty. That was his brilliance. That was his gift to me and others.

I have had the opportunity, having lived mostly in India for the last 27 years, to see all sorts of flamboyant swamis promoting so many unauthorized philosophies. They minimize rules and regulations to attract cheap followers. They talk about whatever is popular and pleasing, just like politicians. They get so-called followers in large numbers because in general people want something instant without any sacrifice. Both such swamis and their followers are the cheaters and the cheated.

I am so grateful to Śrīla Prabhupāda that he gave us the truth as it is.

*tasmāt tvam indriyāṇy ādau niyamya bharatarṣabha*  
*pāpmānaṁ prajahi hy enaṁ jñāna-vijñāna-nāśanam*

**“Therefore, O Arjuna, best of the Bharatas, in the very beginning curb this great symbol of sin \[lust\] by regulating the senses, and slay this destroyer of knowledge and self-realization.” (*****Bhagavad-gītā*** **3.41)**

Now it is up to me to follow Śrīla Prabhupāda’s instructions, beginning with a strong foundation. I or anyone else can have access to Śrīla Prabhupāda’s mercy and guidance, but we have to make some sacrifice from our side. All of our problems related to body and mind can be solved with a little patience. All of our questions can be answered. All of our doubts can be destroyed with Śrīla Prabhupāda’s help. The only question remaining is “Do we really want his guidance completely?”

He is our pillar of strength. Do we want to follow him? The choice is ours. The advantage is ours.

**Candanācārya:** One time you said that Kṛṣṇa consciousness is not difficult, but to remain determined, that is difficult. Determination is difficult.

**Prabhupāda:** That determination comes by *tapasya*. Therefore we have got rules and regulation. If you follow the rules and regulation, then you will be determined. Otherwise, you will be victim of *māyā*. The rules and regulation is there just to keep you fixed up in your determination. But if you don’t follow, then you fall down. Just like physician says, “You don’t take this along with medicine. You take this.” That is rules and regulation. If you follow, then you do not become victim of disease again. If you don’t follow, then you... What the physician will do?

**Candanācārya:** But it also requires determination to follow the *tapasya*.

**Prabhupāda:** Then if he cannot, then he is cat and dog. He is not a human being. Why he should accept initiation? Let him remain a cat and dog. He promises to follow, and if he cannot follow, then he is nothing but cat and dog. In the court, they take promises, that “In the name of God,” “In the name of Bible.” So that means he will speak the truth. Similarly, before the fire, before Deity, before guru, before devotees, he is promising something, and if he does not follow, then he is cat and dog. He cannot advance. It is not possible. That is the distinction between cat and dog and human being. Cat and dog, they cannot promise. It is not possible. But a human being can promise. And if he keeps his promise, then he is human being. Otherwise cat and dog. Word of honor. The cats and dogs, they have no sense of honor. Either you kick him or pat, he does not know what is the difference. That is cat and dog. He does not know the distinction. A human being knows what is promise, what is word of honor.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, thank you so much for showing me the path and giving me the choice. I am weak and unsteady. My physical and mental problems seem innumerable, especially at this time. But I don’t blame anyone else. I am not a victim. Your hand is outstretched to me, inviting me to cross over the ocean of nescience. You are kind, compassionate, loving, caring, and patient. When will I surrender? Today is almost over. Will it be tomorrow?

Your mercy is all that I am made of!

Śrīdhar Swami

## Suhotra Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace on this most auspicious day of your transcendental appearance, which calms our primeval fear.

What is fear? When I take shelter in my particularity, that is fear. Fear flees from you because you gather particularity (all those *jīva*-particles) into a basket and offer it up to Kṛṣṇa.
