It sounds so simple. But who other than you can do that? I fear losing myself to others (but not losing myself to myself—yes, that Māyāvādī contamination lingers in me). If it were not for your soothing voice, I’d have leapt from this basket long ago. I hope I’m not staying in here just to try to subsume others into my particularity, like a black snake swallowing smaller ones—hissing all the while that this is actually what *you* want—spreading my fear through the ranks. You are fearless. I’m certain that’s what I really want, what every *jīva* really wants: freedom from fear. I’m certain you can give us that, because I’m certain that by offering us to Kṛṣṇa you are giving us Kṛṣṇa’s shelter. Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, please help me overcome my particular fear of this process. It’s a worn cliche, but apart from this fear, we have no fear.

Your humble servant,  
Suhotra Swami

## Trivikrama Swami

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

One becomes what one chooses to be; your life and person inspire us to the highest ideal, but we have to want to follow that ideal. Kṛṣṇa never told Arjuna to sit back and relax. No! He said to “stand and fight.” Similarly you have ordered us “to strictly follow,” but we see practically that it is not so easy unless we have very strong determination.

So we must become qualified, but without your mercy how is it possible to remain determined or to be qualified? The task before us, then, is to keep the right attitude, the right mentality, so we don’t block the natural flow of mercy that is emanating from your person. Then there is a chance to overcome Māyā; otherwise, since this is the material world, the kingdom of Māyā, what chance do we tiny souls have against such a formidable opponent?

You have kindly given us the tools that enable us to maintain this vital service attitude. First we have the *mahā-mantra,* the great chanting, which, when done without a motive for sense gratification, delivers us from the hell of mental concoction. Then our mind, which has been our greatest enemy for countless births, gradually becomes our best friend. What a real friend you are, to give us such a wonderful gift! How can we possibly repay you?

But that is not all that you have gifted us with, for along with this most potent medicine, *hari-nāma,* you have given us the knowledge that enables us to see that sense gratification is actually poison. Your books are again and again directing us to this proper conclusion. Whether from this side or that, sense gratification is the same tasteless business of chewing the chewed. That knowledge you are repeatedly drilling into our dull brains, either in your books, your lectures, your conversations, or your letters. And we need to hear it over and over again, because since time immemorial we have maintained this foolish misconception, thinking ourselves the predominating enjoyers here in this world.

Along with the chanting of Hare Kṛṣṇa and the knowledge of the futility of trying to enjoy this world, you have also gifted us with the most valuable method of engaging ourselves in practical devotional service. By creating your ISKCON society, you have enabled us to harness our energies in an attempt to fulfill your order of maintaining and expanding this worldwide society of devotees. And that is proving to be such a substantial challenge that no one can say “there is no service for me.” Everyone is welcome to come and assist you in your mission of delivering the whole world from the clutches of Māyā. What a practical benediction you have bequeathed to all your followers!

Therefore on this most auspicious day of your appearance, allow me to reaffirm and strengthen the commitment I made to you years ago when you kindly accepted me as your menial servant and connected me to this Madhva-Gauḍīya-sampradāya by initiation. Please continue to correct and guide me so that I will be of some use to you in your glorious mission of broadcasting the holy name of Kṛṣṇa to every town and village. Keep me as your humble servant, safe in the shelter of your holy lotus feet.

Your insignificant servant,  
Trivikrama Swami

## Vaidyanātha dāsa

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances at the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to you!

I was thinking today about one of your famous *līlās*. It was in 1967 at Stinson Beach. You were sick at that time and were planning to go back to India. Your disciples did not know whether you would come to the USA again or not, and some of them decided to ask you to send one of your Godbrothers as a replacement for you. You were reluctant. And then, after some silence, tears started flowing from your eyes, and in a choked voice you said, “My Guru Mahārāja was no ordinary spiritual master.” Then you paused for some time and in an even more choked voice said, “He saved me.” Why did you say this? To teach us the proper etiquette and proper mood toward our guru? Yes, but not only this. You also showed us what it really means to be saved by the guru. It basically means to be able to say with tears in our eyes, “My Guru Mahārāja saved me.” It means to always remember the saving grace of the guru and the sacrifice he has undergone to save us, and to be eternally grateful. If we feel this gratitude, it means that we are saved, and if we don’t, it means *mṛtyu-saṁsāra-vartmani* again, and again, and again.

Unfortunately, we have seen devotees who become *guru-bhogīs* or *guru-tyāgīs*. Some are trying to enjoy the inheritance of their guru, and others reject him, seeing him as an impediment for their enjoyment. This is going on and will go on in the future. We see this happen in other religions as well. However, my hope and strong belief is that more and more people will wake up to this simple understanding: “Śrīla Prabhupāda has saved me. He was no ordinary guru.” I hope that sincere tears of gratitude to you will decorate the eyes of more and more people in the future, and I pray to you on this day to be one of them.

Sometimes I imagine what we would do if you came to Moscow now. I see how we would show you around everything, and how you would be happy but at the same time concerned. I hear a few words of encouragement from your mouth, and I see the expression of grave concern on your face. I would probably complain to you how difficult it is sometimes to struggle alone, without your shelter and direct support, and how you would probably reply that all necessary instructions are there in your books, and we should simply take shelter of the *vāṇī,* not the *vapu,* of the spiritual master. I have to agree to this. Yes, the whole ISKCON is a manifestation of your *vāṇī,* all the devotees in ISKCON are created by your *vāṇī,* and your books are your *vāṇī* in the most pure form. And your books, your followers, and your organization will go on saving people of this age at least for the next ten thousand years. After all, I have never seen you personally, and still I have been given a chance to be saved. Thank you for this. Indeed, you were not an ordinary guru—not only have you saved your sincere disciples who had your direct association, but you have also created the system that is capable of delivering your association to generations of people to come, thus saving them. This is perhaps the most amazing of all your achievements.

Nowadays some people around ISKCON and even in ISKCON itself say that the system you have created is inadequate to save people, that your followers are not up to the proper standard, and that we need help from *mahā-bhāgavatas* outside of ISKCON. Well, to say this is to say that you were an ordinary guru. Somehow I can’t believe it. They may call it blind faith. But the future will show who was right and who was wrong in this dispute.

Kṛṣṇadāsa Kavirāja Gosvāmī writes in the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* (*Madhya,* 22.62):

*‘śraddhā’-śabde—viśvāsa kahe sudṛḍha niścaya*  
*kṛṣṇe bhakti kaile sarva-karma kṛta haya*

**“*****Śraddhā*** **is confident, firm faith that by rendering transcendental loving service to Kṛṣṇa one automatically performs all subsidiary activities. Such faith is favorable to the discharge of devotional service.”**

In other words, we, being devotees, are supposed to believe that simply by following the path of devotional service to the Supreme Lord we can attain all perfection.

Everyone has to have faith. Without faith it is impossible to make spiritual progress or achieve the ultimate goal of life. Back in 1967 you showed us what our faith should be—confident, firm faith with tears in the eyes: “Śrīla Prabhupāda was no ordinary guru. He created the system by which everyone can be saved. Simply by following him one can attain all perfection. There is no need of anything else.”

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

The servant of your servants,  
Vaidyanātha dāsa

## Vyāsa-Pūjā Homages from Sannyāsīs

## Bhakti Balabh Purī Goswami

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my respectful obeisances at your lotus feet, which are my shelter, abode, and purifier. All glories to you, O master at whose divine feet all other masters reside!

Thank you once again for engaging me in your service and the service of your devotees. That has kept me safe. As you have said, “This material world is no place for a gentleman.” I am no gentleman, but by your association and by attempting to please you in my insignificant way, I am seeing a little of what you may have meant by that. It is frightening, the things that are going on, and that is only the tiny little that I may see. Just what can Māyā do if she wants or the Lord allows? Frightening to ponder. You once made the comment that your disciples are not sufficiently afraid of Māyā. How to develop this fear and see the naked truth of this material world at all times, and see that it really is “danger at every step”? Please give that understanding to this undeserving soul; otherwise I will become one who will seek comfort in this world or try to enjoy the toys in Māyā’s backyard, like some bewildered child. Then death will come so quickly, and once again all will be gone! A fool once again caught by trying to think that I can live happily and comfortably in this temporary material world of danger.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, please save me from this foolishness and illusion. You have brought me to this path and shown the child how to walk, talk, eat, read, write, and do so many other things meant for the service of our Lord—the Lord you gave me! Now, Śrīla Prabhupāda, please hold onto me and never let me stray from your path, never let me get caught in my own self-comforting. Save me, O Gurudeva, please save me!! You have brought me this far by your mercy. Please take me right on to the end without deviation or failure. You have done it for others—this I have seen. So please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, my prayer is, Please do the same for me, as only you can do.

Thank you for the many great things you have done for me and all others. I hope to be able to appreciate them one day and return the love you have showered upon me and others, although knowing that I can never repay you for what you have done for me and others. But if I had a glimpse of it, then maybe I would surrender with great enthusiasm and boundless eagerness to please you, as others are trying to do.

All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda! All glories to you again and again! Hopefully my activities will be pleasing to you and I will gain the mercy needed to please you more and more.

By your mercy I have been given a nice service in Scotland and Hungary. Please help the souls in these places take the great gift you have given. Please help me deal with them in a way that will be pleasing to you, and that will encourage them to take more and more of your mercy. I need your mercy to understand these souls more and more so as to serve them in the way needed. You have put me there, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Please direct me and give me the intelligence and desire to follow your directions. Thank you for this service.

I wish to remain in your service and the service of the devotees and fallen souls.

Your insignificant servant, the worthless  
Bhakti Balabh Purī Goswami

## Bhakti Abhay Charan Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most respectful and heartfelt obeisances at your divine lotus feet and forgive my innumerable offenses.

You are inevitably in my heart and in the subtlest fibers of my being every day that goes by. Your indomitable striver personality incites me to accept challenges with more braveness than I possess. You are the only guarantee for me to assimilate correctly the knowledge I receive. You are present every moment of my life as hope, as shelter, and as the voice of my consciousness. You nourish my life in every sense. Despite my not feeling a spontaneous bent for devotional service, by your mercy I am able to practice it. I owe you the happiness of being connected with Kṛṣṇa. Besides being my savior, you are my best source of knowledge so I can learn how to see things clearly and not get confused by the many opportunities that life drives me into, through the different and unusual ways of learning. I find in you inspiration and censorship, encouragement and admonition, the transcendental path and the light to go through. Living without you is unthinkable. When my rebelliousness tries to ignore you, my soul misses you hopelessly. Every time I read your books or speak about them, I become proud of you. I owe to you the ever-present festivity deep in my heart and the subsequent happiness. There would not be temples or devotees without you. By your grace, the desert zones in my heart are becoming arable soil.

Fortunately the seeds of your labor have given fruits of different qualities and flavors that shine splendidly in the tray of your movement, in which different types of sincere persons get near to taste them. But there is still much left. Even now, there are moments in which doubts and perplexities confuse us. Help us understand our deficiencies in order to transform them into practical wisdom. Give us the intelligence to understand that without your blessings we are nothing. You are the incarnation of Kṛṣṇa’s mercy, the *paramparā* synthesis, and our eternal well-wisher. With your kind help, we will lack nothing. Your faith in Kṛṣṇa, your absence of fear, and your wise instructions have fertilized our soul. Definitely our success depends on the proper interpretation of your teachings and on learning the art of applying them according to our circumstances. Besides renewing our consciousness, we must give special emphasis to reforming our ways of living in order not to get confused. Ever since you’ve gone, we have made many mistakes but have also learned a lot. The wonderful thing is that behind any kind of confusion it is you who always clear up our doubts and save us. The convulsive situation of the world forces us to renew our commitment of giving the spiritual reorientation needed by the innocent souls. Your extraordinary prowess of establishing Kṛṣṇa consciousness on the planet is still the pillar that supports our faith. Obeying the inspiration that spontaneously flows from our hearts while listening to your lectures or reading your books, we manage to survive spiritually and progress. In fact, the movement consolidates and expands due to the solid basis of your books. However, the adversity in the world is becoming so oppressing that we get frightened. Nihilism and apathy have taken root so much in the system that our scheme has not caused the thunderous effect we would have liked for the well-being of people. On the other hand, our internal conflicts have partially restricted our dedication. In order to detect our mistakes and redefine our mission, we need your help more than ever. We need humility, love, and trust to reach optimum in our philosophical understanding and to strengthen our will. The material illusory energy attempts to grab us, but we must not weaken. Even when the foreseen astrological influences for the upcoming years will make the worldly leaders lose their minds, nothing will make us succumb if we do not give up the chanting of the holy name and if we take shelter in your divine teachings. We must convert pretty phrases into actions. Deeds are worth more than words. We must transform the theoretical realizations into deeds worth being offered to the Lord through your mediation. The intellectual findings become relevant spiritual facts through obedience. Obeying you is the guarantee of obeying the Truth. *Bhakti* is a science, and as such it gives fruits when properly understood and practiced. Always protect us. You know us: we are very weak; but with your blessings we will continuously go ahead.

Your grateful son,  
Bhakti Abhay Charan Swami

## Bhaktividyā Pūrṇa Swami

**The Path of Perfection**

Engulfed  
in the fog  
of material existence,  
I stand  
unsure,  
without direction.

Slowly  
the mist rises  
to reveal a path,  
the Lord’s path,  
a path carefully delineated  
by you  
out of your compassion.

To the right—  
the trap  
of impersonalism;  
to the left—  
the lure  
of *sahajiyāism;*  
down the middle—  
the razor’s edge,  
the path of perfection.

vyavasāyātmikā buddhir  
ekeha kuru-nandana  
bahu-śākhā hy anantāś ca  
buddhayo ’vyavasāyinām

“Those  
who are on this path  
are resolute  
in purpose,  
and their aim is one.  
O beloved child of the Kurus,  
the intelligence  
of those who are irresolute  
is many-branched.”

By your grace  
may such one-pointed determination  
blossom within my heart.

May I follow this path  
without deviation.

May your instructions  
guide my every step.

May I follow behind you,  
the dearmost of the Lord.

May I please you—  
the perfection of my life.

dasa A. V. Bhaktividyā Pūrṇa Swami

## Bhakti-vighna-vināśa Narasiṁha Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace, who have established Lord Caitanya’s *saṅkīrtana* movement throughout the entire world.

Anyone who had the good fortune to hear or see Your Divine Grace can never forget you because you are the Supreme Personality of Servitor Godhead, the personification of pure devotional service. You live forever by your divine instructions found in your books, letters, lectures, and conversations. You are also manifest through the missionary activities of the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement, of which you are the founder-*ācārya*. The celebration of your appearance in this world is always the most joyous of all Vaiṣṇava festivals in the year. It is a day for every devotee, *āśrama* inmate, and congregational member alike to put aside all other affairs and focus on the glories of Your Divine Grace.

As the years progress, your ISKCON continues to expand, both in its members and its centers. More than ever before, the devotees are dedicated to studying and distributing your books. Each year we find the Māyāpur project moving closer to becoming the transcendental city you predicted. As in the past, there are problems to be faced, legal battles to be fought, and challenges to be met, both individually and collectively.

As we approach the turn of the century, prophecies abound, and all are bleak and dark, but was this not also the case five thousand years ago? At that time all the sages gathered at Naimiṣāraṇya to hear the glories of the Lord. Now, by your mystic potency, the glories of the Lord can be heard throughout the world. *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 1.7.6 tells us, “The material miseries of the living entity, which are superfluous to him, can be directly mitigated by the linking process of devotional service.”

In *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 10.14.8 (purport), Śrīla Śrīdhara Swami has commented that just as a legitimate son has to simply remain alive to gain an inheritance from his father, one who simply remains alive in Kṛṣṇa consciousness, following the regulative principles of *bhakti-yoga,* automatically becomes eligible to receive the mercy of the Personality of Godhead. To stay alive in Kṛṣṇa consciousness, we have to be involved in the *saṅkīrtana* movement. No one will be satisfied if they simply maintain their existence.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, please bestow your mercy on all your devotees and give us all shelter at your lotus feet. Allow this insignificant servant of yours to be engaged in the service of your servants, for this is my heart’s delight. May I live according to your divine instructions, as well as those of your representatives, and may my example never bring discredit to you or your institution. I also fervently pray that somehow or other all of your disciples, disciples of your disciples, and the coming generations of devotees may be united at your lotus feet.

Your unworthy servant,  
Bhakti-vighna-vināśa Narasiṁha Swami

## Bhakti Vikāśa Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Most days of the year I offer *guru-pūjā* to you in one of the many temples and preaching centers you have established throughout the world. Nowadays you mostly manifest yourself in these temples in your beautiful deity form, although in some places you still preside from the *vyāsāsana* in your beautiful picture form. In your temples you also manifest as your instructions, your books, and the whole way of life and meaning of life for those who reside there. You also manifest within the hearts of those devotees whose heart’s desire is only to please you, and you guide such devotees toward Kṛṣṇa’s lotus feet. You guide them by the instructions and inspiration given in your books, and also through the directions given by those of your disciples who have taken up your order to preach and guide others according to your orders.

As I offer you *guru-pūjā,* I try to meditate on at least one of these great gifts you have given us, and on how much you mean to us all. Your *guru-pūjā* is an essential part of the indispensable *sādhana* program you have given us. At *guru-pūjā,* all members of your family come together to acknowledge the gifts you have given us and to confirm your preeminent position in our lives.

Daily as I offer the *ārati* items to you, I resubmit myself as an eternal servitor at your lotus feet. I pray that you guide me and bless me to fulfill the tasks I have set myself in your service. That you are already guiding and blessing me is not denied. You are my guru, and the guru’s function is to guide and bless the disciple. Furthermore, if you are not guiding and blessing me, how could I even be in a position to ask your guidance and blessing? Nevertheless, I daily seek your guidance and blessings, knowing that without constantly looking for your mercy, I may become complacent. If I were to think myself not dependent on your mercy, I would again fall into the pit of suffering you so kindly pulled me out of.

I offer the articles one by one, meditating on the limbs of your transcendental body, hearing the prayer composed by Narottama dāsa Ṭhākura that so aptly and ecstatically describes your divine qualities. I am so small before you, yet my feelings of insignificance mix with the happiness and sense of security that stem from my understanding that you have accepted me. You know my faults and pretensions, yet you have accepted me into your shelter.

You want to me be purified. That I am not, even after so many years of at least superficially following your instructions, demonstrates my lack of sincerity and purpose. Yet, you still do not reject me. My happiness in having such a merciful master mixes with the shame of not serving you properly. Yet still I prostrate myself before you daily and pray that at least during this day may my thoughts and activities be properly centered on you.

In submitting myself to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and begging for your mercy, I cannot but think that you want something from me also. You want me to be your worthy representative and thus become a suitable candidate for manifesting your mercy in the world. Please bless me in this way, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Please allow me to attend your *guru-pūjā* every day for eternity, and please make me an instrument in your mission to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness to every home and heart in the world. It is your desire that not a single living entity in the universe be bereft of Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

And it is our desire, as your disciples, that you be properly honored for establishing ISKCON to give everyone the chance to be Kṛṣṇa conscious. We want there to be thousands upon thousands of temples of your International Society for Krishna Consciousness, in which thousands upon thousands of devotees will assemble together daily to roar their hearts in appreciation: “*Jaya* Śrīla Prabhupāda!”

Śrīla Prabhupāda, the seed of this Vyāsa-pūjā offering manifested in my mind while offering *guru-pūjā* to you on your Vyāsa-pūjā day 1998 in Zagreb, Croatia, in a hall packed with several hundred devotees. Śrīla Prabhupāda, I am very happy to report to you that your desires for the spreading of Kṛṣṇa consciousness are gradually being fulfilled, even in places where it was not previously thought possible.

As I compose this offering, I have just returned from a preaching tour in South India. I witnessed Pejawar Swami, the venerable pontiff of the Madhva *sampradāya,* publicly declare that “ISKCON is the only organization capable of spreading *viṣṇu-bhakti* throughout the world.” And in the many prominent Śrī Vaiṣṇava temples we visited, the *brāhmaṇa* priests, without fail, recognized and honored us as members of your great society because of its unprecedented work in spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, your society is now facing severe challenges that during the innocent days of your personal presence we could not have imagined. Nevertheless, seeing the tremendous effect your movement is having throughout the world, and how the process of Kṛṣṇa consciousness is the endurably simple, sweet panacea for the world’s problems, we are confident that, by your continued blessings, this vitally important Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement will persist in expanding its essential work throughout the world.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, please keep me always in your movement. May my days and lives go on eternally with *guru-pūjā* and *guru-sevā*. All glories to your lotus feet, Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Your servant eternally,  
Bhakti Vikāśa Swami

## Candramauli Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Please accept my humble obeisances in the dust of your divine lotus feet. All glories to your mercy and compassion upon the fallen conditioned souls!

Being the supreme mercy manifestation of the Lord, Śrī Nityānanda Rāma is loved and glorified for extending Kṛṣṇa’s mercy in a most extraordinary way by delivering with great difficulty those two sinful and degraded creatures Jagāi and Mādhāi. When one wants to point to mercy extraordinaire, one remembers this pastime of Lord Nityānanda.

In establishing Kṛṣṇa consciousness worldwide, Śrīla Prabhupāda, you extended Śrī Nityānanda’s mercy even further by rescuing thousands of Jagāi’s and Mādhāi’s and simultaneously doing whatever was necessary to build a worldwide spiritual organization.

Traveling worldwide, opening temples, installing Deities, meeting with devotees and guests, preaching at public programs and temples, writing the incredible Bhaktivedanta purports, training devotees in spiritual philosophy and organizational management, dealing with spiritual and managerial crises...the list of your accomplishments goes on and on. They are totally inconceivable and practically impossible, especially at such an advanced age. They comprise no small miracle under any circumstances.

Through our own insignificant attempts to preach the message of Kṛṣṇa consciousness and follow in your footsteps, it becomes rather difficult at times just to travel from place to place, let alone preach effectively.

Through these experiences the Lord is helping us develop a greater appreciation for you and a deeper realization of your compassion and mercy upon the fallen conditioned souls in Kali-yuga.

Personally I am bereft of anything significant to offer, nor have I any of the qualities it takes to become Kṛṣṇa conscious, but I have firm faith in your guidance and instructions.

Begging for your mercy,  
Your insignificant servant,  
Caundramauli Swami

## Candramukha Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

**“I offer me respectful and humble obeisances unto the lotus feet of Śrīla Prabhupāda, who is like the sun of Kali-yuga. He has arisen to dissipate the darkness of ignorance and wonderfully bestow benediction upon all.”**

In a temple of jewels in Vṛndāvana, underneath a desire tree, Śrī-Śrī Rādhā-Govinda sit upon an effulgent throne. In this transcendental place, They are served by Their most confidential associates, the perfect souls. Indeed, feeling compassion for the fallen souls suffering in the material world, one of these confidential servants came to inform us about the spiritual world’s activities. Here is name is Abhay Caran. Being empowered by the Divine Couple to come to the material world, he has no fear of anything because he belongs to the transcendental plane, beyond the reach of illusion. Resplendent with the radiance of molten gold, this confidential servant of Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Govinda’s has appeared in the Age of Kali only to bestow the most sublime and confidential knowledge, and I am one of the fallen souls suffering in the material world. Therefore, my duty is to offer my humble obeisances unto this most confidential servant. I hope that he will eternally confer upon me his divine benediction. I hope that he, who is known as the Kali-yuga *pāvana,* will stay eternally in the innermost chambers of my heart. I hope that he will be eternally the object of my constant meditation, because he is the divine incarnation of the Lord’s devotee and therefore I really need to always take shelter of him. I know that he always acts as the most confidential servitor of Lord Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu and that he is therefore none other than the personification of pure devotional service, *gaurāṅga-bhakti*. He is the life and soul of those devotees who want to satisfy the desire of the Lord and are always trying to spread the holy name in every town and village, and my only hope is to be one of these devotees of Gaurāṅga.

O all-merciful Śrīla Prabhupāda, I am lame and ill advised, yet you are my director, and your lotus feet are everything to me. Please let me live eternally with your servants, and let me understand and develop love for your mission. I have no other desire. You are eternally situated in the most elevated position, and without your mercy even the most erudite mundane scholar cannot understand your divine mission.

Your eternal servant,  
Candramukha Swami

## Candraśekhara Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Thank you for the Holy Name.

Thank you for the association of the Vaiṣṇavas.

Thank you for my service in preaching here in New Vrindāvan.

Thank you for the beautiful forms of Rādhā-Vṛndāvana-candra.

Thank you for all your translated literatures—*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam, Bhagavad-gītā, Caitanya-caritāmṛta,* and all the others.

Thank you for *prasādam*.

Thank you for your abundant mercy upon my small existence.

And thank you for the struggles, the confusion, and the pain—they have helped me grow.

I want to please you more than anything else.

Your servant,  
Candraśekhara Swami

## Dānavīr Goswami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

All glories to Your Divine Grace!

Among stars within the dark sky of Kali-yuga, you are the glowing benediction moon. As the moon bestows its soothing rays upon even the homes of *caṇḍālas,* so you enrich the poor and most fallen souls of the age with effulgent love of Kṛṣṇa.

Among gurus, you are the lion, the *siṁha-guru*. With your razor-sharp words you rip apart all speculative philosophers.

Among authors, you are the monarch. Your books have conquered the eight directions, and your learned agents administer to your subjects.

Among societies, yours is the holy ISKCON banyan tree, spreading its trunks and branches unlimitedly. Born from the seed of your pure heart, this auspicious *kalpa-vṛkṣa* shelters everyone and fulfills the devotees’ desires.

Among sacred rivers of devotion, you are the undeviating Ganges ever flowing to Śrī Govinda’s lotus feet. Unaffected by ugra-karmic pollution, you miraculously purify sinners who bathe in and drink of your sweet waters.

Among jet-age fliers, you are *paramahaṁsa* Garuḍa, carrying beautiful Kṛṣṇa upon your shoulders. Casting aside homely comforts, you untiringly soar from Vṛndāvana to Venezuela to Valencay.

Among sages, you are the replica of Vyāsa, compiling the *Vedas* into innumerable languages.

You are the external manifestation of the Supersoul, seated in the hearts of all sincere devotees.

All opulent, beautiful, and glorious Kṛṣṇa consciousness springs from but a spark of your splendor. You are the living force (consciousness) of the modern worldwide *bhakta-saṅga*.

With a single stroke of your lotus foot, you kick out all stumbling blocks on the path back to Godhead.

Dear Gurudeva, I pray with all the sincerity at my command that you will kindly engage me in your blissful service eternally.

Your unworthy servant,  
Dānavīr Goswami

## Devāmṛta Swami

**“Crying Alone in the Wilderness”**

Allow me to meditate upon you in 1948.  
For two years you had struggled to get your *Gītā* manuscript published.  
Suddenly the clouds parted,  
the glorious moment shone.  
A Mr. Chetty of South India requested you send your manuscript to  
Mr. Allagappa in Madras.  
“There he will arrange for what God has meant for you to do in your  
religious duty.”  
Your first-class, royal edition of the *Gītā* was imminent.  
Allagappa, “the famous Birla of the South,” would back you completely.  
You went home where the manuscript was safely stored.  
It was gone.  
Whether servant or even your own son, the thief spoiled years of work.  
Thwarted: a priceless opportunity for India and the world.

Later, on a street in Delhi, I see you collapse—  
gored by a stray cow.  
Then I see you again, in 110-degree weather,  
distributing Kṛṣṇa conscious literature.  
Determined to ignore the hot dusty winds,  
half unconscious, you totter and fall—  
heatstroke.  
For Mahāprabhu’s mission, you appealed to the president of India:  
“I am crying alone in the wilderness at the present moment.”  
His Excellency never replied.  
In your article “Hope against Hope,” you noted:  
Eighty percent of the Delhi people you met on *saṅkīrtana* were atheists—  
and that was back in 1956.

Japan reached for you, in 1961—  
Congress for Cultivating the Human Spirit.  
Immediately your innovative vision seized the time.  
Fifty lessons for illustration,  
twenty thousand words poured onto paper.  
I marvel, wherefrom arose your relentless drive?  
All expenses in Japan would be paid.  
A Mr. Nakano would await you at the airport:  
“Watch for me, I’ll be holding a Japanese flag.”  
You never got to see it.  
For the monumental journey there, no money came your way.  
Desperately you appealed:  
“I am a humble creature. I am just trying to do my bit in this connection.  
Because I was so ordered by my spiritual master.”

In 1965 I watch you ascend the gangway to the *Jaladuta*.  
The day before you had visited Māyāpur-dhāma,  
seeking strength at your spiritual father’s *samādhi*.  
Now you disappear into the old black cargo ship.  
Down on the wharf, only a casual acquaintance had bid you farewell:  
“He was alone. A lone fighter.  
When he left, there was no one on the shore to bid him good-bye.  
No friends, no supporters, no disciple, nobody.”

In the cabin of the *Jaladuta,* can I feel for you?  
Sailing to America at the age of 69.  
Dizziness, headaches, nausea—Bay of Bengal.  
Then, in the Red Sea, two violent heart attacks—in two days.  
Alone at sea, death nearby, you write:  
“I am feeling separation from my Lords  
Sri Govinda, Gopinath, Radha Damodar.  
I have no qualification.  
But I have taken up the risk.  
Just to carry out the order of His Divine Grace.  
I depend fully on Their mercy—  
so far away from Vrindaban.”

Through decades of setbacks, reversals, impediments,  
you always soldiered onward.  
Just for the pleasure of Lord Kṛṣṇa and your Guru Mahārāja.  
My conclusion:  
Even before you landed in New York,  
I was incalculably indebted.

Trying, though feebly, to be an unconditional servant,  
Devāmṛta Swami

## Gaṇapati dāsa Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace.

**When They Ask Me**

When they ask me—“What’s your name?”  
I tell them, “Prabhupāda’s dāsa—my only fame.”

When they ask me—“Where’s your home?”  
“Prabhupāda’s lotus feet—all I’ve ever known.”

When they ask me ’bout my profession,  
“Distributing Prabhupāda’s books—my real obsession.”

When they ask me—my “appearance” day,  
“I was born at initiation”—is all I can say.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, help me to be *guru-ātma-daivataḥ*—  
“he for whom the spiritual master is his very life and worshipable deity.”

Please help me to be:

Stable without being stubborn.  
Invested without being insolent.  
Faithful without being fanatical.  
Un-ass-uming without being an ass.  
Attentive without being an alarmist.  
Systematic without being sophisticated.

Your aspiring servant,  
Gaṇapati dāsa Swami

## Guṇagrāhi dāsa Goswami

Dearmost Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet.

The following is my meager attempt to glorify you on this most auspicious day of your divine appearance:

**Our Hope**

Lord Kṛṣṇa sent you here  
To spread the holy name  
And teach the Vedic way.

Your sojourn on this earth  
Brought daytime to the night.  
Illusion stood at bay.

The wave of Kali broke  
And washed back out to sea.  
Checked was Māyā’s ploy.

A miracle! A first—  
For me and for the world.  
We bathed in newfound joy.

But when you left our midst,  
And signs of darkness loomed,  
Threatening the light,

We knew not where to turn,  
But tried to carry on  
With what we thought was right.

Oh! When great saints depart,  
It’s oft so hard to know  
What’s best for one’s improvement.

And so it came to pass:  
Illusion swept right in,  
Endangering your movement.

And history’s told all:  
Of battles won and lost,  
The times we’ve gone off course.

We know in days gone by  
Vaiṣṇav *saṅgas* of yore  
Have also suffered loss.

Even disappearing!  
Leaving hardly a trace  
Of a flourishing past.

Despite their good beginnings,  
Māyā then consumed them.  
What depressing repasts!

Your legacy: so sacred.  
You fought so hard to build it.  
Will *your* victory stand?

You said, “It will, of course,  
If you show your love and trust  
By working hand in hand.

Your only cause of failure  
Will be weakness from within.”  
You warned us: “Please be strong!

Be pure in word and deed!  
Don’t focus on the faults!  
At all costs, get along!

Treating all as master  
In humble serving mood,  
You’ll earn the Lord as guide.”

We’ve struggled to comply.  
And when we’ve gained success  
And cast away our pride,

Then all of Kali’s tries  
To separate and conquer  
And orchestrate discord,

To allure us with power  
And force us to exploit,  
Abuse, and overlord.

Her tricks to have us prowl,  
Seeking adoration,  
Profit, and distinction,

Have failed to overturn us.  
The triumph has been yours!  
Her threat has met extinction.

“Increase, please, more and more.  
“At least keep what I’ve given,”  
You begged with folded hands.

Our sole hope is to take  
Your order as our life,  
Your wish as our command.

Begging to remain serving eternally at your lotus feet,  
Guṇagrāhi dāsa Goswami

## Hanumatpresaka Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please let me offer my humble obeisances in the dust at your lotus feet. We are possibly the most simple of your disciples and members of ISKCON, so our attempt to glorify Your Divine Grace will certainly be short. Actually, all I can understand is the you are present in your books and your devotional service. We chant our rounds, follow the four principles, maintain the full morning program, go on *saṅkīrtana,* and recommend that others also do all these things. Then throughout the day so many wonderful opportunities arise to serve you and associate with the devotees. I can see that we are winning. We are slowly purifying our own hearts, resolving our internal problems in ISKCON, and spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness throughout the world. The problems that come are challenges and very wonderful. I think, however, that without your special mercy we could never pass any of these different tests and do something substantial for Kṛṣṇa. Please pray for all of us simple devotees that we don’t get eaten by the tigers of nescience and that we are able to continue for just another year as your servants. Your mercy is really just about all that we are made of.

Hanumatpresaka Swami

## Indradyumna Swami

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories unto you!

I write to you on a train traveling through Siberia, in the middle of a cold Russian winter. I’m on my way to preach in Omsk, a further two-day journey eastward. The train is old and dirty, and the freezing wind outside blows through the cracks in the floor. On either side of me, two men drinking vodka are singing patriotic songs from the 1917 Russian revolution. I can hardly hear the holy names as I finger my beads...

Closing my eyes, I remember an instruction you gave me in Paris in 1974. I had just come back from *saṅkīrtana,* and you inquired from me how the preaching had gone that day. I replied that it was somewhat difficult. To encourage me and teach me tolerance, you smiled and said, “Preaching is not always easy.”

No doubt it hasn’t been easy serving as your traveling preacher for most of my life. Living on the road for thirty years has cost me dearly. The strain of constant travel has weakened my health, and several times I have nearly lost my life. But, Śrīla Prabhupāda, if I had the chance to do it all over again, I wouldn’t change a thing. Austerities are your grace, helping me to remain detached from material life, and preaching for you is undoubtedly my greatest pleasure. My constant source of inspiration is a letter you wrote to my Godbrother Prabhaviṣṇu Mahārāja in 1973:

I can understand that it is not an easy matter to travel extensively over long periods of time without proper food and rest. And sometimes it must be very cold there also. Still, because you are getting so much enjoyment, spiritual enjoyment from it, it seems like play to you. That is advanced stage of spiritual life, never attained by even the greatest yogis and so-called jyanis.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, what are the troubles I encounter in my service compared to those you underwent to save me? I can never repay my debt to you! Please, therefore, accept this life that you have given me and use whatever years remain as you see fit. Turning fifty, I have just crossed the threshold of old age, yet I have no desire to retire for an easier life of seclusion. I beg you for more service, responsibilities, and opportunities to preach the glories of the Lord. Please allow me to be part of your greater plans to spread the holy names of the Lord far and wide.

Now is the opportune moment, for grim prophecies abound regarding the immediate future of this planet. Your prophecy alone is the only one that gives even a glimmer of hope. In 1972 you told Guru dāsa, “This movement will save the world in its darkest hour.” If time passes and nothing happens, we will take it that your movement held back the tide of Kali-yuga. But if destiny has it that the world will suffer, your movement alone will give people solace.

Could it be that these things are just around the corner? If so, I pray for the strength to bear all trials and tribulations and for the privilege to preach your transcendental message. You have written:

Sometimes the members of the Kṛṣṇa Consciousness Society are afraid of the impending danger of world war and ask what would happen to them if a war should occur. In all kinds of danger, they should be confident of their protection by the Viṣṇudūtas or the Supreme Personality of Godhead, as confirmed in *Bhagavad-gītā* (*kaunteya pratijānīhi na me bhaktaḥ praṇaśyati*). Material danger is not meant for devotees. This is also confirmed in *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*. *Padaṁ padaṁ yad vipadāṁ na teṣām:* in this material world there are dangers at every step, but they are not meant for devotees who have fully surrendered unto the lotus feet of the Lord. The pure devotees of Lord Viṣṇu may rest assured of the Lord’s protection, and as long as they are in this material world they should fully engage in devotional service by preaching the cult of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu and Lord Kṛṣṇa, namely the Hare Kṛṣṇa movement of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. \[*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* 6.3.18 purport\]

Śrīla Prabhupāda, if I live to know a brighter day, may it be to see you famous as the source of light in the world’s “darkest hour.” Should I lose my life that it may happen, may you remember that in loving you, I gave you all I had.

Your servant,  
Indradyumna Swami

## Jayādvaita Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

While offering obeisances to Śrīla Prabhupāda, and particularly on the occasion of Vyāsa-pūjā, we ought to respect not only Śrīla Prabhupāda himself but also what he taught, and in particular what he taught about the spiritual master—the manifest representative of Vyāsa—and about the system of disciplic succession. Some time ago, I wrote an editorial for *Back to Godhead* to summarize concisely Śrīla Prabhupāda’s most basic teachings on these topics, teachings he gave us consistently from 1966 through 1977, teachings I thought clear and plain enough for all ISKCON devotees to agree on. Later, I referred to these teachings as “plain vanilla,” using an American idiom for anything simple, basic, unadorned, and standard, the allusion being to vanilla ice cream, taken to be the most basic flavor. But some people, to my surprise, were ready to argue against even these most basic teachings. “Plain vanilla,” it seems, needed to be made plainer. So I here offer that BTG essay again, this time with footnotes citing authority for each point made. I am convinced that within Śrīla Prabhupāda’s plain and simple teachings lie the answers to even the most complex of questions. I hope that by this simple presentation, Śrīla Prabhupāda will be pleased.

Hare Kṛṣṇa.

Jayādvaita Swami

**From Master to Disciple**

The *paramparā* is the chain of spiritual masters and disciples through which Kṛṣṇa consciousness is taught and received.In *Bhagavad-gītā* Lord Kṛṣṇa says, “I taught this ancient science of yoga to the sun-god, Vivasvān. Vivasvān taught it to his son Manu. And Manu taught it to his son Ikṣvāku. In this way, through the system of *paramparā,* disciplic succession, the science was understood by the saintly kings.”

In the *paramparā* system, then, the original teacher, the original spiritual master, is Lord Kṛṣṇa, God Himself.The Lord gives perfect knowledge, and that knowledge is handed down from master to disciple.It’s like a ripe fruit handed down from person to person, from the top of the tree to the ground.

In the chain of *paramparā,* each spiritual master has the duty to transmit the knowledge of Kṛṣṇa consciousness as it is.He is not to add anything, subtract anything, or change anything.He simply has to deliver the message, just as a postman delivers a letter, contents fully intact.

According to the Vedic scriptures, one who is serious about attaining self-realization or God realization or the ultimate goal in life must approach such a bona fide spiritual master.It is not optional; accepting a bona fide spiritual master is essential.

The method of accepting the spiritual master is explained in *Bhagavad-gītā:* one must surrender to him, inquire from him, and serve him.Inquiry alone is not enough.One must humbly submit oneself before the spiritual master,accepting him as a representative of God.

The spiritual master is not God, and any so-called master who claims to be God should at once be rejected as bogus.But the spiritual master is honored as much as God because he intimately serves God through the disciplic chain.Because each spiritual master serves his own spiritual master, all the members of the chain are ultimately servants of God and therefore very dear to God.More precisely, the bona fide spiritual master is the servant of the servant of the servant of God, or Kṛṣṇa.

This is one of the secrets of the *paramparā* system: to be a genuine master, one must be a genuine servant.The student, therefore, surrenders to the spiritual master as a disciple and serves him, and the master responds by answering the disciple’s questions, enlightening him with transcendental knowledge.For the sincere disciple who has full faith in Kṛṣṇa and equal faith in the bona fide spiritual master, all the truths of spiritual realization are factually revealed.

The genuine disciple feels everlastingly indebted to the spiritual master and continues to serve him forever.In this way, even when the spiritual master leaves this world, the master and disciple are connected.The disciple continues to serve the spiritual master by following what the master has taught him,and by teaching it to others.Thus the bona fide disciple becomes a bona fide spiritual master,and the chain of succession continues.

## Keśava Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Again on the day of your Vyāsa-pūjā we sit down near your lotus feet to offer humble prayers. And also to meditate deeply on your transcendental activities and your mission. That is the mission of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu.

The *ācārya*, the authorized representative of the Supreme Personality of Godhead, establishes the principles of devotion and religion, as you have done. The goal of human life is to think about Kṛṣṇa, to become His devotee, to worship Him with love and devotion, and to convert this world into a world of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. When you were with us, all was very easy and perfect with harmony, but when you disappeared, things became disturbed again. The perfect disciple of the *ācārya* tries sincerely to follow his instructions and to live eternally with the spiritual master. In this posture is validated the perfection of the *guru-paramparā*. You have said, “Your love for me will be proved by how you cooperate among yourselves after I am gone.” This movement is actually you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, all so we should all cooperate to offer real peace and happiness to this world full of controversy.

In your instructions you said that you wanted to organize *varṇāśrama* society—to divide society into *brāhmaṇas* (intellectual classes), *kṣatriyas* (administrators), and so on. There should be social division, you said, just as there is a division of the different parts of our body. You knew that this would help people. You often enjoined us not to waste the human form of life by sinful living. You reminded us that you had already given us everything we needed in your books, and you insisted that we all read them.

In your *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* (3.29.17, purport) you write:

In *Bhagavad-gītā,* Thirteenth Chapter, it is clearly stated that one should execute devotional service and advance on the path of spiritual knowledge by accepting the *ācārya*. *Ācāryopāsanam:* one should worship an *ācārya,* a spiritual master who knows things as they are. The spiritual master must be in the disciplic succession from Kṛṣṇa. The predecessors of the spiritual master are his spiritual master, his grand spiritual master, his great-grand spiritual master, and so on, who form the disciplic succession of *ācāryas*.  
It is recommended herewith that all the *ācāryas* be given the highest respect. It is stated, *guruṣu nara-matiḥ*. *Guruṣu* means “unto the *ācāryas,*” and *nara-matiḥ* means “thinking like a common man.” To think of the Vaiṣṇavas, the devotees, as belonging to a particular caste or community, to think of the *ācāryas* as ordinary men, or to think of the Deity in the temple as being made of stone, wood or metal, is condemned. *Niyamena:* one should offer the greatest respect to the *ācāryas* according to the standard regulations. A devotee should also be compassionate to the poor. This does not refer to those who are poverty-stricken materially. According to devotional vision, a man is poor if he is not in Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

So on this, your appearance day, in front of your *puṣpa-samādhi* in Miami, taking refuge in your unlimited benevolence, we fervently pray to you for the strength to spread the *saṅkīrtana* movement of Lord Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu and to follow your instructions faithfully for the welfare of all living beings in this material world. All glories to you!

Śrīla Prabhupāda ki-jaya!

Your servant,  
Keśava Swami

## Krishna dāsa Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Most worshipable Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my *koṭi koṭi daṇḍavats* at the dust of your divine lotus feet.

The history of *sanātana-dharma* is timeless and well recorded in the Vedic scriptures. The general principles are well defined, but from time to time an exceptional personality comes along who not only applies the general principles but also does something incredible, for which due recognition is given to him.

The six Gosvāmīs of Vṛndāvana are famous in the Gauḍīya Vaiṣṇava *sampradāya* for their austerities and revelations obtained through scrutinizing analysis of the Vedic scriptures, but Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura is exceptional because of his single-handed revival of the same Gauḍīya Vaiṣṇavism through his voluminous writings, prayers, and *bhajans,* and he is thus honored with the title of “the seventh Gosvāmī.”

The *ṛṣis* and *munis* of Bhārata are famous for their incredible power and *tapasya,* but sage Agastya is considered exceptional because he changed the order of the *yugas* and made Tretā-yuga appear before Dvāpara-yuga.

This universe of ours is famous for its twelve *mahājanas.* Śrīla Prabhupāda, we may not know what your wonderful spiritual form is in the *nitya-sevā* of Śrī Śrī Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa, but in this material universe of ours, at least, we should recognize your exceptional contribution in giving Kṛṣṇa consciousness to everyone for the next ten thousand years and award you with the title “the thirteenth *mahājana*.” This is the least we can do to honor you in this universe, but actually you are much more than this.

There is an additional honor we can give you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. To date our Vaiṣṇava *sampradāya* has been known as the Brahma-Madhva-Gauḍīya-sampradāya. We should now call it the Brahma-Madhva-Gauḍīya-Bhaktivedānta-sampradāya in recognition of your unparalleled contribution to this world in spreading the teachings of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu.

The *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* (3.25.20) says :

*prasaṅgam ajaraṁ pāśam ātmanaḥ kavayo viduḥ*  
*sa eva sādhuṣu kṛto mokṣa-dvāram apāvṛtam*

**“Every learned man knows very well that attachment for the material world is the greatest entanglement of the spirit soul. But that same attachment, when applied to the self-realized devotees, opens the door of liberation.”**

Śrīla Prabhupāda, may I always be attached to the service of your lotus feet.

The *Mahābhārata* also advises, *mahājano yena gataḥ sa panthāḥ:* “Follow in the footsteps of the *mahājanas.*”

Śrīla Prabhupāda, may I always follow in your glorious footsteps, O thirteenth *mahājana*!

Your very humble servant,  
Krishna dāsa Swami

## Lokanāth Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

For your pleasure, this year I have been working on compiling a book about glimpses of some of the places where I was blessed by your association during your pastimes in India. Here are some introductory thoughts from that book that I would like to offer you on the auspicious occasion of the anniversary of your appearance in this world. It is also my inner and intense desire that all my Godbrothers and Godsisters should also undertake compilation of their memoirs of you.

Here it is:

In last days Prabhupāda was emphasizing how one teaches what one learns. Prabhupāda has taught me different lessons in his talks and dealings with me, and I’m trying to relearn, first of all, those lessons. Familiarize and reflect upon them. Evaluate them. And then teach that, or pass that on, to the future generations so that those valuable moments that he took, that he invested in me, do not go in vain, or do not end up or terminate with me.

The ideal thing would have been to reach out to every single devotee, including members of the congregation, to make the picture, but that is just a dream. That dream could be realized to a certain extent if some other Prabhus compiled and published their memoirs.

“History will mark this period, how the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement changed the world.”—Śrīla Prabhupāda on an overnight train from Allahabad to Calcutta, January 1977. This book is a small attempt at writing a page of that history. Whenever in the future historians attempt to write how this movement was started, *My Prabhupada* may give them some clues.

In my early life, I used to take pleasure in reading the biographies of great people this world has seen. Imagine my delight when I personally came in contact with the greatest jewel among all the lives I had studied.

It was my wish to write my memories of Śrīla Prabhupāda as one of my Centennial offerings, but being busy with overwhelming responsibilities, I could not fulfill that obligation during 1996. Finally, it is here.

On different occasions, like Śrīla Prabhupāda’s Vyāsa-pūjā, or reunions during the Centennial, or whenever the devotees wanted to hear, I took the opportunity to speak about Śrīla Prabhupāda. The devotees cherished remembering Prabhupāda so much that I felt compelled to write a more complete account of my experiences with him. Naturally, I am inclined to reminisce upon my relationship and encounters with Śrīla Prabhupāda, and on the instructions he gave me. As the years have passed, some of those memories have been lost. Such is the influence of the time, especially in this Age of Kali. If you want to remember something, Prabhupāda said, write it down. I’m kicking myself for not having done that. If I had kept account of every time I met him, there would be volumes for me to remember him with. So here I am now, taking stock of the memories I still have, not wanting them to deteriorate even more. Please forgive my imperfect recollection.

I want to see Śrīla Prabhupāda glorified, not Lokanāth Swami, but as they say, “name, fame, glory—same old story.” Probably there is already more of me in this book than there needs to be. I came across a quote in Satsvarūpa Mahārāja’s *Life with the Perfect Master*.

I hope to avoid a “more fortunate than thou” attitude as I tell my story, since a Vaiṣṇava should not try to project an image of himself as a unique, favored disciple. In the name of glorifying Śrīla Prabhupāda one should not think of himself as glorious and pure and conclude that that is why Śrīla Prabhupāda gave him attention and personal association. Śrīla Prabhupāda was equal to all. Of course, whoever personally associated with him was certainly very fortunate—but that fortune was the causeless mercy of the pure devotee. Accounts of life with Śrīla Prabhupāda, therefore, should be told in a sincere attempt to share one’s good fortune with others—to grant the hearers a further, intimate entrance into Śrīla Prabhupāda’s wonderful life—not to gain credit for oneself. Most of what I have read or heard of my Godbrothers’ accounts of life with Śrīla Prabhupāda have been humble and sincere presentations, and I hope to follow that path.

To the extent I am able to write *My Prabhupāda* in the same spirit, I will consider it a success.

Most of Śrīla Prabhupāda’s instructions are applicable to all devotees. A few have mainly personal relevance. All are as valuable today as when they were first written or spoken.

As I seriously got down to the business of reviving my memories of Śrīla Prabhupāda, as I sifted over the different instructions and suggestions Prabhupāda gave me in his conversations with me and in his letters, those memories became more meaningful—they conveyed a deeper meaning to me than they ever had before. As a result, the bond of my relationship with him have further solidified, and I am feeling like getting more properly situated in my relationship with Śrīla Prabhupāda. The process has increased my understanding of who Prabhupāda is, of what his position is, and I am feeling like increasing my dedication to carrying out Prabhupāda’s order and mission.

Śrīla Prabhupāda dealt with each disciple in a way just perfect for that soul. The mellows of Śrīla Prabhupāda’s relationship with his different disciples bear close study. At the same time, most of Prabhupāda’s dealings or instructions with one disciple are not just for that individual; they are universal, like Kṛṣṇa’s instructions to Arjuna. In this way I hope *My Prabhupāda* will help readers remember and establish their relationship with Śrīla Prabhupāda, much as other devotees’ remembrances of Śrīla Prabhupāda have helped me find inspiration and direction.

My life has been governed by the principles he taught me with his example. This book is a compilation of countless brief accounts, encounters I had with my beloved spiritual master. I hope to immortalize those moments. May they continue to keep me alive in Kṛṣṇa consciousness. This is one book I want to write and to study.

To know or remember Prabhupāda more completely, it’s necessary for every single devotee Prabhupāda interacted with to write accounts of their moments with Śrīla Prabhupāda. What I am writing is not even the tip of the iceberg. There are hundreds and hundreds of other authorities on Śrīla Prabhupāda, with whom he spent much, much more time than me. And yet there are others who had hardly any direct contact, but even they could contribute something, however tiny or fleeting, to make up the whole picture. Bhakti Vikāśa Swami, for example, had very little personal contact with Śrīla Prabhupāda, but he has still written a small book remembering Śrīla Prabhupāda. If everyone writes, our base of knowledge on Prabhupāda would be a more complete one. Only the generation of devotees who are direct followers of Prabhupāda are capable of doing this service. If we do not do it, the future generations will curse us for not having left behind a sufficient literary legacy.

Prabhupāda captured us. We became sold out to his mission, with no other interest in life. We were one hundred percent his, and he also let us know that he was ours. His dealings with us were so personal that each one of us felt “Prabhupāda is my Prabhupāda.” He may be others’ also. He may be this Swami’s Prabhupāda or that Prubhu’s Prabhupāda, but first of all he is my Prabhupāda. I understand he is busy, he has so many spiritual children, but he still cares for me. I am on his mind, in his heart.

This is another understanding of how he was the true representative of Kṛṣṇa for all living entities. Each living entity knows that Kṛṣṇa is “my Kṛṣṇa.” Certainly He is others’ as well, but first He is my Kṛṣṇa. Prabhupāda was able to make every single one of us feel like he was mine. And he didn’t have to give so much time, and we didn’t demand so much to get that feeling. He gave a quality of time that was from the heart, and we would get that feeling that he is my Prabhupāda. And even though he hasn’t been physically present with us for so many years, we still feel that Prabhupāda is my Prabhupāda. I am confident that this feeling will be with us for the rest of our lives.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, may the words of this offering provide some inspiration to my Godbrothers and Godsisters to also take up this service of compiling their memoirs so that your glories will be documented and preserved for the benefit of all the future generations.

Your unworthy servant,  
Lokanāth Swami

## Mahāvishnu Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

By your shining bright mercy, Śrīla Prabhupāda, you sent your disciples to England, and one of them mercifully found me and released me, who was locked up in Māyā’s dark chains. You had empowered their words to send a “not-in-order” messenger to awaken the glimpse of Divine Truth (Kṛṣṇa) in my devilish heart. Even in a simple phrase—“You are not that body”—repeated by that *vartma-pradarśaka-guru* lay the seed of the new life you generously offered me.

The holy names—Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa, Hare Hare / Hare Rāma, Hare Rāma, Rāma Rāma, Hare Hare—repeated by him became accepted somehow or other, and I took up the chanting apparently seriously. Then you seemed to come even closer to me in the words of your *Bhagavad gītā As It Is*. I recognized in your words the authority of the Supreme. You and your representatives were representing Kṛṣṇa as the external manifestation of the Supersoul, who had been staying hidden within my dirty heart, forgotten, for how many lifetimes? I cannot say. By your divine grace something stirred within, and a little fervent fluttering aspiration to go back to home, back to Godhead, was awakened.

“According to the deliberate opinion of all the revealed scriptures, the spiritual master is nondifferent from Lord Kṛṣṇa. Lord Kṛṣṇa in the form of the spiritual master delivers His devotees.” (*Caitanya-caritāmṛta*, *Ādi-līlā* 1.46)

I was allowed to take up practicing devotional service in 1971 in Bury Place under the lotus feet of Their Lordships Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Londonīśvara and Their Lordships Jagannātha, Baladeva, and Subhadrā. I saw your disciples and followers bowing to your picture on the *vyāsāsana,* and I thought, “When they are more advanced (like me) they will realize they do not have to do this bowing down.” Naturally I also quickly learned to bow down. “Why not?” I justified it to myself.

You initiated me in due course with first and later second initiation. Seeing my dirty (Ratha-yātrā-car-paint) fingernails, you queried, “Oh, you want to be a *brāhmaṇa*?” Seeing my fear of your rejection, you showed me your pearly fingernails and shrugged as if to say to Kṛṣṇa, “In for a penny, in for a pound.”

So many days, weeks, months, and years have passed now since those early days, and so many tests I have taken and failed and some maybe passed. You are surely still there, trying to deliver me, but I remain here—stuck—still hitched up by Māyā’s deadly tight noose of lusty desires.

“One must be able to pass the test of the spiritual master, and when he sees the genuine desire of the disciple, he automatically blesses the disciple with genuine spiritual understanding.” (*Bhagavad-gītā* 4.34, purport)

How long will your patience last before you will reject me as actual garbage? “He is not genuine!” My days are numbered, it seems.

Although it seems that my heart of stone may never melt, at least I can pretend to write this offering with love and submission, as it is the duty of this so-called disciple to try and offer you, my spiritual master, some thankful words of praise on this, your Vyāsa-pūjā day.

I am hoping against hope that one day I may again feel those early fluttering aspirations rise again with a fresh striving for perfection under the shelter of your lotus feet. At least if I can remain faithful to your faithful followers, maybe there is some hope you will lump me in with them even though I am the black sheep. By their association I may pull through.

Where in this dismal tale is some ray of hope? You are that brightest of all—the great ray of hope! You can give us Kṛṣṇa! Today is your Vyāsa-pūjā day again, Śrīla Prabhupāda! Please hear us! We will now strive sincerely to pass all the tests. By your grace it may not be too late!

I am hoping and resolving today to somehow improve myself to become a truly worthy son of yours. I want to somehow pass the test and hold strictly and closely to your lotus feet. Then only may I be able to truly help you please your own Guru Mahārāja.

“By his strong desire \[the desire of Śrī Śrīmad Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura\] the holy name of Lord Gaurāṅga will spread throughout all the countries of the Western world. In all the cities, towns, and villages on the earth, from all the oceans, seas, rivers, and streams, everyone will chant the holy name of Kṛṣṇa.” (“Prayer at the Lotus Feet of Kṛṣṇa,” stanza 2)

Resolving to one day become a genuine disciple of yours,  
Mahāvishnu Swami

## Nityodita Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to your never-ending service! Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, help me! How can I serve you now?

When I was in Vṛndāvana this year, I sat in your most sacred rooms at the Rādhā-Dāmodara temple. I reflected on how you walked with Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura at Rādhā-kuṇḍa, your last meeting with him. Your spiritual master was very distressed by the quarreling among his disciples. He told you, “*Āgun jvalbe*” (“There will be fire”). One day, he knew, there would be fire in the Calcutta Gauḍīya Maṭha, and that fire of party interests would spread and destroy.

As predicted, that fire broke out almost immediately after Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta’s departure. As you state in your purport to *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 4.28.48, “The *ācārya,* the authorized representative of the Supreme Lord, establishes these principles \[of devotional service\], but when he disappears, things once again become disordered.” The disciples of Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī, your Godbrothers, who were once best of friends and *saṅkīrtana* partners, now found themselves face to face as bitter enemies pressing their claims through lawyers in court. They began wasting valuable time and money in legal battles distracting them from their sacred mission of spreading the message of Lord Caitanya. Quarreling and fistfights broke out. The preaching of the Gauḍīya Maṭha greatly suffered and practically ground to a halt.

I look around today...History is painfully repeating itself!! Now your movement is in flames!! What are we to do now? You most mercifully instructed us that we could show our love for you by how we cooperate after you departed. Now we find our love being tested by how diligently we fight against our own Godbrothers and children in the courtrooms of the world! Is this how we are to serve you now? In a forest fire, the destruction is terrible, yet the purification is great. Much valuable forest is destroyed, but dead wood and underbrush that stagnate growth are also cleared away. Within “no time,” by nature’s clock, a new forest appears, better than the one before. Out of the ashes of the Gauḍīya Maṭha fire, you emerged alone, to give birth and nurture your International Society for Krishna Consciousness. There was nothing to lament. All over the world there are thousands of dedicated devotees cooperating to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness, just as you wanted. “The perfect disciples of the *ācārya* try to relieve the situation by sincerely following the instructions of the spiritual master.” (*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 4.28.48, purport) Despite the present conflagration, these sincere followers are ready to tend to the burnt remains. On this most auspicious day, I am praying that the prickly bushes, brambles, and rough shrubs of our past misdeeds and mistakes that were choking the ISKCON forest will soon be burnt away. My only hope and source of strength is your inspired example to push on despite all obstacles.

Your child,  
Nityodita Swami

## Prahlādānanda Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

The basic principle of Kṛṣṇa consciousness is to always remember Lord Kṛṣṇa and never forget Him. This is easily done if one is in love with Lord Kṛṣṇa. This love for Lord Kṛṣṇa is natural and is dormant in everyone’s heart. Since He is all-attractive, this love for Lord Kṛṣṇa becomes manifest as He reveals Himself to His devotee, just as the sun gives sunlight to one in its presence. The Supreme Lord reveals Himself in proportion to the service rendered by the devotee. The most potent services to help one become absorbed in Kṛṣṇa consciousness are chanting the holy name of the Lord, hearing books such as *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* serving the Deities, serving the devotees, and living in holy places dedicated to the service of Lord Kṛṣṇa.

ISKCON, the movement dedicated to spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness, is based on the above principles. Śrīla Prabhupāda explains in his purport to *Bhagavad-gītā* 18.65:

Life should be so molded that one will always have the chance to think of Kṛṣṇa. One should always act in such a way that all his daily activities are in connection with Kṛṣṇa. He should arrange his life in such a way that throughout the twenty-four hours he cannot but think of Kṛṣṇa. And the Lord’s promise is that anyone who is in such pure Kṛṣṇa consciousness will certainly return to the abode of Kṛṣṇa, where he will be engaged in the association of Kṛṣṇa face to face. This most confidential part of knowledge is spoken to Arjuna because he is the dear friend of Kṛṣṇa. Everyone who follows the path of Arjuna can become a dear friend to Kṛṣṇa and obtain the same perfection as Arjuna.

Śrīla Prabhupāda created a society based on these principles. Even Śrīla Prabhupāda’s system of management was simple: a GBC body whose members’ primary duty is to be Kṛṣṇa conscious and see that the rest of ISKCON’s devotees are also properly practicing Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Śrīla Prabhupāda created temple presidents, who, along with their assistants, should be examples of Kṛṣṇa consciousness and whose main service is to participate in and encourage Kṛṣṇa conscious programs within their temples. On the basis of strong practice of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, the members of ISKCON then become enthusiastic to share the most confidential part of knowledge, which was spoken to Arjuna.

*Anarthas, aparādhas,* and material desires tend to cloud over our consciousness and make things more complicated. If there are problems within ISKCON, elaborate structures, systems, and bureaucracy by themselves can’t solve them and may simply add more complications. However, if we try the best we can to understand and institute the simple instructions that Śrīla Prabhupāda left us, then gradual progress toward the goal is assured.

Prahlādānanda Swami

## Rasānanda Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

It is said there is no difference between *vāṇī* and *vapu*.  
In order to understand *vāṇī,* one needs some realization.  
You said that you never felt alone. You always felt the presence of  
your beloved spiritual master, Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura.  
I understand your feeling as realization of *vāṇī*.  
As for me, I cannot realize your presence anywhere.  
It is said that you are present in your books and instructions.  
I am failing to realize this truth.  
I cannot see or perceive you; therefore there is only darkness.  
Please, be kind to me.  
Reveal your pure instructions and your lotus feet to me.  
Everything is so dark. I really need your blessings upon me.

Your eternal servant,  
Rasānanda Swami

## Śacīnandana Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

**What If You Had Not Come?**

Who would have had the tolerance to preach to us fallen Westerners,  
the power  
to uplift and engage us,  
the wisdom  
to apply the ancient teachings of the *Vedas*  
to the time, place, and circumstance of the twentieth century,  
the softness of a rose  
to attract us,  
and the hardness of the thunderbolt  
to smash the granite of our egos?

Who would have had the surrender  
to leave every conceivable security behind  
and depend solely on Kṛṣṇa,  
the purity to perceive the Lord everywhere,  
the transparency to direct all attention to Kṛṣṇa only,  
and the selflessness not to become disturbed by the selfish  
and tirelessly work for their benefit?

Who would have had the compassion of Vāsudeva Datta  
in praying for the deliverance of the fallen souls,  
the courage and skill of Arjuna  
in spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness against all odds,  
the intelligence of Uddhava  
in applying the eternal principles of the *Vedas* to modern times,  
and the literary power of Vyāsadeva  
in writing transcendental books?

Who but you could have distilled  
from the vast ocean of spiritual teachings  
a medical formula that would work for the people of the twentieth century?  
Who could have shown so clearly  
that by having unflinching devotion  
to one’s guru’s lotus feet one becomes possessed with the great power  
with which one can elevate the people of this world?

Who but you could have given such wonderful examples  
of enthusiasm, perseverance, and patience,  
even in the face of the greatest obstacles?

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*What if you had not come?*

On this divine appearance day of yours, please accept my serious invitation and always stay in my heart.

Your humble servant,  
Śacīnandana Swami

## Satsvarūpa dāsa Goswami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*
