*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

The spiritual master and the disciple always keep company “as long as the disciple follows strictly the instructions of the spiritual master.” That’s what’s left for us disciples of Śrīla Prabhupāda. His instructions. Chant and hear; preach and cooperate.

The purport to *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 4.28.48 discusses how after the *ācārya* disappears insincere persons and cheaters introduce unauthorized processes and things become disordered. This reads as a prophecy about ISKCON post-November 14, 1977. Somehow we’re still moving forward, despite waves of disorder created by persons within and without ISKCON.

Perfect disciples follow the guru to relieve the situation and “to bring actual peace and happiness in the world.”

I didn’t cry immediately when Śrīla Prabhupāda passed away. I was too numb. But I’ve been grieving ever since in one way or another. I need him; I lack the guide to whom I can surrender absolutely. We all have this problem. Now I’m scarred, but still with him. The bond is strong. Kṛṣṇa consciousness is my life, but progress is slow. “Could do better.” They’re still writing that on my report cards.

“. . . To execute the will of the spiritual master, the disciple should be prepared to lay down his life and abandon all personal considerations.” (*Bhāg.* 4.28.50, purport) We don’t have to throw ourselves on the funeral pyre to show faithfulness to Śrīla Prabhupāda. Rather, we are meant to live for him. Like Kuntī-devī, however, who didn’t live as a “merry widow” but only to care for her five children, live to take care of Prabhupāda’s devotees. Preserve yourself as *celā* and as active servant and preacher.

Satsvarūpa dāsa Goswami

## Smita Krishna Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet!

You are my spiritual master, my savior from the illusions of material existence. Without you, where would I be now? Without you, who would I be now?

In the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Ādi-līlā* 1.34, Kṛṣṇadāsa Kavirāja Gosvāmī offer his respects to his spiritual masters. In the purport you explain:

*Gurūn* is plural in number because anyone who gives spiritual instructions based on the revealed scriptures is accepted as a spiritual master. Although others give help in showing the way to beginners, the guru who first initiates one with the *mahā-mantra* is to be known as the initiator, and the saints who give instructions for progressive advancement in Kṛṣṇa consciousness are called instructing spiritual masters. The initiating and instructing spiritual masters are equal and identical manifestations of Kṛṣṇa, although they have different dealings. Their function is to guide the conditioned souls back home, back to Godhead.

You are my initiating spiritual master: you gave me my name, and also Kṛṣṇa’s name, the *mahā-mantra,* to chant. You are also my instructing spiritual master. Through your books, lectures, and morning-walk talks you have instructed me and are instructing me. Thus by your statement in the above-mentioned purport, I know you to be a manifestation of Kṛṣṇa.

Whatever I hear, see, or learn from anybody I can measure against your transcendental instructions. And against them I can measure my own state of affairs. That can be a painful experience, but it is a pain that pushes me to go further in my devotional life.

I like to quote from your purport to verse 35 of the same first chapter of the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta’s Ādi-līlā:* “There is no difference between the spiritual master’s instructions and the spiritual master himself. In his absence, therefore, his words of direction should be the pride of the disciple.” So you are present in your instructions, and I can serve you by remembering those instructions and by trying to execute them.

And sometimes you manifest yourself in the dreams of the devotees. I have heard from both your first- and second-generation disciples that they have dreams of you. Significant dreams. You are alive in so many ways.

Am I alive? Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, give me some life.

The house: an offering unto you. A desire to implement your idea of simple living and noble thinking. A house of straw, clay, and wood at your farm in Sweden where I have my base. Just getting ready in time for the millennium bug. But who cares for millennium bugs? The point is that it is one of your instructions.

In that house there is no electricity. The idea is a house of self-sufficiency, a house of communication, from heart to heart. In that house I would like to welcome you, and my wish is that the loving cooperation of the inhabitants will make you feel at home so that you will sit down and tell us of Kṛṣṇa and His devotees, inspire us to make further progress in our devotional life.

The ideas that are written down tend to become true. So I have written this down—the dream I wish to come true is that I meet you again. Then I will be better prepared to interact with you in personal exchanges—realizing that you never left us, just like in the dream.

Your attempting servant,  
Smita Krishna Swami

## Umāpati Swami

**The Doorbell Marked Bhaktivedanta**

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

As I type the obeisances to Śrīla Prabhupāda, I stop on the name Bhaktivedanta. Suddenly I remember the first time I heard it. It was 1966. I had finally decided to go to the morning classes in Śrīla Prabhupāda’s apartment, and I would have to ring Śrīla Prabhupāda’s downstairs doorbell to be let in.

I would have to come off the street through an entranceway next to the temple and walk down a short corridor with a door at the back, which would be locked, and I would ring Prabhupāda’s doorbell there. Then Śrīla Prabhupāda or someone else in the apartment would press a button to unlock the door, and I could go through the courtyard to the back building.

But I did not know what name to look for on the doorbell.

“What’s the Swami’s name?” I asked Rāyarāma.

“Bhaktivedanta,” he said.

I was delighted. The name was beautiful. I knew what *bhakti* meant, and I sort of knew what *vedānta* meant. I looked at Hayagrīva.

“Bhaktivedanta!” I said. “You know what that means, don’t you?”

I had found a new treasure in this name. It seemed to contain everything that the Swami was teaching. How could anyone have such a perfect name?

The next morning, when Second Avenue looked fresh and clean in the early sunlight and the traffic was still quiet, I walked in the front entrance, walked to the back, looked over the doorbells, and found the one marked Bhaktivedanta. It felt special to find that button and press it. The door buzzed, and I walked across the courtyard and up the stairs to Śrīla Prabhupāda’s apartment and my first morning class.

Every morning it was the same. First I would ring the doorbell marked Bhaktivedanta, and then I could go to class. And there was always something special about seeing the name and pressing the button.

I forgot about the doorbell over the years, but I still cannot do anything without thinking about my spiritual master’s name first, as I touch my head to the floor and offer obeisances. I like this name. I like to say it, and I like to hear it, and I sometimes wonder whether my disciples enjoy hearing me offer obeisances to Śrīla Prabhupāda as much as I used to enjoy hearing Śrīla Prabhupāda sing obeisances to his own spiritual master as he led the morning prayers.

“I like hearing the Swami offer obeisances to his spiritual master,” I once told Hayagrīva. Offering obeisances with the name of the spiritual master was the link to the eternal disciplic succession. Even great devotees like the Swami did it.

And now, thirty-three years later, I wonder whether I can pass on the sweetness and simplicity and nostalgia to those coming after me just as Śrīla Prabhupāda passed them on to me.

I can do it. I know. I just have to remember the doorbell. I just have to take some time to remember the name Bhaktivedanta and the great spiritual master it belongs to before I start anything.

All glories to His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, the founder-*ācārya* of the Hare Kṛṣṇa movement!

Your servant,  
Umāpati Swami

## Varṣāṇā Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to you on this most auspicious day.

As I continue to gaze at the sacred photo that formed the basis of my last Vyāsa-pūjā offering, deeper dimensions of profound realizations flood my mind, my heart, my life, and my soul. As I reflect on the way you entered our lives, how you gave us life and a purpose for living, my heart becomes filled with bittersweet feelings. I write them down with hopes of finding where they lead me.

We all know that physical presence  
is destined to pass away,  
and those who walk and talk with us now  
will all be gone someday.

The only true loving relationships  
are those which tread the path to God,  
enduring when all else fades away.

As all of nature reminds us,  
our eternal father has passed out of sight,  
leaving his children behind

and so we look for you wherever we turn.

See the trees above the path,  
where we used to walk and talk with you...  
they seem to be weeping  
as their leaves are falling,  
drifting to the ground...

it is autumn now.

All the daisies in the meadow  
where we used to pick flowers  
to spread on your path  
are now showering down their petals...

they remember.

The lake mirrors the sunset  
while the sky reflects our sadness,  
hues become deeper each moment  
on this dark-moon night

in the month when you departed.

Though the breeze still carries  
the sound of your *bhajan*  
drifting across the valley  
from your *puṣpa-samādhi...*

it is not the same.

It seems the darkest hour is upon us  
and all we can do  
is hope and pray that its presence  
foretells a coming dawn.

You have granted us faith that from darkened times  
something brilliant and glorious will eventually be born,  
and it is that burning desire in times of separation  
that will surely unite us again someday.

So may this grief of separation from you  
serve to fully tear apart  
all unwanted desires  
lingering in my heart.

And may it reveal the mystery  
of communion in your absence,  
helping us to serve you more sincerely  
than we did when you were present.

Even though all our hopes and dreams  
have been focused on you alone,  
we never came to know you  
as deeply as we should.

In your final days amongst us  
in the mortal form we knew,  
you asked us not to leave you,  
agreeing that you would stay.

And yet, something shifted...  
then everything changed.

You suddenly longed to go  
for a picnic on Govardhana Hill.  
How far was it possible in your physical condition?  
The thought was very painful to us then,

But we see its significance now...  
Is that where we will find you?

How we hankered to go with you  
on *parikrama* that day,  
but you departed without us,  
leaving us behind, crying and lamenting.

Feeling abandoned.

...as if we could restrain you  
when Kṛṣṇa called you home.

Now I have begun to appreciate how  
from within your final pastime  
grows deeper inspiration  
to cherish your instructions more carefully

And gradually we are seeing  
with proper understanding  
that you are with us, leading us, just as before,  
forever present in your *vāṇī*.

Your words give me conviction,  
remembrance of you gives courage and strength  
to follow in your footsteps  
on the path of pure devotion.

It is your loving hand that continues  
to guide us on our way,  
beckoning that we follow

Though the path is steep and rugged now...  
we know that you are with us

As we await that most blessed event  
dawning from this darkened night  
when we join with you again

Let the pain of separation completely burn away  
any hopes of finding shelter in this mortal place.

May we always be faithful  
to follow in your footsteps  
through all transformations  
that come our way.

Our association with you now  
is in your words and instructions,  
and as much as we follow with faith and conviction,  
you live and walk and talk with us still.

Your servant,  
Varṣāṇā Swami

## Vedavyāsapriya Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

This year especially has been marked by my intense remembrance and awareness of your instructions. To me, at least, that feeling is most urgently called for and the heaviest. You were expert in commanding your movement by scriptural instructions and applying them with love and trust. You became an example of simple Supreme Truth. You never deviated in your *parā-bhakti,* which is beyond speculative misinterpretation or logic. You simply depended upon the mercy of the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mahā-mantra,* which established your spiritual empire, ISKCON. You never conducted any motivated effort of your own to do something, but everything came about by the purity of your chanting of the *mahā-mantra*. Seemingly you underwent so many physical difficulties during your service, but you never complained. You never showed shortcomings. Rather, you patiently showed your perseverance. Thus you showed us how to conquer Māyā by your commitment to Guru and Kṛṣṇa with a style of both spiritual and physical attitude that every sincere disciple should aspire to follow.

As the century is closing down, the devastating material potency of the Lord, manifested through Kali and his agents, becomes predominantly rampant. It can overwhelm even a reputed and sincere mind and make one commit acts of abominable stupidity; thus one is stripped of the power, position, and reputation won in the service of your movement. In such trying times, what else is there but your overall exemplary character to protect us?

Please accept this *aṣṭaka* offered on this auspicious day of your appearance. Even though I am not qualified in Sanskrit grammar, and thus committed many mistakes, it is an outpouring of glorification of your character from the bottom of my heart.

**Śrīla Prabhupāda Tri-Sandhyāṣṭakam**

*1) prātaḥ-kāle smarāmi prabhupāda-caraṇaṁ*  
*naiṣṭhikī bhaktiṁ pradāti pāda-dvandvam*  
*guru-praṇāma mantra vadāmy udgite*  
*sthira-matiṁ sthāpayāmi yasya carite*

*2) sarasvatī devasya ājñānupalanaṁ*  
*vitarati śrī-caitanya-śikṣāmṛtam*  
*ati-vṛddhāvasthān saṅkalpa dṛdha-mate*  
*sthira-matiṁ sthāpayāmi yasya carite*

*3) yasya deve parā bhakti siddhayetā-karaṇam*  
*yathā guror vākya hṛdayenāṅkitam*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi sarvatra tārite*  
*sthira-matiṁ sthāpayāmi yasya carite*

*4) madhyāhne smarāmi prabhupāda-caraṇaṁ*  
*sadā sarva kāryeṣu bhakti-vardhanam*  
*nirbandhe kṛṣṇa-sambandhaṁ pradarśite*  
*sthira-matiṁ sthāpayāmi yasya carite*

*5) sarva saṅkīrtaneṣu saṁsthānaṁ sthāpituṁ*  
*karmasu kauṣalaṁ sampūrṇa-yogam*  
*racituṁ ’niyamaka maṇḍalādhikṛte*  
*sthira-matiṁ sthāpayāmi yasya carite*

*6) ‘āmāra ājñāya’ sūtra paramparā-vardhanaṁ*  
*sanniṣṭha kāraṇa mātra mūla-dharam*  
*yathā śṛnu bhāgavatam tathaiva vartate*  
*sthira-matiṁ sthāpayāmi yasya carite*

*7) sandhyā-kāle smarāmi prabhupāda-caraṇam*  
*asmādīya navadheṣu ātmāvalokanam*  
*kiṁ vā prabhupāda prasīdati prayatne*  
*sthira-matiṁ sthāpayāmi yasya carite*

*8) ahar-niśaṁ kariṣye sarvāparādhaṁ*  
*prāyaścitta vimarśān dhvānta-pāpam*  
*he prabhupāda kṛṣṇa-kṛpā-mūrte*  
*sthira-matiṁ sthāpayāmi yasya carite*

1\) Early in the morning I remember Śrīla Prabhupāda’s lotus feet, which bestow unwavering devotional service. I sing aloud his *guru-praṇāma* mantra and establish steady intelligence on his characteristics.

2\) He faithfully carried out the order of Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī and distributed the nectarean instructions of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. Even though he was at an advanced age, his determination was undaunted. On this characteristic of his I establish my steady intelligence.

3\) To perform transcendental devotional service to his Lord, he inscribed in his heart the commandment of his guru and delivered souls everywhere from the nescience of impersonalism and voidism. On this characteristic of his I establish my steady intelligence.

4\) In the afternoon I remember Śrīla Prabhupāda’s lotus feet, whose service is ever increasing my devotion. His detachment from the results of such service demonstrated his intimate relationship with Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa. On this characteristic of his I establish my steady intelligence.

5\) To perform every kind of *saṅkīrtana* activity, he founded ISKCON, the organization which teaches that offering the results of one’s activities with love to Lord Kṛṣṇa is the perfection that establishes complete reunion with the Lord. To oversee this organization he created the Governing Body Commission. On this characteristic of his I establish my steady intelligence.

6\) To continue the disciplic succession he used Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s *‘āmara ājñāya’ sūtra,* in which sincerity in propagating Kṛṣṇa’s teachings is the only foundational qualification. His behavior exactly exemplified what he had heard from *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*. On this characteristic of his I establish my steady intelligence.

7\) In the evening I remember Śrīla Prabhupāda’s lotus feet, by whose grace I am allowed to examine my own ninefold service of the day. His lotus feet inevitably ask me whether Śrīla Prabhupāda was pleased by my endeavors. On this characteristic of his I establish my steady intelligence.

8\) What I find lamentable is that day and night I commit all kinds of offenses in my services, for which *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* prescribes the hardest and sole atonement, which can awaken me to Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Seeing my pitiable condition, I have no other way but simply to cry, ‘O Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa’s mercy manifestation!’ Therefore on this characteristic of his I establish my steady intelligence.

Your eternal servant,  
Vedavyāsapriya Swami

## Vipramukhya Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to you.

When I look in your books, I find this passage written by you:

The main business of human society is to think of the Supreme Personality of Godhead at all times, to become His devotees, to worship the Supreme Lord and to bow down before Him. The *ācārya,* the authorized representative of the Supreme Lord, establishes these principles, but when he disappears, things once again become disordered. The perfect disciples of the *ācārya* try to relieve the situation by sincerely following the instructions of the spiritual master. \[*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 4.28.48, purport\]

Please enable us to sincerely follow the instructions you have left, not by word jugglery and strained interpretation, but in spirit, understanding what makes you and Kṛṣṇa happy.

Here’s another quote, in which you say what would make you and Kṛṣṇa happy: “I want to see my disciples become bonafide Spiritual Master and spread Krishna consciousness very widely, that will make me and Krishna very happy.” (Letter, December 2, 1975)

The real “no change in ISKCON paradigm,” it seems, is this:

So similarly, as Kṛṣṇa says, this is called disciplic succession. Lord Caitanya, although He is Kṛṣṇa Himself, but He has accepted the disciplic succession from Kṛṣṇa...I have given you the process of disciplic succession: from Kṛṣṇa, Brahmā; from Brahmā, Nārada; from Nārada, Vyāsa; from Vyāsa, Madhva; from Madhva, Mādhavendra Purī; from Mādhavendra Purī, Īśvara Purī; from Īśvara Purī, Lord Caitanya. So *evaṁ paramparā*. So in the *paramparā* system in that disciplic succession, you will find no change. The original word is there. That is the thing. They are not foolish to manufacture something new. What new? People are after something new manufactured by this tiny brain. What new you can manufacture? That is all nonsense. If you want really thing, then you have to take the old, the oldest. You cannot change anything. Can you change any law of the sun rising or sun setting? The old laws, they are going on, and you have to follow. So you cannot manufacture anything. \[Lecture, December 1, 1966\]

These are troubling times, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Please give us the strength to follow your instructions to help relieve the situation.

Your servant,  
Vipramukhya Swami

## Vyāsa-Pūjā Homages from ISKCON Centers

## Aarhus

Our dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
Beloved spiritual master,  
Please accept our most humble obeisances at the  
dust of Your Divine Grace’s lotuslike feet.

This is the most happy time of the year—  
to write something on this holy day—  
because it is your appearance day, or  
Śrī Vyāsa-pūjā day.

You have given us the highest conception  
of God realization, if we stick to it by  
practicing very carefully and with determination,  
we can get God realization.  
The means is chanting the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mahā-mantra,*  
Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa, Hare Hare /  
Hare Rāma, Hare Rāma, Rāma Rāma, Hare Hare,  
the most intimate name Śrī Kṛṣṇa, for God realization.  
That is Your Divine Grace’s revelation.

You instructed us in your purports  
of the *Bhagavad-gītā,* which are your God realizations.  
In this way you gave us very clear explanations.

In your purport to Bg. 18.65 (*man-manā bhava mad-bhakto*... “Always think of Me, become My devotee, worship Me, and offer your homage unto Me...), you write, “These words stress that one should concentrate his mind upon Kṛṣṇa—the very form with two hands carrying a flute, the bluish boy with a beautiful face and peacock feathers in His hair.... One should fix his mind on this original form of Godhead. Kṛṣṇa. One should not even divert his attention to other forms of the Lord.”

You revealed the personal form of the Supreme God, along with his name, Śrī Kṛṣṇa, His *hlādinī-śakti,* Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī, his residence, Śrī Vṛndāvana-dhāma, his fathers, Nanda Mahārāja and Vasudeva, His mothers, Yaśodā and Devakī, His brother, Śrī Balarāma, his favorite instrument, the flute, his hobby, herding cows, his girlfriends, the *gopīs*, his wives in Dvārakā, etc.

**How to approach these subjects**

In the very begging of the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* (1.1.8), your translation tells us how to approach this knowledge:

“Because you \[Sūta Gosvāmī\] are submissive, your spiritual masters have endowed you with all the favors bestowed upon a gentle disciple.”

**Science**

Your Divine Grace has given us the science of God realization, which is explained on the next line of the above-mentioned verse: “Therefore you can tell us all that you have scientifically learned from them.” This process is the science of God realization; there is no mental speculation or sometimes imagination or theoretical hypothetical realization with no clear explanation. “Maybe this. Perhaps that.” This knowledge is not a fanciful creation of someone’s bright intellectual speculation.

“If one very carefully reads *Bhagavad-gītā, Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam, Teachings of Lord Caitanya* and this *Nectar of Devotion,* that will give him sufficient knowledge to understand the science of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. One need not take the trouble of reading other books.” (*The Nectar of Devotion,* Chapter Seven, p. 65)

You have presented the science of God realization. In your books are both the theory and the practice, the daily *sādhana,* and according to how we apply the theory and the practice, we will enjoy the result, love of God, the highest form of God realization.

**How to study**

Your Divine Grace has explained how to study these subjects in your translation and purport of the following verse from your *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* (2.2.34): “\[Translation:\]The great personality Brahmā, with great attention and concentration of the mind, studied the *Vedas* three times, and after scrutinizingly examining them, he ascertained that attraction for the Supreme Personality of Godhead Śrī Kṛṣṇa is the highest perfection of religion.” “\[Purport:\] Although Brahmājī was to hear Vedic instructions directly from the personality of Godhead, in order to satisfy the inquisitiveness of all prospective students of the *Vedas,* Brahmājī, just like a scholar, studied the *Vedas* three times, as generally done by all scholars. He studied with great attention, concentrating on the purpose of the *Vedas,* and after scrutinizingly examining the whole process, he ascertained that becoming a pure, unalloyed devotee of the Supreme Personality of Godhead Śrī Kṛṣṇa is the topmost perfection of all religious principles.”

**Mythology?**

Later in your *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* (3.6.39) you wrote an explanation of your realizations: “The froggish philosophers and mundane wranglers in science and mathematical calculation may not believe in the inconceivable potency of the Supreme Personality of Godhead, but they are sometimes puzzled by the wonderful jugglery of man and nature. Such jugglers and magicians of the mundane world are actually puzzled by the jugglery of the Lord in His transcendental activities, but they try to adjust their bewilderment by saying that it is all mythology. There is, however, nothing impossible or mythological in the Supreme Omnipotent Person.”

**Aural reception**

You have explained in your translation and purport to *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 1.7.7 that there is no need of high qualifications to advance in Kṛṣṇa consciousness, only the humble aural reception of the instructions from Your Divine Grace and the willingness to apply your instructions with great faith, conviction, and determination: “\[Translation:\]

Simply by giving aural reception to this Vedic literature, the feeling for loving devotional service to Lord Kṛṣṇa, the Supreme Personality of Godhead, sprouts up at once to extinguish the fire of lamentation, illusion and fearfulness.” “\[Purport:\] As for the validity of Śrī Kṛṣṇa as the Supreme Personality of Godhead, there are hundreds and thousands of evidences from revealed scriptures, and there are hundreds and thousands of evidences from personal experiences of devotees in various places like Vṛndāvana, Navadvīpa, and Purī. Even in the *Kaumudī* dictionary the synonyms of Kṛṣṇa are given as the son of Yaśodā and the Supreme Personality of Godhead Para-brahman. The conclusion is that simply by hearing the Vedic literature *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* one can have direct connection with the Supreme Personality of Godhead Śrī Kṛṣṇa, and thereby one can attain the highest perfection of life by transcending worldly miseries, illusion, and fearfulness. These are practical tests for one who has actually given a submissive hearing to the readings of the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*.”

**Remembering Your Divine Grace**

On this holy day let us recall one of the days you blessed us with Your Divine Grace’s personal association.

**Coming Down**

The first morning came  
and you come out  
from Your Divine Grace’s room,  
which was on the first floor,  
for your morning walk.

Then slowly you came down  
like a spiritual swan  
from the spiritual world.

You were holding a cane  
with your hand, to support  
Your Divine Grace while  
you were stepping down  
on the staircase.

You were giving the chance  
to the cane to support your  
divine self by holding it with  
your hand, when you  
came down.

As you came down,  
walking on the  
staircase, the cane  
seemed to be so proud,  
because it was held  
by Your Divine Grace’s hand,  
supporting each step down.

But the staircase was  
smiling so brightly  
at the cane because  
he was carrying  
the lotus feet of  
Your Divine Grace.  
Therefore the staircase  
was full of bliss.

**Morning walk**

After you came down  
you took all of us  
for your morning walk;  
we followed behind  
to the Chapultepec park.

We were all so-called  
young boys only  
twenty years old,  
but because  
you are a realized soul  
you were walking so fast,  
like pure spirit soul.

A tree had fallen  
across the sidewalk—  
I saw it as I walked behind.  
I was thinking to help you,  
thinking of you as an old man.

On that very spot,  
as I walked behind,  
you taught me my first lesson:  
not to think of the spiritual master  
as an ordinary person.

You walked so fast on the sidewalk  
while avoiding the fallen tree  
that I was very surprised.  
I tried to hold you by your arm,  
but you were very fast in avoiding  
the fallen tree and my ordinary hand.

Now I understand:  
you are not an ordinary man;  
you don’t need the help of an  
ordinary hand.

We now know that you are  
a Goloka man.  
Please help us go back to  
the Supreme Lord, because  
we are ordinary conditioned souls.

Your servants at Govindas Cafe, Aarhus, Denmark.

## Adelaide

Our dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our respectful obeisances. All glories unto Your Divine Grace on this most auspicious day of you Vyāsa-pūjā!

We can feel a great momentum taking place in the *saṅkīrtana* movement, Śrīla Prabhupāda. All over the world we are seeing ISKCON achieving victories in its preaching endeavors, but at the same time, while trying to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness we are facing so many obstacles, both internal and external. We are blocked by our own material desires, conflicts among devotees, and Kali’s forces trying to stop our preaching activities.

All of this forces us to go deep within ourselves and analyze what is motivating us in our service. The conclusion we have come to is that to continue preaching we have to become pure in our desires and develop a respectful attitude toward others.

We want to become successful in our preaching here, Śrīla Prabhupāda, because we know it will give you great pleasure to see us overcome our obstacles and become successful in establishing Kṛṣṇa consciousness in this place.

Please bless us that we may co-operate to fulfill your desires, and that we may develop a loving attitude toward one another while serving side by side.

Our strength, determination, and success depend upon your mercy. Please help us to help you.

We remain,  
Your insignificant servants in Adelaide, Australia.

## Agartala

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Beloved Śrīla Prabhupāda,

At this time we feel particularly close to you because last March 21 we held a special installation ceremony for a beautiful *mūrti* of Your Divine Grace made in Italy. And now, because the temple room is very small, your presence is felt in a very grand way. For years your ISKCON temple in Agartala has been suffering both material and spiritual poverty, but now your presence in the form of your life-size *mūrti* is giving us the feeling that now you yourself will direct the life of the temple and so there will be no more unsolvable problems. Then we shall soon see the blossoming of the new complex, consisting of Govinda’s Restaurant and a guesthouse with a jewellike temple at the top. This is all according to the desire of your disciple Bhaktisvarūpa Dāmodara Swami, who is the guru of many devotees here in Agartala. We want to increase the distribution of the transcendental literature you have left us as a precious inheritance, and we want to follow your teachings regarding the proper use of the temple as a preaching center to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness and in regards to keeping a high standard for the worship of the Deities, who are Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Govinda, Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi, and Lord Jagannātha, Lord Baladeva, and Śrīmatī Subhadrā, along with Śrī Lāḍḍu Gopāla, Śrī Śālagrāma-śilā, and Śrī Govardhana-śilā.

We feel great love for you and great inspiration to fight against ignorance and impersonalism, which in this area are present in large quantities. Therefore, dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, please never stop giving us your assistance and valuable guidance, even for one moment. Only in this way we will be able to succeed in vanquishing *māyā* and making Kṛṣṇa consciousness and *kṛṣṇa-prema* flourish all around us. In this way all the conditioned souls who live around the temple will greatly benefit, for by experiencing the higher taste o Kṛṣṇa consciousness they will become happy and feel the desire to join us in the spreading the nectar of the *mahā-mantra:* Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa, Hare Hare / Hare Rāma, Hare Rāma, Rāma Rāma, Hare Hare.

Please engage us all in pure devotional service, so that we will not have the time to be involved in unnecessary and harmful quarrels. We want to become pure in our hearts and be dear to Śrī Kṛṣṇa. We want to hear your voice in *kīrtanas* and always hear your encouragement for preaching. So please give us your special grace, give us the intelligence, the humility, and all other qualities necessary to please our spiritual master, which means pleasing you and the whole *paramparā,* up to pleasing Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa. Then we will all be happy, and we will be a powerful center of attraction for all the unhappy and confused souls. Please accept our sincere obeisances again and again, and please make us chant with tears in our eyes,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

Hare Kṛṣṇa.

Your eternal servants in Agartala, Tripura, India .

## Almviks Gṛrd

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to your auspicious appearance in this world!

**Gurv-aṣṭaka from Your Swedish Farm Project**

1\. “The spiritual master is receiving benediction from the ocean of mercy. Just as a cloud pours water on a forest fire to extinguish it, so the spiritual master delivers the materially afflicted world by extinguishing the blazing fire of material existence. I offer my respectful obeisances unto the lotus feet of such a spiritual master, who is an ocean of auspicious qualities.”

Since ISKCON acquired Almvik in 1981, we have gone through the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* up to the Tenth Canto, Chapter 14, in the morning class and have now come again to the First Canto, Chapter 13. In this chapter the merciful Vaiṣṇava devotee Vidura appears at the eleventh hour to save the condemned soul Dhṛtarāṣṭra. Similarly, Śrīla Prabhupāda mercifully appeared in the West with the *Bhāgavatam* just in time to save the condemned souls. I therefore offer my obeisances unto the lotus feet of such a spiritual master, who is an ocean of auspicious qualities.

2\. “Chanting the holy name, dancing in ecstasy, singing, and playing musical instruments, the spiritual master is always gladdened by the *saṅkīrtana* movement of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu. Because he is relishing the mellows of pure devotion within his mind, sometimes his bodily hairs stand on end, he feels quivering in his body, and tears flow from his eyes like waves. I offer my respectful obeisances unto the lotus feet of such a spiritual master.”

Starting with *maṅgala-ārati,* we have the shelter of *saṅkīrtana*. Even in the cow-barn Śrīla Prabhupāda’s *bhajans* can be heard. When our offenses cease, we will become attentive to the great legacy Śrīla Prabhupāda bequeathed us in the form of *harināma*. I therefore offer my humble obeisances unto the lotus feet of such a spiritual master.

3\. “The spiritual master is always engaged in the temple worship of Śrī Śrī Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa. He also engages his disciples in such worship. They dress the Deities in beautiful clothes and ornaments, clean Their temple, and perform other, similar worship of the Lord. I offer my respectful obeisances unto the lotus feet of such a spiritual master.”

The Pañca-tattva overlook offenses, and we therefore worship Their Lordships in full confidence that Śrīla Prabhupāda will offer Them our service despite the farm devotees’ inability to uphold the very elaborate standard we would like someday to come up to. I therefore offer my most respectful obeisances unto the lotus feet of such a spiritual master.

4\. “The spiritual master is always offering Kṛṣṇa four kinds of delicious food \[analyzed as that which is licked, chewed, drunk, and sucked\]. When the spiritual master sees that the devotees are satisfied by eating *bhagavat-prasādam,* he is satisfied. I offer my respectful obeisances unto the lotus feet of such a spiritual master.”

Self-sufficient in grains and honey, the farm takes special pride in producing milk from cows with a life-time guarantee of protection. Śrīla Prabhupāda introduced us to the understanding that the cow is our mother. Previously, we butchered her and ate her flesh, blind to the dire consequences awaiting us for our vicious sin. I therefore offer my most humble obeisances unto the lotus feet of such a spiritual master.

5\. “The spiritual master is always eager to hear and chant about the unlimited conjugal pastimes of Śrī Śrī Rādhikā and Mādhava, and about Their qualities, names, and forms. The spiritual master aspires to relish these at every moment. I offer my respectful obeisances unto the lotus feet of such a spiritual master.”

Since 1979 the members of the *gṛhastha* community here have educated their children at a small *gurukula*. All through the years the government school department unenthusiastically tolerated our “home education.” Finally, in a breakthrough decision this year, our *gurukula* was given sanction to start a government-subsidized private school. To Śrīla Prabhupāda goes the credit because he practiced what he preached. I therefore offer my most humble obeisances unto the lotus feet of such a spiritual master.

6\. “The spiritual master is very dear because he is expert in assisting the *gopīs,* who at different times make different tasteful arrangements for the perfection of Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa’s conjugal loving affairs within the groves of Vṛndāvana. I offer my most humble obeisances unto the lotus feet of such a spiritual master.”

This morning after class there was an *iṣṭagoṣṭhī* to discuss points for the upcoming regional council meeting for Scandinavian ISKCON. In the wake of our former GBC’s departure from his post, we are challenged to live up to Śrīla Prabhupāda’s expectation that we cooperate. Śrīla Prabhupāda, who considered himself a member of ISKCON, is the world’s greatest expert organizer of *saṅkīrtana* in the age of quarrel. I therefore offer my most humble obeisances unto the lotus feet of such a spiritual master.

7\. “The spiritual master is to be honored as much as the Supreme Lord because he is the most confidential servitor of the Lord. This is acknowledged in all revealed scriptures and followed by all authorities. Therefore I offer my respectful obeisances unto the lotus feet of such a spiritual master, who is a bona fide representative of Śrī Hari \[Kṛṣṇa\].”

Here at the farm our glorification of Śrīla Prabhupāda is in the form of our very humble efforts to follow his instructions. As he once stated while visiting Germany in 1974, the devotees living in Kṛṣṇa conscious rural communities should live without problems and show everyone that they are happy eating nicely and chanting Hare Kṛṣṇa. Śrīla Prabhupāda was empowered to deliver the entire world, yet he accepted the most insignificant service from his followers and was pleased when it was in Kṛṣṇa consciousness. I therefore offer my most humble obeisances unto the lotus feet of such a spiritual master, who is a bone fide representative of Śrī Hari, Kṛṣṇa.

8\. “By the mercy of the spiritual master one receives the benediction of Kṛṣṇa. Without the grace of the spiritual master, one cannot make any advancement. Therefore, I should always remember and praise the spiritual master. At least three times a day I should offer my respectful obeisances unto the lotus feet of my spiritual master.”

It is our great blessing that here at the farm we can live off the land and escape the rat-race of commuting to the city. Let us always remain in the association of devotees and live simply, remembering that this short life is a chance to go back home, back to Godhead. If Kṛṣṇa wants, however, let us drop our spades and go to the hellish city to perform His service there. Our only request is that we may not forget that we are His servants and that we are aware of it by the mercy of His pure devotee, Śrīla Prabhupāda. To such a spiritual master, I offer my most humble obeisances.

The aspiring servants of Śrīla Prabhupāda and the Pañca-tattva at Almviks Gṛrd, Sweden.  
(written by Yogīndra dāsa)

## Amsterdam

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to your lotus feet!

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for your extreme kindness upon all of us, your servants in Amsterdam. Amsterdam was blessed by your visit in the past, and we all feel that you want Kṛṣṇa to be present here. Whenever we have *harināmas,* organize festivals, distribute books, and distribute *prasādam,* the devotees here feel in our hearts that you are smiling upon us. Distributing Kṛṣṇa consciousness gives a lot of satisfaction to your servants here. The preaching spirit here in Amsterdam is certainly appealing. This spirit was here in the beginning of the 70’s, when ISKCON was established here, and it is still here very much today.

Unfortunately, another aspect of ISKCON Amsterdam is its rapid turnover of devotees. If we look at a picture of the devotees here ten years ago, we find only one who is still active in our movement. At the moment we have a nice group of young devotees. We wouldn’t like to see, however, that after ten years again maybe only one is still active. We want devotees to be devotees for the rest of their lives. To succeed in this we have to put time into caring for our devotees by training and educating them. We are happy that in the last GBC meeting the top priority in management was stated to be caring for devotees. By following this instruction, we would like to present you in the future with a group of devotees with even brighter faces.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, we see a bright future. We feel your protection by following your instructions. For your pleasure we especially want to practice cooperation between the devotees in the mood of *amāninā mānadena.*

We hope very much that devotees throughout the world will be merciful upon us and want to bless us with their association.

Your servants in Amsterdam, Holland.  
(written by Mahāprabhu dāsa)

## Auckland

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. I have been asked to write this offering on behalf of the community of devotees serving at New Varṣāṇā, in Auckland. I honestly feel very unqualified, and I am embarrassed to say that after all these years I still feel very far from being a devotee. Still, I can only hope that some way or other my writing this offering may help bring me to the devotional platform and that I may be able to serve the devotees better. Your desire is always that we become pure devotees. I know you’ve given us all the ingredients to attain that—the mercy is there; we just have to grab out and take hold of it.

In 1973 you came to Auckland and installed the Deities of Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Giridhārī. In 1976 you came again and installed Śrī Śrī Jagannātha, Baladeva, and Subhadrā, and Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi. The temple was then a house in the city. Now your beloved Lords reside on a ninety-acre farm twenty-five kilometers from Auckland. I’m sure you’d like it here, Śrīla Prabhupāda. The climate’s not too cold. Especially in the summer we would be able to serve you delicious fresh vegetables grown on the land. I’m sorry we can’t offer you fresh milk from our cows or honey from our hives, even though New Zealand is known as the land of milk and honey; we’d have to buy them outside. Perhaps a simple word from you would change that.

I often wonder what you’d say if you were to reside here as you did in your physical presence. I’m sure you wouldn’t be pleased that there’s a half-built building at the back of the property meant to be a temple for the Deities. Buildings and houses have gone up all around, but the temple remains—almost a haunting reminder of our lack of urgency about your mission. Perhaps I’m wrong, but I feel you’d chastise us “something proper” for this state of affairs.

“Why is this building standing here half built? I’ve been coming here every year for the past twelve years, and still you say, ‘It is opening very soon, Prabhupāda.’ What is this nonsense? How can you embarrass me like this? Hindu temples have come up all around Auckland. You with your so-called Western brains and wealth can’t get it together in a million years.”

“But, Śrīla Prabhupāda, a lot of books are going out here, and new devotees are joining.”

“Very good, but...”

“The congregation is increasing, *nāma-hattas* are going on every night in the city.”

“But finish the temple! What’s wrong with you? Aren’t you coming to *maṅgala-ārati*? Are you following the regulative principles? Aren’t you coming daily to *Bhāgavatam* class or chanting your rounds with attention? Don’t you undergo austerity anymore? Don’t you go on *harināma* or distribute books? Don’t you read my books? Don’t you take *prasādam* with the devotees or worship the Deities? Why is your present temple dirty and empty most of the time? Why are there weeds \[gorse\] growing everywhere? Why are the roads in such bad shape? What’s that rubbish doing there? Are you giving attention to the cows? I’ve given you all these properties; don’t think they are yours. They belong to Kṛṣṇa. That’s your problem: you don’t realize or even believe this. Just lip service, no action. Even the devotees living on this property are not coming to the programs. Don’t think I can’t see all of this. I’m not a fool like you. Do you understand?”

“Well, Śrīla Prabhupāda, I try...

“That’s not enough. You promised. Do it! How else will you get my mercy? Don’t mislead again. Don’t embarrass me again.”

“I’m sorry.”

“If you are, then change. Be sincere.”

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, I’m crying. Please be merciful to me. Please help me to change, to be sincere. I know I’m a nonsense and am not serious. Please don’t kick me away. Please give me the strength to carry on. Your chastisement wakes me up—I’ve been sleeping or in a drunken state. Please always be kind and stand by my side, chastising me, guiding me, pushing me as you wish. May I role and bathe in the divine dust of your lotus feet.

My dear King Rahūgaṇa, unless one has the opportunity to smear his entire body with the dust of the lotus feet of great devotees, one cannot realize the Absolute Truth. One cannot realize the Absolute Truth simply by observing celibacy, strictly following the rules and regulations of householder life, leaving home as a *vānaprastha,* accepting *sannyāsa,* or undergoing severe penances in winter by keeping oneself submerged in water or surrounding oneself in summer by fire and the scorching heat of the sun. There are many other processes to understand the Absolute Truth, but the Absolute Truth is only revealed to one who has attained the mercy of a great devotee. \[*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 5.12.12\]

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, without you I am completely lost. Attachment to your lotus feet is all that I need. May I always ask myself over and over whether that which I am doing is pleasing to you. What would you say if you were here? If I meant this, then I would see you are here. I would feel your clear guiding hand. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are nonetheless my guide, father, savior, everything of worth to me. Why do I still think of groveling in the urine of an ass, interfering in your mission, doing something displeasing to you? You gave your life for this mission. What an ungrateful fool I am! Am I mad? Have I no interest in my own future or spiritual life? Why do I look away from you for solace? Why do I take shelter of mundane solutions? Why do I take pleasure in the problems resulting from the difficulties of managing your mission? Why am I so keen to pull others down, justify myself, find fault, give my opinions on this and that, go to court for what I want, go outside of your shelter to get helter skelter, think I am always right? Got to fight for my rights. You nose-picking fool. I guess I want to be God. I don’t want to really surrender. I don’t want to take the medicine of *uttama-śloka-guṇānuvādāt*. I’m a killer of my own self, wasting the valuable moments of this human life. Please may I see the faults in myself and not in others. I have faith that even a dirty heart like mine can be clean, but only by your mercy. Please give me your mercy. With one kick of your lotus feet, please kick out my nonsense.

I know this is not much of an offering and doesn’t seem to be glorifying you. Maybe its more of an outpouring of frustrated feelings. But how can I really glorify you by a show of words unless I’m sincere to follow your instructions? What kind of devotee am I? Who am I to touch your body in the form of ISKCON? What kind of big devotee am I? So qualified. I remember the incident of Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Mahārāja’s stopping the rascals from dragging the divine body of Śrīla Gaura Kiśora dāsa Bābājī through the streets. Only pure persons can touch the body. Who am I to say how this movement should be? Its all here; I just have to become pure enough to see it. I just have to have faith in your basic instructions.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, I want to touch your body within my heart, I want to embrace your instructions as my life and soul. I want the privilege of seeing you smile when you walk around New Varṣāṇā. I’m ashamed to say I haven’t achieved this in even a small way. Please help me, please help all of your followers, please help us keep your mission together, with the *ācārya* in the center, for your pleasure. Only by your mercy will that be possible. Only by our sincerity will we take your mercy. Your mercy is all that I am made of.

The devotees at the New Varṣāṇā farm community in Auckland, New Zealand.  
(written by Janānanda dāsa)

## Austin

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace.

My wife, Padma, and I came to a new understanding of the Hare Kṛṣṇa movement seven years ago through your disciple Saṅkarṣaṇa Prabhu and his wife, Viṣṇupriyā-devī dāsī. On your order they have been running the Austin Hare Kṛṣṇa Center since 1992. We have learned a lot from them through their sincere efforts in conveying spiritual knowledge passed down from you. All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda!

In the seventies, I had dismissed this movement as being one of the fads that the hippies were going through. I did not see sincerity in them at the time, nor did I care to find out about you, the founder-*ācārya* of the movement. Like many Westerners and Easterners, I did not have any respect for the hippies, nor for their lifestyle. I thought that this was a fad they were going through and that before long they would be done with it and have another fad. As years passed, I observed that the Hare Kṛṣṇa “fad” did not vanish but spread. I was wrong in my assessment of the hippies this time. What neither I nor the rest of the world knew was that you had performed a miracle by spreading the Hare Kṛṣṇa movement all over the world against all odds. This is your kind mercy. This cannot happen except through a pure devotee of the Lord, and so we offer our obeisances unto you, a pure devotee of the Lord!

As we read your books and hear from *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is* through your disciples and grand-disciples, we are learning more and more about the Hare Kṛṣṇa movement that was started five hundred years ago by Caitanya Mahāprabhu. Thanks to your books and your disciples and grand-disciples, we are learning the real purpose of our lives. They are teaching us how to practice Kṛṣṇa consciousness in our daily lives so that someday we will be able to serve the Lord with devotion in His abode. We thank you for your immeasurable effort by offering you our obeisances. Please accept them.

For over twenty years I have been searching for a path toward spirituality, and I can say with confidence that I have finally found the path in your teachings, in your books, in your disciples, and in you, an example of what a pure devotee of the Lord is supposed to be like. Please accept our gratitude by accepting our heartfelt obeisances.

My wife and I regularly chant the *mahā-mantra* daily. I for one can attest that good changes have taken place in my views and attitudes toward life in general. With your mercy we hope to make progress in our spiritual quest. We take this opportunity to thank you for coming down to show us the right path to get out of this world of dualities. All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda!

The devotees in Austin, Texas.  
(written by Dr. Vitthal Mistry and Padma Mistry)

## Baltimore

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

Thank you for taking the trouble to descend and walk among us and give us the matchless gift of Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

When you were physically present, you visited our temple here in Baltimore. You saw photos of Nitāi-Gaurasundara while you were at the Potomac temple, and you wanted to immediately come to Baltimore to see Their Lordships. You said that they were the most beautiful Gaura-Nitāi Deities in all the three worlds.

In one of the cabins behind the temple, you had a meeting with Bhaktisvarūpa Dāmodara Swami, Ravīndra Svarūpa Prabhu, and others. That meeting was the start of the Bhaktivedanta Institute. Since then that Institute, formally located in different places, has preached in many valuable ways to fulfill your dreams.

Nitāi-Gaurasundara still preside over the Baltimore *yātrā,* melting the hearts of the devotees with Their enchanting smiles and merciful glances. They have been miraculously joined by large Deities of Jagannātha, Baladeva, and Subhadrā, who every year in Ratha-yātrā celebrations give their mercy to crowds of conditioned souls in Washington, D.C., New York, and New Jersey.

Your community here is not a very large one, and most of your devotees have occupations in the secular world, but we are all trying in our own special ways to please Your Divine Grace.

Along with their occupations, devotees manage the temple, dress the Deities, sew for them, make garlands and ghee wicks, cook offerings, do *āratis,* paint the temple, and tend the gardens of organic vegetables and flowers. Some devotees organize festivals, lead *kīrtanas,* and give classes.

For your pleasure, there is a monthly weekend program, where congregational members from Orissa gather to glorify lord Jagannātha. Twice a year there is an *akhaṇḍa-harināma* program, where devotees keep a continuous *kīrtana* going for forty-eight hours. There are also regular home programs, including a boat festival in the summer.

Some devotees steadily distribute your books in large quantities. Others arrange for *harināma-saṅkīrtana*. In the evenings, devotees gather at the temple or in their homes for *kīrtana,* to read and discuss *Bhagavad-gītā* and *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* and to pray for each other and the well-being of your whole society. Plans are being made to renovate and expand the temple building. Although there is no formal *bhakta* program, there are many new followers of Your Divine Grace being cultivated through the networking of the community members. Please accept all these services offered at your lotus feet.

After your return to the spiritual world, your movement has continued to grow and spread wonderfully. It is also in the process of becoming more and more pure. You have given us the example of how butter is made into the finest ghee. In the process, the impurities come to the surface to be scraped off. As you turn up the heat to refine your movement into a more pure state, please bless us all to be able to let go of the *anarthas* in our own hearts, so that as Kṛṣṇa scrapes off the impurities, we are not also scraped off, still clinging to them. Bless us to be able to refine and reorganize in your shelter.

Bless us to be able to refine and reorganize whatever needs to be refined and reorganized, so that your glorious movement can go on spreading dynamically. Bless us to be able to harmonize our individual talents and expertise in order to facilitate this continued expansion. Let the expansion take place internally in the depths of our hearts, as well as externally all over the globe.

Please empower us to become pure instruments to help your society flourish in happy, healthy, wholesome ways. Kṛṣṇa consciousness is a joyful process. Please bless all of us to feel and exhibit this joyfulness in our services and in every area of our lives.

We, your servants at New Kulīnagrāma-dhāma here in Baltimore, fall at your lotus feet today, begging for your mercy so that we may be able to expertly do our small part to distribute the gifts you have given us.

Your servants in the ISKCON temple in Catonsville, Baltimore.

## Baroda

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to Śrī Guru and Gaurāṅga!

You wanted your disciples to cooperate and push on the movement of Śrīman Mahāprabhu.

So the situation is so difficult that...It is very difficult to push on this movement, but still we have to do it. And this is the highest *yoga*. If you push on this movement of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, then you’ll be performing the highest type of yoga. Don’t be misled by so-called yogas. This is *yoga*. Yoga means cooperation, cooperation with the Supreme. Just like the head. Head, if the head...The head means head, chief. Why this part of the body is called head? Because it is chief. The brain is working. It orders the hand, “Come here.” It comes. Oh. Therefore it is head. It at once orders the leg, “Go on,” “Oh, I must walk.” Therefore it is head. So a section of person must be head of the society. They must order, and others will work. Then there will be peace. Then there will be peace. But if the leg takes the part of working like head, how it can work? Leg is accustomed to carry order to walk. It cannot work as the brain. Therefore, when the śūdras or the leg take the part of the head, working like head, it is all disorder. It is all disorder. Therefore this yoga system, Kṛṣṇa consciousness yoga system, should be popularized. It is essential in order to stop all nonsense propaganda. \[Lecture on *Bhagavad-gītā* in San Francisco, 17 March 1968\]

But now the situation has arisen where the path of noncooperation seems to have become more prominent. From your letters to various disciples, we see that you had advised:

The whole management should be done combinedly. Do not fight amongst yourselves, as that is my only anxiety. I shall leave for Vrindaban by 25th July. In the meantime, reply to me at L.A. (Letter to Guru dāsa, 1 July 1974)

Regarding your dealings with Bhagavan dasa, when two GBC’s are concerned, the whole GBC must consider. What can I do? I have appointed the GBC not to fight amongst yourselves but to manage. If there is fighting then how will you manage? So the whole GBC committee must decide if there is fighting.” \[Letter to Haṁsadūta, 29 September 1975\]

**Nārāyaṇa Mahārāja:** You have fulfilled all, everything.

**Prabhupāda:** Don’t fight amongst yourselves. I have given the explanation, my will. Execute like that.

**Tamāl Krishna:** Yes, Śrīla Prabhupāda. \[Room conversation, 8 October 1977\]

Śrīla Prabhupāda, on this, your Vyāsa-pūjā day, we pray that by your mercy and the mercy of your dearmost Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa right intelligence will prevail within our society. As it is said in Hindi, *sabko sanmati de bhagavan:* “Lord, please bestow good intelligence upon everyone!”

Śrīla Prabhupāda, after several years of endeavor, we are pleased to inform you that a grand temple for Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Śyāmasundara, Śrī Śrī Śrī Jagannātha, Baladeva, and Subhadrā, and Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi is ready for inauguration here. We hope that this humble endeavor will help fulfill your desires to push on the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement.

We consider that whatever successes we have achieved in preaching the Kṛṣṇa consciousness mission here is due to your grace. We pray that you continue to bless these humble efforts.

In conclusion, we once again pray that you will prevail upon your dearmost Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa to bestow intelligence upon all of us who consider ourselves to be your followers, so that we can combinedly push on your mission and not destroy it by fighting amongst ourselves.

Your servants at ISKCON Baroda, India.  
(written by Basu Ghosh dāsa)

## Benin

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances at your divine lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

We are looking forward to that time when your Vyāsa-pūjā will be celebrated not only by the devotees but by everyone, for at that time everyone will have recognized your immense contribution of saving the world from self-destruction. Everything material deteriorates with the passage of time, but your fame will continue to expand forever. By starting this movement and dispatching your soldiers all over the world, you have enabled countless people to develop the intelligence to question the usefulness of the modern materialistic civilization. Your powerful teachings in your books have changed the course of world events. We are especially fortunate to belong to your ISKCON. We beg to always remain useful in the service of your servants.

All glories to Your Divine Grace Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Your lowly servants at the ISKCON temple in Benin, Nigeria.

## Bhakti-latā Purī

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Our dearmost Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances at you divine lotus feet.

Today we commemorate the auspicious and blissful day of your appearance before millions of encaged souls in this world. Today many of these souls want to open their hearts and show you their love, which is the highest and most important sentiment of all.

Of course, it is not easy to talk about love when our false egos are always getting in the way,  
when we spend most of our time trying to find out a shortcut and a painless way to become blissful in spiritual life,  
when we leave our brothers and sisters lying out on the battlefield, lost in some foolish conception of the absolute truth and sinking in the whirlpool of the material illusion,  
when we consider the price we are paying to be too high for what you have *already* given us—the light to see God,  
when we want the kingdom of God without God.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, today in this wonderful *saṅkīrtana* movement of Lord Caitanya that you have established through your ISKCON, there are thousands of devotees of Your Divine Grace that love you very much, and they are working very hard to not defraud you. In addition, many of them have an incredible missionary spirit and are ready to give even their own lives for just one of your wonderful smiles. Śrīla Prabhupāda, forgive us for not being like them and not knowing how to give you, or each other, our love. We are trapped here in this material world, but we really do want to go back to the spiritual world together and reside eternally at your lotus feet.

Your unworthy servants at Bhakti-latā Purī farm, Marcos Paz, Argentina.  
(written by Mahājana dāsa Brahmacārī)

## Bhaktivedanta Village

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace.

This year we will make an offering of honesty and truthfulness, since these are very precious in our eyes, especially in the very challenging times in which we presently live. Truthfulness being the last leg of religion, we feel it is our duty to keep that leg healthy. As a consequence, the residents of this community that bears your name are trying to apply this principle of truthfulness to those who will have the hardest time making some necessary changes in their behavior and outlooks—that is, themselves.

Of course, a leg does not exist by itself: it is connected to a whole body. If we compare ISKCON to a cow, we could sadly state that she is not in such good shape now. Her legs are shaking due to some internal dissension and attacks from the outside; many of her calves are not happy or satisfied, and their future is uncertain. However, the fact that she is still giving milk confirms that she has to be some kind of transcendental *kāmadhenu*, a wish-fulfilling cow. Probably through the growing pains of ISKCON, Mother Nature is now acting to heal the many ailments that have been afflicting her—abuses of power and position, mistreatment of women, children, and others—to sum it up, impersonalism. Our strong feeling is that all the members of ISKCON, from top to bottom, East to West, insiders to outsiders, should now take it upon themselves to take care of the most ailing leg, the leg of truthfulness. How? by examining their actions and their impact on others, their mixed motivations, their various *anarthas,* by making a list of all the persons they might have offended, hurt, or misguided, and humbly apologizing to these persons in a very personal way, demonstrating practical concern for their feelings, happiness, and material welfare. That should keep each of us busy for quite some time; maybe internal quarrels will then diminish and the atmosphere in our temples and communities will be more impregnated with love, care, and concern, a state of affairs which, alas, is not always the case these days.

Many good things have been done in and by ISKCON, no doubt. We are very grateful to you for the very valuable gifts you have given us; we would be great misers to ignore these. However, we cannot help but see the serious problems that our society is facing these days. Maybe we should remind you that most of the devotees here were all disciples of Harikeśa dāsa, one of your early disciples whom they had put on such an elevated platform. They are still here in your movement, at your lotus feet, despite the severe shock they experienced. Oftentimes the devotees who have not known you personally have difficulties understanding how some wrong policies, abuses, and bad treatments came into existence in our society and were tolerated. Happily, in this community we have the good fortune to have the association of one of your early disciples who had many interactions with you, Jyotirmayī-devī dāsī. Many times she cleared some of our doubts by explaining how some negative or fanatical attitudes that devotees or leaders displayed at some time in the history of our institution did not originate from you or were in no way condoned by you, connected with you, or approved by you. Even though we already knew this by reading your books and the books describing your dealings and activities, we needed some reassurance in certain areas. Now that this is done, we can appreciate again how you were very personal with everyone, how you were kind to children and cared for them, how you did not condemn anyone, how you did not look down on women, how you gave devotees their freedom of action and decision, never using force but employing love, even in the most difficult cases.

In this 1999 offering, we decided to stick to the reality of the present situation at the Bhaktivedanta Village. We will not make big promises that might not be fulfilled and describe all the big plans we have for the future. From the outside, the situation at Bhaktivedanta Village might look pretty bleak: the farm is being sold, and only a few devotees remain. However, those who stay still believe in you and Kṛṣṇa, they are working on developing more loving and personal relationships with one another, and they are still going on the path you chalked out despite all the obstacles.

Once again, we want to thank you for all you have done for us. We pray that your loving and forgiving spirit descends like a soothing stream upon all the members of ISKCON, so they can focus again on the essentials—loving Kṛṣṇa and all His parts and parcels, including themselves. We also pray that you give us the necessary courage to face ourselves in all honesty, thus healing the suffering leg of the ISKCON cow. We are confident that once these bases are established, the atmosphere will greatly improve and ISKCON will unfold in a more organic way, with love as the driving force, and not force, in whatever shape it may adopt.

Your servants at the Bhaktivedanta Village, France.  
(written by Gaurāṅgī-devī dāsī)

## Bhubaneswar

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Our dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

On this most auspicious occasion of the anniversary of your appearance we are enjoined to make some offering to Your Divine Grace. However, we find ourselves standing before you empty-handed, with nothing to offer. Actually it is you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, who have given so much to so many. You have given freedom from the bondage of repeated birth and death. You have given us the *arcā-mūrti, Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* and Tulasī Devī. You have given us the holy name, service to the Divine Couple, and the topmost realm of Vṛndāvana. You have given us the previous *ācāryas* in this Gauḍīya *paramparā*. You have given *sādhu-saṅga hari-kathā*. You have given us three things not of this material world: *bhakti, bhakta,* and *bhagavān*.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are *akiñcana, niṣkiñcana*—you have nothing and you have everything. You don’t own anything and you don’t want anything from this material world, yet you possess *kṛṣṇa-prema*. As such, you are the wealthiest person. What can we offer you? We are truly *dīna, hīna*—poor and fallen; as such we do not have anything to offer. We are so useless that we do not even possess the dignity to be ashamed.

Our dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, you did not come to this world just to be *mantra-datta,* to formally give *mantra*. You are *āśraya-datta:* you came to give shelter to those sincere souls who truly want it. You did not come here to start an institution, to gain followers, money, or prestige. You came as Kṛṣṇa’s representative, to give Kṛṣṇa and *kṛṣṇa-prema* to those sincere souls who want them.

What can we do for your pleasure?

This year we have opened two new projects for you here in Orissa. In the village of Gadeigiri we have opened a grand new temple for their Lordships Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Gopāl Jiu, the worshipable Deities of your dear disciple Śrīla Gour Govinda Swami. And in the Dhenkanal district of Orissa we have opened up a preaching center known as the Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nityānanda Paramārtha Sevāśrama. We stand before you with these humble offerings. However, we realize that it is not such a cheap thing to serve you. You are looking for more than offerings of brick and mortar. As your revered spiritual master Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura wrote in his *Upadeśāvalī:* “We have not come to this world to be construction workers; we are the bearers of the message of Śrī Caitanya-deva’s instructions.”

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you came to this world to give the fallen souls the greatest wealth, ecstatic love for God. We are the caretakers and servants ordered by you to maintain your house. On this auspicious anniversary of your divine appearance, we are analyzing ourselves. How well are we carrying out your instructions? How much have we understood you? How well are we taking care of the house you built? You have instructed us:

The Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement aims at creating an atmosphere of non-envy. Of course, it is not possible for everyone to become Kṛṣṇa conscious, but the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement can create an exemplary society wherein there is no envy. \[Purport to *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 5.13.11\]

If we have not advanced enough in our devotion to even get along with one another, how can we properly manage your house? How can we represent you to the public? The purpose of your property and buildings is to offer sincere seekers of the truth shelter from the material world. They are meant to be places where one can obtain Kṛṣṇa and *kṛṣṇa-bhakti*. If we cannot rise above envy, then how can we give anyone shelter? We are then your representatives in name only. If we do not have *bhakti,* if we have not even solved our own problems, then how can we serve you and represent you? If we have only understood the external and not touched the substance, then we are no better than ritualistic *brāhmaṇas,* caste Gosvāmīs making a living showing the Deity, or the proprietors of a roadside trinket stand at a tourist spot.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have been so kind to us. You have given us so much. You have given us a place to stay and food to eat. You have given us Kṛṣṇa and the spiritual world. You very kindly gave us the association of your dear devotee Śrīla Gour Govinda Swami. In spite of all of your gifts, on this day we are crying. You have given us so much, but now we have discovered that the pot with which we came to you has holes in it, and hence everything has run out.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, poor and useless as we are, we have no offering for you this year. Rather, we are so shameless that on this sacred occasion we are coming and asking something more from you. Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, forgive our faults, please always chastise and correct us. We don’t own anything that we can offer you. The temples, books, cars, and devotees already belong to you. Our very body belongs to you. Whatever strength or inspiration we have comes from you. We can only beg you, Please shape us into proper instruments for your use. Bless us that we can have sweet gentle dealings with all the Vaiṣṇavas. Bless us that we can develop a taste for *hari-kathā*. Bless us that we can develop *kṛṣṇa-bhakti, kṛṣṇa-prema*. Then we can make your house a place free from envy. If by your blessings we get *kṛṣṇa-bhakti,* then we may begin to truly call ourselves your followers. Then we can manage your projects. Then we can really have something to give to the people who come. At that time so many people will be attracted to come to your house and so many who have left will be attracted to come back. If by your mercy we can come to perform our activities with genuine pure devotion, then at that time we will truly have something to offer you.

Your fallen servants at Śrī Śrī Krishna-Balarama Mandir, ISKCON Bhubaneswar, Orissa, India.  
(written by Mādhavānanda dāsa)

## Boston

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble and respectful obeisances at Your Divine Grace’s lotus feet.

As a gardener gathers up his harvest of various flowers and artistically creates a beautiful garland, you have gathered together souls from diverse cultures, nationalities, and qualities of nature, joining them in your mission for the pleasure of your dancing Lord.

We had no idea that God could dance, for we thought that either He was too old or had no legs. We had nothing in common except our own gross ignorance and your causeless compassion.

You have given us the essence of transcendental Vedic knowledge, although our limited intelligence is unable to truly grasp it. You have infused us with the ability to engage in pure devotional service in spite of the fact that our own mind and hearts stubbornly remain stuck in impure thoughts and desires. You have engaged us in the personal service of the most revered Śrī Śrī Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa, and yet we still remain attracted to lowly mundane forms.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, Lord Kṛṣṇa is yours. We can only run behind you, shouting “Kṛṣṇa! Kṛṣṇa!”

Since you are Jagat Guru and the bona fide representative of the unlimited Supreme Lord, Śrī Kṛṣṇa, our ability to praise you sufficiently is limited. Working together to spread your glorious message is the best way to offer our homage to you. We offer this poem for this purpose. All glories to Your Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Śrīla Prabhupāda!

**For His Mission**

His compassion is unfathomable,  
Much like the ancient seas.  
Bathing us in soothing nectar,  
His mercy will never cease.

He is our lord and master  
From without and from within.  
Neglecting him, we are wretched souls  
Who drown in our own sins.

All schisms, fractures, factions, and sores  
Have shamefully been our own creations,  
Yet some dare blame His Divine Grace  
While sinking lower in their own degradation.

Who are we either to think  
That we can do what he has done  
Or to act on his behalf  
Without his personal bond, consent, or wisdom?

He wanted us to preach his message,  
Reaching souls anew.  
We can only bring them to him—  
He delivers them to Mahāprabhu.

It is ignoble either to defect  
Even if one thinks one is right,  
Or to reject souls to whom one once surrendered,  
Neither camp giving up the fight

Yet we work to maximize  
Our own comforts and positions.  
Putting his instruction to cooperate aside,  
We choose battle and legal consultations.

And then who dares to speak of Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa  
While Their dearest servant cries,  
Ignored by his own children,  
To whom he offered paradise?

We never knew of Kṛṣṇa  
Or Caitanya Mahāprabhu.  
Still, we can never know Them  
Unless through his transparent view.

We are so removed from Kṛṣṇa!  
If we realize our state:  
Eternal children of our founder;  
Only he will protect our fate

Of both sides of every issue  
Honesty must be the judge.  
We must stop beating our dear father—  
Let go stubbornness, can’t each side budge?

By our deviating from his desire (“Cooperate!”),  
Our old dysfunctions come into view.  
Absorbed in duality and “I, me, mine,”  
We fail to see each other as “Prabhu.”

His gift is the divine dimension,  
Free from bodily designation,  
But holding on dearly to false ego  
Has resulted in our spiritual constipation.

Above all, he wanted cooperation.  
He said his children were smart, not fools.  
Can’t we find some way to please him,  
Offering ourselves together, as one of his tools?

Who are we all but mercy cases  
Of him, who wants our cooperation?  
Stop fooling ourselves! He knows who we are.  
Fulfilling his desire is our only emancipation.

Some may think it’s sentimental  
To wish that he could be so pleased.  
But isn’t this the way he taught us?  
For preaching, for the world, for him, our egos cease.

Servant of the servant, as he showed us;  
Lower than the blade of grass;  
Each taking responsibility for our own actions:  
Only then will hate and bitterness pass.

For all we’re worth, let us pray  
For ourselves, for each other, dear brothers and sisters,  
To understand his divine command,  
By respecting and reflecting on each angle of vision  
For his mission.

Śrīla Prabhupāda’s humble servants in Boston, Massachusetts.

## Brisbane

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances at your divine lotus feet.
