All glories to your appearance in this world, which was simply for the service and glorification of Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa and the upliftment of all living entities. We remain forever thankful and indebted to you for incessantly providing us with all facilities for understanding and executing Kṛṣṇa consciousness, even during your so-called physical absence.

You have explained and demonstrated all theoretical and practical aspects of achieving full Kṛṣṇa consciousness, leaving nothing to doubt or speculate about. Thus at every moment you are nurturing our Kṛṣṇa consciousness and consequently offering protection from Māyā. It’s not surprising, therefore, that every aspect of Kṛṣṇa consciousness has the full potential of reminding us of you and your mission.

If it weren’t for you, we would not know who Lord Kṛṣṇa or Lord Caitanya is, we would not be attending glorious *maṅgala-āratis* or worshiping Tulasī Devī. We would not be chanting Hare Kṛṣṇa or honoring *prasādam*. If it weren’t for you, we would not be serving the *arcā* forms of Their Lordships Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi and Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa, we would not be visiting the holy *dhāmas* or associating with Vaiṣṇavas. You filled the world with temples and Vaiṣṇavas so that we may readily get their association. If it weren’t for you, we would not have had your books and most of us would never have known what the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* or *Bhagavad-gītā* is. We would have remained bereft of the nectar and guidance in them. And if we didn’t have any of the above, how could we ever have had the opportunity to engage in the activity that pleases you and Kṛṣṇa most—the activity of helping the conditioned souls revive their forgotten relationship with Kṛṣṇa? How could we ever have changed our mundane desires into spiritual aspirations? In other words, without your mercy, we would be doomed to eternal existence in the material world, and we would be encouraging others on the same treacherous path.

Therefore, Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are truly the savior and benefactor of the whole world in this age. Please continue to bless us all with your mercy so we may increase our preaching endeavors and remain at the dust of your lotus feet for eternity.

Your eternal servants in Brisbane, Australia.

## Budapest

Our dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet! All glories to Śrī Śrī Guru and Gaurāṅga! All glories to you!

We would like to express our thanks again and again for giving us fallen souls the opportunity to know the supreme form of the Absolute Truth and taste love of God by getting Lord Caitanya’s mercy in this horrible age, in this horrible place. The value of this gift cannot be compared to anything, and thus our debt to you can never be repaid. Thank you for showing us so many ways by which one can get closer to Kṛṣṇa. Your requests and instructions are ensuring endless work in devotional service for masses of people for 10,000 years. Book distribution, *prasādam* distribution, restaurants, Sunday festivals, farm communities, Deity worship, preaching—all these activities are for everybody’s benefit: for those who perform them and for those who come in contact with them somehow. You often used the following comparison: by watering the root of the tree we are nourishing the whole plant. In the same way, by engaging in spiritual activities to please Kṛṣṇa, we experience happiness we could never attain by mundane, foolish actions.

We would like to please you by offering you as much service as possible in our center. But we know this is possible only by your mercy. ISKCON is working only by your mercy, and whatever results we attain come only with your help.

Please empower us with your divine energies to be able to help you in the realization of your gigantic plans in spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness, which is “the only hope for the suffering living entities.”

Your servants,  
The residents and congregational members of the Hare Kṛṣṇa temple in Budapest, Hungary.

## Canberra

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

On this most glorious day of your appearance, please accept our humble obeisances at your divine lotus feet.

You are delivering the fallen sinful souls in this dark Age of Kali. That is your mercy. But even after being delivered, we are so stubborn that we still have so many relapses into our old ways, and somehow we reconcile ourselves to them by saying, “Well, it’s Kali-yuga, and we are very unqualified to take to spiritual life; the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* says so.

*prāyeṇālpāyuṣaḥ sabhya kalāv asmin yuge janāḥ*  
*mandāḥ sumanda-matayo manda-bhāgyā hy upadrutāḥ*

**“‘O learned one, in this iron Age of Kali men have but short lives. They are quarrelsome, lazy, misguided, unlucky, and, above all, always disturbed.’”**

But this argument is only an excuse, for we should be totally surrendered to your instructions. We hope we can come to the proper platform of devotional service and push on with renewed vigor for the coming year. We want to make the year 2000 a year of spiritual advancement and a year to please you with some new devotees made, a temple constructed, and everyone engaged in devotional service to their utmost capacity. Please bless us with the determination to carried on in devotional service and make spiritual advancement.

Your fallen servants in Canberra, Australia.

## Cape Town

To our dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances at the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace on the auspicious day of your appearance.

*brahmāṇḍa bhramite kona bhāgyavān jīva*  
*guru-kṛṣṇa-prasāde pāya bhakti-latā-bīja*

**“According to their karma, all living entities are wandering throughout the entire universe. Some of them are being elevated to the upper planetary systems, and some are going down into the lower planetary systems. Out of many millions of wandering living entities, one who is very fortunate gets an opportunity to associate with a bona fide spiritual master by the grace of Kṛṣṇa. By the mercy of both Kṛṣṇa and the spiritual master, such a person receives the seed of the creeper of devotional service.” (*****Caitanya-caritāmṛta*****,** ***Madhya-līlā*** **19.151)**

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have bought us this great fortune. You have given us your association and planted this creeper in the hearts of some lost souls wandering around Cape Town. This is your greatness and magnanimity and our causeless good fortune.

Now we are continuing to preach here—a very simple program, as you instructed: *prasādam* distribution, *harināma,* and book distribution. Our strength does not lie in numbers, or opulence, or any material qualifications, expertise or facilities. Our strength lies in our desire to serve you and please you.

“The big thing is they have given their life, everything to Kṛṣṇa—that is never a mistake.” (Letter to Lynne Ludwig, 1973)

Our strength lies in establishing nice relationships among our Godsisters and Godbrothers, with you in the center.

“How does he explain how so many devotees can live together peacefully and happily if there’s no reciprocation of love? Materialists can’t even live together with their wife for very long.” (Morning walk conversation, 18 June 1976, Toronto)

You taught us in Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī’s *Upadesāmṛta* how to have loving exchanges among devotees. Simply by following these instructions, we can develop a humble service attitude. In this way we can feel true indebtedness toward devotees and appreciation for them.

“The life of the Kṛṣṇa conscious society is nourished by these six types of loving exchange among the members; therefore people must be given the chance to associate with the devotees of ISKCON because simply by reciprocating in the six ways mentioned above an ordinary man can fully revive his dormant Kṛṣṇa consciousness.” (*The Nectar of Instruction,* Chapter 4, purport)

Our strength lies in our enthusiasm to preach and spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness in South Africa.

“Your report is very encouraging to me, that you distributed 110 Gitas in two days in Capetown, South Africa....For the people in general you can hold kirtana and distribute prasadam sumptuously.” (Letter to Puṣṭa Kṛṣṇa Swami, 1974)

“This is the main thing—to always be anxious how to spread the glories of Sri Caitanya Mahaprabhu's mission. I note that you have got this dedication and I am very pleased.” (Letter to Bhūmatā-devī dāsī, 1975)

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you left comfortable, peaceful Vṛndāvana to bring us very fallen and hopeless souls some hope. You sacrificed everything to please your Guru Mahārāja. You bought and continue to bring us the sweetness of Vṛndāvana.

The dust of Vṛndāvana is so soothing because Kṛṣṇa executed His pastimes there. The inhabitants of Vraja are so peaceful because they don’t want thanks, they want only to glorify Rādhā-Śyāma. The *darśana* of Girirāja-Govardhana is so embracing because He is supplying everything for the pleasure of Kṛṣṇa. The mood of the *gopīs* of Vraja is so sweet because they are wanting only to bring each other closer to Kṛṣṇa.

What reason could be so great to leave the land of Vraja? Śrīla Prabhupāda, you taught us by your example.

“Preaching in the snows of Russia is more sweet than any mango.” (Conversation, 1976)

Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu confirms that this *saṅkīrtana* movement will enable us to fully taste the nectar for which we are always hankering.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, wherever you are is Vṛndāvana because Vṛndāvana is in your heart.

By preaching the glories of your books and your pure Vaiṣṇava qualities, we can bring Vṛndāvana right here. By our following your instructions and perfect example, Vṛndāvana may enter our hearts too. By becoming the servant of the servant of the servant, we can give Vṛndāvana to others.

We beg that we may always aspire for your mercy and always be engaged in spreading your glories to everyone we meet.

Śrīla Prabhupāda *kī jaya*!!

Your servants at ISKCON Cape Town, South Africa.  
(written by Gaurī-devī dāsī)

## Chandigarh

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Srila Prabhupada.

Kindly accept our humble obeisances at your lotus feet. Kindly forgive our innumerable offenses and allow us to serve you in some small capacity. You are not at all dependent on us, but we are totally dependent on you, even though we always manage to forget this. What is it you have not accomplished for the service of Śrī Śrī Guru and Gaurāṅga? You have achieved everything single-handedly. Those who desire to do something to please you are the most blessed, and those who cannot are lost heavily, and those who do not despite having the opportunity are verily the Kali-chelās, the servants of Kali. Please bless the devotees serving you nicely all over the world and somehow guide us to follow in their footsteps.

Your servants at Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Mādhav ISKCON Mandir in Chandigarh, India.

## Christchurch

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

Your ISKCON is alive and well, growing stronger with each new day. Taking on new challenges, broadening its horizons, increasing in friends and admirers, and solidifying into a hearty foundation on which to continue the construction work you began all those years ago. Your transcendental movement shines like a bright spring day.

We are honored to have you living in our humble temple, overseeing our *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* classes, looking on as we chant and dance in ecstasy. And you always seem very pleased to see your devotees honoring yet another wonderful, transcendental feast.

You write in your purport to *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 1.5.36:

Therefore, the attempt must go on to convert unwilling men to become His devotees. Lord Caitanya has taught a very simple method in this connection. He has taught the lesson for preaching the transcendental message through singing, dancing and refreshment. As such, fifty percent of our income may be spent for this purpose. In this fallen age of quarrel and dissension, if only the leading and wealthy persons of society agree to spend fifty percent of their income in the service of the Lord, as it is taught by Lord Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, there is absolute certainty of converting this hell of pandemonium to the transcendental abode of the Lord. No one will disagree to partake in a function where good singing, dancing and refreshment are administered. Everyone will attend such a function, and everyone is sure to feel individually the transcendental presence of the Lord. This alone will help the attendant associate with the Lord and thereby purify himself in spiritual realization.

Let us remain forever simple-hearted souls who are always happy to be with you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. We feel sheltered and safe within your ISKCON, and we count our blessings to be with the wonderful Vaiṣṇavas you have made as we chant, dance and feast our way back to the spiritual world.

Your servants at the Śrī Śrī Nitāi-Gauracandra Mandir, Christchurch, New Zealand.

## Columbus

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Hare Kṛṣṇa. All glories to you on this, your Vyāsa-pūjā day!

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

*mūkaṁ karoti vācālaṁ paṅguṁ laṅghayate girim*  
*yat-kṛpā tam ahaṁ vande śrī-guruṁ dīna-tāraṇam*

Once again it is that time for your Vyāsa-pūjā, and we contemplate on what to write in glorification of you—your personal qualities, preaching activities, devotion to Kṛṣṇa, and surrender to Him. What can the conditioned soul—even if she is aspiring to become a devotee (the soul being female in relation to Kṛṣṇa)—say to the pure devotee who resides eternally at Kṛṣṇa’s lotus feet and who in his infinite mercy has descended to this material realm to give pure devotional service to the fallen souls? What does one say to that glorious personality who has come to save him from the endless world of *saṁsāra,* to which one is bound by the three *guṇas*? The only way one can be extricated is thorough the mercy of Kṛṣṇa’s pure devotee. It is you who have come in this dark Age of Kali to give your mercy out freely. What can we possibly say to glorify you and thank you for giving us the lifeline of Kṛṣṇa consciousness?

You are so near and dear to Kṛṣṇa—we are so far away; what can we possible know of your glories? I feel like a fool even trying to make the attempt. I think only Kṛṣṇa and possibly His pure devotee are truly qualified to make the attempt to describe your glories. Only Kṛṣṇa truly knows the sacrifice you have made for us, for it is He who knows the spiritual realm that you left to come to this material world to save us. How can we adequately and appropriately describe your qualities—your magnanimity, compassion, and love for the conditioned souls? Your pastimes are endless and unfathomable, what to speak of your unconditional love for Kṛṣṇa and your unceasing desire to carry out Kṛṣṇa’s desires and those of His pure devotees in the Gauḍīya Vaiṣṇava disciplic succession. Can we, as limited conditioned souls aspiring to develop love for Kṛṣṇa, *truly* glorify you, Kṛṣṇa’s ambassador to this earth planet, you who are *jagat guru?*

You are not *just* a pure devotee but rather a *śaktyāveśa-avatāra* who has come with the mercy and compassion of Lord Nityānanda. Even to those of us who possessed the most wicked of hearts, some of us even being contaminated by envy of you, you still gave the opportunity to become Kṛṣṇa conscious. You would not deny your mercy to anyone. “Here it is; now take it.” Then it was up to us to take the mercy. Even somehow if we were so foolish as to reject you at some point, you would cry in compassion at our foolishness. You would attempt to bring your foolish disciples back if they left or wanted to leave. You are our true father, both spiritually and materially. How often you expressed concern with regard to devotees’ health, both spiritual and material. You were the doctor, not only giving spiritual prescriptions but ones for the material body as well. Even for those of us who never met you, because of your spiritual all-pervasiveness you were the closest person to our hearts. You are Father, Mother, greatest friend, and Kṛṣṇa’s personal representative, minister and ambassador. For some of us, it is only because of our attachment to the lotus feet of you, Kṛṣṇa’s pure devotee who truly loved and still loves us, that we are able to continue in our attempts to surrender and serve your beloved Kṛṣṇa. It is you only who convinced us that Kṛṣṇa is the Supreme Personality of Godhead.

You have descended from Goloka Vṛndāvana specifically to distribute Lord Caitanya’s mercy in this Kali-yuga. You traveled from Vṛndāvana and came to the most degraded New York City, where you turned hippies into “happies.” You got us to give up our drugs and drink to take *prasādam*. We went from reading Karl Marx, Plato, Shakespeare, and Carl Jung to reading *Bhagavad-gītā, Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*, and *Caitanya-caritāmṛta.* We turned our eyes away from psychedelic and pornographic art to look at windows to the spiritual world. Movies and TV were replaced by transcendental plays and movies of Kṛṣṇa and His pure devotees. Our speech went from the realm of the material ferris wheel affairs of gossip, gutter *kathā,* mundane talks, newspaper headlines, business talks, and dry philosophical speculative talks to speaking about the names, forms, pastimes, and glories of Kṛṣṇa and His pure devotees. You taught us to decorate the body of Kṛṣṇa, explaining that this would give us greater satisfaction than our trying to decorate our own bodies for sense gratification, and you gave us a house in which the whole world could take shelter. You gave us the *mahā-mantra* and its extensions. You taught us that, and how to please Kṛṣṇa.

What can an aspiring disciple say to you on your Vyāsa-pūjā day? Even if one were to use all the words that comprise the realm of language, it still would not be enough. Maybe it is really only Kṛṣṇa who can *truly* speak of your glories.

For a genuine disciple, every day will be acknowledged as your Vyāsa-pūjā day, for in one sense—spiritually speaking—every day the vigilant disciple sees a new birth in his relationship with you. For the true disciple your presence becomes increasingly manifest in his heart and his love for your increases daily. The relationship expands beyond the limits of time and space; its transcendental depth increases unlimitedly. S /he becomes increasingly aware of your mercy and becomes increasingly indebted to you. You are his /her most glorious and worthy object of worship. It is you who has given to her /him the most precious commodity, Kṛṣṇa, love of Kṛṣṇa, and the process of devotional service. Thus every day is seen as your Vyāsa-pūjā day.

How can one possibly repay the debt s/he owes to you?

*brahmāṇḍa bhramite kona bhāgyavān jīva*  
*guru-kṛṣṇa-prasāde pāya bhakti-latā-bīja (Madhya 19.151)*

Only you have the ability to deliver Kṛṣṇa to the fallen souls and bring them to Kṛṣṇa’s lotus feet. You say to Kṛṣṇa, “Here is this soul,” and Kṛṣṇa agrees to take. Who can repay such a debt? I myself have no understanding of who you are or the mercy you have brought. We are still simply struggling with the basics of human and spiritual life. We are a product of your mercy, and we continue to await for your on-going shower of mercy. I am simply a fool trying to offer some words of glorification to you on this, your Vyāsa-pūjā day. I am like a small child trying to hold a small wooden match as an offering to the sun. You who are giving what no other spiritual master has given—who can say enough to glorify that personality who came to the East Village and Haight Ashbury district to give Kṛṣṇa consciousness to drugged-out freaks, some of whom genuinely wanted to take up your message and receive your mercy. You gave your gifts freely, with no expectation of remuneration.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we have no understanding of you or who you are, nor can I pretend to fathom the causeless mercy you are so freely giving. We can only pray to Kṛṣṇa that somehow we may become your faithful servants, and that someday we may become an asset to your mission. Other than that, there is no other possibility of our offering any form of glorification to such a great personality as you.

We beg that on this day of your Vyāsa-pūjā you will accept this small offering unto your lotus feet.

We remain  
Your servants at the Columbus, Ohio, and Louisville, Kentucky, Hare Kṛṣṇa Centers.

## Coventry

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at the dust of your divine lotus feet. Years are passing by, leaving us feeling separated from Your Divine Grace more and more. When we read your books, or hear your lecture tapes, or view your videos, or interact with the *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is* interactive CD, we feel your presence immediately. Pure devotees of Śrī Kṛṣṇa are our true shelter and inspiration. We feel that even though you are being missed, you will never be forgotten. In fact, your glories are being sung all over the three worlds.

The new millennium is coming, and the whole world is ready to spend so much on foolish monuments and silly projects. Materialists are planning to have great parties, spending millions to greet the year 2,000. What are they hoping to see? Birth, death, old age, and disease—so what is new? The foolish are thinking that the new era will bring them greater fortunes. They want to make bigger beds and buildings for more intense sense gratification and move further away from Śrī Kṛṣṇa. We know that you wanted the whole world to move closer to Śrī Kṛṣṇa and surrender to Him, and that is why Your Divine Grace translated the wonderful Vedic scriptures and set up centers all over the world. As far as the Vaiṣṇavas are concerned, our millennium started when you set up ISKCON. All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda!

We, the devotees at the Coventry *yātrā,* are so happy to be serving in this center of yours. We feel that by using the formula you have so mercifully given us, we are actually feeling attached to our service to Śrī Śrī Śacīnandana and Nitāichand. Already we need to extend our building to accommodate more congregational members. Śrīla Prabhupāda, please giving us your mercy, without which we will never succeed.

Slowly and steadily your worldwide spiritual family is growing. From time to time we experience some ups and downs, but nothing is easy in Kali-yuga. The good thing is that devotees continue to chant, dance, and distribute your books. These functions alone will keep us on your ship heading back to Goloke Vṛndāvana.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for all your hard work and abundant mercy, which are pushing us toward the desired goal, love of Godhead. Lord Caitanya is very much in our minds. The pastimes of Lord Nityānanda continue to bring joy to our hearts. All the Gauḍīya Vaiṣṇava *ācāryas* keep us inspired. All this has happened by your hard work, and no one can deny that. All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Your humble disciples and grand-disciples in Coventry, England.  
(written by Haridāsa dāsa)

## Curitiba

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances. All glories to Śrī Śrī Guru and Gaurāṅga!!

You, Śrīla Prabhupāda, are and always will be our eternal guide in spiritual life. The revolution you caused in the entire world by the simple fact of surrendering yourself completely to the orders of Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura, cannot be measured.

You inculcated truthfulness into the hearts of liars, mercy and compassion into those who caused suffering and were supporting it. You brought bold and clear spiritual guidelines to people who were completely apart from God. And, above all that, you inspired the search for truth and love into dry and skeptical hearts.

For that, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we are eternally grateful to you. We hope we may always be counted among those who are trying to serve you in some way. Please do not let us deviate from the shelter of your lotus feet and correct us whenever necessary.

Your eternal servants in the Curitiba, Brazil.  
(written by Raṇacora dāsa and Gaura-Naṭarāja dāsa)

## Czech Republic

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Please accept my most humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

Since the beginning of Kali-yuga, many words have been spoken and many books written. But no words are worth hearing and no books worth reading or distributing except those about the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Śrī Kṛṣṇa. Though the message of Godhead had been lost while being passed from speaker to speaker, we finally found the very same message of the *guru-paramparā* in your books. These books are absolute. Not one word in them needs any interpretation; they are not relative in any way. There is no problem not discussed in them and by you as you traveled the globe. Consequently, there is no solution that is not given in these books to all problems. There are no new problems and themes to discuss. With great care and devotion you wrote these guidelines for the conditioned souls appearing in the next ten thousand years, so they can find out the way out of the mental labyrinth created by this dark Age of Kali.

As the hypocrites are intoxicating themselves by their speeches, trying to rationalize the unrationalizable, your words shine even more in this mental darkness. These words, once printed and distributed into peoples homes, are printed incarnations of Lord Caitanya’s mercy. One page from your books can solve more problems then millions of volumes of mundane literature concerned with human minds and false egos. Taking shelter in your books, we realize that the most difficult becomes most easy. Turning away from these books, the most easy becomes most difficult. Among the materialists, there is no fame and glory for those who have given and are giving their lives to spread your words and glories. But their names are carefully recorded by the *guru-paramparā,* and their path back home, back to Godhead, is clear. As one generation of conditioned souls passes on, another appears, and the process of *saṅkīrtana* goes on eternally. Many come and many go, but those who live in your heart, in your books, are protected from the stormy days of Kali-yuga.

All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, sitting at your oil lamp in Rādhā-Dāmodara temple, writing the first volumes of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*! All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, embarking with trunks of these books on the *Jaladuta*! All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, who tolerated so many obstacles so we can read your books and chant the holy name.

I saw you in London holding a newly printed book, crying in ecstasy. May I never forget you in this form. All Glories to Your Divine Grace!

Your servants in the Czech Republic.  
(written by Maṇidhara dāsa)

## Dallas

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

Here in Dallas we are always longing for the shelter of the dust of your most exalted lotus feet. By this dust we can surely become purified and enter into the eternal pastimes of the spiritual realm. All great souls like Prahlāda Mahārāja and Dhruva Mahārāja know well that without taking the dust from the feet of a pure devotee like yourself one cannot be successful in one’s spiritual endeavors.

We have taken that dust and shared your tireless and heroic pastimes of preaching Kṛṣṇa consciousness over the entire known world. Everywhere, without fear because of your perfect and pure Kṛṣṇa consciousness, you instilled enthusiasm for Kṛṣṇa consciousness into everyone you came in contact with. You spoke to us of the Absolute Truth, set us free from the vices of this material world, and led us to desire an eternal relationship of love with the Supreme Personality Godhead, Kṛṣṇa, and His associates.

We pray, Śrīla Prabhupāda, that your words will be the only guide for our minds and souls. We are fully confident in what you have said. Your messages will carry us out of the dark ocean of material existence because they are as powerful as Kṛṣṇa Himself. It is a fact that your message is nondifferent from Kṛṣṇa Himself.

Kṛṣṇa is ever inviting the fallen conditioned souls to join Him in His eternal loving pastimes, and you have come to extend this invitation from the Personality of Godhead. It is out your fathomless compassion and extreme kindness that you came into this world to invite us fallen souls to revel in the pastimes of love of God. How wonderful it is to note that you are the representative of the Supreme Lord and His associates! How wonderful it is!

We will always worship you in awe and reverence and pray that you will engage us in your transcendental loving service. All our endeavors will be geared to please you. We understand that by pleasing you we certainly will have our place in the spiritual abode of our Supreme Lord, Kṛṣṇa. Śrīla Prabhupāda, we are eternally in your debt. Let us not forget this. Thank you very much.

Your servants in Dallas, Texas.

## Denpasar

All glories to Śrī Śrī Guru and Gaurāṅga! All glories to Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Śrīla Prabhupāda, please accept my most humble *daṇḍavat* obeisances under your holy and merciful lotus feet.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I am so fallen, foolish, and sinful, so how I can compile a nice prayer to glorify your sublime activities and greatness? This is impossible! However, it is my duty to offer you glorification and prayers on your Vyāsa-pūjā day, so I will make the attempt.

You are such a noble and great *ācārya,* O Śrīla Prabhupāda! By your nectarean discourses on the Vedic scriptures you have wiped out the darkness and forgetfulness of God experienced by almost 99.99% of the world’s population. You have done what anyone would have thought impossible. By your causeless mercy, the dog and hog civilization, which has flooded the entire world, will gradually diminish.

You successfully took Lord Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s teachings from India to the other side of the world, the Western world of English-speaking countries. You are well known by everyone all over the world as a very bold preacher who spoke only on the Absolute Truth, Śrī Kṛṣṇa. You brought the sacred Hare Kṛṣṇa *mahā-mantra* cross the Atlantic Ocean. Only by your causeless mercy has every corner of the world been flooded by the holy name of God. You are famous as the greatest scholar of the century, and this fame will continue throughout the many centuries to come.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, please be merciful to me. May you always find us active in devotional service and submissive to your disciples’ spiritual instructions! I, one of your spiritual grandsons, have been chanting the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mahā-mantra* for some time now, and, by the mercy of you and your disciples, I have experienced the happiness from that chanting, i.e., the sweetness of the *saṅkīrtana* movement. Without following this *yuga-dharma* (the performance of the *saṅkīrtana mahā-yajña*), there is no hope that I will be able to know about guru and Śrī Kṛṣṇa, what to speak of rendering devotional service to guru and Śrī Kṛṣṇa. All of these possibilities of mine have resulted from your compassion and affection, which come down through the enlightened *paramparā* teachings.

Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura, your spiritual master, must be very satisfied and proud to have you as his disciple, one who is able to impart Kṛṣṇa consciousness even to the lowest of men in society, like me. You have said that even a child can take part in this chanting, or even a dog can take part in it. Now your word has been proven true, for the most fallen souls, like me, have attained a chance to participate in your spiritual movement.

O Śrīla Prabhupāda, I humbly request you to please be merciful, to give bless me with the strength to make continual effort to gain some of the perfect Vaiṣṇava qualities. Without these qualities, what is the meaning of my life? It is just trivial and farcical. Please give me your mercy in this very tangible and practical way, so that I can actually become a humble devotee. Only then can I convince others to do the same, because these qualities are basic requirements by which one can advance in devotional service.

*Jaya nitya-līlā-praviṣṭa oṁ viṣṇu-pāda paramahaṁsa parivrajakācārya aṣṭottara-śata Śrī Śrīmad Abhaya Caraṇāravinda Bhaktivedanta Svāmī Mahārāja Prabhupāda ki jaya*!!!

Your servants in Denpasar, Bali, Indonesia.  
(written by Sadāśiva dāsa)

## Devasadan Mandir

Our most beloved Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace, who gave us the most magnificent temple in North America, The Devasadan Mandir and The Bhaktivedanta Cultural Center in Detroit. It is only with your mercy that we are able to continue the preaching process, which you established.

O dearest disciple of Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura, we would like to thank you again and again for giving us inspiration and power for increasing book distribution. When you were on this planet, two of your most dedicated disciples, Śrīmān Ambarīṣa Prabhu and Śrīmatī Lekhaśravantī Prabhu, joined hands to buy the Fisher Mansion and pleased you by offering it to you. Now history is repeating itself: to establish a strong book distribution program, Śrīmān Ambarīṣa Prabhu has given the means, and Śrīmatī Lekhaśravantī Prabhu, along with Śrīmān Bhūṣāya Prabhu, have given their very intelligent and energetic devotee son, Prahlāda. With him we have a team of very young devotees going out every single day to airports, malls, and concerts to distribute your books, and they are feeling very happy in Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

O savior of the whole universe, please bless this wonderful effort of this team of book distributors, Puruṣa-sūkta dāsa, Prahlāda dāsa, Mārkaṇḍeya dāsa, Taruṇa-Kṛṣṇa dāsa, Gāndharvikā-devī dāsī, and Śyāma-kuṇḍa-devī dāsī, as well as all the other devotees in your service. Without your blessings we all are powerless and bewildered. Many times, under the influence of the external energy, we tend to run away from your shelter. Please protect us from all attacks and disturbances, which can disrupt the mission of pushing forward this project of yours.

O divine son of Śrī Gaur Mohan De, we are very fortunate to have three of your most powerful leaders as our GBCs. His Grace Ravīndra Svarūpa Prabhu, with his scholarly intelligence and devotion, guides us to advance on the path o f devotional service. His Holiness Bhaktitīrtha Swami, with his unparalleled energy and inspiration, prepares us to face the twenty-first century. And the most wonderful Vaiṣṇavī Mālatī Prabhu tenders to us with love and affection by frequently visiting and nourishing us with the memories of her association with you.

O Devasadan Nivāsī, please forgive us for all the offenses we may have committed, knowingly or unknowingly. Finally, dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, if you so desire, please send us more book distributors.

Your unworthy servants at Devasadan Mandir in Detroit Michigan, USA.  
(written by Jagadguru dāsa)

## Dublin

**November ’77**

Vṛndāvana evening gloom—  
It was chanting as usual  
In Prabhupāda’s holy room.

O Prabhupāda, O Prabhupāda!  
Prabhupāda, Prabhupāda!  
Jagat Guru, Jagat Guru!  
We’ll follow you,  
We will follow you.

As we try to continue on,  
We’re inspired by your words  
“Your love for me will be shown  
by your co-operation.”

O Prabhupāda, O Prabhupāda!  
Prabhupāda, Prabhupāda!  
Jagat Guru, Jagat Guru!  
We’ll follow you,  
We will follow you.

You can bend a single bough,  
But you cannot break the tree,  
Once the seed has taken root,  
It will forever be.

O Prabhupāda, O Prabhupāda!  
Prabhupāda, Prabhupāda!  
Jagat Guru, Jagat Guru!  
We’ll follow you,  
We will follow you.

And the nations of the world  
Will worship you, worship you.

Your servants in Dublin, Ireland.  
(written by Praghoṣa dāsa)

## Fortaleza

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

As the years have passed, we have come to notice that spiritual life is not an option but the true position of the living entity. And you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, so kindly came to show this to us.

“O devotee of the Lord, the purpose of the visual sense is fulfilled simply by seeing you, and to touch your body is the fulfillment of bodily touch. The tongue is meant for glorifying your qualities because in this world a pure devotee of the Lord is very difficult to find.” (*Hari-bhakti-sudhodaya* 13.2)

We were given senses use in the transcendental service of Kṛṣṇa, but we were captured by the glitter of material existence and wanted to enjoy the senses.

The material world is created so that the conditioned souls can go back home, back to the Supreme, and His servants, such as Brahmājī, Nāradajī, Vyāsajī, and Your Divine Grace, all perform same work for the Lord, that is, trying to recover the conditioned souls from the field of the enjoyment of the senses and helping them return to their normal state.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda.  
Your servants at the ISKCON temple in Fortaleza, Brazil.

## Freeport

My most dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances prostrated at your feet. All glories to you, O Paramahaṁsa Śrīla Prabhupāda! All glories to our worshipable Deities, Their Lordships Śrī Śrī Nimāi-Nitāi.

It is your Vyāsa-pūjā day, and it is my goal to glorify you. You have so many qualities, and one of them that really captures my attention is your power to motivate your followers. Here at the Śrī Śrī Nimāi-Nitāi Mandir we have seen the power your instructions can have on your followers. As you know, O divine master, our temple was about to close in 1996, the year of your hundredth appearance anniversary. The temple had no funds, the guests had stopped coming, and bill collectors were pressing. We had only one devotee in the temple and two or three guests at the Sunday Feast. The few devotees participating in the temple functions decided to pray to Śrī Śrī Nimāi-Nitāi and Your Divine Grace for inspiration. We started reading your letters about not closing a temple, and your instructions struck our minds hard and, like magic, renewed our determination. We read in one of your letters:

“Once a temple has been opened it must never be closed.” (Letter to Murāri dāsa, December 1970)

We took this instruction very seriously. We brought as many congregational members together as we could and, carrying the banner “Once a temple has been opened it must never be closed,” we gradually worked our way out of that unfortunate situation.

The last two years have been glorious for us, and the temple is doing well. We paid all the bills—thanks to Kṛṣṇa and his sincere devotees, who are dedicating part of their hard-earned money to fulfill the mission of the *saṅkīrtana* movement. Four new devotees joined, and three were initiated. New congregational members came, and we had wonderful festivals, *harināmas,* and book distribution. We have also worshiped Lord Śrī Śrī Nimāi-Nitāi and Your Divine Grace with new outfits in every major festival.

The devotees of the Śrī Śrī Nimāi-Nitāi Mandir would like to thank you for inspiring us and allowing us to keep this temple open, even in the face of great difficulties.

Please bless us so we can build a wonderful temple for Śrī Śrī Nimāi-Nitāi. We know that by your mercy and inspiration this is completely possible. Please keep inspiring us so that this temple will never be closed and will expand more and more for your satisfaction.

Your aspiring servants,  
The devotees of the Śrī Śrī Nimāi-Nitāi Mandir, Freeport, New York.  
(written by Līlānanda dāsa)

## Gainesville

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace on this auspicious day of your appearance!

Śrīla Prabhupāda, it is by your divine mercy that ISKCON has a center here in Gainesville, Florida, and because this center exists many frustrated souls are finding solace in devotional service. It is our sincere desire to continue to maintain and expand this program because it pleases you. On July 29, 1971, you visited the newly formed Gainesville center on Depot Avenue and remarked, “It is so nice to see so many young boys and girls here chanting Hare Kṛṣṇa in this remote corner of the world,so far away from the birthplace of Lord Caitanya.” You were pleased to see the mercy of Kṛṣṇa being distributed, and we also feel your pleasure as we try to do this as well—pass on Kṛṣṇa Consciousness to others here in Gainesville. To have the guidance of your direct instructions is a special and rare benefit. There is no question of what is to be done—you have given the order: “So do something wonderful there in Gainesville. Wonderful means simply you chant loudly and distribute prasadam. That is not very difficult. It is very easy. Simply if you do it enthusiastically and sincerely, then success will be there.” (Letter, June 1971)

We are trying to follow this simple program. For the last twenty-eight years the Gainesville center has distributed *prasādam* at the University of Florida, and as long as we are able to continue this program and perform this service, our connection with you is secure. We are confident that by our carrying out your order great things will happen in Gainesville.

As our benevolent grandfather, please help us hear your guiding words and follow your instructions so that we may grow up and make you proud of us.

Begging to remain at your lotus feet, we remain,  
Your menial servants at the preaching center in remote Gainesville, Florida, USA.  
(written by Savyasācī dāsa)

## Gītā-nāgarī

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our respectful obeisances at the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace on your Vyāsa-pūjā day.

On this most auspicious day, we (your spiritual children and grandchildren), having taken shelter of Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Dāmodara, are enthusiastic and determined to fulfill the second part of your mission: to establish *varṇāśrama-dharma* and self-sufficiency at this Gītā-nāgarī farm for your pleasure. We know that Gītā-nāgarī remains one of the spiritual farm communities that is dear to your heart. By your causeless mercy, we consider ourselves fortunate that we have been placed in such an important and beautiful environment, where we can cultivate the land and protect the cows for the pleasure of Their Lordships.

Our community is steadily growing. Several devotees have been inspired to join us. The task before us now is to become trained to undertake appropriate duties according to our propensities, since we would like to be situated properly according to *varṇa* and *āśrama*.

We are aware that the situation is getting worse in the cities, with industrialization and false propaganda steadily increasing people’s miseries all over the planet. Therefore, we are working hard to make the Gītā-nāgarī farm community a living example of “simple living and high thinking.”

This year, we hope to move Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Dāmodara to Their new temple. We are distributing your books and *prasādam* daily. We are now growing wheat, oats, and beans, as well as a variety of vegetables, fruits, and flowers, for Their Lordships, the devotees, and our upcoming Food for Life program. We are installing a windmill to provide water for our cows. We hope to have a health clinic completed by the end of this year to provide proper health care for the devotees, as well as the secular community. Our children and adult devotees are flourishing because we are committed to developing loving relationships among one another.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, only by your mercy can we hope to achieve any of these goals. Please help us to be fully surrendered to becoming Kṛṣṇa conscious. May this day help to remind us of how special you are and how blessed we are to know you through your *mūrti,* books, tapes, personal training, and instructions.

Although all of us are full of faults and so conditioned by material nature, we humbly ask you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for your mercy and shelter.

Your servants at the Gītā-nāgarī farm community in Port Royal, Pennsylvania, USA.

## Goloka Dhāma

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

As we look from the distance of time at your divine qualities, we can see that your personality resembles a big mountain: according to the perspective one takes, an endless number of secret sceneries come into view. Today we would like to glorify you by concentrating on some of the principles you lived by, as far as we are able to recognize and understand them.

**Śrīla Prabhupāda’s Principles**

Your most apparent principle is your absolute connection with the personality and orders of your spiritual master. You said that Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura was always in your heart, directing you from within. That automatically meant that you gave the credit for your success to him only, along the principle of “He is the gold, I am the glimmer”

Another of your principles is that you sacrificed your life entirely for the mission of the Lord. You were fully Kṛṣṇa’s servant and would not become the servant of anyone or anything else—certainly not of public opinion, customs, or the usual Kali-yuga illusions. In this way you are an outstanding example of the perfect instrument in the hands of Śrī Kṛṣṇa and Lord Caitanya. Often it is heard that a higher state of being means a higher form of connectedness. You are the perfect example of this eternal principle of *bhakti-yoga*.

*Purity is the force:* You often pointed out how so many preachers came before you to talk on the *Bhagavad-gītā,* but because they did not present the *Gītā’s* teachings purely not a single person could become a devotee of the Lord by hearing from them. You, however, kept everything pure, and in this way the pure force of the Lord could act.

*Yukta-vairāgya:* You were the best example of using everything you could in Kṛṣṇa’s service and not rejecting anything, thinking it to be material. In your transcendental hands, dirty hippies became dancing angels, dictating machines were laced with a golden effulgence due to recording the words from your lotus mouth, and houses and estates, previously whirlpools of sense gratification, turned into *āśramas* and temples where glorification of the Lord could go on.

*Live by dharmic principles:* This principle shines very much forth. You gave truthfulness, integrity, peacefulness, self-control, austerity, purity, tolerance, honesty, knowledge, wisdom, religiousness, and adherence to guru, *sādhu,* and *śāstra* a new effulgent form. Even if the rest of the so-called thinking world was deeply steeped in illusion and did not endorse some of your perspectives, you stood by your dharmic principles—and dharma came to your aid.

*Keeping spiritual strength by following śravaṇam, kīrtanam, and smaraṇam:* In *The Nectar of Devotion* we learn that the whole essence of following the rules and regulations is to always remember Kṛṣṇa and never forget Him. These were your teachings from the very beginning of your preaching:

One should always act in such a way that all his daily activities are in connection with Kṛṣṇa. He should arrange his life in such a way that throughout the twenty-four hours he cannot but think of Kṛṣṇa. And the Lord’s promise is that anyone who is in such pure Kṛṣṇa consciousness will certainly return to the abode of Kṛṣṇa, where he will be engaged in the association of Kṛṣṇa face to face. This most confidential part of knowledge is spoken to Arjuna because he is the dear friend of Kṛṣṇa. Everyone who follows the path of Arjuna can become a dear friend to Kṛṣṇa and obtain the same perfection as Arjuna. \[*Bhagavad-gītā* 18.65, purport\]

*Always praying to Kṛṣṇa that one will not fall down:* This principle is directly derived from one of your main principles, that is, total dependence on Kṛṣṇa. In Hamburg you once told Śivānanda Prabhu (ɱ1969) that he should pray to Kṛṣṇa that He would protect you.

*Some principles in relation to people:*

“Example is better than precept.”

You said that the devotees are our most important assets and must be nurtured first. For this principle you placed traditional, institutional, and other concerns often in the second or third place.

You said we should treat devotees according to what they can become—keeping in mind what they are at present and need at this stage of their lives.

You said that one should concentrate on what is within one’s reach while simultaneously pursuing a vision, which is given by guru and Kṛṣṇa.

“Krishna Consciousness Movement is for training men to be independently thoughtful.”

As we write this offering, it is obvious that to look at you only to try to find out the principles you lived by is a limited vision of you. It is not possible to understand your greatness in such a way, just as one can never understand the mountains through the vision of an ant, no matter what and how many perspectives one takes. It takes a pure devotee to understand another pure devotee—at least partially. For instance, your love for Kṛṣṇa, the depths of your compassion for the fallen souls, and, more than anything, your amazing degree of surrender to guru and Kṛṣṇa, totally escape analysis through the method chosen in this offering. Our exercise was therefore humbling for us: happily we again saw our natural position before you. We are ants in front of you. Please nevertheless shower your mercy on us. Just as little baby birds are totally dependent on the mother bird, we feel totally dependent on you.

Please forgive our amateurish endeavors to glorify you.

*Jaya* Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Your servants at Goloka Dhāma, in Abentheuer, Germany.

## Hong Kong

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace! All glories to your ISKCON society!

Year after year the devotees in your society go through one anxiety after another. This is no ordinary anxiety, for it us undergone in your service and service to Lord Caitanya. Unfortunately, a lot of energy is spent not in pushing Kali back but in generating and trying to clear obstacles, thus dissipating useful resources with very little gain. Somehow, some of us are very headstrong and fixed in our ways, so we refuse to budge from our positions. We find all means to justify how we are right and disregard the superior system of understanding given by our *ācāryas*—the checks-and-balance system of guru, *sādhu,* and *śāstra*. Our excuse is that certain things have not been done properly or that certain mistakes have been made and that our views are therefore correct; in this way we refuse to see how you wanted all your followers to cooperatively discuss issues among themselves, come to an understanding, and run your society. Although we all have our views, one thing many of us overlook is that your society is still relatively new. This is not an excuse but a fact. We are sure you would prefer that your followers assist one another toward spiritual growth individually and collectively for the benefit of your society, rather than placing obstacles in one another’s way.

As a society that is still new, we are going through growing pains, learning pains, and reforming pains, and in general we are still in the process of maturing toward being able to work cooperatively and keep our common goals in focus. Our situation is no different from that of other growing companies, organizations, or new families. During the growth and development period there are “teething” problems and many lessons to be learned: mistakes are made and losses incurred, and then new problems often appear. In the process some morale may be lost, and new energy is gained, and so on. From all these incidents, experience is gained, and adjustments are made for long-term survival of the establishment. Mistakes, corrections, adjustments are all part of the natural process of growth and development for any new setup. This process is also good for individual and collective spiritual development, for it is humbling. At the same time, it makes us appreciate your exalted position even more.

Please do not let us dwell on all the negatives, since these are part of the process of growing and maturing. Some of us keep going back and seeing devotees as they were twenty years ago. This is *nagna-mātṛkā-nyāya,* the argument that since one’s mother was born naked, she should always remain naked. We should be prepared to accept that those who have been following the spiritual process properly have gradually made spiritual advancement and that they are not the same as they were many years ago. Similarly, devotees have made decisions that were not the wisest at certain times, but they have also corrected bad decisions and are maturing. It’s faulty understanding to think we should continue to see all situations in the same way as we did twenty years ago.

There have been many wonderful developments as well: new temples have come up, many new centers have been established, millions of books have been distributed, important people have taken note of your society, and millions of people have taken to the chanting of the Hare Kṛṣṇa mantra all over the world. Still, many people are still not fully familiar with the extraordinary culture we are trying to offer them. With all the effort, we are no more then specks in this vast material ocean, although gradually some very important events are being widely celebrated by ISKCON temples and devotees all over the world. The transcendental festivals of Janmāṣṭamī, Rāma Navamī, Gaura Pūrṇimā, and Ratha-yātrā are now celebrated with hundreds of thousands of people attending and millions of plates of *prasādam* distributed. There is a lot of karmic purification of the society being achieved through the efforts of the devotees; in fact, we are sure that without your society there would surely have been major catastrophes like world wars, economic collapse, devastating earthquakes, etc. We all realize the possibilities of these dangers to the living entities, and instead of working against your establishment, trying to dismantle it, let us work to put positive energy into preaching—into maintaining and expanding your society for your pleasure and for the good of all entities.

On this sacred day of your Vyāsa-pūjā, we pray that devotees can give up their headstrong nature and humble themselves to cooperatively serve your most urgent preaching mission, thus reducing your suffering, the Vaiṣṇavas’ suffering, and the suffering of all living entities. Although last year was a very difficult year for the society in general, we pray for and look forward to a brighter future, with devotees coming together to serve you for your pleasure. We look forward to appreciating your mercy in all our trials and tribulations, as well as our festivals. O merciful master, O great soul, please be kind to all the passionate and ignorant souls bereft of devotion to Kṛṣṇa, which you want them to have. Please bless them with Kṛṣṇa consciousness so they may forget their foolish material obsessions and look forward to many wonderful developments that are yet to unfold through this ISKCON movement of yours.

We remain committed to your service.  
Your insignificant servants in Hong Kong.

## Ibadan

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

We beg to offer our humble obeisances at your divine lotus feet. All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, ISKCON’s founder-*ācārya*!

We are most grateful to Your Divine Grace for enabling us to participate in the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement, study your teachings, and help pioneer a new civilization. Indeed, we are unable to properly express our indebtedness to you for making this ancient wisdom available to us in this dense forest of ignorance known as Kali-yuga.

We are praying that you please give us the willpower to be able to endeavor to prosecute Kṛṣṇa consciousness till our last breath, without any interruption. All glories to your Vyāsa-pūjā!

*Your children at the ISKCON temple in Ibadan, Nigeria.*  
(written by Kamalākṣa dāsa)

## Israel

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances at your divine lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

You are my absolute standard in life. In the *Mahābhārata* it is said that no logic or argument can ascertain the Truth but that the Truth is hidden in the heart of the *mahājanas*. You are my *mahājana*. Therefore, although I encounter and study so many religions, so many philosophies, and so many scientific theories, I accept them all as subordinate to the conclusions found in your books, and in this way my vision stays clear. Your absolute resolution to serve the mission of your guru is the supreme example to be followed for me. Your unlimited compassion toward the fallen souls is my guide for how to penetrate the hearts of the conditioned souls and guide them to the right path.

My service to you is currently very meager, but I am trying my best to prepare myself to please you and soon increase very much my service to you.

I am very pained that there is no unity among your family. Is it a natural and unavoidable pluralism in your grand family? Is it a natural outcome of different stages of advancement? But our strength is in unity. Please pray for us to be able to unite and turn this trend of Kali-yuga back, back to Godhead.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, we don’t want to become just another religion. We want to transcend the modes and come to the stage of pure devotional service. By fixing you, your example, your vision, your teachings, and your purity in our hearts, let us make our life worthy and by your mercy accomplish the mission you entrusted to us.

Your children and grandchildren in Israel.  
(written by Guṇa Avatāra dāsa)

## Johannesburg

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our obeisances. All glories to Śrī guru and Gaurāṅga!

On this 103rd anniversary of your appearance amongst us, we are simply amazed at the extent of your mercy to the fallen souls in various corners of this planet at the closure of this millennium.

Here in the “rainbow nation” of South Africa, we have directly witnessed the power of your mercy. In April 1997 the country was stunned as they watched on national television a historic Food for Life event, in which over 60,000 blissful children took *prasādam* in what the press dubbed “The World’s Largest Children’s Picnic!” The T.V. newscaster watched in disbelief as the leaders of all the rival political parties came together for the event, including President Nelson Mandela, who called it his “happiest day.” He said that what ISKCON achieved by bringing together such bitter political opponents was in stark contrast to the troubled political past of the country, and he praised the event as a positive sign for real unity and peace in the future.

Gradually the Golden Age is being ushered in by your mercy. In Soweto, the largest black township in the world and the center of mindless racial violence and slaughter during the apartheid era, the holy names of Lord Gaurāṅga are being sung daily and distributed there by your devotees.

Every week in the major cities of this country—Johannesburg, Pretoria, Durban, and Cape Town—programs are held in the main universities and colleges, where the science of *bhakti-yoga* as taught by Your Divine Grace is revealed to the future leaders of this country. Many have taken up the process, following the regulative principles and chanting the *mahā-mantra,* and a few extremely fortunate souls have decided to dedicate themselves totally to serving your mission.

Thousands of your literatures are being distributed and read every month all over the country. We pray that you may bless us to be able to translate and print your books into the main local languages within the shortest possible time.

The Ratha-yātrā festival you ordered us to hold decades ago when you came to South Africa is now listed as a main tourist attraction in Durban, and the Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Rādhānātha temple, which you also ordered, is now one of the main landmarks on the Durban map.

The Food for Life program in this country is the largest in the world, with over one million plates distributed each year. And earlier this year, right next to our temple here in Auckland Park, the authorities of the South African Broadcasting Corporation practically pleaded with us to establish our Govinda’s restaurant at their headquarters rent-free because they had become hooked on *kṛṣṇa-prasadam*!

But now Kali is striking at the jugular, weakening devotees’ faith in the *guru-paramparā*! We pray to you on this day that both individually and collectively we will not be an embarrassment to the disciplic heritage you have so mercifully bestowed on us.

We also pray that we remember to take a cue from the Pracetās. Because of their friendly dealings with each other, Lord Viṣṇu was pleased to give them a benediction—anything they wanted. And what did they ask for? That wherever they might wander in the universe, and in whatever species of life they might reside, they would remain in the company of devotees absorbed in hearing and chanting the glories of the Lord.

Therefore, we pray to you to always keep in our hearts the value of *sādhu-saṅga,* so that *vaiṣṇava-aparādha* will be a thing of the past in our ISKCON society. Then we can properly take our place to guide mankind not only for the next millennium, but also for the next ten thousand years.

All your grandsons and granddaughters at the Auckland Park Hare Krishna Center, Johannesburg, South Africa.  
(written by Ātmārāma dāsa)

## Jos

Your Divine Grace Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humblest and insignificant obeisances at the dust of your divine lotus feet.

All glories to your all-auspicious and highly glorious appearance day, which has quenched the thirst of our spiritual longing as the summer rain mercifully saturates a dry desert river-bed. All glories to Śrī ISKCON, a merciful expansion of your divine self!

Your Divine Grace Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are not only the meaning but also the personification of love, compassion, mercy, humility, courage, determination—in fact, your divine qualities are innumerable.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are the ocean of mercy, the friend of the poor, and the lord and master of the devotees. O Śrīla Prabhupāda, be merciful unto us, give us the shade and shelter of your lotus feet. May your fame be spread all over the three worlds.

*Jaya* Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Your grandchildren at the ISKCON temple in Jos, Nigeria.  
(written by Utpala dāsa)

## Kwazulu Natal

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

In 1975, after many years of struggle, devotees in South Africa were able to obtain a tourist visa for Your Divine Grace’s visit to the southernmost tip of this dark continent.

When you arrived in apartheid-riven South Africa, the immigration and airport officials were amazed when they saw white-bodied Europeans bowing down, offering their obeisances to an Indian *sādhu*. Right at the beginning of your visit to South Africa, your very presence broke through the bodily concept of life, and without any fear or hesitation you demonstrated to everyone that we are not this material body, we are not black, we are not white; we are not Indian, we are not African, we are not European: we are eternal servants of Kṛṣṇa.

In 1975 we were a very motley crew, comprising one *sannyāsī,* six *brahmacārīs,* and one *brahmacāriṇī*. We had one rented house costing about $35 a month, and two vehicles. That was the entire ISKCON South African *yātrā.*

Twenty-four years later, ISKCON South Africa has somewhat expanded. We now have six centers and perhaps four hundred initiated devotees. We have just completed a four-day Ratha-yātrā festival on the Durban beachfront, which was extremely successful. We were able to distribute over 80,000 plates of *prasādam,* and for four continuous days we preached to tens of thousands of interested tourists and holiday-makers. Your BTG magazine, the backbone of ISKCON, has now reached a circulation of 4,200, being distributed every other month. Since your departure in 1975, your disciples, granddisciples, and followers in general have tried to fulfill your instructions and establish Kṛṣṇa consciousness in South Africa. You write in the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Ādi-līlā* 1.34, purport:

The service of the spiritual master is essential. If there is no chance to serve the spiritual master directly, devotees should serve him by remembering his instructions. There is no difference between the spiritual master’s instructions and the spiritual master himself. In his absence, therefore, his words and directions should be the pride of the disciple.

By your kindness you are manifesting yourself in many ways to your sincere followers, but of all your manifestations, your instructions are the very heart and pride of your followers. It is therefore essential that anyone who claims to be your sincere follower should make themselves familiar with the instructions contained within your Bhaktivedanta purports. By reading your books, we can understand exactly what is your desire and can also gain your association.

In *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 3.24.12 (purport) you write, “To honor the spiritual master means to carry out his instructions word for word.”

Of all your instructions, we consider most important your last statements regarding our cooperating among ourselves. There you expressed that our love for you will be shown by how much we can cooperate together to keep this institution going after your departure.

To maintain a preaching mission like ISKCON is very difficult, especially in this Age of Kali, which is saturated with misunderstanding, cheating, and hypocrisy. Nevertheless, I am convinced that if we can put aside our personal motives and simply try to do the needful to cooperate, we will receive your mercy and thereby become qualified to make further advancement. In a letter to Upendra dated 6 August 1970, you wrote,

Now all my disciples must work combinedly and with cooperation to spread this Sankirtana Movement. If you cannot work together then my work is stopped up. Our Society is like one big family and our relationships should be based on love and trust. We must give up the fighting spirit and use our intelligence to push ahead.

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, please empower your followers to give up their fighting spirit and use their individual intelligence to cooperate and work as a combined unit to fulfill your desire. This ISKCON movement is the main vehicle for spreading the mercy of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu in this Kali-yuga, and those who claim to be sincere and serious followers of Your Divine Grace will cooperate under the banner of ISKCON. Every endeavor is covered with some fault, as stated in *Bhagavad-gītā,* so we can expect so many difficulties, but if we actually want your mercy we should cooperate and base our relationships between each other on love and trust.

In a letter to Kṛṣṇavesa dāsī dated 16 January 1975, you wrote, “Our movement is based on love and trust, so if we do not co-operate, then how is that love and trust?” And in a letter to Rameśvara dāsa dated 15 July 1975, you wrote, “Differences may be there, but still you have to cooperate together, otherwise where is the question of my being relieved of so many problems and decisions?”

By your grace and kindness we have expanded your ISKCON society in South Africa, but if we fail to understand the need to continue cooperating, then your mission may be in jeopardy.

I humbly pray that you give your followers the intelligence to work together in a harmonious mood.

You have written to Brahmānanda dāsa on the 21st December 1967, “May Krishna give you more and more strength my dear child. I am very poor but Krishna is very rich. I can simply pray to Krishna. But Krishna is very great and magnanimous; He can do everything for you and for us all.”

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, if you pray to Kṛṣṇa on our behalf—that we can work together to establish your desire and mission in this material world—then surely Kṛṣṇa will reciprocate.

On this occasion of your divine appearance day, the members of ISKCON’s Kwazulu Natal *yātrā* would like to reassert our allegiance to Your Divine Grace and humbly beg that you except all of us as your eternal servants.

All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Your devotees in Kwazulu Natal, South Africa.  
(written by Pārtha Sārathi dāsa)

## Lagos

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at your divine lotus feet. All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, ISKCON’s founder-*ācārya*!

On this, your most auspicious appearance-day celebration, we wish to express our profound gratitude to you for allowing us to participate in this Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement. We are low-born, and without your grace, who among us could have ever had the blessing to hear and chant the glories of *svayaṁ bhagavān* Śrī Kṛṣṇa? Therefore, we are very much indebted to Your Divine Grace for distributing Kṛṣṇa consciousness to all and sundry out of your causeless mercy. We are praying at your lotus feet to help us become useful servants of your disciples in spreading the message of Godhead to every town and village. All glories to your Vyāsa-pūjā!

Your grandchildren at the ISKCON temple in Lagos, Nigeria.

## Liverpool

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

By Lord Caitanya’s causeless grace,  
You came to this confusing place  
To pick us up and set us free.  
Without your mercy, where would we be?

Your gift of love, a matchless gift—  
Pure *bhakti* brings the greatest lift.  
With the sweet sound of the holy name,  
This world can never be the same.

Your words bring to us new life,  
Free from illusion and mundane strife.  
The path to Godhead you do pave.  
To Māyā’s whims we no longer slave.

A wonderful mission you inspire—  
A worldwide chanting and dancing empire!  
To free us from that lust and greed,  
It’s love for Kṛṣṇa that we need.

We never met you in person, it’s true,  
But our greatest friend is certainly you.  
Who else would have saved the West?  
Who would have endured so many tests?

In life we may go so many places,  
Where we may see so many faces.  
Please help us focus on the goal:  
To bring love of Kṛṣṇa to each fallen soul.

You have left us to push on this mission.  
Please give us a more transcendental vision.  
If we forget your instructions we meet defeat.  
Please give us the dust from your pure lotus feet.

Your humble servants at ISKCON Liverpool, UK.  
(written by Cakra-nadī-devī dāsī).

## Longdenville

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to you on the occasion of your most auspicious appearance day—the most auspicious day of the entire year.

*tava kathāmṛtaṁ tapta-jīvanaṁ*  
*kavibhir īḍitaṁ kalmaṣāpaham*  
*śravaṇa-maṅgalaṁ śrīmad ātataṁ*  
*bhuvi gṛṇanti ye bhūri-dā janāḥ*

**“The nectar of Your words and the descriptions of Your activities are the life and soul of those suffering in this material world. These narrations, transmitted by learned sages, eradicate one’s sinful reactions and bestow good fortune upon whoever hears them. These narrations are broadcast all over the world and are filled with spiritual power. Certainly those who spread the message of Godhead are most munificent.” (*****Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*** **10.31.9)**

The *gopīs* declared that those who spread throughout the world the message of Godhead—namely the nectar of Kṛṣṇa’s words as found in *Bhagavad-gītā* and the descriptions of His activities as recorded in *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*—are the most munificent welfare workers. This is a perfect description of you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. You spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness all over the world, and your message is full of spiritual power. It transforms lives. It turns low, sinful people into saintly persons. Those who know nothing of Lord Kṛṣṇa or Lord Caitanya become lovers of God. Those who know only suffering and who can find no way out of it attain real happiness and solid direction in their lives.

When Lord Caitanya heard this verse recited by Mahārāja Pratāparudra, He became ecstatic. We also feel blissful when we hear it, just thinking how aptly it depicts our dark and dismal lives and your sunlike entrance into them.

But this verse can be applied to you in another way, Śrīla Prabhupāda. The nectar of your words, which are always Kṛṣṇa conscious, and any description of your pure and perfect activities are the life and soul of your followers, who are suffering here in separation from you. Certainly hearing your words and activities, which are filled with spiritual power, destroys sinful reactions and bestows the greatest good fortune upon anyone blessed to hear them. And those who spread your glories to others, thus bringing such fortunate souls in contact with Kṛṣṇa’s most pure and spotless devotee, are most munificent.

Vyāsa-pūjā is a day to remember all of this. Of course, we should always be doing so, but on this day we can focus more intently on these matters. Remembering the unsurpassable benediction you have given us and the entire world, we can rededicate ourselves to you, our eternal master and well-wisher. We can resolve to give more time and attention to the message you brought in the form of your books and instructions, and to hearing about you. We can rededicate ourselves to the movement you created for the express purpose of spreading the message of Godhead. And we can endeavor more to follow in your hallowed lotus footsteps in whatever way we can, working together only on your behalf as your menial servants, to be munificent welfare workers and bring the souls suffering in this material world to the nectarean shade of your lotus feet.

Your servants at ISKCON Longdenville, Trinidad.

## Madrid

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept this humble offering, together with my endless appreciation for everything you have given to me. I know that I will never be able to compensate in even the most minimum way for so much mercy you have given us.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, it was thirty-four years ago that you arrived in the West with your wealth of knowledge and devotion, desiring to introduce us to Kṛṣṇa make us conscious of Him.

Now, Śrīla Prabhupāda, at the end of the millennium, after twenty-four years of my spiritual life in this *saṅkīrtana* movement, I ask myself, What has happened all this time? How have I co-operated with you in the establishment of your movement? What have I given of myself for the benefit of the conditioned souls?

Allow me, my dear master, to confess before you with all sincerity: I haven’t been able to comply with your orders, Guru Mahārāja! I feel I have lost much valuable time in a vain fight against my obstinate mind, which has been my worst enemy and my greatest obstacle on the path of self-realization!

Now I have a premonition that my life is fading away. As my strength decreases, I feel sorrowful, thinking that I may not be able to fulfill the splendid requests you made to us before your departure: “Please distribute my books, please make everyone Kṛṣṇa conscious!”

I may be pretentious to say so, Śrīla Prabhupāda, but I want to do just a little of what you so wonderfully did! Yet now I am only immersed in a ocean of lamentation!

This is why I request you, I *implore* you, to please make me a useful servant! Please give me the mystic force the wise men fight *māyā* with! Empower me, Śrīla Prabhupāda; otherwise I will always feel I did not respond to your lotus feet!

Śrīla prabhupāda, the only thing I can do is offer you this homage full of grouses. Yet I do so with love, because without your valuable instructions and the remembrance of your transcendental pastimes, all of which I keep with me, it would have been impossible for me to maintain any hope in Kṛṣṇa consciousness, my beloved spiritual master!

It is due only to your immense compassion that I have made it as far as I have, O dear Śrīla prabhupāda! Millions and millions of reverences at your lotus feet! Unlimited millions of “Thank you’s” for letting me, your useless servant, join your divine service!

Holy is your saintly name forever. Śrīla prabhupāda! This planet will remember and worship you eternally.

Śrīla prabhupāda *kī jaya*!!

Your eternal servants in Madrid, Spain.  
(written by Hari Cakra dāsa)

## Malaga

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Please accept our humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet.

First of all we want to thank you for being so kind to us, coming as you did in the form of your *mūrti* to our temple last year. Now we have the opportunity to render personal service to you and come to the temple at any time to reveal our minds to you and pray for guidance and protection. When new people come to the temple, they are impressed to see you, and it is our pleasure to introduce them to you.

Here in Villa Varshana, the number of devotees is increasing, and although personally we do not have good qualifications, we remember the story you once told about a man who found a wire, a stick, and a pumpkin, put them all together and made a *vīṇā,* and then started to play wonderful music. In the same way, you have found all of us, lost in the material world, and if we just give ourselves into your hands so that you can do whatever you like, your divine plan to distribute love of God to the suffering souls can be manifested in this area of Spain and all over the world.

Please forgive our offenses and allow us to continue serving you and your dear devotees.

Your servants in Malaga, Spain.

## Malaysia

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my respectful obeisances. All glories to ISKCON.

Without Your Divine Grace, it is not the same. When we read about Your Divine Grace from the books written by your disciples, we get so much enlivened.
