At the same time that the book *Bhāgavata* appeared before the people of this world, the devotee *Bhāgavata—*Your Divine Grace—also appeared, and now the unfortunate people in this Age of Kali can definitely get out of the darkness of ignorance by taking shelter of these two *Bhāgavatas*.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you gave us the message of Lord Caitanya, that our eternal home is Lord Kṛṣṇa’s abode. There the Lord is manifested in His all-attractive form, surrounded by His associates. There everything is eternally blissful. At the same time, Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have manifested before us the spiritual world—ISKCON, which has the same quality and paraphernalia as those in Goloka Vṛndāvana. Lord Kṛṣṇa’s transcendental form is also present in ISKCON in the Deity form. By worshiping the Deity in the temple, one can feel how the Lord is all-powerful. In your ISKCON, there are so many excellent devotees, who are all associates of Lord Caitanya. Because in ISKCON everything is centered on Lord Kṛṣṇa, all activities are full of bliss. Anyone who wants to go back to the spiritual world can surely feel the spiritual ecstasy simply by joining ISKCON and loudly chanting “*Jaya* Śrīla Prabhupāda!”

It is by your mercy, Śrīla Prabhupāda, that ISKCON is constantly opening more and more centers and that *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* is being printed and distributed more and more. Sooner or later the whole world will be illuminated by these two *Bhāgavatas*. And then the spiritual world of ISKCON will overflood the whole world.

ISKCON and BBT founder-*ācārya* Śrīla Prabhupāda *kī jaya*!!

Your insignificant servants in New Navadvīpa (Khasadeśa).

## New Navadvīpa (Ukraine)

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances in the dust of your divine lotus feet. All glories to you! All glories to you! All glories to you!

Śrīla Prabhupāda, thank you very much for coming to this material world and giving all of us this wonderful and invaluable gift—the gift of Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

You said many times that your only qualification was that you repeated exactly and without adulteration the teachings of your Guru Mahārāja. Certainly, your books are the most important thing you gave us. However, if we would have had only the book *Bhāgavata* but not the person *Bhāgavata—*you—then how would we be able to make use of this gift? How would we be able to believe that all this is real? The absolute necessity of the person *Bhāgavata* is confirmed by your own words: “Example is better then precept.”

As Kali-yuga progresses, people are becoming more and more entangled in the net of their sinful reactions. Now they are losing whatever remnants of goodness they had left. We have many troubles due to this turmoil all about us: It is so difficult to control the mind and senses. It is difficult to restrain anger and to conquer lust. Often we cannot even materially maintain ourselves because we are not expert enough in material activities. We often quarrel with one another about insignificant nonsense, and our ambitions lead us to intrigues and plots, etc., etc., etc.

As it is said, “Owls can hardly imagine the daylight.” Similarly, we can hardly believe in the spiritual world. But when we see your example and the example of your strict followers, the words of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* become reality and we find the enthusiasm by which we can overcome all fits of unbelief.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, just as we are inspired by your example as a perfect Vaiṣṇava, please bless us so we ourselves can become good examples and thus inspire other conditioned souls to follow you.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, thank you for creating this wonderful ISKCON family. This is a spiritual family because the center of it is Kṛṣṇa. We want to preserve it and expand it. We want to build pure Vaiṣṇava relationships, which will be based on service to and love for Kṛṣṇa. In other words we want the unfortunate people of Ukraine to understand at last that this Society is the bona fide representation of the spiritual world here on earth. We want every sincere soul who comes to visit your temple never to even think of leaving.

However, it is certain that we need your special mercy to fulfill this aspiration. Please pour this mercy on us constantly. Without your mercy, we are simply good for nothing. The fire of material existence is burning the desert of our hearts, and these deserts can be revived only by the rainstorm of your mercy. Please come into the core of our hearts and then never leave them, even if some of us may demand that in madness.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for everything!

All glories to Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Trying to become useful in fulfilling your mission,  
Your insignificant servants in New Navadvīpa Dhāma, Kiev, Ukraine.

## New Pāṇihāṭī

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances in the dust of your merciful lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

We pray that one day the entire world will become aware of the depth of your mercy and begin to apply your message in their lives, and we pray that we may also become better examples of how your mercy changes lives for the better.

The coming change of the millennium offers an incredible opportunity for us to increase the awareness of the general public about the importance of your place in history. Your appearance on this world has been one of the greatest occurrences in this turbulent and eventful century. You established the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement during this century, a movement that has the potential to lift the world out of its current state of ignorance and confusion. As the historians mark the most important events of this century, your achievements should be placed at the very top of the list. But how will they know about your incredible life story unless we, your humble followers explain it to them? This is the time for us to marshal all of our combined resources in a worldwide effort to promote your divine example and teachings. We should take note of how our brothers and sisters who follow Lord Jesus Christ have so masterfully projected his example of sacrifice and compassion into the consciousness of the world. Granted, they have made many mistakes in the effort, but still we cannot and should not discredit their sincere attempts; rather, we should take inspiration from them and follow their example and spread your glories for the benefit of all mankind.

Someone may argue that we should not place so much emphasis on promoting your life but should rather simply glorify the Lord. To this I say that your life was nothing but glorification of the Lord and that by hearing your story people will see the greatest example of how one can surrender to the Lord.

The world is desperate for a message of hope, especially with all of the focus on the anticipated disasters, either man-made or by the hand of God, which are predicted to occur at the turn of the century. Because of these concerns, they may be more inclined to hear information that can offer them hope. Your teachings are filled with messages of hope and encouragement for the world’s misdirected people. Surely they would be more interested in hearing from you rather than the speculations of materially confused mental speculators or fanatical religionists who are preoccupied with matters beyond their control. You taught us to always depend on Kṛṣṇa and to endeavor with intelligence. We should be practical in all our earthly dealings, but we should never forget that the results of all endeavors belong to Kṛṣṇa and that any and all success fully depends on His blessings. It would be of no benefit to the spreading of Kṛṣṇa consciousness to disturb people any more than they already are with messages of doubt and fear. It would be better to use this important time in history to bring people to a higher level of consciousness by spreading your divine message of love of God.

So we pray to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, to please inspire us to visualize a better world filled with the joy that comes from being in contact with the Absolute Truth. A world where caring and compassion are the norms. A world dominated by persons of higher character, whose lives are governed by their faith in God and by following the examples of great personalities such as yourself. Yes, people will follow your example if only they can hear about you. This is important for us, your followers, to understand. We should not think that your example is relevant only to members of the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement. We have practical experience of people from all walks of life who have been moved and inspired after hearing about you. What greater thing could we do than present you to the world? This would be a true act of compassion and love for mankind. This would indeed prepare mankind for the coming millennium. This would insure a bright future for the world.

So on this auspicious day of your appearance, I hope and pray that all of your servants from around the world can come together and truly do good for mankind by telling your story in as many ways as possible. As they say, the best way to make a point is to tell a story, and what better story to tell than yours, Śrīla Prabhupāda? A story of how to love God and serve mankind.

Your humble servants and followers at New Pāṇihāṭī Dhāma, ISKCON’s temple in Atlanta, Georgia, USA.

## New Ramaṇa Reti

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Rare is the opportunity given to a conditioned soul to get the association of a pure devotee of the Lord. Such priceless association should be seen as the most rare gift. It is enacted on the spiritual platform, under the direction of the internal energy of the Supreme Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa and the spiritual master.

In the person of His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, we find such a spiritual mater, who has brought the Godhead before us as the Supreme Person, Śrī Kṛṣṇa. By Śrīla Prabhupāda’s fulfilling the desire of his spiritual master, Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Gosvāmī Mahārāja, we now stand at the threshold of complete spiritual perfection, praying that the Lord will give us the intelligence to somehow respond to that absolute grace given by Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Śrīla Prabhupāda has shown us, both by precept and example, pure devotional service and its transcendental nature. Just see how he has manifested the qualities of a powerful devotee and is therefore worthy of all respect! The respect due great devotees like Śrīla Prabhupāda is shown in the pages of the *Bhāgavatam*. As Śrīla Śukadeva Gosvāmī took his seat before the sages on the bank of the Ganges, there were not only many saintly persons in the assembly but even incarnations of God. Śrīla Prabhupāda comments: “This means that the devotee of the Lord is more honored in the world than the Lord Himself. One should therefore never minimize the importance of a devotee like Śukadeva Gosvāmī.” (*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 1.19.30)

We are simply struck with wonder at the clear presentation of the Vedic literatures rendered by Śrīla Prabhupāda. Tirelessly he worked for the satisfaction of his beloved spiritual master, to give everyone the benediction of hearing the *mahā-mantra*,

*Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa, Hare Hare /*  
*Hare Rāma, Hare Rāma, Rāma Rāma, Hare Hare*

and to flood the world with knowledge of the Supreme Personality of Godhead. His kindness to us is reflected in a statement by Vidura to the great sage Maitreya: “O best among the *brāhmaṇas,* those who are spiritual masters are very kind to the needy. They are always kind to their followers, disciples and sons, and without being asked by them, the spiritual master describes all that is knowledge.” (*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 3.7.36)

Śrīla Prabhupāda has left to the world not only his transcendental books but also an amazing number of audiotapes. His sincere followers can take advantage of these recordings at any time and hear such great descriptions of pure devotees and their actions, all told with great transcendental enthusiasm by Śrīla Prabhupāda. Take, for example, the story of Dhruva Mahārāja, whose austerities caused the universal breathing to be choked up. Śrīla Prabhupāda has recorded this history on tape, and we also find a wonderful description of the pure devotee Dhruva in one of Śrīla Prabhupāda’s purports:

This example of Dhruva Mahārāja’s closing the holes of his personal body and thereby closing the breathing holes of the total universe clearly indicates that a devotee, by his personal devotional service, can influence all the people of the whole world to become devotees of the Lord. If there is only one pure devotee in pure Kṛṣṇa consciousness, he can change the total consciousness of the world into Kṛṣṇa consciousness. This is not very difficult to understand if we study the behavior of Dhruva Mahārāja. (*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 4.8.80, purport)

So we can see what Śrīla Prabhupāda has done by his personal devotion to Lord Kṛṣṇa.

Our study of Śrīla Prabhupāda and his activities will certainly surcharge our consciousness with Kṛṣṇa consciousness, because this is the way to perfection—simply to hear about the Supreme Personality of Godhead and his pure devotees. Moreover, considering what he has done for ourselves and the entire world, we can never appreciate his unlimited causeless mercy enough. If we could only remember the lotus feet of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, in whose line such powerful *ācāryas* as Śrīla Prabhupāda are coming, then perhaps we could slightly understand what Śrīla Prabhupāda has actually done. He has connected us to the Brahma-Madhva-Gauḍīya-sampradāya, which has uncovered Vṛndāvana and revealed the pastimes of Śrī Śrī Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa. He has borne the mercy of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu and connected us to that divine Lord through the shelter of his lotus feet. He has revealed the pastimes of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu through the translations of the *Śrī Caitanya-caritāmṛta* and caused us to look toward the shelter of the six Gosvāmīs as a means of protecting our very delicate devotional creepers.

There are really no words to express our gratitude to Śrīla Prabhupāda for bestowing his mercy upon us, even though we have tried to expressed ourselves in words. But what can we do? An affectionate parent understands the sincere attempts of the child, even if they’re riddled with faults.

Śrīla Prabhupāda would often say that he has no qualification. But he would also say that his main qualification was that he would go on hearing his spiritual master, even if he did not understand everything spoken by him. Let us therefore glorify Śrīla Prabhupāda by reading his books, distributing his books, constantly hearing his wonderful lectures and *bhajans,* pushing on this Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement despite all obstacles, and praying that Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa will help us properly glorify Śrīla Prabhupāda by the power of our collective devotion to his lotus feet.

Śrīla Prabhupāda *kī jaya*!

The residents of the New Ramaṇa Reti rural community in Alachua, Florida, USA.

## New Śāntipura

**A New “Long March”**

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances at your lotus feet again and again. All glories to Your Divine Grace and your sincere followers!

It is already five years that we, a handful of devotees, have been in Śāntipura struggling to survive materially and spiritually.

So many people have come and gone. We have faced so many challenges and lived so many adventures. We have gained much success and enjoyed much spiritual pleasure, but we have also suffered failures and pains, and it has all passed like clouds across the Yang Zi River. But you have remained like the sky, which is eternally situated above our land.

Your desire to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness in this part of the world never wavered, and we can still feel that desire permeating above and throughout every corner of our Śāntipura City.

This is your Śāntipura City, Śrīla Prabhupāda, the “Far Eastern New York.” Thirty years ago, you arrived alone and practically penniless in the “Western New York,” the very heart of *māyā*. But gradually, from zero, hundreds of temples, hundreds and thousands of devotees, and millions of books appeared, simply due to your unwavering devotion to Kṛṣṇa. And you marched: You marched from West to East, and from the “Western New York” to the “Far Eastern New York,” though you were manifested here in a different way.

Although what you have achieved seems like magic to the common man, you never took anything for granted. When you wandered through the New York streets alone, you once said to a stranger, “We have our temples, our devotees, and our books; only time is separating.” You know everything—past, present, and future—because you are never separated from your beloved spiritual master and your beloved Lord, Śrī Kṛṣṇa. Your faith in the instructions of your Guru Mahārāja, which are nondifferent from Kṛṣṇa’s, made you as omniscient and omnipotent as God Himself. You are a servant-God. So as everything belongs to God, everything belongs to you also. Śrīla Prabhupāda, this “Far Eastern New York” belongs to you, just as the “Western New York” does. Only time is separating, as you said.

Hundreds of temples, hundreds and thousands of devotees, and millions of books will appear here in due course of time, simply due to your unwavering devotion to Kṛṣṇa manifested here in “Far Eastern New York,” in your Śāntipura Dhāma. There should be no doubts about this. You have already proved the potency of your devotion in the West; now let it manifest in the Far East.

What is the use of us, a handful of immature junior devotees, struggling to survive materially and spiritually day after day, year after year? We are simply fortunate to be allowed to witness the advent of your “Eastern New York.” By your and our guru’s mercy, we also have the opportunity to follow you and watch your fast-moving but heavy footsteps, your straight steel-framed back, your highly-raised head like a swan’s, and your brilliant waving saffron robes as you pass through the Śāntipura streets, not caring for surprised onlookers or the laughter and shouts of the mundane fools with iron-cold material faces.

There is no mercy greater than yours, Śrīla Prabhupāda. You have pulled us from material illusion and frustration and allowed us to approach you and become “your men,” to participate in your mission despite our unqualified condition.

Maybe the advent of your “Far Eastern New York” will be a “long march” compared to the triumphant advent of your “Western New York,” but the result will be the same. “Victory is on your side,” Sañjaya said at the end of the *Bhagavad-gītā*. “Wherever there is Kṛṣṇa, the master of all mystics, and wherever there is Arjuna, the supreme archer, there will also certainly be opulence, victory, extraordinary power, and morality. That is my opinion.”

You are Arjuna, the supreme archer, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and you have Lord Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, the master of all mystic power, as your chariot driver. You are our “Chairman Mao,” leading us, marching toward a new kingdom—Kṛṣṇa’s kingdom.

Please bless us with the strength to firmly cling on to our position as “your men,” and let us continue the “long march” under your command, following in the footsteps of your senior followers, so that we may be among the groups that ultimately arrive at “Yan An”—the last destination of our “long march.”

Thank you very much, Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Your eternal servants at Śāntipura Dhāma, Khasadeśa.  
(written by Siddha Deha dāsa)

## New Tālavan

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our *daṇḍavats* in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to your devotional service! All glories to the previous *ācāryas*! All glories to the Personality of Godhead, by whose divine will everything is living!

How wonderfully great it is for the Supreme Lord to maintain and broadcast Vedic culture! He is so kind, and you are His divine agent, magically transforming weeds into flowering fruits full of love for Him, Śrī Kṛṣṇa. Those living entities moving directly under His influence are dancing through time, space, and all other forces, accomplishing unheralded, glorious feats beyond the scope of what ordinary conditioned souls can do.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are such a divine person. Fully absorbed in thoughts of the Lord, you are delightfully dancing and singing to His tunes. In this way you have attracted your disciples and followers.

We are so lucky. There is no earthly calculation capable of ascertaining our fortune. Yet we as individuals, or sometimes as an organization, appear to neglect your order, inspiration, direction, or Kṛṣṇa consciousness. However, as a loving father and spiritual master, you continue to play your devotional music and sing your songs to lead us back home to Godhead. Actually, there is no question of being misled, because your writings are perfectly clear. We simply must become more and more like you: firm yet loving, humble yet determined, great yet modest, strict yet compassionate.

Oh, how wonderful you are! Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, help all of your Prabhupādānugas advance more and more in Kṛṣṇa consciousness, until we can all ride the waves of material nature as gracefully as you do. May we please you via our actions, and may you lovingly smile, observing us serve your lotus feet. We all long for the chance to gain your appreciation, and we want no reward other than your pleasure. Please accept our humble obeisances and bless us to gain pure loving devotion to your lotus feet.

Begging for a small place in your divine retinue,  
The devotees at New Tālavan, ISKCON’s rural community in Mississippi, USA.  
(written by Yogīndra Vandana dāsa)

## New Vrajamaṇḍala

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

On this auspicious occasion of your Vyāsa-pūjā celebrations, we are very happy to recommit ourselves to your service and your mission.

Also, we are glad to express our deepest gratitude for everything you are giving to us. You are giving us your wonderful books, especially your *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam;* your temples; your followers; and your strength and inspiration to continue serving Śrī Kṛṣṇa. You are giving us the holy name, the Deity of the Lord, delicious *prasādam,* and the nectar of the preaching mission. You are giving us Māyāpur and Vṛndāvana. You are giving us the loving glance of Lord Caitanya and Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa. You are giving us the association of the Vaiṣṇavas. Actually, you are giving us so many invaluable presents that we cannot name them all. Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for your kindness and your love.

Unfortunately, often we do not properly value and use all the assets, both internal and external, that you are giving us, and therefore we are often not so successful in our services. Please empower us with the vision and the strength to serve the Lord properly and to develop New Vrajamaṇḍala in a way that would be very pleasing to Your Divine Grace.

Your little servants at New Vrajamaṇḍala, the rural ISKCON community in Spain.

## New Vraja Dhāma (Hungary)

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to you on this most auspicious day of your appearance!

It is a warm, sunny day today. Spring has come, the season that represents the Supreme Lord because of its beauty and joyfulness. Our cows are happily gazing, eating the fresh green grass as a service to Kṛṣṇa, to produce milk for His *maṅgala-ārati* sweets. The birds are chirping to glorify the Lord, and the flowers are blooming to be offered to Kṛṣṇa. You have taught us to see how nature is glorifying the Supreme Person, and we have a nice opportunity to practice that type of vision here in New Vraja Dhāma, the abode of Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Śyāmasundara.

There was a time when the whole world was under one Vedic culture, the culture of Kṛṣṇa. In that culture there was harmony among all living beings, and the people were educated in God consciousness. They were trained to attain human perfection, to gradually rise to the platform of love of God and return to the spiritual world, where they would eternally perform loving service to the Lord there.

With the progression of the ages, Vedic culture has been lost, brahminical qualifications have disappeared, and irreligion prevails. Dharma is unknown to the people, and due to the so-called advancement of civilization there is only godlessness, corruption, gross sense enjoyment, violence, and scarcity.

You explain in your books that any civilization not based on brahminical culture or guided by *brāhmaṇas* is certainly a condemned civilization, where the aim of life cannot be fulfilled. Due to your boundless compassion for the suffering souls, you have made transcendental revolutionary plans to save the world in its darkest hour. You have been tirelessly preaching the principles of dharma and have trained your followers to do the same in order to gradually establish Vedic culture again. You have instructed us to study and distribute your books and in this way destroy all misconceptions and make the real path of perfection bright and clear. You have also instructed us to establish Kṛṣṇa conscious communities as places for devotees to practice Kṛṣṇa consciousness in a conducive environment and as models for the whole society to follow.

A few years ago a handful of devotees came to this little Hungarian village and, under the expert guidance of His Holiness Śivarāma Swami Mahārāja, started to develop a spiritual city. We are following your motto of simple living and high thinking, which you exemplified. After five years of endeavor in the open fields, a little village has appeared, with the beautiful temple of Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Śyāmasundara, Śrī Govardhana Lāla, Śrī Gaura-Naṭarāja, and Śrī Śrī Lakṣmī-Nṛsiṁha-deva at its center. There is also a *gośālā,* an art studio, a pottery, a bakery, a vegetarian restaurant, a guesthouse and camping site, an apiary, fifteen little houses for the eighty resident devotees, and tens of thousands of visitors a year who come to learn about real civilization. And we know that this is only the beginning of your plans.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are the commander of a great world mission. You are sitting on the *vyāsāsana* in the back of the temple room gazing intently at Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Śyāmasundara, who gaze back at you smilingly. You both know that your movement will spread throughout the world. There is no doubt that you are living with us and that by following your divine instructions we may live with you.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, we are your stumbling grandchildren on the path of spiritual life, and we need your guidance and shelter so much. May we always hear your directions clearly and carry them out with great determination, with no other motivation than to please you. May we imbibe your ways of perfect simplicity and pure thought, eagerly working to implement your glorious vision. May you bless us to properly see and serve Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Śyāmasundara, Śrī Govardhana Lāla, Śrī Gaura-Naṭarāja Mahāprabhu, Śrī Śrī Lakṣmī-Nṛsiṁha-deva, and the Vaiṣṇavas. May we become your useful instruments in making New Vraja Dhāma into a place where all people may gain inspiration and take part in the eternal festival of *nāma-saṅkīrtana*. May we always remain at the shelter of your lotus feet. All glories to you!

Your servants in New Vraja Dhāma, Hungary.  
(written by Mañjarī-devī dāsī)

## New Vraja Dhāma (Khasadeśa)

Our dear grandfather Śrīla Prabhupāda,Please accept our most humble obeisances. All glories to you!

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

At a recent initiation ceremony, one of our spiritual masters, His Holiness Umāpati Swami, asked us this question: “Who is Śrīla Prabhupāda?”

“He is our grandfather,” we answered. “He is the spiritual master of our spiritual masters.”

Dear grandfather, please forgive our degradation and ignorance. Though fully living in your mercy, we constantly forget your glories, feeling so much distance between you and us and feeling so unfamiliar with you. But whenever we hear our spiritual masters say, “I am only an ordinary man; all my knowledge and strength come from Śrīla Prabhupāda,” we remember your glories and feel that we are being brought closer to you.

A few years back we celebrated the hundredth anniversary of your appearance in this world. At that time our spiritual masters were frequently separated from us, but they told us, “The grandfather takes better care of the children than the father does.” At that time we did not know what they meant, but we soon realized that the anniversary of your appearance in this world was not an ordinary year because we increased our thinking of you that year.

There is a portrait of you painted in oil hanging in one of our rooms. Your eyes are concentrated, and your mood is absorbed. It seems that you are in *samādhi,* and we pray that you help us also to be deeply immersed in the holy name of the Lord.

“Śrīla Prabhupāda instructed us, ‘Work now, *samādhi* later.’” Our spiritual masters have told us this. Our hearts are filled with so much sin that we can hardly dare to gaze directly into Kṛṣṇa’s eyes. It is only by trying to be sincere devotees and strictly follow the instructions of our spiritual masters, making every effort to preach, that we can wash off all the stain and dirt.

Some time back, we took a long train ride up north to a distant place to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness. We were filled with excitement mixed with nervousness. We were quite worried that we would not fulfill the instruction of our gurus. But as soon as we remembered their blessing—“Spiritual strength comes from Śrīla Prabhupāda’s books”—the sun rose above the horizon in our hearts. We feel your unlimited mercy as we present *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is* to people and tell them, “This book was written by Śrīla Prabhupāda, the spiritual master of our spiritual masters.”

Dear grandfather, we understand that we are only unqualified grandchildren of yours, and we are eternally indebted to you for bestowing your mercy upon us. The only way we can avoid disappointing you is to try to follow our spiritual masters closely, becoming 100 percent disciples and serving their lotus feet. We are fully confident that our grandfather will never forsake his grandchildren, not even the worst of them.

We bow down at your lotus feet again and again. May your glories endlessly purify our hearts!

Your fallen grandchildren in New Vraja Dhāma, Khasadeśa.

## New Vrindavan

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

You left the pristine quiet of Vṛndāvana. You crossed the Atlantic on a freighter and, en route, suffered a heart attack. You arrived on these shores at the age of sixty-nine with only a few rupees in your pocket. Most people seek to relax in their retirement years. Yet it was exactly at this point in your life that you set forth on your mission. You knew no one here. You wanted nothing for yourself. You undertook great hardships only to fulfill the order of your guru by presenting the message of Caitanya Mahāprabhu to a most unqualified audience. You came to America with only one purpose—to draw people closer to Lord Kṛṣṇa. You did this out of your great compassion and love.

Fearlessly you expanded your new movement, fully confident that Kṛṣṇa is always by your side. Over the years you continually traveled throughout the world to nurture the movement—to guide us, encourage us, expose our foolishness, and intervene in our disputes and correct us. Śrīla Prabhupāda, we relished the time when you walked among us just like a loving father. We are sorry that we couldn't follow your instructions perfectly, and that we are still struggling to understand them.

Here in New Vrindavan there are so many things you gave us to do—we can never complain you aren’t keeping us busy. We hope you aren’t too disappointed in us. But please know that even if we can’t be perfect disciples, we know that with you as the perfect example, we will always have the opportunity to improve, and we are so grateful for that. Now you are watching us to see how we continue the mission and how we treat each other, and you are also waiting for us to return to our eternal home.

Around the world, hundreds and thousands of devotees offer their actions and words and energy to you like so many flower petals offered at your lotus feet. Please bless us that we may always remember your great sacrifice. Bless us that we may always offer you and your devotees words filled with love and devotion. Bless us so that we may put these words into action.

We grow older, and as the years flash by we feel our little worlds rushing into the mouths of Kṛṣṇa’s universal form ever more quickly. Thus it’s all the more imperative that we remember your instructions, which open the doors to the spiritual world and to the loving embrace of Their Lordships Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Vrindāvan-Chandra. You told us that we have become fortunate and now we should make others fortunate. The dictionary defines fortunate as “receiving some unexpected good.” Our fortune is certainly unexpected and undeserved. O Śrīla Prabhupāda, please bless us so that we will never forget the great fortune we have received from you.

One of your heart’s cherished desires was to create a sacred place in the West where Lord Kṛṣṇa would be glorified and His pastimes remembered—a place of pilgrimage just like Vṛndāvana. Therefore, in 1968 your transcendental vision manifested “New Vrindaban” in America. O Prabhupāda, we are very grateful to you for giving us an opportunity to serve this sacred vision. Here in New Vrindavan our good fortune is very evident in the form of the land, the cows, the association of wonderful devotees, and in the most merciful glances of Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Vrindāvan-Chandra. Even if we served you constantly for eternity, we can never repay the great fortune we have received from you.

O Prabhupāda, in this frenzied, chaotic world we hear you beckoning us with the clear and beautiful names of Kṛṣṇa, and how wonderful that is! May we always remember your pastimes again and again in the core of our hearts. You are the captain of the ship. We know without a doubt that you will always be there for us, and we pray that we will always be there for you.

The inmates of New Vrindavan, West Virginia, USA.

## New York (Brooklyn)

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*śuddha-bhakata- caraṇa-reṇu*  
*bhajana-anukūla*  
*bhakata-sevā, parama-siddhi,*  
*prema-latikāra mūla*

**“Dust from the lotus feet of pure devotees is conducive to devotional service, while service to the Vaiṣṇavas is itself the supreme perfection and the root of the tender creeper of divine love.”**

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

All glories to Your Divine Grace! And all glories to the transcendental Vyāsa-pūjā *mahā-mahotsava,* the source of real bliss and the life and soul of *divya-jñāna* that you so lovingly transmit unto our stonelike hearts. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are our primary source of transcendental Kṛṣṇa conscious knowledge and you are our infallible guide, master, and ever well-wisher on this most auspicious path of *kṛṣṇa-bhakti*. Your Divine Grace leads us to the light of the *Bhāgavata,* you lead us to the bliss of the nectar of devotion, and you lead us to the shores of *prema-bhakti,* where we shall never again be tossed about by the waves of the cruel ocean of nescience.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are the infallible captain that guides our ISKCON ship, whether it be through treacherous waters or through the calmest seas, when the winds of favorable Kṛṣṇa conscious devotion bless us with the grace of Lord Kṛṣṇa, our dearest friend and benefactor.

In the *Bhagavad-gītā* (15.16) Lord Kṛṣṇa describes the nature of a person fully absorbed in Kṛṣṇa consciousness. “There are two classes of beings, the fallible and the infallible. In the material world every living entity is fallible, and in the spiritual world every living entity is called infallible.” So the defects are there only on the material platform. One who is acting in Kṛṣṇa consciousness is beyond those defects. Your Divine Grace always acted on the platform of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. As Lord Kṛṣṇa’s empowered representative, you descended from Kṛṣṇa’s abode to deliver us back home, back to Godhead. Therefore you are always the infallible pure devotee of Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Govinda. You never in your life knew what was meat-eating, illicit sex, intoxication, and gambling. You are always sinless and pure, and, like the sacred Ganges, you purify us by your refreshing transcendental association.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, your glorious nature is revealed again in a verse by the great Vaiṣṇava *ācārya* Śrīla Narottama dāsa Ṭhākura, in his *Vaiṣṇava Vijñapti,* where he sings:

*tomāra hṛdoye sadā govinda-viśrāma*  
*govinda kohena—mora vaiṣṇava parāṇa*

**“Your heart is always the resting place of Lord Govinda, and Lord Govinda says, ‘The Vaiṣṇavas are in My heart.’”**

Śrīla Prabhupāda, once you were asked by a reporter, “Swamiji, how far back can you remember thinking of Kṛṣṇa?” Your response, Śrīla Prabhupāda, reveals more of your infallible nature. You told the reporter, “There isn’t a time I can remember when I wasn’t thinking of Kṛṣṇa.”

Your infallible nature can’t be imitated. It is a clear indication of your love for Kṛṣṇa, your pure devotion for Kṛṣṇa, and your realization of your eternal relationship with Kṛṣṇa. This is something that a conditioned soul just can’t fake. It is in your own perfect words that we find the best example of your wonderful and infallible nature. You state in your purport to *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 2.6.34:

Lord Brahmā is the original speaker of Vedic wisdom to Nārada, and Nārada is the distributor of transcendental knowledge all over the world through his various disciples, like Vyāsadeva and others. The followers of Vedic wisdom accept the statements of Brahmājī as gospel truth, and transcendental knowledge is thus being distributed all over the world by the process of disciplic succession from time immemorial, since the beginning of the creation. Lord Brahmā is the perfect liberated living being within the material world, and any sincere student of transcendental knowledge must accept the words and statements of Brahmājī as infallible. The Vedic knowledge is infallible because it comes down directly from the Supreme Lord unto the heart of Brahmā, and since he is the most perfect living being, Brahmājī is always correct to the letter. And this is because Lord Brahmā is a great devotee of the Lord who has earnestly accepted the lotus feet of the Lord as the supreme truth. In the *Brahma-saṁhitā,* which is compiled by Brahmājī, he repeats the aphorism *govindam ādi-puruṣaṁ tam ahaṁ bhajāmi:* “I am a worshiper of the original personality of Godhead, Govinda, the primeval Lord.” So whatever he says, whatever he thinks, and whatever he does normally in his mood are to be accepted as truth because of his direct and very intimate connection with Govinda, the primeval Lord. Śrī Govinda, who pleasingly accepts the loving transcendental service of His devotees, gives all protection to the words and actions of His devotees. The Lord declares in the *Bhagavad-gītā* (9.31), *kaunteya pratijānīhi: “*O son of Kuntī, please declare it.” The Lord asks Arjuna to declare, and why? Because sometimes the declaration of Govinda Himself may seem contradictory to mundane creatures, but the mundaner will never find any contradiction in the words of the Lord’s devotees. The devotees are especially protected by the Lord so that they may remain infallible. Therefore the process of devotional service always begins in the service of the devotee who appears in disciplic succession.

Therefore, we want to surrender to you and serve your transcendental lotus feet in this life and for the remainder of eternity, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Your Divine Grace is our primary, perfect, and infallible Guru Mahārāja, as well as our father, friend, and ever well-wisher, and for us there is no one who will ever take your place in our life or in our hearts.

All glories to the topmost swanlike devotee of the most beautiful all attractive Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Govindadeva: His Divine Grace Śrīla Prabhupāda *kī jaya*! We beg to remain your servants forever,

Your fallen insignificant children at the Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Govinda Mandir in Brooklyn, New York, New York, USA.  
(written by Rāmabhadra dāsa)

## New York (26 Second Avenue)

My dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Please accept my prostrated obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet! All glories to Your Divine Grace and the *saṅkīrtana* movement of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu!

I was just about to sit down to write this offering when the phone rang and a devotee friend told me with great excitement that he had just spoke to a lady who told him that she and her husband had interviewed you at the original “Matchless Gifts” storefront thirty-three years ago. She said that her realization at the time was that you were very outspoken and deliberate, unconcerned with charisma and very patient, and happy to start small. She also had concluded that although it wouldn’t be very easy to follow your path, it would certainly be worthwhile and exceptional to do so!

Well, Śrīla Prabhupāda, I never had the good fortune of meeting you personally, as the lady and her husband had done thirty-three years ago (although you have very mercifully appeared to me in many dreams). But being able to chant my rounds, have *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* class, and attend your *guru-pūjā* in this most holy of all holy places—the 26 Second Avenue “Matchless Gifts” storefront—is certainly a benediction of a special kind.

The magic and the potency of your presence is still readily available to anyone who happens to put his or her foot into this most sacred space on the planet. And we, your 26 Second Ave. inmates, are daily reminded of this fact.

You wanted to transform Western so-called civilization, and you choose New York City to start with! What a place! You called it Māyā’s capital, or in other words sense-gratification-loka, and you were right! Every day we see the conditioned souls flocking around bars, restaurants, and stores, wasting their valuable time with intoxication, meat-eating, illicit sex, and gambling. Who could be so bold as to dare try to entice these living entities to stop all that nonsense and try to cultivate an attraction for Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu and His amazing pastimes?

Only you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, were and still are able to accomplish this astounding feat, and I’m a living example of that.

You chose New York, and somehow or other you made me surrender to your mission here, and now I feel a new surrender at the place where it all began: Matchless Gifts, 26 Second Ave.

The potential for preaching here is incredible! It’s the karma of the city to be a trend-setter in art, education, and business for the whole world. So many talented and gifted people come here from afar to become successful. Meanwhile Lord Caitanya has sent His most empowered representative, Your Divine Grace, to teach them what real success is: Pure unalloyed love for God, the ultimate intoxication.

We at 26 Second Ave. are trying to serve this amazingly palatable and splendid nectar to everybody who visits, and how can we do that? By first tasting it ourselves. Therefore we are very fortunate to have Dayānanda Prabhu and his good wife Nandi Mukhī Mātājī, who teach by example that Vaiṣṇava relationships are based on love and trust. In this way you are attracting countless living entities to your lotus feet and making sure that they are well cared-for. This affection is not of this world: it’s the Vaikuṇṭha atmosphere, which you imported.

As your dear servant Śrutakīrti Prabhu said in his last talk, you didn’t love to manage, but you managed to love!

I pray on behalf of all the devotees here that we can become transparent via media for your love for Kṛṣṇa and in that way respiritualize the Big Rotten Apple.

Thank you for giving me this opportunity to glorify you and Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s mission,  
The devotees at 26 Second Avenue, New York, New York, USA.  
(written by Yajña-puruṣa dāsa)

## Nijni Tagil

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

This past year we have been convinced again and again of how safe a shelter your lotus feet are. This past year was a very hard time for me and the other devotees of our temple. Our spiritual master left ISKCON. The difficult period continued for several months and was very painful. And as is usual during great difficulties, many things became clear to us.

*Śāstra,* guru, and *sādhu*—these are the authorities a devotee must follow in his spiritual life. The teachings of the *śāstras* given by the disciplic succession, the *paramparā,* are the main authority. The guru is a link in the *paramparā* and gives the śāstric knowledge without change. Only in this case can he be considered a spiritual master. If his words don’t correspond with the teachings of the previous *ācāryas,* he can’t be a guru. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you repeated this many times in your books and lectures. Nevertheless, many devotees struggled to accept this truth—not only others but myself also. For many devotees, accepting this truth was a test of their sincerity in devotional service. Many devotees had to choose between their personal attachment to their spiritual master and the eternal principle of guru. It was seen that those devotees who regularly read your books didn’t lose their sense.

We had no information for a long time because of geographical reasons, and some devotees intentionally misrepresented whatever information we did have, but we survived because we had firm faith in you and your books, which authoritatively deliver the teachings of the *paramparā*.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, please let me thank you for your sincere disciples Jayādvaita Swami and Jayapatāka Swami, who came to us at a time of spiritual crisis and saved many devotees, tactfully and humbly explaining the events as they were and establishing the Vaiṣṇava *siddhānta*. They showed nice Vaiṣṇava qualities such as humility, tolerance, and deep compassion. The devotees of the Ural region are very thankful to them.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I want to tell you that we didn’t stop distributing your books even for a moment and continue to do so now.

There is a hard economic crisis in our country, and we distribute your books practically at BBT cost. Although our results are not very great, we feel great purification from this process in all respects. Not long ago it was very profitable for the temples and devotees to distribute books, but now Kṛṣṇa has put us into circumstances in which book distribution doesn’t bring much money or glory or great results. But it is joyful to see that many devotees have passed this test successfully, and their motives in devotional service remained pure. Certainly, some devotees have left the movement; still, it seems to us that everything that has happened has not only brought a crisis but also many positive things. Many devotees have become more serious and grown up spiritually; they are more honest with themselves and aware of their mistakes. The leaders of the temples now realize the necessity of cooperation with *gṛhastha* devotees and the importance of *nāma-haṭṭa* programs. But we are most grateful to you for your gifts to us of the holy name and your books, which are always with us. In any circumstance we may always be connected with Kṛṣṇa through the holy name and through your books. Nothing can prevent us from taking advantage of these but our own material desires, so it is very important to associate with sincere senior devotees who can help us see and deal with our *anarthas*.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, please inspire your disciples to come to our Ural region more often. Since the economic crisis began, most devotees here have no opportunity to go not only to India but even to the central region of Russia (Moscow, St. Petersburg), where the spiritual masters often come. We hope that in the future we can gather enough means to invite the senior Vaiṣṇavas and spiritual masters to our region. Śrīla Prabhupāda! On this auspicious day of your Vyāsa-pūjā, we bow down to your lotus feet and beg you to pour your mercy upon us and forgive us for our inability to glorify you properly.

The humble servants of your servants in Nijni Tagil, Russia.  
(written by Vigatarati dāsa)

## Nova Gokula

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Twenty years ago, your dear and confidential disciple Hridayānanda Mahārāja founded Nova Gokula, and for the last twenty years the many sincere devotees here have endeavored to maintain a nice *gurukula*, Deity worship, cow protection, *sādhu-saṅga,* and many other projects. They did this in order to please you. Nowadays it is so difficult to maintain these programs that devotees are starting to think that it will be impossible to succeed. But you said that the word “impossible” is in the fool’s dictionary. Therefore, today, we, the devotees of Nova Gokula, are simply asking you to bestow on us faith in and devotion to your lotus feet, because we think that only if we have these will the impossible become possible, and also in this way our foolishness will come to an end.

*Jaya* Śrīla Prabhupāda! Please engage us in satisfying your desire until the last breath of these bodies.

Your servants in Nova Gokula, ISKCON’s rural community in Pindamonhangaba, Brazil.  
(written by Guneśvara dāsa)

## Osaka

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, our spiritual grandfather,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

Fortunately I am under the guidance of your disciples. I couldn’t take your direct association, but mercifully you have given us your representatives all around the world.

By associating with the devotees of the Lord one can have firm faith in devotional service of the Lord. Then, by performing devotional service, one awakens his dormant love for Lord Kṛṣṇa and his material miseries come to an end.

Nothing is more important than devotees’ association. The other day, one of your *gṛhastha* couples visited Osaka. Japan is a small island surrounded by ocean, and we often feel isolated from the rest of the world of devotees. But Lord Kṛṣṇa mercifully arranged that we could have Vaiṣṇava association. The man and his wife both have jobs, but they are still are fixed in devotional service and were so merciful toward us. They told us that the atmosphere at the Osaka center was just like that of the first days of ISKCON, when Your Divine Grace were working very hard to establish Kṛṣṇa consciousness in the States. I have realized now that preaching is not only for *sannyāsīs: gṛhasthas* can also preach. Our Guru Mahārāja told us that you once said that if you could make even a single pure devotee, your preaching would be successful. Now there are pure devotees all around the world. A lot of devotees are working to fulfill your mission.

Our Guru Mahārāja’s desire is nondifferent from yours. Please kindly guide us so that we can also take part in fulfilling your mission.

Your aspiring servants at the ISKCON center in Osaka, Japan.  
(written by Satsvarūpa dāsa)

## Pandharpur

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our must humble obeisances at the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

We, the devotees of ISKCON Pandharpur, are eternally indebted and grateful to you for giving us such a wonderful place of work, worship, and preaching—Pandharpur. You have built a house in which the whole world can live, and ISKCON Pandharpur is a special place in that house. Pandharpur is considered a *dhāma,* nondifferent from Dvārakā, because it is a place of pastimes for the Lord and His expansions. We summarize the three most significant pastimes as follows:

**Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s Visit to Pandharpur**

Lord Gaurasundara’s visit to Śrī Pandharpur *dhāma* is explained in the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Madhya-līlā*, Ninth Chapter. Usually, during the course of His travels, Lord Gaurāṅga would stay in one place only one day or night. But He stayed in Pandharpur for at least eleven days. He bathed in the holy river Candrabhāgā and chanted and danced in ecstasy through the streets of Pandharpur with tears flowing down His cheeks, until He reached the temple of Lord Viṭhṭhala. Then Caitanya Mahāprabhu danced in *kīrtana* in front of the self-manifested Deity of Lord Viṭhṭhala. Lord Viṭhṭhala is so merciful that He allows the millions of pilgrims who come to see Him to place their head upon His feet and receive His mercy.

It is also significant that it was in Pandharpur that Lord Caitanya met with Śrī Raṅga Purī, a disciple of Mādhavendra Purī.

**The Samādhi of Śrī Viśvarūpa**

When Lord Caitanya was a young boy, His elder brother, Viśvarūpa, took *sannyāsa* and left home. Later, Lord Caitanya Himself took *sannyāsa* and set out traveling, partially with the purpose of finding His elder brother, now Śaṅkarāraṇya Svāmī. Lord Caitanya achieved this purpose in Pandharpur, where He learned that it was here that His brother had “achieved perfection”—i.e., entered into *samādhi* and left this world.

**Lord Nityānanda’s Initiation**

We learn the following pastime from the *Bhakti-ratnākara*.

During his travels, Nityānanda Prabhu visited Pandharpur and resided at a house of a *brāhmaṇa* who was a disciple of Śrīla Lakṣmīpati Tīrtha and thus a Godbrother of Śrīla Mādhavendra Purī. At that time Lakṣmīpati Tīrtha was traveling and also came to that *brāhmaṇa’s* home. He did not know that Nityānanda Prabhu was there. Lakṣmīpati Tīrtha asked the *brāhmaṇa,* “Is someone special here?” Then he went into a secluded room and spontaneously began chanting the glories of Lord Balarāma. He prayed, “I am the most fallen and sinful. Please pick me up by Your mercy.” Then he fell unconscious.

Lord Balarāma appeared to him in a dream. His complexion was the color of a spring cloud, His turban was blue, He eyes were like lotus petals, and he had soft limbs. Lord Balarāma said, “You are My devotee, and I am most pleased with you. A young *avadhūta* will soon come and ask you for initiation. You should initiate him with this mantra.” Lord Balarāma then whispered a mantra into his ear.

Lakṣmīpati Tīrtha woke up, and immediately Lord Nityānanda walked into the room. The Lord offered his obeisances to Lakṣmīpati Tīrtha and prayed, “Please initiate me. Please initiate me. Please initiate me.” Lakṣmīpati Tīrtha gave that mantra to Nityānanda. Then Nityānanda offered some *seva* to his Guru Mahārāja, and after some sweet words, He left.

Then Lakṣmīpati Tīrtha had another dream. Lord Nityānanda came to him and revealed that He is Balarāma. Lakṣmīpati Tīrtha asked, “Why have you cheated me? I didn’t know that You and Balarāma are one and the same.”

After Lord Balarāma had embraced him in the dream, Balarāma transformed into Lord Nityānanda, and again they embraced. Then Lakṣmīpati Tīrtha awoke from his dream. He was so intoxicated with transcendental bliss that his disciples couldn’t understand what had happened to their guru. Soon after this incident, he left this world.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we knew about the earlier pastimes of Lord Caitanya and Viśvarūpa, but since learning of the initiation pastime of Lord Nityānanda we are even more inspired to serve you here. Pandharpur is not just another holy place, but it is a place that has been blessed by these three great personalities, whose pastimes here have enhanced its greatness.

We hope to please you more by building a major ISKCON project here.

Hoping to attain the position of a speck of dust at the lotus feet of the servant of the servant of your servants,  
Your devotees at ISKCON Pandharpur Dhāma, India.  
(written by Ekalavya dāsa)

## Paris

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances at the dust of your divine lotus feet on this glorious day of your appearance in this world. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

This year again we gather to express our feelings of appreciation for your divine work, your accomplishments, your character, your mercy; for how you have created this movement single-handedly with the help of a few hippies, how you sheltered practically the whole world under the wings of ISKCON, how you have written the law books for the ten thousand years to come—and all this between the ages of 69 and 81. This is an astounding accomplishment, and it is indeed mystical.

One day Rasikānanda Prabhu, a Gauḍīya Vaiṣṇava scholar, a great preacher, and the most beloved disciple of Śrī Śyāmānanda Prabhu, was brushing his teeth while sitting on a big stone when someone informed him that a mystic yogi had just arrived riding a tiger. Immediately he went to meet that yogi riding the very stone where he was sitting. He exhibited mystic power for the sake of preaching, and the yogi became his disciple.

On a hot summer afternoon in a train in Hyderabad you quenched the thirst of thirty to forty devotees with just one coconut *dob;* since there was still some left, a young boy took the *dob* and ran away to enjoy it privately. One of your minor miracles, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Devotional service, *kṛṣṇa-prema,* actually begins after one transcends the platform of yoga *siddhis* or mystic powers, which for a Vaiṣṇava like yourself are no more than children’s games. But those who are fond of miracles can look at what you have accomplished. Has anyone ever performed the miracle of changing the world with Kṛṣṇa consciousness in such a small period of time?

A great magician came to Vṛndāvana once and performed mind-boggling tricks before you. You asked him, “Can you free people from birth and death?”

He answered, “No.”

And you replied, “So, I am a greater magician than you.”

There is no doubt, Śrīla Prabhupāda, that you can purify the three worlds because you have taken complete shelter of the supreme power, Śrī Kṛṣṇa.

Here in Paris our accomplishments seem insignificant, our victories quite modest. But our greatest success will be to fulfill your wishes, for in the words of Śrī Kṛṣṇa, “He who says he is My devotee is not My devotee; but he who says he is a devotee of My devotee is actually My devotee.”

Your humble servants at the ISKCON temple in Paris, France.  
(written by Rāja Dharma dāsa)

## Pecs

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our respectful obeisances. All glories to you!

Today in the afternoon there is a Bhakti Vṛkṣa program. I just looked at the data of all the members. I was surprised to see that there is retired brick-layer and several women working in a kindergarten, a business office, and a government office. Some of the members are in college, one of them is a dance teacher, etc.

Once I was at New Vraja Dhāma, our rural community here in Hungary, and a devotee said to me, “How nice it is that there are so many people who take part in our Bhakti Vṛkṣa programs. They help each other, they want to preach, they take responsibility to help ISKCON.” When he said that, I started to think that although they come from different backgrounds, because they were work together and learn Kṛṣṇa consciousness together, they became real friends. And if there is a problem in their Kṛṣṇa consciousness they help each other; they push each other to do more and more service. In this way they develop a real preaching mood, your preaching mood.

These Bhakti Vṛkṣa members are doing a great service for you, for ISKCON, and for Kṛṣṇa. When I see them working together, when I see their eagerness for doing service and learning the philosophy, when I see their enthusiasm while reading your purports to the *Bhagavad-gītā,* there is one sentence that comes to mind:

“He built a house in which the whole world can live.”

There is nobody but you who could engage so many different people—in fact, *everybody—*in the service of Kṛṣṇa. In the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* you write in a purport in the First Canto, Sixth Chapter, that one of the qualities of the living entity is that he cannot sit still even for a moment. These devotees were doing so many unwanted things, but by your inspiration they have given up most of their bad habits and started to chant, read, and preach. All of this is by your mercy.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for giving me the opportunity to be in the association of the devotees. Please give me your mercy so that I will never leave the association of the devotees and will always remain in their service.

Your servants at the ISKCON center in Pecs, Hungary.  
(written by Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa dāsa)

## Philadelphia

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

A true Godsend, you arrived unexpectedly and without fanfare from the land of Kṛṣṇa. Like a divine eagle magnificently descending amidst a flock of bickering birds, you came and heartened our empty lives with the steadfast strength of your devotion, and you even astonished the incredulous by your presence. You sang the mantra of transcendental love, taught us the holy names, and let us join the chorus. We danced to your rhythms, basked in your glory, and felt true joy.

Inspired by Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura’s order, you were the new voice proclaiming Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s message, expertly harmonizing realization and kindness so that we could understand and follow. You touched and transformed our souls as no one else could. And with the meticulous care of Rāghava Paṇḍita making His offerings to Kṛṣṇa, you caused millions of delicacies to be presented to Lord Caitanya, to the purifying delight of multitudes around the world.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, on your Vyāsa-pūjā day we ask to remain your followers. Please grant us the vision to see the true path of *bhakti* as shown by you and to not be led astray. Give us the compassion to help make ISKCON the society you and its members will continue to be proud of, an association that continues to be meaningful and welcoming to all sincere seekers of Kṛṣṇa. And kindly remain in our minds and hearts so that we may become more receptive to your instructions, the holy name, and the company of devotees.

Your disciples and granddisciples at the ISKCON temple in Philadelphia, Pennsylvania, USA.

## Portland

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

One day recently a book order arrived from the BBT. It was the biggest order yet for our *yātrā*—five hundred boxes of your books. We packed the boxes in the basement of our temple. Spontaneously one of the devotees started *kīrtana,* others joined in, some brought *mṛdaṅgas,* and soon all the devotees were jumping up and down around your books. As the *kīrtana* intensified, one of the devotees brought from his room a small *mūrti* of you and placed you near the biggest pile of boxes. The *kīrtana* literally started to rock the house. Devotees danced in bliss. We knew that you were there. All the devotees realized, “Śrīla Prabhupāda is here! And not just now, but he is always with us—on street *saṅkīrtana,* at the Sunday Feasts, and while we’re cooking or doing anything connected with ISKCON and the preaching mission of Lord Caitanya.”

We pray to always be engaged in preaching and helping as much as we can to strengthen and expand your ISKCON movement.

Your servants in Portland and other points in the Pacific Northwest of the USA.

## Porto Alegre

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble and respectful obeisances unto your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

Here in Porto Alegre, Brazil, we have just transported Lord Jagannātha, Lord Baladeva, Subhadrā Mahārāṇī and Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi to ISKCON’s land that we have named Prabhupāda Ashram.

On this glorious day of your Vyāsa-pūjā, we want to glorify you by offering you the service of expanding and making successful your Prabhupāda Ashram.

Due to your mercy in allowing us to constantly associate with you through your books, we feel enthusiasm to dedicate our time, love, and lives in the service of your mission: to propagate Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Thank you very much for everything.

Your servants at the Prabhupāda Ashram in Porto Alegre, Brazil.

## Poznan

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to you!

One can say that it is not difficult to serve Kṛṣṇa because He himself asks for very little: “If one offers Me with love and devotion a leaf, a flower, a fruit, or water, I will accept it.” However, when we try to understand more deeply what Kṛṣṇa is asking for in the *Bhagavad-gītā,* we come to realize that in fact He is asking for everything we can do for Him. That means everything according to our capacity and not just some limited quantity. Nowadays we see that there is a worldwide tendency to measure our activities according to various scales and in this way judge somebody or something as “in good standing.” Despite that, it would be wrong to think that we have done enough to serve you. Our activities are not spectacular, but we have a goal. Because of you we know that it doesn’t matter where one is at the moment; what is important is whether one is sincerely trying to become better.

It is time for preparation. Day by day we can appreciate more and more those simple things you gave us in *The Nectar of Instruction:* enthusiasm, confidence, patience... Simple truths are often very difficult to apply in daily life. That’s why we pray to Kṛṣṇa to give us enough time to come to the standard He considers “in good standing.” We don’t know what will happen in the next life. We hope that wherever we will be, our determination will direct us to the same transcendental goal.

Reorganization of our spiritual life was not easy. We wished we had passed it better. In this way Kṛṣṇa reminded us that without spiritual knowledge we will not survive. Learning and teaching emerged more important than we thought before.

Your humble servants in Poznan, Poland.  
(written by Maharddhi dāsa and Mahasvetā-devī dāsī)

## Rāḍhadesh

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

You have given us a treasure of inestimable value. That treasure is realized by sincere application of your divine instructions in the association of your devoted followers. Therefore this association is also of inestimable value.

In the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta,* Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu asked Rāmānanda Raya, “Of all kinds of distress, what is the most painful?” Rāmānanda replied, “Apart from separation from the devotees of Kṛṣṇa, I know of no unbearable unhappiness.”

You are nondifferent from Kṛṣṇa, Śrīla Prabhupāda. You are our lord. You love Lord Kṛṣṇa. You have an intimate relationship with Lord Kṛṣṇa and His dear devotees. You are giving us the treasure of His name, form, qualities, and pastimes, and, through your books, the association of those who, like your exalted self, are steeped in unalloyed devotion to Him.

You told us that our love for you would be shown by how we cooperate with each other to follow your instructions after your physical disappearance. Therefore if we want to develop unalloyed love for you, we cannot allow ourselves to be separated from your devoted followers.

Separation is not a question of physical distance. It is spirit being separated from spirit or heart being separated from heart. When the influence of the illusory energy comes between two spirit souls, crippling their capacity to unite in love for you and each other, this is the greatest pain. Only the influence of illusion makes such pain bearable. And when hearts unite in love for you and yours, no physical separation, pain, or any other condition can separate them.

We pray for the benediction to value every soul, simply because you have explained to us that every living entity is part and parcel of Lord Kṛṣṇa. We pray to properly cherish the special souls who are endeavoring to apply your sublime instructions in their lives. May we unite with them, heart to heart, in the appropriate loving exchanges that will enhance our Kṛṣṇa consciousness as per your desire. In this way we will give you pleasure, which is nondifferent from giving pleasure to Lord Kṛṣṇa. This is the perfection of life.

Your humble servants in Rāḍhadesh Dhāma, Belgium.

## Recife

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

On this auspicious day, let us offer our respectful obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

Although I am unable to do so, yet I dare to glorify you, taking the risk of committing a lot of offenses. You gave the world the hope of truth and love, turning the ghosts of this decaying society into angels and saints, forming an army of preachers, and empowering these servants of yours to distribute the medicine that will resuscitate this decadent so-called human civilization.

You have created many heroes, who are your expansions. These warriors are your representatives, and although some may tumble, victims of Māyā, we know that this is part of the battle and Your Divine Grace even foresaw it. You didn’t care about anything except Kṛṣṇa, and you planned everything so nicely that we wouldn’t make any mistake—if we just hold tight to your lotus feet.

Thank you very much, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for your being so direct. The world has certainly changed since the *Bhāgavatam* spoke of itself as “transcendental words directed toward bringing about a revolution in the impious lives of this world’s misdirected civilization,” but you knew what words were needed and put everything in your books. The flaws in our execution of your instructions are due to our ignorance, and whatever problems exist are there because we don’t study your instructions seriously. It is very easy to be happy if we take refuge at your lotus feet, for you are a manifestation of Nityānanda Prabhu, who came to turn the unfortunate ones into the fortunate.

Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, accept us at least one year more within your ISKCON, with its saintly devotees and your wonderful books.

Your humble servants at the ISKCON temple in Recife, Brazil.  
(written by Haricakra dāsa)

## Riga

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you “walked through fire” to establish ISKCON. The risks you took are legendary and beyond the call of duty. Leaving the transcendental shelter of the holy *dhāma,* you crossed the ocean, suffered heart attacks, and put yourself in the association of uncultured Westerners. You tolerated the struggle and loneliness of a stranger in New York, the toll of world travel at the age of seventy, the criticism of your Godbrothers, and the foolish mistakes of young disciples. No one took greater risks than you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, to save us by establishing this International Society for Kṛṣṇa Consciousness.

We, your granddisciples, are eternally indebted to you because you are the founder-*ācārya* and *śikṣā-guru* for all. Let us remember that only because you established ISKCON do we have an opportunity today to become Kṛṣṇa conscious and thus get free from the cycle of repeated birth and death. Let us always remain faithful to you by giving all our energy to the service of those who are strictly following your instructions and have remained in ISKCON.

Śrīla Prabhupāda *kī jaya*!

Your granddisciples at the ISKCON temple in Riga, Latvia.  
(written by Devakī-devī dāsī)

## Rovno

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

With all humility we offer you our obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

Some may think it strange that in this message we will address you as a person who is actually present with us. Especially nowadays, more and more devotees are speaking of you in the past tense. Some are absorbed in recollections of those times when you were on this mortal planet, and they are not interested at all in present things. Others say that when you departed you left no successor, so the only thing we can do now is consider you the only spiritual master forever. A third party attempts to prove that some of your precepts are mistaken. And a fourth party is trying to appropriate your real successes, as if you aren’t still present looking down on them.

As for us, we say that our spiritual experience, though perhaps tiny, gives us the right to assert that you are with us now and that your presence increases day by day. As it is said, “Those who possess eyes will see.” So, we can see Your Divine Grace present in your books and while *guru-pūjā* is being performed. Also, you are permanently present within the altar room and in your numerous instructions.

As ISKCON, your child, has gradually grown up, your everlasting love and real fatherly care for us have become more and more palpable. Whenever we think of *saṅkīrtana,* the “boiling-the-milk” process, the concept of plain living and high thinking, *varṇāśrama-dharma,* or the Māyāpur project, it’s you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, whom we then remember automatically. You are still craving for this world to change, for it to turn into Vaikuṇṭha, where there is no engagement other than serving the Supreme Person, Śrī Kṛṣṇa. That’s why you never get tired of supporting and leading us.

Though we never personally met you in our current life or directly heard the Vedic precepts from your mouth, you are very dear to us. The priceless gift a spiritual master gives is the truth about the Supreme Lord, who makes everyone happy, who possesses everything, who is the enjoyer of all, and who is the perennial friend and well-wisher of all living entities:

*bhoktāraṁ yajña-tapasāṁ sarva-loka-maheśvaram*  
*suhṛdaṁ sarva-bhūtānāṁ jñātvā māṁ śāntim ṛcchati*  
*(Bg 5.29)*

That’s why our main treasure, the books on Kṛṣṇa consciousness you so mercifully presented to us and to humanity at large, are truly priceless. Even if one reads a single word of your amazing books, some irrevocable change at once occurs within one’s heart, giving one the chance to attain the real destination of life.

Fifty centuries ago three divine revelations occurred, by which the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* the ripened fruit of the tree of Vedic knowledge, became sweeter and sweeter. The first occurred when Śrīla Vyāsadeva narrated *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* to his son Śukadeva Gosvāmī in Badarikāśrama. The second revelation was when Śukadeva Gosvāmī related the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* to his disciple Mahārāja Parīkṣit before the audience of thousands of sages. The third revelation occurred in Naimiṣāraṇya forest, where numerous sages, headed by Śaunaka Ṛṣi, received this very same knowledge from Śrīla Sūta Gosvāmī. After these three revelations of the *Bhāgavatam,* it has been handed down via the chain of disciplic succession. And the most recent narration of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* belongs to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, who translated the text into English, commented on it in a perfect way, and then made this work available to all the suffering people in this demoniac world.

Your uncompromising way of preaching the message of the *Bhāgavatam,* as well as your entire life, proves that complete devotion to the lotus feet of Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa is really needed. By your mercy, every one of your sincere followers tries to follow the path you have chalked out, going from *sambandha* (the process of establishing one’s relationship with the Lord), to *abhidheya* (developing one’s relationship with Kṛṣṇa by practicing devotional service), and finally to *prayojana,* the final destination of life—love of God.

You lovingly lead your real followers along this path of *sambandha, abhidheya,* and *prayojana*, Śrīla Prabhupāda. While only a few may be able to complete the course during the present lifetime, even if we take only a few first steps on this path, following in your footsteps, we will gain such a great good that it is impossible for us to realize now. You often said any person, even the lowest of mankind, would be able to elevate himself to the Vaiṣṇava platform by associating with a real Vaiṣṇava. Therefore it is only because you are ever with us that we can achieve all hope, mercy, and protection.

*mūkaṁ karoti vācālaṁ paṅguṁ laṅghayate girim*  
*yat-kṛpā tam ahaṁ vande śrī-guruṁ dīna-tāraṇam*

**“I offer my respectful obeisances unto my spiritual master, the deliver of the fallen. By his mercy, the dumb can become eloquent speakers and the lame can cross mountains.”**

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, kindly accept our affection and gratitude and bless us so that we can gain the taste for rendering devotional service unto you, your true disciples, and Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa.

*Your insignificant, menial servants at the ISKCON temple in Rovno, Ukraine.*

## St. Louis

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Thank you for your *vāṇī*. Whenever I’m low, I get uplifted by hearing from your books. That is your mercy—giving so many people the chance to gain spiritual benefit. One can’t help but smile while bathing in the soothing rays of your transcendental words.

Here in St. Louis we’re struggling—with the *anarthas* both within and without. Books and *prasādam* are being distributed. Wherever and whenever our desires conflict with yours, please give us the strength to surrender to you. We pray that we may come to the point where there is no more conflict between our desires and yours.

*vihāya kāmān yaḥ sarvān pumāṁś carati niḥspṛhaḥ*  
*nirmamo nirahaṅkāraḥ sa śāntim adhigacchati*

**“A person who has given up all desires for sense gratification, who lives free from desires, who has given up all sense of proprietorship and is devoid of false ego—he alone can attain real peace.” (*****Bhagavad-gītā*** **2.71).**

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for giving us the chance to achieve real peace.

You have taught us our eternal dharma, Śrīla Prabhupāda. As I just heard you say in one of your classes,

Similarly, if you take sugar and if you find it salty. Then you... “Oh, what is this!?” So everything has got some characteristic. So we are living entities. We have got our characteristics. That is *sanātana*. I am *sanātana,* eternal, and my characteristic is to serve God.”

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Your servants in St. Louis, Missouri, USA.

## Saltillo

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,
