Let us always remember these instructions of yours, in *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 4.28.48:

\[TRANSLATION:\] O best of kings, please get up! Get up! Just see this world surrounded by water and infested with rogues and so-called kings. This world is very much afraid, and it is your duty to protect her.

\[PURPORT:\] Whenever an *ācārya* comes, following the superior orders of the Supreme Personality of Godhead or His representative, he establishes the principles of religion, as enunciated in *Bhagavad-gītā.* Religion means abiding by the orders of the Supreme personality of Godhead. Religious principles begin from the time one surrenders to the Supreme Personality of Godhead. It is the *ācārya’s* duty to spread a bona fide religious system and induce everyone to bow down before the Supreme Lord. One executes the religious principles by rendering devotional service, specifically the nine items like hearing, chanting and remembering. Unfortunately, when the *ācārya* disappears, rogues and nondevotees take advantage and immediately begin to introduce unauthorized principles in the name of so-called *svāmīs, yogīs,* philanthropists, welfare workers and so on. Actually, human life is meant for executing the orders of the Supreme Lord, and this is stated in *Bhagavad-gītā* (9.34):

*man-manā bhava mad-bhakto mad-yājī māṁ namaskuru*  
*mām evaiṣyasi yuktvaivam ātmānaṁ mat-parāyaṇaḥ*

**“Engage your mind always in thinking of Me and become My devotee. Offer obeisances and worship Me. Being completely absorbed in Me, surely you will come to Me.”**

The main business of human society is to think of the Supreme Personality of Godhead at all times, to become His devotees, to worship the Supreme Lord and to bow down before Him. The *ācārya,* the authorized representative of the Supreme Lord, establishes these principles, but when he disappears, things once again become disordered. The perfect disciples of the *ācārya* try to relieve the situation by sincerely following the instructions of the spiritual master. At the present moment practically the entire world is afraid of rogues and nondevotees; therefore this Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement is started to save the world from irreligious principles. Everyone should cooperate with this movement in order to bring about actual peace and happiness in the world.

And let us also not forget these instructions of yours, given in *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 4.18.5:

\[TRANSLATION:\] A foolish person who manufactures his own ways and means through mental speculation and does not recognize the authority of the sages who lay down unimpeachable directions is simply unsuccessful again and again in his attempts.

\[PURPORT:\] At the present moment it has become fashionable to disobey the unimpeachable directions given by the *ācāryas* and liberated souls of the past. Presently people are so fallen that they cannot distinguish between a liberated soul and a conditioned soul. A conditioned soul is hampered by four defects: he is sure to commit mistakes, he is sure to become illusioned, he has a tendency to cheat others, and his senses are imperfect. Consequently we have to take direction from liberated persons. This Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement directly receives instructions from the Supreme personality of Godhead via persons who are strictly following His instructions. Although a follower may not be a liberated person, if he follows the supreme, liberated Personality of Godhead, his actions are naturally liberated from the contamination of the material nature. Lord Caitanya therefore says: “By My order you may become a spiritual master.” One can immediately become a spiritual master by having full faith in the transcendental words of the Supreme Personality of Godhead and by following His instructions. Materialistic men are not interested in taking directions from a liberated person, but they are very much interested in their own concocted ideas, which make them repeatedly fail in their attempts. Because the entire world is now following the imperfect directions of conditioned souls, humanity is completely bewildered.

Also, it is so sinful to be irresponsible with one's dependents. Therefore, please give all your mercy to our leaders, the GBC, so that they may develop more and more capacity. And please let many, many souls become committed to the lotus feet of Śrī-Śrī Rādhā-Govinda.

Your servants in Saltillo, Mexico.

## Salvador

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humbles obeisances. All glories to Śrī Guru and Śrī Gaurāṅga!!

We (the temple devotees and the congregation) are working together to push on your movement in our city. In particular, we are trying to increase the number of devotees. We hope they will serve you full- or part-time.

To accomplish this task, we are

•We are performing all regular spiritual programs in the temple, preaching in the schools and universities, etc.

•We are trying to distribute books responsibly.

•We have Food for Life programs every week in churches, hospitals, schools, public squares, etc.

•We’re putting on festivals every Sunday, with *bhajans, kīrtana,* lecture, and *prasādam*. Sometimes we include theater, video, or slide presentations.

•We have three Bhakti Vṛkṣa groups, which meet weekly.

•We have two hundred devotees in the congregation, and thirty are Life Members.

We pray for your blessings in our fight against *māyā*.

Thank you very much, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Your servants in Salvador, Brazil.

## Sao Paulo

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our respectful obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

As the times goes on, we can see more clearly the effects of your divine presence in this world. It seems that people’s hearts have become softer. Sometimes we can perceive that even the gross materialists are making spiritual advancement. The New Age mentality, the growing vegetarian population, the ecumenical movements that are inducing people who had been killing themselves to join spiritual groups—all this reminds us that your divine presence has purified this entire planet.

When you said that in the next ten thousand years Kṛṣṇa consciousness would become prominent in this world, you were simply seeing what nobody else could see, just as you did back in 1966, when you described to a gentleman on a park bench the big spiritual movement you were leading—even though nothing had manifested yet.

So many divisions have occurred in your movement since your departure. So many devotees have quit ISKCON. Your sons and daughters are scattered in so many places, sometimes fighting among themselves. But we cherish the hope that by your grace one day everything will be all right again, for if you are changing even the hearts of materialistic persons, why will the hearts of your followers not change?

Vaiṣṇavism is your gift to the whole world. *Prema* is the supreme nectar you made available by teaching us the chanting of the holy names and merging our souls in the preaching of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. In this auspicious environment, this planet surely will come to the point an astrologer predicted concerning you—the house you built would be one where the entire world could live.

Your humble servants in the ISKCON temple in Sao Paulo, Brazil.  
(written by Advaya dāsa)

## Scotland

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our respectful obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace on this holy day of your appearance within this world for the benefit of all fallen souls.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, our disciplic succession is certainly full of many wonderful personalities, the Lord’s direct associates who deliver transcendental knowledge for the upliftment of all mankind. If we look over the members of the *paramparā—*their history, their background, their *līlā,* their contribution to the popularization of the science of Kṛṣṇa consciousness—your own name stands supreme. Of the feats performed by the many great *ācāryas* and saints, your endeavors, Śrīla Prabhupāda, have manifested the most amazing results. It was you who fulfilled their desire to see Lord Caitanya’s mission spread throughout the world. For generations to come, your mission should be remembered and glorified as that which fulfilled the desires of the previous *ācāryas*.

Please help us realize the greatness and importance of your advent and of your special contribution to a very fallen mankind. Please help us recognize the extent of your compassion and how urgent it is that we become competent instruments in your expert hands. Lord Caitanya’s magnanimity is embodied in your personality; His mission is yours, and His desires are being fulfilled by you and by all who follow strictly your sublime formula, embodied in the four dictums: Preaching is the essence; Purity is the force; Utility is the principle; Your books are the basis.

You gave us our contact with the previous *ācāryas* and revived our dormant relationship with the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Lord Kṛṣṇa. All over the planet, Kṛṣṇa conscious centers are providing all the necessary shelter for the conditioned souls, giving them facility to become purified and enlivened by the process of *śravaṇaṁ kīrtanaṁ viṣṇoḥ*. This is possible only due to Your Divine Grace—“Divine Grace” in both senses: your divine personality and the loving grace you readily bestow on all, regardless of race, creed, color, or caste. From the humble beginnings of ISKCON to the manifestation of your worldwide mission, you have certainly been the pride of Lord Gaura and all His associates, who so desire that His mercy spread to one and all.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, please help us in our endeavors to be part of this great heritage by preaching the Lord’s glories in our designated place. For what we have achieved so far, we are extremely grateful, but for the future we want to increase more and more the popularity of this Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement so that as many others as possible can also take advantage of this mission and gain unlimited fortune. We have many plans. Please look kindly upon us so that they may manifest and bring pleasure to Your Divine Grace, the *paramparā,* and Lord Caitanya. May we become as dedicated as you would like us to be and become instrumental in bringing others to the shelter of the lotus feet of the entire *paramparā*.

All glories to Your Divine Grace Śrīla Prabhupāda! All glories to the distribution of your transcendental books and the Lord’s holy name!

Your insignificant servants in Scotland.

## Seattle

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

**Śrīla Prabhupāda Is His Name**

Who came from the East  
and put our minds at ease,  
to dance and chant and feast  
and work like bumblebees?

Who was it who never did cheat,  
whom no one could defeat,  
who had the lotus feet  
that could shade us from all heat?

Who had a golden complexion  
and eyes that glowed with the perfection  
of life’s supreme blessing,  
beyond all contesting?

Who when he spoke gave us hope;  
all problems we could cope  
with the power of his scope  
in every book he wrote?

Who walked with a cane,  
always chanting Kṛṣṇa’s names,  
and everywhere he saw with *prema*  
our Lord, life’s only aim?

Who was beyond belief,  
and yet all could see  
he was beyond all grief  
and this world of misery?

Who had deepest compassion,  
which guided every action,  
and answered every asking  
to our satisfaction?

Who could look into our soul  
and make us all know  
all he did know,  
and with no doubt could show?

Who could look into our hearts  
and awaken our spiritual spark,  
and make us play our part  
with his inconceivable art?

Who never fell asleep  
into our illusion deep,  
and all that he could speak  
was wisdom we could keep?

Who was personified scripture,  
the external manifestation of Kṛṣṇa,  
and always painted the perfect picture  
that put us all in bliss here?

Who when he raised his hands  
all fell under his command,  
to enter the blissful dance  
of love of Godhead’s trance?

Who when he chanted  
our wishes were granted,  
completely enchanted  
by his loving attraction?

Who had the grace,  
so easy to embrace,  
and purified every place  
by his countenance chaste?

Who had the glance  
to melt hearts in the trance  
of life’s supreme romance,  
of life’s supreme dance?

These memories come to me—  
I must share them with thee!—  
of the greatest company  
of Kṛṣṇa’s pure devotee.

Who was not blind,  
who could read every mind,  
yet always humble and kind  
and blissful all the time?

Who could be like thunder  
if one of us would blunder,  
so we didn’t have to wonder  
about the power we were under?

Who could be soft like a rose  
and be closer than close,  
whose life was the perfect pose  
for the guidance of all souls?

Who had a heart so tender  
he caused us to surrender,  
knowing he was our only friend here,  
whose love would never end here?

Who finally opened our eyes  
with all-relieving sighs  
to see our Lord all-wise,  
who answered all our cries?

Who could not tolerate sin  
and came to only make us win  
and leave this world where all looks dim  
so in love’s ocean we could swim?

Who gave the greatest proof  
of the Supreme Absolute Truth  
at every single moment  
and in every perfect move?

Who did we know to be divine,  
beyond all space and time,  
who came to stop all crime  
with the Lord’s name and chime?

Nectar most sweet  
we knew we did greet  
when we fell at his feet,  
eager to hear him speak!

Who made rascals flee  
and the humble dance in glee,  
who set us all free  
when chanting the names of Hari?

Śrīla Prabhupāda is his name,  
and Śrīla Prabhupāda is the flame  
that keeps us moving toward life’s aim,  
pure and perfect *kṛṣṇa-prema*.

He was and is the personified proof  
that Kṛṣṇa, God, exists in truth,  
with a proof none could ever disprove,  
the truth that makes my very pen move!

Move on, O wayward soul,  
away from that black hole,  
where nothing you could know,  
and nowhere you could go.

Move on, O aimless spirit,  
to the Truth—he made me hear it;  
move beyond this dying fold,  
born to die, get sick, and old.

Just move to Kṛṣṇa by chanting His names;  
just follow Prabhupāda and he’ll explain  
with the supreme mercy, mercy rain  
of his words, which Kṛṣṇa has ordained.

His name is Prabhupāda;  
that’s all you have to know  
if you want to know God,  
and there you want to go.

There’s no need to look elsewhere;  
he did not disappear.  
Just try to obey him  
and you’ll know he’s always near.

Near as Kṛṣṇa, right in your heart,  
as caring as Kṛṣṇa, never to depart,  
near as Kṛṣṇa, always in His names,  
is Śrīla Prabhupāda, beyond doubt and pain.

This body we need not,  
we are eternal servants of God;  
know this of Prabhupāda:  
he cannot fail his job!

He shall guide those in God’s light  
if they obey him and do right,  
and all will be in sight,  
with no need to guess or fight!

Prabhupāda sees everything clear,  
and there’s nothing he can’t hear;  
Prabhupāda just can’t disappear  
because Kṛṣṇa’s placed him here.

Prabhupāda’s path is simple  
for those simple like children,  
but the proud shall stay bewildered  
and forever remain crippled.

“What’s the difficulty?” Prabhupāda said.  
The difficulty is we’ve all gone mad;  
so just chant Hare Kṛṣṇa and your life will be sublime,  
and all becomes easy when we chant all the time!

harer nama harer nāma harer nāma harer nāmaiva kevalam  
kalau nāsty eva nāsty eva nāsty eva gatir anyathā

“All glories to Prabhupāda!”  
now kindly shout,  
because he’s the only one  
who can help this whole world out!

Thus this Anubhāvānanda dāsa,  
although sorely unqualified,  
is praying to Prabhupāda to stay on his side  
so he may always hear him guide...  
Closer than our jugular vein  
he said he would abide  
when we adhere to his orders;  
or lost, misled, we glide.

All glories to Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
friend of the most fallen!

Hare Kṛṣṇa!!

Śrīla Prabhupāda’s servants at the ISKCON temple in Seattle, Washington, USA.

## Sydney

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

It was in the last century, the 96th year,  
the first of September, that you did appear.  
And an omen sure it was, as we heard you later say,  
how the Ṭhākur’s holy book was to herald that day.

Kṛṣṇa’s own abode was the place from which you came,  
preaching His glories and spreading His fame.  
In this sad world, so dark all about,  
you brought the real light, destroying all our doubt.

Then upon the path you lay our weary feet,  
toward home, back to Godhead, the transcendental seat.  
Sure and safe it was, you made the *bhakti* way,  
the goal achieved for sure, so long we didn’t stray.

Yet, despite all your struggle, your sacrifice and work  
in delivering this gift and teaching all it’s worth,  
to our dying regret and our mortal shame,  
we have not really taken to the Lord’s holy name.

When we first came to you, now so long ago,  
nothing seemed so difficult, what was there to know?  
Transcending illusion? Of our worries much the least  
when all we had to do was to chant, dance, and feast!

Of course, enthusiasm’s cheap when there’s not so much to lose,  
but as time goes on, it seems, even harder now to choose.  
And as our carefree youth does enter middle age,  
it’s then we start to balk at living like a sage.

Now it’s nearly autumn in a long and wasted life,  
nothing much to show for all our trouble and our strife.  
Of all our foolish dreams and all our naive hopes,  
the only thing remaining is the echo of the notes.

And when the desperate days of the end set in,  
it’s then we start to reap the wages of our sin,  
And it’s now that we discover how we’re really all alone,  
when it comes to face the future and the great unknown.

Then by the grace of a merciful spark,  
like Gajendra there ignites a prayer within our heart.  
And on whom do we call, to whom do we plea,  
to save us from our suffering and set our souls free?

To our dear Prabhupāda we cry out in our mind,  
in a desperate desire that your shelter we may find.  
Forgive us our offenses and our foolish neglect;  
we surrender to you now in our every respect.

Now again there swells the great good hope  
that all will be well if we take hold the rope  
of your memories so sweet, your merciful glance,  
and of your loving kindness, our only real chance.

If we studied all the *Vedas* till there’s nothing more to know,  
and if we wandered all the *dhāms* till our beards did grow,  
if we chanted all the day and on Ekādaśīs through,  
and if we sat in the Gaṅgā till our face turned blue,  
if we listened to the *sādhus,* what all they had to say,  
and if we offered up a *yajña* every other day,  
if we went and lived in India for years and years and years,  
and if we so-called got the mercy till it came out our ears,  
and if we heard the *rasa-śāstras*—well we just wouldn’t dare,  
and if there’s something else available, well we just don’t care.

So, what, may you say, is the secret that we share,  
that of all the precious treasures there be nothing quite so rare?  
It’s the mercy of our guru of which we boldly speak,  
save for which, the *śāstras* all confirm, there’s nothing more to seek.  
And though so many great souls have all done their bit,  
it’s at the feet of Prabhupāda that all the others sit.  
So if we want to get to Kṛṣṇa and never more fret,  
then listen to the answer, the best we’ll ever get:  
We must surrender unto Prabhupāda, then Kṛṣṇa we shall meet,  
because that’s the place the Lord resides: those two lotus feet!

Prabhupāda, you came and brought so very much,  
but the really special gift was your personal touch.  
Though blessed we may be, in every single case  
the greatest gift of all was your divine grace.

To cherish that treasure is the ultimate test  
of a purified heart and a sober intellect.  
And if anyone claims they be followers of you,  
then let them demonstrate it with a loyalty true.

Upon this refrain let all of us unite,  
giving up our quarrel, nothing more to fight.  
It’s as sons of the father that triumphant we may stand,  
when we’re linked altogether and holding on to your hand.

And if within ourselves it’s conflict there may be,  
and in our world of ISKCON we all cannot agree,  
when all’s said and done, it just comes down to this:  
without the help of Prabhupāda, then everything’s amiss.

And as far as Kṛṣṇa goes, if we think that we’re in love  
and imbued with all the sentiments to meet with Him above,  
well, no matter who we are and no matter what we do,  
without pleasing Prabhupāda, we can whistle for that too.

So let us on this blessed day surrender to his grace,  
and let his sacred lotus feet our hearts and minds embrace;  
then perfection we will see, just what it means to be  
a servant of his servant, a leaf upon his tree.

Your servants at ISKCON centers in Sydney, New Gokula, and Newcastle.

## Tampa

Beloved Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
All glories to you! Unlimited *daṇḍavats* at your lotus feet!

Your appearance was  
So fine and pure,  
For diseased America  
You injected the cure.

Every day, for us,  
You gave a class,  
Revealing the spiritual sky—  
We were transported fast.

“You are eternal souls  
In a complex mess.  
Serve Lord Kṛṣṇa  
And you will be blessed.”

Prabhupāda, the love in your  
Heart is so great.  
Your goal is to give  
Everyone a sublime fate.

“Chant Hare Kṛṣṇa,  
Distribute *prasādam* to all.  
This will stop Kali’s Age  
And make Māyā fall.”

Devotion from 4 to 10,  
A torch we must carry.  
“*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* has all knowledge;  
Other books—not necessary.”

“Chant sixteen rounds  
And avoid four sins.”  
You lived the example,  
All struggles were wins.

Which car do you like?  
“A Mercedes only.”  
Without your answers  
We’re feeling lonely.  
Knowledge poured forth  
From your lotus lips  
To stop us from  
Our LSD trips.

Prabhupāda, your words  
Are like the flowing Gaṅgā.  
You said distribute the names  
With *kartāls* and *mṛdaṅga.*

Your teaching is so  
Bold and strong,  
Your opponents would quiver,  
Being rascals in the wrong.

Surrender to you  
Was the end solution.  
We had to bow down,  
Drafted by Caitanya’s revolution.

To Bhaktisiddhānta Swami  
So faithful and dedicated.  
We also want to  
Be so highly rated.

How can we please you?  
What can we do?  
“Preach vigorously like me,  
form a *saṅkīrtan* crew.

“Publish books and  
Distribute far and wide.  
This is more important  
Than the construction site.”

If we preach, giving  
Knowledge to everyone,  
Surely you will see us  
As disciples, sons and nuns.

Three results show  
Our preaching is right:  
Books sold, new devotees,  
And land attract your sight.

Gurus are judged by  
Devotees they created.  
Prabhupāda, you did the most;  
That can’t be debated.

We pray that we will never forget you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Bless us with the special *śakti* to carry out your divine mission.

Your servants in Tampa, Florida, USA.  
(written by Vivasvān dāsa)

## Tauranga

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at your lotus feet on this most auspicious day of the anniversary of your appearance. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

When you were staying at the Rādhā-Dāmodara temple from 1959 to 1965, you would often gaze upon Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī’s *samādhi,* and it was there in those early days that the plans for the International Society for Krishna Consciousness were formulated.

Inspired with the grace of Śrī Rūpa Gosvāmī and your beloved Guru Mahārāja, accompanied by your stalwart desire and devotion to execute their will, you carried the full embodiment of their grace in the form of the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta,* the *Bhakti-rasāmṛta-sindhu,* and of course the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* to foreign shores and planted that transcendental seed, which is ever expanding.

In your purport to *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 3.28.18, you write,

As one becomes purified by chanting the holy name of the Lord, so one can become purified simply by chanting the name of a holy devotee. The pure devotee of the Lord and the Lord Himself are nondifferent. It is sometimes feasible to chant the name of a holy devotee. This is a very sanctified process.

By your mercy you have made it very attractive for us to chant “*Jaya* Prabhupāda! *Jaya* Prabhupāda!”, and by doing this we have become purified and attached to your ISKCON Society.

Theological historians have recorded that most religious institutions endured many hardships in establishing themselves after the demise of their *ācārya,* or teacher. Similarly, after your disappearance many issues became difficult for us to comprehend and implement correctly, which created formidable stumbling blocks to surmount.

To point out the folly of material consciousness based on the mistake that “I am this body,” you would often cite the analogy that if a complicated mathematical equation begins with a minor miscalculation, then all other calculations based on that premise would also be incorrect. So, due to our immaturity and impurity, our mistakes compounded into many situations that created negativity and general malaise within the Society—sadly the toll was paid.

On the positive side, though, through the grace of Śrī Guru and Gaurāṅga it seems that now we have become more aware and willing to address our shortcomings and work on creating an ISKCON tat is more aligned with your intent and instructions. To continue working and desiring in the right direction, though, we must constantly pray for your continued mercy. In this way we will individually and collectively become more able to your follows your words and desires, to push on the preaching mission with steady enthusiasm, to develop loving relationships with others, and to become more attached to the processes you delineated for our purification.

As you received divine inspiration and meditated deeply on such great personalities as Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī Prabhupāda and Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Prabhupāda and felt them always present, please help us remain inspired to continue struggling to advance on the path of pure devotional service. And let us take full shelter of your transcendental wisdom and knowledge in our attempt to become the pure devotees you wanted us to be, and in our endeavor to create a spiritual society that caters to the spiritual well-being of every living entity.

Eternally indebted,  
Your servants at the ISKCON community in Tauranga, the Western Bay of Plenty, New Zealand.  
(written by Kṛṣṇa Premavatī-devī dāsī)

## Tel Aviv

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

Every year we find ourselves in the same situation, trying to find some appropriate words to describe your unlimited glories. But the attempt is futile. The only thing we can think of is a small comparison between your great achievements and our shortcomings.

The first and most admirable of your qualities is humility. You are the founder-*ācārya* of the International Society for Krishna Consciousness. All over the world people know about the “Hare Krishnas,” but most of them have never heard about you. The reason is that you always put Kṛṣṇa in front of yourself. As for ourselves, whenever we try to be humble, we end up thinking how wonderfully humble we are.

You’ve carried the instructions of your Guru Mahārāja throughout your whole life, meditating on how to fulfill his order. You’ve molded your life according to this order. On the other hand, we may often say to our spiritual masters, “Dear Gurudeva, I’ll do whatever you want me to,” but we try to suit his instructions to our own desires, thus serving ourselves instead of the Guru.

Your faith in Guru and Kṛṣṇa prevailed through all circumstances. Through your marriage, through the failure of the Gauḍīya Maṭha as a worldwide preaching institution, you remained fixed on your desire to serve your Guru. As Śrīla Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura said, “The world may come to an end, but I’ll never leave the instructions of my spiritual master.” This was your firm conviction, Śrīla Prabhupāda. As for us, what can be said? The tiny little spark of faith we have becomes weaker and weaker with every new crisis ISKCON undergoes. But the only thing we know is that our only shelter is your lotus feet and your ISKCON, which may not be perfect as a society but which is still our only way to that place where every step is a dance and every word is a song—our eternal home.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, please let us always remain in ISKCON and never go away.

Your servants in Tel Aviv, Israel.  
(written by Jugala-prīti-devī dāsī)

## Tokyo

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

Our intelligence, being quite shallow, cannot penetrate into the greatness of Your Divine Grace. You have given us the most wonderful gift of Kṛṣṇa consciousness and freedom from the miseries of the material world.

Being bound by our many shortcomings, we can only observe your activities from a distance and thus are unable to properly appreciate you. But actually, to appreciate your activities is not so difficult: We can simply look around and see so many Vaiṣṇavas chanting Hare Kṛṣṇa. You are the real miracle man.

We can say something superficial in glorification of you, but not from the natural spontaneous feelings of the soul. Maybe when we meet again and you touch our heads with your lotus hand—then the words will flow naturally like the Ganges River.

How can we measure your greatness? With our material measuring devices, what can we measure anyway? Just by a moment of your association we can attain all success.

We regret that we lack the necessary purity to fully comprehend what you have planted in our hearts. But as the creeper of devotional service begins to mature, a new life unfolds, a life free from the material miseries, a life with Kṛṣṇa and his eternal associates in His eternal abode.

Due to our offenses to Kṛṣṇa, to Kṛṣṇa’s holy name, to Kṛṣṇa’s devotees, and to other living entities, our vision is obscured, and we cannot fully see the light of your effulgent personality. But as our offenses diminish our vision will become clear, and our joy will be boundless as we watch you dance in Lord Caitanya’s *saṅkīrtana* party.

Our impurity thus disqualifies us from entrance into the confidential regions of your heart. Making a show—only licking the bottle, not tasting the essence—is our business. But by your causeless mercy, that is all changing, and one day we hope we can also join you in the *saṅkīrtana* party.

We make a sentimental show of so-called compassion, but only for our own prestige, to enhance our flimsy material fame and distinction in this world. But real Kṛṣṇa consciousness is the awareness that “Kṛṣṇa is master, I am servant.” This is Kṛṣṇa consciousness, and this is what you taught us. Thus your compassion is not mundane, but is on the basis of the pure spiritual soul.

Ours is a pitiable plight. Still, you are able to penetrate our deception and situate us in our correct position. You inspire us to preach Lord Caitanya’s *saṅkīrtana* movement—“Go village to village, town to town. This is Lord Caitanya’s prediction.” The world is in chaos. “Unless they take to Kṛṣṇa consciousness, there is no hope. That’s a fact. There will be more chaotic conditions, and everyone will suffer and perish.”

Śrīla Prabhupāda *kī jaya*!

Your servants in Tokyo, Japan.  
(written by Jagad Vīra dāsa)

## Tsudanuma

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

It is our pleasant duty to celebrate the auspicious event of Your Divine Grace’s appearance day. As your followers and admirers, we would like to submit ourselves at the shelter of Your Divine Grace.

It is well known that the Japanese lifestyle makes Japanese people unconcerned about spirituality and even one another, but, as we hear from senior devotees, this was not the case when you appeared in Tokyo. Due to having some aspects of Vedic culture remaining here and there, and more accurately due to having some piety in their backgrounds, some Japanese people were able to recognize you as a real shelter, or, as they say here, *obosan,* and in this way a few were blessed. And as for the rest of the 120 million Japanese people who could not see you, serve you, and take spiritual instructions from you personally, we, your followers, are trying our best to spread the glories of Your Divine Grace, the perfect embodiment of pure and unadulterated devotional service. We are always astonished to hear about your pastimes of spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness, and we firmly trust that the success you achieved is due to your being a pure devotee of Kṛṣṇa, devoid of any desires for personal gain, prestige, or recognition. We take great inspiration from your personal example.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, because we trust you as our eternal guide, we would like to beg you for your participation and presence in our preaching efforts here in Japan. Please help us. Especially now, when we have the great opportunity of having a nice temple and even more plans of opening a restaurant, we beg you for your guidance so that we can avoid mistakes.

Although you wanted to come to Japan even before coming to the USA, and although you wrote the book *Light of the Bhāgavata* especially to preach the glories of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* in Japan, Kṛṣṇa had a different plan with you, sending you first to the USA. Our *yātrā* should reciprocate with your kindness toward the Japanese people, but we feel very bad because out of so many people living on this island, only a few have become devotees.

Now the whole world, and especially the Japanese people, like to imitate the USA, so we hope that the Japanese will take to Kṛṣṇa consciousness with the same eagerness as the Americans.

All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Your servants in Tsudanuma, Chiba, Japan.  
(written by Maṅgala dāsa)

## Tucson

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

In 1965 you sailed to the West.  
Other swamis tried, but you were the best.  
In ’66, many came forward,  
giving up everything to follow your order.

Health was weak, but no longer alone,  
so in ’67 you returned home.  
You sent your disciples to London in ’68  
with the *mahā-mantra;* what a sensation did they create!

In ’69 we saw our first farm,  
churning butter for Kṛṣṇa, milking cows in the barn.  
By 1970 you circled the earth;  
in every town and village, your movement took birth.

The communist regime was no match for you;  
your impact in ’71 no one else could do.  
In ’72 your main Indian temples took root,  
and the whole of Bharat-bhūmi soon followed suit.

After a hard struggle, victory was sweet:  
in ’73 Mr. N. was finally beat.  
Then in ’74 the BBT party was created;  
such exposure for your books was unabated.

’75 saw book distribution soar,  
and all devotees strived to have a big score.  
Lord Jagannāth rode down 5th Avenue in ’76,  
as all of New York City looked on transfixed.

Then in ’77 our worst fears were met:  
You left us before we could repay our debt.  
So since that time till ’99  
I am praying to please you, but still far behind.

You’ve given your life, so let me give mine,  
be finished with *māyā* and give all my time.  
You gave us the formula for perfect success:  
Follow your instructions and spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

Your servants in Tucson, Arizona, USA.  
(written by Sandāmini-devī dāsī)

## Turkey

*āpane ācare keha, nā kare pracāra*  
*pracāra karena keha, nā karena ācāra*

*‘ācāra’, ‘pracāra’,—nāmera karaha ‘dui’ kārya*  
*tumi—sarva-guru, tumi jagatera ārya*

**“Some behave very well but do not preach the cult of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, whereas others preach but do not behave properly. You simultaneously perform both duties in relation to the holy name by your personal behavior and by your preaching. Therefore you are the spiritual master of the entire world, for you are the most advanced devotee in the world.” (*****Caitanya-caritāmṛta Antya*** **4.102–103)**

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances prostrated in the dust of your lotus feet. Your lotus feet offer all benedictions and complete shelter to all who sincerely approach and serve them, and they are the source of all benedictions within this world. You perfectly exemplify the above statement of Sanātana Gosvāmī. Your Divine Grace’s personal behavior and preaching capacity are flawless and superb. You are the ideal spiritual master, and therefore you are the actual spiritual master of the whole world. Everyone can benefit simply by surrendering unto your lotus feet and following your divine instructions, and both your lotus feet and your instructions are self-effulgent and illuminating. Personally I have no qualification to offer devotional writings in praise of Your Divine Grace. Please bestow your causeless mercy upon this fallen soul so that I can do this.

O my dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, by your causeless mercy my illusion is now gone, I have regained my memory, and I am now firm and free from doubts and prepared to act according to your divine instructions. I know that if you are satisfied, then we are satisfied. Lord Kṛṣṇa states in the *Bhagavad-gītā* that He cannot be known as He actually is merely by austerity, charity, philosophical speculation, or any other process except loving devotional service. Similarly, one cannot obtain the mercy of the bona fide spiritual master by any process except humble and loving service. But such loving service must be executed exactly according to the order of the spiritual master. If the master asks for water, one should not bring milk. Similarly, the bona fide disciple should spread the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement exactly according to the order and example of Your Divine Grace. Such strict obedience is the preliminary basis of love.

My dear Śrīla Prahupāda, when I first met the devotees in Moscow, by Kṛṣṇa’s mercy they immediately introduced me to you. They gave me your photo and *praṇāma-mantra* and taught me how to worship you. I started the very next day. I offered you food, paid obeisances, and prayed to you, but I did everything as a matter of duty. Things were going on like this until I got a tape of one of your lectures. I was captivated by your first words. It was *śabda-brahman*. It was wonderful. All the knowledge I had received from your books was revealed to me. I realized the importance of guru, of following his instructions, of the mission of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, and of the process of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. I danced in ecstasy. That was the day of my initiation in my heart. I was so happy that I had finally found you, my eternal spiritual master. From that day, the mission of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, which is also your mission—printing and distributing books—became my life and soul. From that particular day I realized how important these activities are and how they are the only way to please you. I have never given up that mission since then.

I immediately started translating your books into the Azerbaijan language, printing them underground and distributing them. Despite so many impediments, such as persecution, harassment by the KGB, imprisonment, and finally military service, I never gave up that mission because you were always with me, instructing me, protecting me, giving me advice, taking care of me, and giving me shelter. Your lectures and your transcendental voice were my only source of inspiration. It was very obvious to me that I was nothing without you—*guru-mukha-padma-vākya, cittete koriyā aikya* happened automatically.

Preaching to Muslims is not an easy task, but by your mercy everything is possible. Actually, you are the preacher and we are simply your assistants. Despite all difficulties, by your causeless mercy a temple was established in Baku, Azerbaijan. During this time I started to contemplate preaching in Turkey. I reprinted some books in Turkish and started to distribute them among Turkish tourists. After a while I got an order from the GBC to go and preach in Turkey. Even Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa doesn’t care about these hopeless countries. But you are even more merciful than Lord Kṛṣṇa. This is the qualification of a pure devotee. I am just an instrument in your hands. You are the spiritual surgeon. Actually, nobody believed that something could happen in Turkey. But, by your causeless mercy, the impossible became possible. In spite of the hopeless situation and of me being the worst instrument, you achieved success. Now in Turkey we have a preaching center and a Food for Life program, and a temple is being constructed. You have followers in all the big cities of Turkey—Istanbul, Ankara, Izmir, and Antalya. Nobody could have imagined this. These people are so sinful and hopeless that only by your causeless mercy can they be saved. Otherwise, they will rot in this material world for countless lifetimes. Just from your slight desire, Lord Kṛṣṇa revealed His mercy. Lord Kṛṣṇa is so pleased with you that He fulfills even your slightest desire.

When I met His Holiness Vipramukhya Swami in Calcutta before Gaura-pūrṇimā, he told me about the instructions you gave him on preaching in Turkey. He said that your main desire was to establish a temple in Istanbul. You said that Istanbul is a very important city and that we should have a temple there. We are now trying to fulfill your desire despite all difficulties. We know that your desire is Kṛṣṇa’s desire, and if you desire something to happen, it will happen sooner or later. Please give us your blessings that one day we may accomplish this difficult task for your pleasure.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are the greatest example of a *mahā-bhāgavata*. Your unlimited mercy is inconceivable to these most insignificant servants of yours. Being most untrained, we do not know how to please you. On this most auspicious day of your divine appearance, we express our deep gratitude to Your Divine Grace for picking these most fallen souls up from the deepest well of nescience. Please bless us so that someday these most insignificant servants may fully surrender to you.

*Jaya* Śrīla Prabhupāda!! All glories unto you!!

Your humble servants in Turkey.  
(written by Airāvata dāsa)

## Tyumen

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to you!

More then three decades have passed since you registered ISKCON in July 1966. In the larger scheme of things, this is not so long ago. But who can measure the significance of what you did back in that summer in New York City? Since then the transcendental sounds of the grand *mahā-mantra* have spread all over the world. Thousands of people in many countries have been initiated into the practice of *sanātana-dharma* and are taking part in and distributing the Vaiṣṇava culture. They have received a priceless treasure, the greatest mercy a living entity can get in the material world—*bhakti,* the supermost gift.

But the most wonderful thing is that this is only the beginning: your faith and pure devotion to Lord Kṛṣṇa will provide the basis for a golden era of ten thousand years in which Lord Caitanya’s *saṅkīrtana* movement will predominate in the world. By your mercy we have the most invaluable treasure—the holy name, which is nondifferent from the Supreme Lord, and despite all difficulties we can always take shelter of this most merciful incarnation of Kṛṣṇa. We bow down to your lotus feet, dear Śrīla Prabhupāda.

We love you and thank you. We glorify you. Hare Kṛṣṇa!

Nobody is a stranger in the kingdom of God:  
He pours His mercy on everybody.  
Everyone is free to live in that world,  
where there is no enmity or distress.

But under the cover of material dust  
in the madness of desires and passion,  
we criminally lost and forgot  
that place, and came to this world of woe.

Cherished way back to our native world  
in the embrace of our tolerant father.  
The devotees of the Lord are worthy to come,  
their hearts are brave and pure.

How can I purify my heart,  
not to drown in the gulf of distress?!  
You, Prabhupāda, gave me a ticket  
to the planet of perfection.

These are our hearts becoming free:  
Prabhupāda! Prabhupāda!  
*Karatāls* and *mṛdaṅgas* are singing:  
Prabhupāda! Prabhupāda!  
To the past we turn our sight  
to understand you better:  
Prabhupāda! Prabhupāda!

Śrīla Prabhupāda *kī jaya*!

Your humble servants in Tyumen, Russia.

## Uppsala

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura writes in his book *Shri Chaitanya Mahaprabhu—His Life and Precepts:*

While located in the *mayik* world man must live peacefully, with the object of cultivating the spirit. In his society he must lead a pure life, avoid sins, and do as much good as he can to his brother man. He must be himself bearing difficulties of life with heroism, must not brag of any goodness or grandeur he has, and must treat everyone with respect due to him....Spiritual cultivation is the main object of life. Do everything that helps it and abstain from doing anything which thwarts the cultivation of the spirit. Have a strong faith that Krishna *alone* protects you and *none else*. Admit Him as your only guardian. Do everything which you know that Krishna wishes you to do and never think that you do a thing independent of the holy wish of Krishna. Do all that you do with humility. Always remember that you are a sojourner in this world and you must be prepared for your own home. Do your duties and cultivate *bhakti* as a means to obtain the great end of life, *Krishna-priti.* Employ your body, mind, and spirit in the service of the Deity. In all your actions, worship your great Lord.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I think you are the personification of this ideal life of a Vaiṣṇava Śrīla Bhaktivinoda is describing here. He says “\[the Vaiṣṇava\] must do as much good as he can to his brother man,” and you dedicated your whole life to serving humanity in order to give them the supreme taste of *bhakti-rasa*! Is not that the highest good you can do to your brother man? Yes, Śrīla Prabhupāda you fulfilled the eager hopes and dreams of Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura by spreading the teachings of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu in the Western word. In *Śrī Kṛṣṇa-saṁhitā* Śrīla Bhaktivinoda talks about the mood and heart of the Vaiṣṇavas: “We are even ready to throw the happiness of our life in the water in order to benefit our brothers.” Śrīla Prabhupāda, you were always absorbed in the spiritual welfare of others and never concerned about your own comfort.

Later in the same book Śrīla Bhaktivinoda describes the duty of the Vaiṣṇavas: “The primary engagement of Vaiṣṇavas is to deliver their fallen brothers from the well of material existence.” Śrīla Prabhupāda, you saved us! You are the true Vaiṣṇava following in the footsteps of Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura!

Today we pray to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for spiritual strength and compassion to “do as much good as we can to our brother man.”

Your servants in Uppsala, Sweden.

## Vancouver

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisance at your divine lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

Śrīla Prabhupāda, may your glories be known in every town and village! Thus Lord Caitanya’s prediction—that His name would be chanted in every town and village—would be naturally fulfilled. Of all your unlimited glories, one of the most extraordinary is your ability to spread the chanting of the holy name. Along with this gift of the holy name, you gave us clear instructions meant to purify our hearts, making them the fertile ground to develop pure, ecstatic love of God.

What a perfect package! What a wonderful gift!

Śrīla Prabhupāda, your absolute faith in the order of your Guru Mahārāja empowered you with a dynamic determination to fulfill his and Lord Caitanya’s desire. That is gradually becoming a reality: in every town and village, the holy name is being chanted.

In the same spirit, you empowered us with the same instruction, and it is up to us to cooperate with one another to propagate more and more this “matchless gift” of Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

This is our debt to you. Yes, in fact: our eternal debt.

Now, even though many devotees have been tirelessly endeavoring to preach, so much more work is needed!

The reading, distributing, and especially the practical application of your books is the healing medicine for all souls, indeed, the only real solution for all the problems of life.

Unfortunately, the reality is that the spreading of Kṛṣṇa consciousness has such a long way to go. For example, there are many gigantic bookstores here in Vancouver, one of which is the famous Chapter bookstore. People buy thousands of books of all kinds from this store and read them, but, Śrīla Prabhupāda, your books should be the ones read instead. Your books should be studied, along with all the spiritual books that present the same Kṛṣṇa conscious conclusion, because they are empowered to spread knowledge about the Supreme Absolute Truth, Śrī Kṛṣṇa.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, this is a major dream of ours: a gigantic library of Kṛṣṇa conscious literature as popular as Chapter bookstore. Wouldn’t that be great?

Will that dream ever come true? When, oh, when will that day be ours?

Even though we feel useless, even though we have failed you miserably over and over again, we still dare to beg you on this Vyāsa-pūjā day: please continue to not give up on us.

On so many occasions, Śrīla Prabhupāda, you miraculously spoke to us through your lectures, books and various devotees, and in this way divine intervention made its incredible way into our hearts and our weak faith was strengthened. Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda—it means so much to these insignificant souls.

However, one thing we certainly know: we have such a long, long way to go.

Whatever one deserves or needs is administered expertly. It is called the fire of ordeal, which tests and ultimately strengthens the devotees, no matter how hard it often is to see these difficulties as divine blessings in disguise.

Your Divine Grace certainly went through many challenges, especially in the early days while trying to establish Kṛṣṇa consciousness in the Western world. But all those ordeals didn’t stop you from moving forward with your missionary vision. On the contrary, you prayed to the Lord for His guidance, and now your victories are spreading and touching so many of us in various ways, and these victories are often depicted as countless spiritual miracles.

If we follow in your footsteps with the same spirit, remaining determined no matter how many obstacles we face, total victory will come to us somehow or another, sooner or later, although with today’s dangerous circumstances, sooner would definitely be better.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, our debt to you is unlimited and shall remain so, for the more mercy you bestow upon us, the more indebted we become. The more we try to repay you, the more mercy you bestow, thereby increasing our debt more and more! O Prabhupāda, where does it end? There will be no end, so what can we do? What can we possibly do except pray at your divine lotus feet again and again to purify our hearts and engage in sincere devotional service, such as hearing, chanting, and preaching, eternally?

What else can we do but practice, as your children, respecting one another daily, treating one another with sincere care, free from envy, and thus creating a Vaikuṇṭha atmosphere based on love and trust? This much you really wanted us to do. What else can we do but pray to welcome and retain more and more sincere souls who are ready to dive and swim in the ocean of the sublime solution you offered us under the divine name of Kṛṣṇa consciousness?

We, the devotees in Vancouver, pray to be blessed with your causeless mercy, life after life after life.

*Gaura premānanda!*

Savior of the whole world Śrīla Prabhupāda, *kī jaya*!

Your servants in Vancouver, Canada.

## Villaggio Hare Krishna

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances. All glories at the dust of your lotus feet.

At the present moment ISKCON is going trough a very hard test. Time has shown how your dear Society can also be utilized for personal comforts if one is not careful. We must therefore think of you and your valuable example. The result of forgetting your example is disaster, as Kṛṣṇa says in the *Bhagavad-gītā* (18.58): “If, however, you do not work in such consciousness but act through false ego, not hearing Me, you will be lost.”

Śrīla Prabhupāda, the difference between a conditioned soul and you, a great soul, is that you always followed the divine instructions of your spiritual master and Kṛṣṇa. That’s why you were so successful from the very beginning of your preaching; you always put Kṛṣṇa in the center, without any compromise.

Again and again you told us, “You are not this body; you are the servant of the servants of Kṛṣṇa.” as Lord Caitanya says, *gopī-bhartuḥ pada-kamalayor dāsa-dāsānudāsaḥ.* We are learning from our mistakes. You told us we would; nevertheless, in Kali-yuga the memory is very short. You, Śrīla Prabhupāda, are our real friend and most loving master, full of unlimited compassion (*suhṛdaṁ sarva-dehinām*).

Kṛṣṇa is so kind to send His dearmost devotee, under whose guidance all the world will be saved. Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, help the members of your ISKCON Society, which is very dear to you, work together and develop a personal attitude and a deeper sense of love. You were always ready to fight for Kṛṣṇa without caring for personal comforts. When will we learn this simple lesson of performing our duty to you without desire for gain or fear of loss?

You gave us everything; there is nothing we can lament for. Your desire is simply that we try to develop more love for guru and Kṛṣṇa, and then everything else will follow. We look to nobody else except you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for our guidance.

All glories to your BBT! All glories to your ISKCON! All glories to the preaching mission of Lord Caitanya and Lord Nityānanda!

We hope to always remain the faithful servants of your servants.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for all the mercy you have given us, even though we don’t deserve it.

The devotees at Villaggio Hare Krishna, in Bergamo, Italy.  
(written by Durgama dāsa)

## Villa Vrindavana

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

This holy event of Śrī Vyāsa-pūjā is a special occasion to glorify you. But to glorify you correctly, in a meaningful way, we must deeply meditate on you. Why should we want to glorify you? Who are you? How has your association changed us? What is your mission?

The present times are times of great changes. Your ISKCON is being endangered externally and internally—externally by those who do not recognize your absolute position and subtly try to minimize your unequaled stature, and internally (and this is the most dangerous) by disunity due to different interpretations of the subtle meanings of your philosophical and managerial views.

But as for us here at Villa Vrindavana, we are convinced that one instruction of yours is very clear: “Cooperate with one another under the direction of the GBC body.” We’re sure that if we stick to that instruction, not ignoring the problems but trying to solve them as loving brothers and sisters do in a united family in which the beloved father is the center and his instructions are the guide, all problems will be satisfactorily resolved.

We accept your instruction as final because we know you as the Supreme Personality of Servitor Godhead, the one empowered by Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu to preach His mission, and also because we have seen our lives transformed to a degree that would be impossible even to imagine without your association.

As always, trying to glorify you leaves us satisfied, because the attempt brings us in contact with the nature of transcendence, in which we become completely absorbed in thoughts of your activities and consciousness. But the attempt to glorify you also leaves us unsatisfied, because once more we realize our inability to appreciate even a drop of the ocean of your transcendental glories.

Today we take the opportunity to submit a plea to your lotus feet: please be merciful to us, give us shelter in your International Society for Krishna Consciousness, and infuse us with the strong desire to follow your instructions and develop this project of Villa Vrindavana so that one day it will become a real refuge for the suffering people in this part of the world.

Your servants at Villa Vrindavana, Florence, Italy.

## Vladimir

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please let us offer humble prostrations at your divine lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

You blessed the world by your coming here and traveled extensively to preach the message of *Bhagavad-gītā* and *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*. At that time one could not distribute Kṛṣṇa consciousness freely in Russia, but still you came and invested as much energy as you could, despite all the obstacles imposed. This *līlā* is wonderfully depicted in the *Prabhupāda Līlāmṛta*. You made one disciple, Ananta Śānti dāsa, in whom you found a sincere and promising preacher who could continue your task. By the mercy you invested in him in three days, he was able to sustain his spiritual life and preach to others. It happened that he came to our small town to preach back in the seventies. In this way we feel you presence, Śrīla Prabhupāda, in the opening of the branch of the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement here in Vladimir, although we never had the good fortune of having your lotus feet step on this land.

Once, when there were only a few hundred devotees in the movement, you said that if everyone in the movement became as dedicated as you are, then ISKCON would really influence the world. That is our desire also, Śrīla Prabhupāda—to become as determined in preaching Kṛṣṇa consciousness as you are and make a major impact on the future of this otherwise miserable and contaminated world. Obviously, you monitor the present activities of this movement, both personally and through your staunch disciples. Therefore we humbly beg for your blessings to become successful in our attempts establish *sanātana-dharma* here in Vladimir and glorify your service to your beloved spiritual master and Lord Kṛṣṇa.

Hoping to remain the servants of your servants,  
The devotees at the ISKCON temple in Vladimir, Russia.

## Vodice

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

**I offer my respectful obeisances unto His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, who is very dear to Lord Kṛṣṇa on this earth, having taken shelter at His lotus feet. Our respectful obeisances are unto you, O spiritual master, servant of Sarasvatī Gosvāmī. You are kindly preaching the message of Lord Caitanyadeva and delivering the Western countries, which are filled with impersonalism and voidism.**

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

**I offer my respectful obeisances unto my spiritual master, who has opened my eyes, which were blinded by the darkness of ignorance, with the torchlight of knowledge.**

There are many incarnations of the Lord, and every one is special, engaging in unique pastimes to fulfill Their own purposes. But of Them all, Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu is most merciful because He distributed pure love of God to all conditioned souls. Similarly, there are many *ācāryas* in this world who are very learned in the science of God and very dutiful in performing devotional service to the Lord, and who teach by their own example, but you are unique because you are the personification of pure love of God and your mercy toward all conditioned souls and your realization and knowledge of God are unmatched. You are performing devotional service in such an incredible way! My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda all glories to you, the unique *ācārya* among all *ācāryas*! We offer our humble obeisances to the dust of your lotus feet again and again.

Due to your great determination to fulfill the instruction of your spiritual master, the whole world is being drowned in love of God. So who can be a greater well-wisher of humanity than you? No one, and therefore we worship your lotus feet with great enthusiasm. You are a cloud of mercy inundating everyone with knowledge about Śrī Kṛṣṇa’s name, form, qualities, pastimes, and teachings—knowledge that takes one who imbibes it back home, back to Godhead. Therefore we beg to become your servants, life after the life.

You taught us how to worship Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa and thus informed us about our real eternal duty. Before you came, Śrīla Prabhupāda, there was no hope for us Western people, who accept sense enjoyment as the ultimate treasure of life, but now we can realize what the real purpose of life is. Śrīla Prabhupāda, please therefore accept us as your eternal servants.

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, even though we hear about your glories, we are lamenting because we feel we are unqualified to please you. We are still drowning in the ocean of material desires. We don’t have any good qualities or knowledge, nor the determination to cut our ties to the material energy. All we have is a little faith in your books, and we pray that that sword of knowledge given by you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, will cut all our attachments and make it possible for us to engage in unalloyed devotional service. We were in the deepest darkness of ignorance, but you kindly opened our blind eyes with the torch of knowledge. Because of that we accept your mission, Śrīla Prabhupāda, as our life and soul. Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda! Thank you for giving us the opportunity to go back home, back to Kṛṣṇa!

Your devotees in Vodice, Croatia.

## Vṛndāvana

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

**Śrīmate-Bhaktivedānta-Svāmin-Vṛndāvana-Līlā-Smaraṇa-Maṅgala-Stotram**

*sad-bhaktaiḥ satatāñjasaika-śaraṇaṁ tat-kalpitaṁ bhaktive-*  
*dānta-svāmi-padāravinda-yugalaṁ no śūnya-vādyādibhiḥ*  
*līlās te ’stv api no guṇānu-kathanaṁ bhāvyaṁ sadā sattamair*  
*vande ’haṁ prabhupāda-padma-caraṇau vṛndāvane tiṣṭhatau*

*sat-bhaktaiḥ*—by saintly devotees; *satata*—always; *añjasā*—easily; *eka-śaraṇam*—exclusive shelter; *tat-kalpitam*—accepted as such; *bhakti-vedānta-svāmi*—of Bhaktivedanta Swami; *pada-aravinda*—the lotus feet; *yugalam*—the pair of; *na u*—never indeed; *śūnya-vādi-ādibhiḥ*—by voidists etc.; *līlāḥ*—pastimes; *te*—your; *astu*—may; *api*—also; *naḥ*—our; *guṇa-anu-kathanam*—talks about your wonderful qualities; *bhāvyam*—to be contemplated; *sadā*—always; *sattamaiḥ*—by the virtuous devotees; *vande*—I offer obeisances; *aham*—I; *prabhupāda-padma-caraṇau*—unto the two lotus feet of Śrīla Prabhupāda; *vṛndāvane*—in Vṛndāvana; *tiṣṭhatau*—situated (the lotus feet).

**Saintly devotees always accept the easily available exclusive shelter of Śrīla A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda’s lotus feet. But voidists, impersonalists, and others of their ilk cannot accept this shelter at all. O Śrīla Prabhupāda, may your pastimes, which saintly devotees always meditate on, be the subject of our talks as we discuss your wonderful qualities! I offer my obeisances unto the two lotus feet of Śrīla Prabhupāda in Vṛndāvana.**

*śrīmad-bhāgavatānuvāda-kara-rātry-ante ’tha tac-chikṣakaḥ*  
*pūrvāhṇe sva-gaṇair lasaty upavane tair bhāti madhyāhnake*  
*yaḥ puryām aparāhṇake nija-gṛhe sāyaṁ tataḥ prāṅgaṇe*  
*tvaṁ śrīla-prabhupāda bhāva-kṛpayā rakṣasva no sarvaśaḥ*

*śrīmad-bhāgavata-anuvāda-kara*—translator of and commentator on *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam; rātri-ante*—at the end of night; *atha*—thereafter; *tat-śikṣakaḥ*—one who lectures from *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam; pūrvāhṇe*—in the forenoon; *sva-gaṇaiḥ*—with his followers; *lasati*—he enjoys; *upavane*—in the garden; *taiḥ*—with them; *bhāti*—he remains; *madhyāhnake*—at midday; *yaḥ*—who; *puryām*—in the temple compound; *aparāhṇake*—during the afternoon; *nija-gṛhe*—in his own home; *sāyam*—at dusk; *tataḥ*—thereafter; *prāṅgaṇe*—in the temple courtyard; *tvam*—you; *śrīla-prabhupāda*—O Śrīla Prabhupāda; *bhāva-kṛpayā*—by your affectionate mercy; *rakṣasva*—please protect; *naḥ*—us; *sarvaśaḥ*—in all respects.

**At the end of night Śrīla Prabhupāda writes his** ***Bhāgavatam*** **translations and commentaries. In the morning he lectures from** ***Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*****. At midday he enjoys the company of his devotees in his garden, and then in the afternoon he sometimes tours the temple complex. In the evening he returns to his home for** ***darśana*** **and then goes to the temple courtyard \[for a lecture on the** ***Bhagavad-gītā*****\]. O Śrīla Prabhupāda, please protect us in all respects with your affectionate mercy!**

We offer our most humble obeisances to Śrīla Prabhupāda and his transcendental activities and qualities. As we remember Śrīla Prabhupāda in Vṛndāvana, we are overwhelmed with separation from him. Where is such a great soul who dedicated his life to Lord Caitanya’s mission up to the last breath? From the moment he awoke each day, he was absorbed in the compassionate service of preaching Kṛṣṇa consciousness. He was devoid of any self-interest; his only concern was how to save the fallen souls of Kali-yuga. As we slept in ignorance, Śrīla Prabhupāda worked hard to translate his books for our benefit. And even as we reluctantly awoke, Śrīla Prabhupāda was already chanting, watching everything, after his entire night had been spent translating. When *maṅgala-ārati* ended, Śrīla Prabhupāda would come from his house for a brisk morning walk on the Vṛndāvana roads. He would open our eyes to see Vṛndāvana as the sun rose above us. “You are all blind, only I can see,” he said as he circumambulated the temple. Twice around the temple he repeated the same statement; then he pointed to our blind spot. The lights were still on at the back of the temple, though it was already daytime. Yes, we are blind, but we are trying to train our eyes to see Vṛndāvana as you did. How many details did you magnify to us to reveal our foolishness, our lack of vision? Practically you taught us everything we know, and we’re still having difficulty with the simplest tasks. How can we meet the preaching challenge you have given us here? You wanted us to be servants to the temple guests. Repeatedly you insisted there always be *prasādam* for anyone who visits. You showed us by example how to welcome guests with sweet words, a place to sit, and some *prasādam*. You ordered, “Now you can never say the stock is finished.” Your Vaiṣṇava standards remain beyond our comprehension, though we are striving to reach them. At least we have your perfect example to remember and inspire us.

Vigilantly you trained us, personally correcting our mistakes big and small, our blunders that cost you time and money. “Breaking and building”—the Western disease, you called it. “But I am your friend! Just break this wall, only one lakh!” you imitated the well-wisher’s advice. You saw our inexperience and forced us to become responsible. Our life was and is to please you. There is no greater fear than displeasing you. Our life was finished when you expressed displeasure. And you are still watching from your window. We feel your presence and know that you can hear and see everything still. You are still with us, Śrīla Prabhupāda, watching us all, trying to guide us as you always did.

Your quiet garden, even when it was a mere construction site, was a place of joy for you. As the foundations were dug for the guesthouse, you sat with your peers in the evening, speaking in Bengali, laughing with them. None of us could understand you. What wonderful things you let us see, Śrīla Prabhupāda, at home with friends and family, peacefully talking about subjects beyond our comprehension! How you encouraged us, your little children, because you knew it was difficult for us! But just remembering you there in your garden makes us peaceful; all the struggles are worthwhile just to get a glimpse of you.

Then when the construction was complete and your garden became private, you enjoyed your massage and bath there, even to the point of bathing in the beautiful fountain—a quiet place of solitude to meet with your disciples and visitors. Surrounded by auspicious trees, beneath a thatched roof above your *āsana,* you were at home in Vṛndāvana at last. Of course, the weight of managing your international society was also present, but at least Kṛṣṇa had brought you back home. “Vṛndāvana is my home.” And here also you would check every detail. Feeling the dirt beneath Tulasī, you instructed that she should be watered three times a day. And if she wasn’t, you would notice immediately.

Sometimes in the afternoons you would walk around the temple grounds to see how the project was developing. Or sometimes you would meet with devotees in your room. Evening was always spent with the devotees and guests, who could come to your room for public *darśana*. How cordial and gentlemanly you were, impossible to imitate, because you actually cared about everyone who came to see you. Always an encouraging word, a personal question, a smile, and of course a sweet.

Then the sandwich program. Sandwich? Yes, that was what you called it here in Vṛndāvana. Morning program, service, evening program. “Don’t be a greater *paramahaṁsa* than your Guru Mahārāja. I am following, so you should also follow.” Without fail you attended morning and evening program in the temple. And if any of us were missing, you would inquire, “Where is Pradyumna? No, *pūjā* must be done at another time. Call him.” “Where is Yamunā? No, women should not go out at night.” Nothing missed your scrutiny. You were following, giving classes morning and evening, expending so much energy for our benefit. At first you only expected us to attend the classes. Later, your own disciples gave classes in the temple, even in Hindi, and you would enjoy listening to them under the *tamāla* tree.

You were directly empowered by Lord Kṛṣṇa; otherwise, how was it humanly possible that from 1:00 a.m. till 11:00 p.m. you tirelessly served? Day after day, year after year, around and around the globe, even at home, where you could have rested.

Remembering your pastimes inspires us. Maybe we can assist you, try to follow, try to serve as you taught us. Please let us be with you again, Śrīla Prabhupāda. We miss you and need your guidance as ever.

Your children and eternal servants at your beloved Śrī Śrī Krishna-Balarām Mandir, Vṛndāvana.

## Yugoslavia

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,Please accept our most humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet! All glories to Your Divine Grace! All glories to all the Vaiṣṇavas in the wonderful *guru-paramparā*!

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

O Lord, who resemble the shining sun, You are always ready to fulfill the desire of Your devotee, and therefore You are known as a desire tree \[*vāñchā-kalpataru*\]. When *ācāryas* completely take shelter under Your lotus feet in order to cross the fierce ocean of nescience, they leave behind on earth the method by which they cross, and because You are very merciful to Your other devotees, You accept this method to help them. \[*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 10.2.31, translation\]

Actually, we can’t find words to describe our thankfulness toward you and your great true followers. We are very fallen and sinful persons suffering in the ocean of material existence. This year we received more opportunity to meet more sufferings and miseries. Now many towns in Yugoslavia are lost, having been destroyed by a barrage of bombs in the war between NATO and Yugoslavia. Many people now suffer here. Yet even though there is so much trouble, we can still feel your great mercy, dear Śrīla Prabhupāda. You give us so many nectarean instructions through guru, *sādhu,* and *śāstra,* and even now in this hard time these spiritual grandchildren of yours can take shelter of them and become enthusiastic and happy in Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

The more we face difficulties, the more we draw the attention of Krsna. So in that way we shall be prepared to meet all kinds of difficulties in our preaching work. Such attempt gives us a lift on our progressive march in Krishna Consciousness. \[Letter to Upendra dāsa, 22 April 1970\]

So at this time we have the opportunity to make our faith in guru and Kṛṣṇa stronger. We are also now realizing how powerful the association of devotees is. Many of them are helping us a lot in these terrible times. Many of your followers have offered their transcendental hands to help us, encourage us, and give us more spiritual strength. Many devotees are also offering prayers to Lord Nṛsiṁha-deva, which also give us great support. You, dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, once said, “Your love for me will be shown by how you cooperate with one another after I am gone.”

Association with other devotees is a very valuable thing, because through association with devotees we have the opportunity to associate with you. Even though we weren’t lucky enough to meet you personally, through your disciples and your great books we can associate with you and drink the nectar of your teachings and the holy name. In our fallen condition we often have less-than-elevated desires, but one desire we do cherish is to make our lives useful in your *saṅkīrtana* mission. Please, dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, bless us that we will become filled with more spiritual strength and faith, that we can become Kṛṣṇa conscious and also help other fallen souls become Kṛṣṇa conscious. Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, bless us that we can be properly connected with the *guru-paramparā* and thus become empowered by you to transmit the nectarean teachings of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu to the generations who come after us.

We are very eager to learn more and more from your great true followers. We wish to offer our humble obeisances to all the Vaiṣṇavas engaged in the wonderful *saṅkīrtana* mission. Please, dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, bless us that we can easily cross all the difficulties that now surround us. Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda! We have so many requests for you. We always ask you for something. We always expect some transcendental present from you in the form of your unlimited mercy. We are always ready to accept, but never ready to give. Please, dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, teach us the valuable secrets of devotional service so that one fortunate day we can be in a position to offer some transcendental results before your lotus feet. Please!...Please!...Please! For now we can only say...

“Thank you, our beloved Śrīla Prabhupāda!”

Your servants in Yugoslavia.

## Zagreb

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to you!

By your and Lord Kṛṣṇa’s mercy we survived one more hard year in our devotional life. Many things have happened in the meantime, but you always teach us that devotion to the Lord is just like hot sugarcane—it brings joy, but it also brings pain.

You often described this world as “the blazing fire of material existence.” Right now there is every possibility that the Third World War will begin here in the Balkans. In your purport to *Bhagavad-gītā* 2.51 you write:

The liberated living entities belong to that place where there are no material miseries. The *Bhāgavatam* (10.14.58) says:

*samāṣritā ye pada-pallava-plavaṁ*  
*mahat-padaṁ puṇya-yaśo murāreḥ*  
*bhavāmbudhir vatsa-padaṁ paraṁ padaṁ*  
*padaṁ padaṁ yad vipadāṁ na teṣām*

**“For one who has accepted the boat of the lotus feet of the Lord, who is the shelter of the cosmic manifestation and is famous as Mukunda, or the giver of** ***mukti,*** **the ocean of the material world is like the water contained in a calf’s footprint.** ***Paraṁ padam,*** **or the place where there are no material miseries, or Vaikuṇṭha, is his goal, not the place where there is danger in every step of life.”**

Owing to ignorance, one does not know that this material world is a miserable place where there are dangers at every step. Out of ignorance only, less intelligent persons try to adjust to the situation by fruitive activities, thinking that the resultant actions will make them happy. They do not know that no kind of material body anywhere within the universe can give life without miseries. The miseries of life, namely birth, death, old age, and diseases, are present everywhere within the material world. But one who understands his real constitutional position as the eternal servitor of the Lord, and thus knows the position of the Personality of Godhead, engages himself in the transcendental loving service of the Lord. Consequently he becomes qualified to enter into the Vaikuṇṭha planets, where there is neither material, miserable life nor the influence of time and death. To know one’s constitutional position means to know also the sublime position of the Lord. One who wrongly thinks that the living entity’s position and the Lord’s position are on the same level is to be understood to be in darkness and therefore unable to engage himself in the devotional service of the Lord. He becomes a lord himself and thus paves the way for the repetition of birth and death. But one who, understanding that his position is to serve, transfers himself to the service of the Lord, at once becomes eligible for Vaikuṇṭhaloka. Service for the cause of the Lord is called *karma-yoga* or *buddhi-yoga,* or in plain words, devotional service to the Lord.

It is you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, who brought the boat of the lotus feet of the Lord for us poor conditioned souls in this material world. That boat is ISKCON. So how can cold, darkness, and fear exert their power over one who always stays in the brilliant sun of this ISKCON boat? How can any intelligent person who has boarded this boat and has any gratitude to you ever give up your lotus feet, Śrīla Prabhupāda?

You teach us that we must expect both joy and pain in our devotional life and that devotional service is transcendental and free from any material motivation. So this pain is our teacher who helps us again and again become conscious of your divine presence and mission on this planet.

Whatever we offer to the Lord or His pure representative without personal motivation, even if they are externally useless, can elevate us to the perfection of spiritual life.
