This year we are offering at your lotus feet our modest *saṅkīrtana* results from the preaching fields. By your mercy this year we will open a nice temple for our dear Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi.

Obeisance’s unto you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Please bless us that we may have undeviating attachment to your lotus feet and always remember your transcendental qualities, which are countless. Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, accept us and always keep us like dust under the protection of your lotus feet.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Your servants in Zagreb, Croatia.

## Zurich

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

Today for the first time in a long time I entered your room in the Zurich temple. Although there was no temple in Zurich when you came to visit Switzerland and blessed the country by your presence, you visited Geneva, where we had a temple, and St. Moritz. But you must have crossed Zurich on the way. And for the last twenty years the Swiss devotees have maintained a very nice temple here. The main attraction is Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi. When you saw Their picture at the Māyāpur Expo in 1976, you stopped in front of Them and asked, “Where are these Deities?” Someone told you They were in Switzerland and you said, “They are very special; please take care of Them nicely.”

Since then, Lord Jagannātha, Subhadrā, and Baladeva have appeared here. All these Deities are very merciful and most attractive. But people are really surprised when they go to the next floor and enter your room: although you never visited this place, everyone can feel that you are very much present here. At the center you sit writing a letter, and on the table is the Sanskrit *Bhāgavatam* with the commentaries of the previous *ācāryas,* along with a small picture of Kṛṣṇa-Balarāma and your spiritual master. In the bookshelf are all your books in many different languages. A bronze statue of you is in the middle. You look very aristocratic, very grave, and you seem to be gazing at Kṛṣṇa, His external energy *māyā,* and the living entities at the same time. You appear like Śrīla Vyāsadeva—full of compassion for all the conditioned souls because they have lost their orientation due to the dense darkness of this age. You are full of compassion for the fallen conditioned souls encaged in this insurmountable *māyā*. Therefore you came to deliver them by speaking your timeless message, the message of Godhead. Your message is clear like the sunshine, and you spoke it for the benefit of everyone for at least the next 10,000 years. Your books shed the only light in this world. It really is becoming more and more clear that this world is not a fit place for a gentleman. Every year more craziness. You look very stern and grave, but we know why—because of the fallen condition of the world.

Once in a park on your morning walk, it was such a chilly morning that some of the puddles on the path had become slightly frozen. With grave look you took your cane and pushed through the ice, one puddle after the other. The devotees all what you meant by doing that. Later you explained that the ice was *māyā* and you had come to smash it. Although it is a hard and thankless job, you never gave up: you were *dhṛta-vrata*. There is a story in this connection:

Once a tourist walking down a deserted Mexican beach at sunset saw a local man in the distance. As the two men grew nearer, the tourist noticed that the native kept leaning down, picking something up, and throwing it into the water. Time and again he kept hurling things out into the ocean. Soon the tourist noticed that the man was picking up starfish that had bean washed up onto the beach and, one at a time, throwing them back into the water. The tourist was puzzled. He approached the man and said, “Good evening, friend. I was wondering what you are doing.”

“I’m throwing these starfish back into the ocean. You see, it’s low tide right now and all these starfish have been washed up onto the shore. If I don’t throw them back into the sea, they’ll die up here from lack of oxygen.”

“I understand, but there must be thousands of starfish on this beach. You can’t possibly get to all of them. There are simply too many. And don’t you realize this is probably happening on hundreds of beaches all up and down the coast. Can’t you see that you can’t possibly make a difference?”

The native smiled, bent down, and picked up yet another starfish, and as he threw it back into the sea he replied, “Made a difference to that one!”

This story is called “One at a Time.” You also very persistently tried to save one soul at a time from *māyā*. Although it looked hopeless and impossible, for an empowered *ācārya* nothing is impossible. And by your and Lord Caitanya’s mercy, this Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement has spread all over the world, and it will continue to spread more and more and overcome all obstacles.

Some people become shocked when they see your grave face. When I saw your picture the first time my heart started to beat faster. But when the devotees told me about you and your message, the fear disappeared and I realized that you are my eternal father and spiritual guide, and I surrendered my life and soul to your lotus feet and wonderful mission. Later when I looked at the same picture of yours again, I felt a warm smile emanating from your face.

A few days ago one of our congregational members came to my room. We had an interesting conversation. He is a very smart man. First he pointed out that when he first read in your *Life Comes from Life* that “the scientists are rascals,” he thought he would write a letter to the president of the country and ask him to pardon Bhaktivedanta Swami for writing such blasphemous words about the scientists. But now, some years later, he told me he is happy he didn’t write the letter to the president because he sees now that you were right. In fact, he now says you were too soft with the scientists!

Like your Guru Mahārāja, who was known as Siṁha Guru, with your first step you cut to pieces the whole plane of exploitation and sense gratification, with your second you crushed the speculations of the scientists, Māyāvādīs, and so-called scholars, and with your third you established the worship of Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa and took us beyond Vaikuṇṭha.

As you wrote in the Preface of your *Bhāgavatam*:

*tad-vāg-visargo janatāgha-viplavo*  
*yasmin prati-ślokam abaddhavaty api*  
*nāmāny anantasya yaśo ’ṅkitāni yac*  
*chṛṇvanti gāyanti gṛṇanti sādhavaḥ*

**“On the other hand, that literature which is full with descriptions of the transcendental glories of the name, fame, form and pastimes of the unlimited Supreme Lord is a transcendental creation meant to bring about a revolution in the impious life of a misdirected civilization. Such transcendental literatures, even though irregularly composed, are heard, sung and accepted by purified men who are thoroughly honest.”**

Please let us go on distributing your books so that Kṛṣṇa consciousness will spread more and more in this country, and so that the people here will become truly rich.

Your servants at the ISKCON temple in Zurich, Switzerland.  
(written by Rohiṇī-sūta dāsa)

## Vyāsa-Pūjā Homages from the Publishers

## Bhaktivedanta Book Trust (Australasia)

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my prostrated obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace.

On this auspicious occasion of your Vyāsa-pūjā celebration, I would like to express my eternal gratitude to you for giving me the opportunity to obtain your mercy through your BBT and the service of directly working with the publishing and distribution of your transcendental books.

I am simply trying to appreciate your appearance in this world and the great benediction you have brought into my life by giving me the opportunity to serve you to the best of my ability.

As the Supreme Lord appears on this planet and performs His superhuman pastimes, so do His pure devotees, who also come to execute His mission. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you came to deliver the unchanged message of the Supreme Personality of Godhead for the absolute benefit of fallen conditioned souls like myself.

I want to thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for all the association I receive through your divine books, your devotees, the festivals, the temples, the *prasādam,* and everything else you have given us, especially your perfect example.

May I always be engaged in your divine service to assist you in your mission of spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness to every town and village all over the world. Please be merciful to me. Please give me the shade of your lotus feet. Your mercy is all that I pray for.

Your servants at the Bhaktivedanta Book Trust offices in Australasia.  
(written by Gaura Jagannātha dāsa)

## Bhaktivedanta Book Trust (Brazil)

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

On this most blessed day of the divine appearance of Oṁ Viṣṇupāda Paramahaṁsa Parivrājakācārya 108 Śrī Śrīmad Bhaktivedanta Swami Śrīla Prabhupāda, all perfection of life may be achieved just by glorifying him. In fact, everything that has any connection with Śrīla Prabhupāda is perfect and sublime.

Śrīla Prabhupāda gave us our own life. He rescued us from the ocean of nescience. He also showed us the actual way to humanize humankind and bring auspiciousness to this fallen age. Every time we remember Śrīla Prabhupāda, our minds immediately become full of goodness and knowledge and we realize that we are definitely happy despite any inconveniences we have to pass through during our voluntary stay in this material world.

We owe everything to him, and we will never be able to liquidate this debt, no matter what we try to do for him or his society. We are just like drops of rain falling into many filthy places, and Śrīla Prabhupāda is like a very powerful sun that evaporates us and elevates us to the infinite spiritual sky.

On this most blessed day, we just want to remember him and be at his lotus feet forever.

Śrīla Prabhupāda’s eternal servants at the Bhaktivedanta Book Trust offices in Brazil.

## Bhaktivedanta Book Trust (Chinese)

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace and to your sincere followers!

More than one billion people on the surface of the earth speak Chinese. The translation and publication of your books in the Chinese language is therefore of the utmost importance for spreading the *saṅkīrtana* movement. It is by Kṛṣṇa’s causeless mercy that we, the members of the Chinese BBT, have been chosen to do this service, but we feel unqualified to execute this mission properly.

We therefore pray that our endeavor to please you by translating, publishing, and distributing your books may be successful. We pray that you may request Kṛṣṇa to give us the necessary intelligence, devotion, humility, and purity of heart to properly carry out our service. We pray that the community of Vaiṣṇavas will relish the result of our efforts and that it will help them become Kṛṣṇa conscious. We pray that the people who buy your books will be attracted to Kṛṣṇa.

Your humble servants at the Chinese BBT.

## Bhaktivedanta Book Trust (Mumbai)

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances at your lotus feet on this most auspicious day of your appearance on this planet. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

On this auspicious day we would like to remember some of your transcendental instructions and gain renewed enthusiasm to increase our service to your lotus feet. By writing your books you have delivered all the living entities who come in contact with them while translating, proofreading, typesetting, printing, selling, buying, and reading them, and even seeing or just touching them! And you will continue to do so for the next ten thousand years.

You wrote,

Regarding the book distribution, somehow the distribution must be doubled and tripled as far as possible. Do it. \[Letter to Rameśvara dāsa on 14 November 1974\]

Krsna becomes more and more pleased by seeing the increment of book distribution. \[Letter to Maṇidhara dāsa and Rohiṇī-sūta dāsa on 27 October 1976\]

Now you order huge quantity of Hindi and Gujarati books from our BBT office in Bombay. Distribute as much literature as possible, English, Hindi, and Gujarati. That is real preaching. Thank you very much. \[Letter to Vāsudeva on 3 November 1976\]

We are feeling great bliss to inform you that because of the successful December marathon in India, new prices, and attractive book covers, BBT Mumbai almost tripled its book production from 570,080 books in 1997 to 1,585,000 in 1998. This is our offering for your satisfaction on this most auspicious day.

You also wrote:

I have also heard that you are making plans for a very large order of my books from the BBT to be sold on a door to door basis on the style of the encyclopedia sales. If you can do this it will revolutionize our book distribution and afford greater spreading of Krsna Consciousness than any book distribution thus far. I want that every respectable person has a full set of Bhagavatam and Caitanya Caritamrta in his home. \[Letter, 24 January 1977\]

Our Vedic Encyclopedia department is being organized as per your instructions, and we are planning to sell the Vedic Encyclopedia door-to-door (in installments also) on a big scale to every respectable person in Mumbai, and throughout India as well. We have managed to sell one hundred sets in the past eight months.

Praying for your causeless mercy, we remain  
Your servants at the BBT offices in Mumbai, India.

## Bhaktivedanta Book Trust (North America)

Dear Śrīla Prabhupada,

Please accept our humble obeisances in the dust of your supremely merciful, transcendental lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

In the equipment room at the BBT offices in Los Angeles hangs a painting depicting living entities undergoing various types of torture as a result of their sinful activities.

In one quadrant is a person tied to a rack over a fire-filled spit. While being roasted alive, he is simultaneously being forced by a Yamadūta to watch his own disembowelment by vultures and dogs. Another person is wrapped tightly in the coils of a large, malefic snake. Simultaneously he is being bitten by a multitude of insects, including flies, spiders, and scorpions.

In another quadrant a line of hapless souls is being marched to the head of a cliff from which Yamarāja's assistants forcibly throw them to crash in pain. In still another area some Souls are suffering dismemberment via the trunks of aggressive, maddened elephants.

And perhaps most terrifying to behold, in another sector a group of sinners are biting and eating one another's flesh. Some are even eating their own flesh.

This painting and others like it were used to illustrate your commentated translation of the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam.* The scenes they show clearly remind us of the fate from which you have saved us, and countless other souls.

Out of compassion you insisted that we concentrate on printing and distributing your books. You knew well that your books are the prime means of salvation for this age.

Accordingly, you were very keen to hear how many books were being distributed and how many new languages the books were translated into. In February, 1977, the following exchange took place in Māyāpur between you and disciples engaged in BBT work:

**Prabhupāda:** So? What other news?

**Rādhā-vallabha:** We have the figures for the production of your books in every single language since the beginning, twenty-three languages.

**Rāmeśvara:** This took several weeks of very careful work to prepare. We were calling up printers... We threatened Dai Nippon, they must give us the figures. We called up every BBT office around the world, so this is very accurate. And also I estimate that at least ninety to ninety-five percent of all these literatures that have been published have already been distributed. These are the figures for publishing, but most of them have been sold already.

**Rādhā-vallabha:** So English is first. 43,450,500 literatures. (laughter)

**Prabhupāda:** Where is such publisher?

**Rādhā-vallabha:** That’s one for every five people in America. And second is Spanish: 2,947,000. Third is German: 2,125,500. Then Japanese, I had to make a guess. I’m not sure, but this is pretty close: 2,125,000. Fifth is French: 1,670,000.

**Prabhupāda:** There should be... A big board should be hang.

**Rāmeśvara:** We have a big display of this for the Māyāpura exhibit. We made this up as a...

**Prabhupāda:** Yes.

**Rādhā-vallabha:** In the exhibit there is a framed picture, each language and the books, each title, and then there is one big frame, one showing international.

**Prabhupāda:** This is the blessing of my Guru Mahārāja. He wanted it. And because we are trying to do this, he is giving us all blessings. He told me personally, “I wanted to sell this marble and publish some books.” Calcutta, that Gauḍīya Maṭha is also marble floor. Now, he said that “Since this temple has been given by Mr. Datta, our men are fighting, ‘Which room I shall occupy?’ So I know there will be blazing fire here. So before that, I wanted to get out this marble and sell it and turn into some books.” He told me, like that. So I noted down that, that he wants books. So I tried to do that. That’s all.

**Rāmeśvara:** At the Śiva-rātri festival in Los Angeles, at the end of a lecture I was reading these figures. We had about a thousand Indians who came to that festival in Los Angeles. When I read these figures they were so struck, they were cheering, “Jaya,” and clapping. They were just amazed. And afterwards, outside they were coming up to me and folded hands: “We had no idea you were doing this work. Is there anything we can do to help your society?” as soon as they heard these figures. The leaders of the Indian American Association and all the Indian associations in LA were coming there, and when they heard these figures they were just offering their service.

**Prabhupāda:** And you did not send to the reporters and the...? No.

**Hari-śauri:** Did you give these figures to the reporters at the airport?

**Rāmeśvara:** Well, we want to have a press conference tomorrow.

**Gargamuni:** We’re going to have... At the Calcutta Book Fair we finished decorating our pandal. It promises to be very successful. We’re just opposite the Americans, who have spent fifty thousand rupees, the American Embassy. And next door to us is the German Embassy, and on the other side is the British Embassy. So we’re in a very good spot, and we’ll have all our books. We’ll have the displays as well as the movie, the BBT movie, and we have our men there, who will take orders and sell books. It starts tomorrow afternoon. And we’ll have a press conference also and release these figures.

**Prabhupāda:** That’s nice.

**Rādhā-vallabha:** Want to hear more?

**Prabhupāda:** Yes.

**Rādhā-vallabha:** Sixth is Portuguese, 835,000. Seventh, Dutch, 593,000. Eighth, Italian, 448,000. Ninth, Hindi, 315,000. Tenth, Bengali, 305,000. Eleventh is Telegu, 115,000. Twelfth is Swahili... This is also another estimate, 110,000. Fourteenth is Chinese... Thirteenth is Gujarati, 90,000. Fourteenth is Chinese, 55,000. Fifteenth is Marathi, 25,000. Korean, 20,000. Yugoslavian, 20,000. Oriya, 20,000. Polish 10,000. Hungarian, 10,000. Czechoslovakian, 10,000. Tamil, 10,000. Russian 5,000. And the total is 55,314,000.

**Prabhupāda:** (laughs) Russian is last. Yes.

**Rāmeśvara:** It’s the most difficult.

**Prabhupāda:** But something is better than nothing. *Kānā māmā*. (“Blind Uncle”)

Since then, by your mercy and the potency of your preaching the numbers have continually increased. The figures below were reported for the month of Febmary, 1999: Seventy-five temples reported distributing the following number of books:

11, 761 Maha-big books

17,954 Big books

17,292 Medium books

25,898 Small books

38,132 Magazines

7 Sets

111,037 literatures for the month

230,620 literatures year-to-date

429,898,349 literatures worldwide since 1965

So to date you have delivered at least 430 million souls froin the material quagmire. Thanks to you they will no longer have to endure torture in hellish conditions of life. When we take into account that anyone who reads, sees, or even touches one of your transcendental books is benefited eternally, then we see that the actual number delivered could be 10 to 100 times greater.

We, your servants at the North American BBT, feel ourselves exceedingly fortunate to be able to assist you in your incalculably munificent service. We pray that you will continue to shower your mercy upon us despite our myriad faults. We pray also that we not become complacent because, with more than 5 billion souls on this planet in human form, there is still a lot of work to be done.

Your servants at the BBT offices in Los Angeles, California, USA

## Bhaktivedanta Book Trust (Northern Europe)

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

We offer our most respectful obeisances in the dust of your wonderful lotus feet and pray that you will permit us to glorify you as a general who has ruined materialistic and decadent empires with the weapons of your books and your devotee soldiers.

When Canto 7, Part 2, of the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* was printed, your two disciples Rameśvara dāsa and Rādhāvallabha dāsa received the first two copies. Excitedly, they quickly went up to your quarters to present these books to you fresh from the printers. Although it was nearly 2 a.m., they found you wide awake. With childlike glee, they hid the books behind their backs as you greeted them, appearing to be surprised and remarking, “Oh, you are here!” Upon noticing that they were hiding something behind their backs and trying to suppress their smiles, you said playfully, “You have something for me?” Upon seeing the books, you exclaimed, “Aaah!” You immediately took one and touched it to your head. Joyfully you started reading the book aloud, seemingly oblivious to everything and everyone else around you.

Your Divine Grace is a most exalted personality, yet you are easily pleased by the dedication of the simplest person who chooses to assist you in the mission of Lord Caitanya. You are particularly happy to receive new copies of your books printed in all the different languages of the world. You said that whenever a book of yours is published you feel as though you have conquered an empire.

Now, striving to conceal our transparent smiles, we, your humble servants at the North European BBT, are also hiding behind our backs a few of your books we have produced during the past year. We, too, have something for you, and we beg you to accept it with pleasure.

Your *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is* appeared in three more languages—Macedonian, Slovak, and Slovenian—and there are new editions of the *Bhagavad-gītā* in Czech and French. Now the people of Iceland can read *Easy Journey to Other Planets,* and the Serbs have *A Second Chance*. The Russian line was also productive: *Rāja-Vidyā, A Second Chance,* and the Fifth and Sixth Cantos of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* were printed. We ask you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, to please look at the new paintings in the Russian *Bhāgavatams*. They were painted by your granddisciples at the BBT offices in Sochi. The devotees in Uzbekistan now read and distribute your biography, together with several small books, and the Turkmenian *yātrā* is happy to have received *The Science of Self-Realization* and *Śrī Īśopaniṣad*. A special achievement for the Ukranian translators, editors, and proofreaders is the one-volume edition of *Śrī Caitanya-caritāmṛta*.

What we are offering you now is practically nothing compared to previous years. There have been so many obstacles hindering our book production. Yet the remembrance of how you dealt with the difficulties of the material world makes us fired with determination to continue in our service. Valorously you accepted every impediment as a challenge to be overcome, and you faced each challenge with enthusiasm. We try to follow suit, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Please bless us with the spiritual strength to carry on the duty you have entrusted to us. We want to be your front-line soldiers, not forlorn with dashed hopes whispered on an icy wind. Please grant us your continuing grace.

By your mercy we have the unimaginable good fortune to participate in producing your books in many languages. This intimate service helps us appreciate the value of your timeless teachings. Your books are a treasure trove of *bhakti* that has made millions of seekers experience the thrill of discovering their real identity as spirit souls.

Just as Lord Kṛṣṇa is the complete whole, your books are complete in themselves. Even the smallest of your books is a complete whole because it describes the complete whole, Kṛṣṇa. Thus even the shortest of your books has the power to make one Kṛṣṇa conscious and enrich one with transcendental knowledge and devotion. Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, thank you for the causeless mercy you continually shower upon us.

Now in this time of emergency, when godlessness expands at every moment, please infect us with your sense of urgency to publish and distribute your books in increasing quality and quantity. Let us follow your rigorous determination and serve Lord Kṛṣṇa with zeal. Please thwart any flimsy excuses and mental speculations that cause us to deviate from the path of the *mahājanas*. Dismiss our useless despair and recharge us with your optimism for spiritual perfection.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, your lotus feet are the safest shelter. Please, we pray, keep us close and guide all our endeavors.

Your humble servants at the offices of the North European BBT at New Rādhā-kuṇḍa, in Korsnās Gṛrd, Sweden.  
(written by Divyāmbarā-devī dāsī)

## Bhaktivedanta Book Trust (Spain)

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

Every year, at the time of the celebration of your divine appearance in this world, we have the fortunate opportunity to glorify you. This year I would like to glorify your books. And not just because my service is to assist you in a small part of your glorious mission of distributing Kṛṣṇa consciousness to the world through your books. I want to glorify your books simply because, for me, they are you. And I think that this is the case also for many other devotees, and it will be the case for many generations of devotees to come.

So many times I see your photos and videos, I also worship you almost every day in the form of your *mūrti,* I often read your pastimes and wonderful qualities in books written by devotees who had the unique mercy of being very close to you while you stayed in this world...In all those forms of yours I can feel your compassion, your love for us, I can see your exemplary behavior, your purity, your strong determination, your unlimited, unfathomable qualities...

But still your books are something different. As the holy name “Kṛṣṇa” is Kṛṣṇa Himself, in your books I can find my Prabhupāda, with me, teaching me, holding my hand and guiding me through all this havoc to the other side, where my real friends are eternally waiting for me. When I read any of your books, I can feel that you wrote it for me, such is your mystic power. And in these books you make yourself available completely and forever to help me walk my long journey back to Kṛṣṇa.

Well, maybe I also want to glorify you in the form of your books because my service is to assist you to distribute them. After all, this is the way I stay at your lotus feet every day, trying my best to put your words into Spanish. This is the form of yours that I am worshiping all the time. This is the way you engage me in Kṛṣṇa’s service.

hā hā prabhu koro doyā, deho more pada-chāyā

Śrīla Prabhupāda, please be merciful to me and keep me at your lotus feet. For a conditioned soul, there is no greater fear than losing the shelter of your feet. Please help me read your books, please help me study your books, please help me edit your books, please help me distribute your books. Please give me attachment for your books. They are my only hope. They are the only way I know to serve you. Please help me hear you in your books. They are the only way I know to be with you.

Your servants at the Bhaktivedanta Book Trust offices in Spain.  
(written by Hanumān dāsa)

## Vyāsa-Pūjā Homages from Other Sources

## Back to Godhead (India)

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most respectful obeisances. All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Your spiritual master, Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura, instructed you, “If you ever get money, print books.” Following this instruction, you launched *Back to Godhead* magazine in 1944.

You had nobody with you. You would personally write, edit, and proofread the articles, visit the printers, and then distribute the copies. There were many obstacles, such as a shortage of paper, a lack of funds, and a dearth of readers. Once while distributing BTG in the burning streets of Delhi, you were attacked by a cow; another time you suffered a heat-stroke—but you carried on.

You released waves of Kṛṣṇa’s mercy from the ocean of the spiritual world in the form of BTG. We have met several devotees who owe their spiritual life to BTG. By the mercy of these devotees, we are now engaged in your service. Though we have not personally met you, we can feel your presence in every aspect of our service to BTG.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you had several visions for spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness all over the world. Most of these visions have manifested, and some are in the process of manifesting. One of your visions was that BTG would sell not in the hundreds and thousands but in the millions. You envisioned that BTG would sell more copies than *Time* and *Life* magazines. Kṛṣṇa has fulfilled all your other desires, so He will definitely fulfill this desire of yours.

On this most auspicious day of your appearance in this material world to save fallen souls like us, we sincerely solicit your blessings so that we may become useful instruments in the service of BTG, the backbone of ISKCON.

Your grandchildren at the Back to Godhead offices in Mumbai, India.

## Back to Godhead (USA)

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

**The River ISKCON**

Just as the sacred Gaṅgā flows from Kṛṣṇa’s lotus feet,  
Another river comes this way, transcendent, strong and sweet.

This river, known as ISKCON, gives out blessings as it flows.  
It emanates directly from Gaurāṅga’s lotus toes.

Where Śrīla Prabhupāda walked, his footsteps set the course,  
And River ISKCON follows them with endless, matchless force.

King Bhaghīrath brought Gaṅgā down to free his fallen kin.  
Prabhupāda’s River ISKCON frees the world from sin.

The River ISKCON’s waters, though they’re filled with mud and foam,  
Can carry any swimmer back to Godhead, back to home.

While wooden boats and silver fish traverse the sacred Gaṅgā,  
The River ISKCON’s currents carry *kartāls* and *mṛdaṅga*.

*Prasād* and *kīrtan-rasa* make the waters swirl and rush;  
Desire trees called Vaiṣṇavs line the banks and make them lush.

Springing up from desert soil, their faces bright and glowing,  
These Vaiṣṇavas love Prabhupād and keep the river flowing.

The boulders in this river are the stones of greed and lust.  
The River ISKCON pummels them, reducing them to dust.

The river runs to left or right, whichever way it likes,  
While laughing at the tiny men who throw up dams and dikes.

My children, when I die please burn my corpse, then kindly spread  
My ashes in the River ISKCON. Leave your tears unshed.

For Prabhupāda’s River ISKCON pours into an ocean  
Full of *bhakti-rasāmṛta* (nectar of devotion).

Śrīla Prabhupāda’s servants at the Back to Godhead offices in Alachua, Florida, USA.  
(written by Kālakaṇṭha dāsa)

## Bhaktivedanta Archives

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Recently during our weekly “Prabhupāda Night,” devotees in our community watched your film biography *Your Ever Well-wisher*. In it the owner of the Radha Press in Delhi was interviewed. He mentioned that in the 1950’s and early 60’s when you were struggling alone, many times you had no money to pay for the printing of your *Back to Godhead* magazine. So one time he asked you, “Swamiji, why do you do this? Why don’t you give it up?” Your reply was, “It is my mission.”

On this day in particular it is the duty of your disciples and granddisciples to understand that we have to make “your mission” our mission. Although there may be insurmountable obstacles and the situation may seem hopeless, simply by our following in your footsteps there will be all success.

In the dedication to *The Nectar of Devotion,* the poem by Śrīla Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura describes that the six Gosvāmīs are “very expert in scrutinizingly studying all the revealed scriptures with the aim of establishing eternal religious principles for the benefit of all human beings.” How well this also describes yourself! You created so many translations and commentaries in such a short span of time, and how pertinent they are to the modern society. But apart from your literary work, you also created so many different kinds of projects: temples, farms, *āśramas,* schools, and even a scientific institute! Your mission has expanded the parameters of the Vaiṣṇava mission beyond anything seen before.

In *The Nectar of Devotion,* Chapter 6, “How to Discharge Devotional Service,” you have outlined the rules and regulations and basic principles to be followed by Vaiṣṇavas. The first five of the sixty-four principles are:

1\. Accepting the shelter of the lotus feet of a bona fide spiritual master. 2. Becoming initiated by the spiritual master and learning how to discharge devotional service from him. 3. Obeying the orders of the spiritual master with faith and devotion. 4. Following in the footsteps of great *ācāryas* (teachers) under the direction of the spiritual master. 5. Inquiring from the spiritual master how to advance in Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

You state that these are the “preliminary necessities for beginning the discharge of devotional service in regulative principles” and “in the beginning, if a neophyte devotee observes the above-mentioned principles, surely he will quickly make good advancement in Kṛṣṇa consciousness.”

On this, your Vyāsa-pūjā day, we strive to reaffirm these principles. By your mercy we have been plucked from the material pool, and only by serving your mission can we remain fixed in devotional service, the “eternal mission.”

Your servants at the Bhaktivedanta Archives, Sandy Ridge, North Carolina, USA.

## Bhaktivedanta Center

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most respectful and prostrated obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

One of the many wonderful aspects of Kṛṣṇa consciousness is that one has tremendous opportunity for glorifying and remembering the bona fide spiritual master, and this is recommended in Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura’s *Śrī Śrī Gurv-aṣṭaka*. It is a fact that you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, are the transcendental “well” we go to for our spiritual sustenance. You are giving the aspiring devotees incalculable and much needed spiritual information, but our attempts at thanking you remain woefully inadequate.

You relate that, in Kṛṣṇa’s “opinion,” the *jīva* is eternally part and parcel of Him. Yet, because of crores of lives in the material world, the *jīva* has become covered over and therefore forgets. Forgetfulness is part of the scenario of the material world, and to appreciate this we need only think back several years to our spiritual position before you came to the West. Practically speaking we were all in an unrecognizable state when you came to America, yet *you* were, and are, perfectly situated. So you took it upon yourself to re-educate us. Our situation resembled that of someone with amnesia, who has to be taught everything all over again. We were all in that position.

In America, where meat is the main course at mealtime, children usually do not want to eat their vegetables, even though their parents wisely instruct them that it is in their best interest to do so. In the role of our spiritual parent, you instruct us in every class and purport that the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mahā-mantra* will give us spiritual health. And just as children mature to the understanding that it is in their best interest to eat their vegetables, we are seeing that this transcendental chanting, though unpalatable in the beginning, is truly our spiritual lifeline.

Mistakenly one may first take up *bhakti-yoga* as a means to save oneself, yet you, always the patient spiritual father, explain by your flawless instructions that being “saved” is simply a by-product of the revival of our eternal relationship with Lord Kṛṣṇa. We’re ashamed to admit that in our so-called culture many *jīvas* know the pedigree of their pet or know who the big sports stars are, yet they haven’t a clue as to their own identity. This is the condition in which you found America in the 60’s.

You explain that the goal of the whole yoga system is to not allow society to become restless, yet born-again materialists, the *vikarmīs,* are convinced that everything extant is for their own personal enjoyment. Their restlessness is the status quo, and so their standard is to engage in anything and everything *except* the taking up of bona fide spiritual life, i.e., Kṛṣṇa consciousness. As you describe, the paths leading to profit, adoration, and distinction lead only to frustration and misery. As many try for these goals wholeheartedly, we’re struck with your assessment that these paths are simply a means of loitering here in the material world. You explain that for success, even materially, one has to simply do one’s business and move on. Your specific recommendation for defeating society’s restlessness, and our loitering habit, is that one simply do the needful, spiritually, and go on to return back home, back to Godhead. Thus you come as the transcendental truant officer to collar the loiterers and bring them to their actual spiritual platform.

Of course, we chose to not be immediately swayed by spiritual instruction and threw up many obstacles in our own path so that we could continue the miserable status quo. Presently we encounter these manmade obstacles quite often in our attempts to preach Kṛṣṇa consciousness at the local university, and the defective reasoning many use to avoid taking up the process is much the same as it was in the 60’s—with only a fresh coat of paint added. All disbelief in the process of Kṛṣṇa consciousness stems from two main objections—the objector doesn’t believe the preacher’s conclusions, or they don’t care. From the not-so-hip self-styled street philosophers of the 60’s to the time-honored professors licking the outside of the glass bottle of Vedic honey, you defeat them all. Not only do you defeat them, but they are charmed by your *vapu* and *vāṇī,* resulting in their becoming devotees of Kṛṣṇa. Hopefully we can follow in your footsteps, fallen as we are, and attempt to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness to this new generation of loitering souls.

Thus it is our desire that we all remember how unfortunate we were until we met you. Before you came we didn’t have a clue as to our identity, we were wasting the human form of life, and our future looked bleak. We didn’t pull ourselves up by our bootstraps. Your Divine Grace pulled us up. Regardless of how we’re Godrelatives, the whole bona fide show is due to your benevolence, which is the hallmark of the *mahā-bhāgavata* and *mahājana*. We pray that we’re able to do some service for you before the curtain falls.

Desiring to remain always in your lotus shadow as your lowly servants, we remain

The devotees at the Bhaktivedanta Center, Charlottesville, Virginia, USA.  
(written by Amit Ācāra dāsa)

## Bhaktivedanta College of Education and Culture

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our prostrated obeisances in the dust of your divine lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Loving Grace!

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have instructed us,

If you feel at all indebted to me then you should preach vigorously like me. That is the proper way to repay me. Of course, no one can repay the debt to the spiritual master, but the spiritual master is very much pleased by such an attitude by the disciple. \[Letter, 14 August 1976\]

Indeed, we feel eternally indebted to you for rescuing us from the darkness of ignorance. You have undergone so many trials and tribulations to give us the brilliant torchlight of transcendental knowledge and the wonderful process of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. How could we possibly not feel indebted to you?

Desiring that we become well versed in your books so that we could preach Kṛṣṇa consciousness by both our actions and our words, you wrote,

I am very much stressing nowadays that my students shall increase their reading of my books and try to understand them from different angles of vision. Each sloka can be seen from many, many angles of vision, so become practiced in seeing things like this. If we are selling the books but we do not know what is inside the book, that will be a farce.... \[Letter, 16 June 1972\]

We want good preachers also. Preaching shouldn’t depend on me only. My disciples should become all good preachers, and that depends on studying the books nicely so that you can arrive at the right conclusion. \[Letter, 5 July 1971\]

So study my books and reproduce the purports in your own language. You should instruct your temple presidents to preach like this. This is preaching. We haven’t got to invent something by our fertile brain for preaching. Everything is there. One who is expert for presenting these things before the audience so they can conveniently understand, this is a successful preacher. You have only to speak what Krsna has said. Then you become a preacher. \[Letter 9 November 1975\]

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have given us everything, and through your causeless mercy, direction, and inspiration you have also given us the Bhaktivedanta College of Education and Culture (BCEC), a forum for systematically increasing *śravaṇam* and *kīrtanam* (hearing and chanting) in the association of devotees. Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for engaging us in systematically studying and teaching all the wonderful books you have given to us with your Bhaktivedanta purports.

Since the inception of the Bhaktivedanta College of Education and Culture in July 1991, by Your Divine Grace’s blessing a total of 1,014 students had graduated our various courses by the end of 1998. From its humble beginnings in Durban, the college has expanded to Dallas, Hong Kong, Houston, Johannesburg, Mauritius, Moscow, Manila, Pretoria, Trinidad, and Washington, DC. Simply by pleasing you and the previous *ācāryas* and by following your instructions, we will enjoy all success:

All the great *ācāryas* strongly recommend that people be given a chance to hear about the Supreme Lord. Then success is assured. The more we cleanse the dirt of material attachment from our hearts, the more we will be attracted by Kṛṣṇa’s name, form, qualities, paraphernalia, and activities. This is the sum and substance of the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement. \[*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 5.18.11, purport\]

Śrīla Prabhupāda, during a *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* lecture in 1972, in Melbourne you said,

I am laboring so hard for you, but you don’t take advantage. Don’t take advantage of eating and sleeping. Take advantage of these books. Then your life will be successful. My duty—I have given you so valuable things, day and night trying to convince you, each word to word. And if you don’t take advantage of this, then what can I do for you?

Every word spoken by Your Divine Grace is the Absolute Truth, and from our experience we can see that the BCEC is benefiting everyone, both the students and the teachers, who are students themselves. There are so many stories and realizations from students that we can share. “At last my search has ended.” “The courses have made me realize that devotees are our real friends.” “I have realized that I have to realign my priorities for ultimate spiritual benefit.” “Our lives are much happier because it revolves around service to God, Kṛṣṇa.” These are some realizations from students who are associating with Your Divine Grace through your precious books.

Many of your wonderful disciples are appreciating the BCEC and encouraging everyone to take advantage of the BCEC. His Holiness Tamāl Krishna Goswami mentioned,

The classes presented by Bhaktivedanta College provide an essential educational experience which is both intellectually stimulating and life-enriching, and which is designed to graduate students as Bhaktivedantas—fulfilled individuals, eager to contribute to the welfare of society.

His Holiness Romapāda Swami added,

Bhaktivedanta College is providing a systematic education, designed to train congregation members how to perform the brahminical activity of training other congregation members in the practices, etiquette, and understanding of devotional service.

His Holiness Bhakti Tīrtha Swami said,

The BCEC program in South Africa is the most sophisticated spiritual institution in the world and is creating a tremendous revolution in consciousness. The program gives students vigorous and scrutinizing techniques on how to appreciate fine aspects of the philosophy and apply it to their lives.

His Holiness Giriraja Swami reminded us that

We should never forget how Śrīla Prabhupāda has saved us from such a quagmire of different speculations, theories, and processes and brought us to the path of true spiritual understanding. The only way to repay one’s debt for this knowledge is to distribute it by teaching it and sharing it with others.

His Holiness Bhakti Chāru Swami said,

His Divine Grace Śrīla A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda gave us everything, and it is up to us to approach his books with the proper understanding, with the proper mood, and with the proper attitude of surrender and gratitude.

His Holiness Bhakti Caitanya Swami mentioned,

Through spiritual education human society will be able to understand the goal of life and how to achieve it; otherwise there is no real possibility of a happy future. So let us work together to fulfill this ambition of Śrīla Prabhupāda.

His Holiness Krishna Dāsa Swami said,

The BCEC is so important to help re-educate us about the greatest necessity of our human life. It is clear that in the near future more and more people from different parts of the world will appreciate the efforts of this College in producing qualified leadership in society.

His Grace Pārtha Sārathi Prabhu mentioned,

By regularly studying Śrīla Prabhupāda’s books in an organized way, one can derive the highest benefit in this human form of life.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, please continue to shower your blessings on us so that we may continue to study, teach, and follow your instructions in your books. So many times you have reminded us of our duty in this regard, as in the following letters:

The information is there in my books so if you strictly adhere to them then your program of teaching will be successful. First of all you must teach by your personal example. This is the principle of Lord Caitanya Mahaprabhu (apani acari bhakti pracaram). So you yourself must chant 16 rounds and follow the regulative principles and automatically they will do as you are doing. Then they will become strong Vaisnavas. \[Letter, 19 August 1974\]

I am pleased to hear that you are chanting 16 rounds daily and reading my books regularly and following the four rules. In my books the philosophy of Krishna Consciousness is explained fully, so if there is anything which you do not understand, then you simply have to read again and again. By reading daily the knowledge will be revealed to you, and by this process your spiritual life will develop. \[Letter, 22 November 1974\]

I have given the information in my books, how to please the spiritual master. Simply by following the rules and regulations very carefully, by chanting at least 16 rounds daily, by reading our books, and by always engaging one’s self in the service of Krishna, a person will please the spiritual master and Krishna both. \[Letter, 28 May 1975\]

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for giving us your eternal shelter in the form of your instructions, and please continue to engage us through the Bhaktivedanta College of Education and Culture. Please forgive us for any shortcomings and offenses that we may have caused unto your lotus feet.

Your eternal servants at the Bhaktivedanta College of Education and Culture, South Africa

## Bhaktivedanta Institute (Berkeley and Mumbai)

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace, by whose mercy we can understand and assimilate the philosophy and theology of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu!

Today is the most auspicious day for all the living beings in this world. On this day you descended into this world from Kṛṣṇa’s abode to give full benedictions to the conditioned souls so that they can attain life’s ultimate purpose, love of Śrī Kṛṣṇa. You have built the most powerful spiritual movement in recorded history, one that will last for 10,000 years. Your purity is the force that is pushing on this movement. Your books are the basis of civilization for this planet. Preaching your glories will be the essence of sustaining this movement through these troubled times.

We owe a great debt to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. You performed great austerities to make the mercy of Lord Caitanya accessible to everyone. Now, although it is impossible to repay that debt, we must try. For us, the form of that repayment can center only on seriously and wholeheartedly following up the extraordinary and unique plan that you yourself unveiled in the scientific preaching front, one that you are enabling us to get a faint grasp of by your own causeless mercy. Your Divine Grace’s plan on behalf of Lord Caitanya seems to include the unveiling of a new science of matter that marks a hitherto unknown midpoint between the gross science of the modern West and the subtle science of the ancient East. When it gets fully manifest, this science will reveal yet another facet of your wonderful personality and unique position in Lord Caitanya’s preaching mission, and add to your already established fame all over the three worlds.

We earnestly hope and pray that whatever we are trying to do, albeit insignificant, properly forms a part of your grand plan and meets with your approval. You have engaged us most mercifully in the Bhaktivedanta Institute, the scientific preaching arm of ISKCON. Our very ability to render any kind of service on this extraordinarily subtle and important preaching front is your divine blessing.

*bhavad-vidhā bhāgavatās tīrtha-bhūtāḥ svayaṁ vibho*  
*tīrthī-kurvanti tīrthāni svāntaḥ-sthena gadābhṛtā*

**“My lord, devotees like your good self are verily holy places personified. Because you carry the Personality of Godhead within your heart, you turn all places into places of pilgrimage.” (*****Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*** **1.13.10)**

You have personally placed your lotus feet on the Berkeley campus and at New Jagannātha Purī. You have addressed the students and faculty on the campus. You have thus turned the campus into a holy place. Now you are also engaging us in your sacred mission. The ways of the Lord and his pure devotee are inscrutable. We pray for the proper intelligence by which we can understand your desires for further development of the Bhaktivedanta Institute in the Bay Area and the proper execution of our duties for your divine pleasure.

Your servants at the Bhaktivedanta Institute branch in Berkeley, California, USA, and Mumbai, India.

## Bhaktivedanta Swami International Gurukula

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

As I walked my seven-year-old son back to the *āśrama,* he reads the marble plaque on the wall, which describes how you laid the cornerstone of the *gurukula* on September 13, 1975. “Which corner?” he asked. I smiled and said I didn’t know.

What I do know is that your gurukula is still here in Vṛndāvana and that we are trying hard to fulfill your dream of a Kṛṣṇa-centered educational system, a dream almost lost through many years of pain and sorrow and failure.

You had a dream, and we have made it our mission to fulfill that dream: to create an environment that inspires the students to take up the challenge of an exemplary life of Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

And it’s not easy. But this is your school, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and we pray you will guide us to make the right changes and choices. “Love them,” you said. “Teach them,” you said. “Feed them well,” you said. “Keep them warm and safe and strong,” you said. “Lead your life in full Kṛṣṇa consciousness and they will follow,” you said.

Last year we spent a lot of money renovating the primary-school *āśrama.* I was worried about the cost. Then someone told me, “This is the last *āśrama* school for young children in ISKCON. Do it nicely.” I felt it was you speaking to me, Śrīla Prabhupāda. I could almost hear you say, “Do it nicely.”

We did it nicely, and I think you would be very pleased.

We pray that we keep the spiritual program strong and dynamic here at your school and that the hearts of the children will be filled with fine memories of their early years in Kṛṣṇa consciousness—a life rich with the flavors and festivals of the Vaiṣṇava culture, of austerities that were fun, and friendships that were strong.

You are our master. We believe in you and your vision for education. We want that your teaching, which saved and changed our lives, will touch and change the lives of others. We want to show the world that real education is something complete and whole, not lacking anything. Real education helps one realize who he is and how he can use his unique gifts and talents in Kṛṣṇa’s service, thus purifying his existence and at the end of life going back home, back to Godhead.

*Jaya* Śrīla Prabhupāda! May you forever speak loudly in our ears and thus keep us faithful to your instructions and mood.

Your servants at the Bhaktivedanta Swami International Gurukula, Vṛndāvana, India.  
(written by Ānanda Vṛndāvana-devī dāsī)

## Campina Grande

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances unto your lotus feet. All glories to you, the savior of the fallen souls of Kali-yuga!

Dear master, our humble offering is always to try to follow your instructions, spreading your mission to our best capacity. So, we gather here every week to chant, preach, and glorify you, and we serve *prasādam* daily in our restaurant as another way to help people develop their Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

We pray to Lord Gaurāṅga that we never forget you in whichever condition we may be, because just by remembering you we can gain the power to remain in Kṛṣṇa’s service.

Please, allow us to always remain as dust at your lotus feet.  
Your servants in Campina Grande, Brazil.

## Caxias do Sul

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

There is no limit to your mercy, as there are no limits to the activities and pastimes of Lord Kṛṣṇa.

There is no value in life if it is only a dead attempt to get relief from pain, fear, and anxiety.

You came and taught us what life really means, and you gave us a chance to live.

Demoniac traditions and customs are not stumbling blocks to your mercy, as shown in our town, where even the butcher’s sons are becoming devotees.

We hope we can always be an instrument of your desire.

The devotees of the Nāma-haṭṭa program in Caxias do Sul, Brazil,.

## College Traveling Saṅkīrtana Party

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

\[Pṛthu Mahārāja said to the four Kumāras:\] The Supreme Personality of Godhead is always anxious to elevate the living entities, who are His parts and parcels, and for their special benefit, the Lord travels all over the world in the form of self-realized persons like you....

Although you are traveling in all planetary systems, people cannot know you, just as they cannot know the Supersoul although He is within everyone’s heart as the witness of everything. \[*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 4.22.16 and 9\]

Śrīla Prabhupāda, by the appearance of Your Divine Grace in this material world, eternally condemned conditioned souls, such as ourselves, have been benefited with the opportunity to know you and the Supersoul, although ordinarily you are both unknowable. You’ve explained this good fortune to be as likely as a turtle in a river coming up for breath and by chance poking his head through the hole in a log floating downstream.

Lord Caitanya describes our good fortune in his teachings to Rūpa Gosvāmī, as follows:

*brahmāṇḍa bhramite kona bhāgyavān jīva*  
*guru-kṛṣṇa-prasāde pāya bhakti-latā-bīja*

**“According to their karma, all living entities are wandering throughout the entire universe. Some of them are being elevated to the upper planetary systems, and some are going down into the lower planetary systems. Out of many millions of wandering living entities, one who is very fortunate gets an opportunity to associate with a bona fide spiritual master by the grace of Kṛṣṇa. By the mercy of both Kṛṣṇa and the spiritual master, such a person receives the seed of the creeper of devotional service.”**

We see so many young, intelligent students wandering all over the universities from one classroom to another, from one major to another, looking for knowledge, eternity, and bliss. If presented with Kṛṣṇa consciousness, they are unable to recognize it as the real substance they are hankering for. This eternally unfortunate condition is unchangeable without some divine intervention.

Lord Caitanya, being always anxious to elevate the living entities, has, for our special benefit, traveled all over the world in the form of your good self (*sākṣād-dharitvena samasta-śāstrair uktas tathā bhāvyata eva sadbhiḥ*). Although you have made yourself easily available in the form of your many books, the souls of Kali-yuga are so unfortunate that they have no attraction for them. Knowing this, you have also given us the secret weapons to infect them with attraction, namely *prasādam* and *harināma-saṅkīrtana*.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we are trying to implement this transcendental formula of yours. Please bless us so that we may act as your assistants in carrying out Lord Caitanya’s sublime mission of delivering the conditioned souls. Although undeserving, we pray to always feel your presence so that we may not lose our enthusiasm despite all difficulties. If you are with us, then we are confident that the entire *paramparā* and the Pañca-tattva are also with us.

Bowing down at your lotus feet,  
Your servants at College TSKP.

## Festival of India

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to you!

How can we ever begin to repay our inestimable debt to you?

You have made this pure *bhakti-yoga* process freely available to each and every person despite mundane disqualifications. Therefore, by your transcendental presence within this Kali-yuga, countless members of the material world’s misguided population are being saved from gliding down into the abominations of hellish existence.

The priceless privilege of performing devotional service to the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa, is no doubt the ultimate gift that could ever be given. No amount of gratitude will ever sufficiently balance the scales with which we may futilely attempt to measure your unlimited kindness to us.

Certainly the words emanating from your lotus mouth are our guiding lights, beacons in the dark night of Kali-yuga ignorance. Moreover, your compassionate understanding provides us with warm welcoming lamps in the windows to the spiritual world. How can we disregard them?

As the captain of a ship skillfully directs his crew to the ultimate destination, you have so expertly plotted the course of our voyage from one celebratory event to another. In this way, we are all able to go back home together.

Yes, the Festival of India resembles a ship plying from harbor to harbor, delivering your treasure to all corners.

The ports of call are your centers for Vedic knowledge and culture (the temples we eagerly serve), and the treasure we deliver is the experience of Kṛṣṇa consciousness—the opportunity to hear and chant the holy names of Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa, Gaura-Nitāi, and Jagannātha, Baladeva, and Subhadrā, along with the taste of Kṛṣṇa’s mercy (*prasādam*), and much more.

Our endeavor to fulfill your vision of a daily festival of Kṛṣṇa consciousness has fallen short of the goal. Indeed, our labors may appear to be no more than a bedlam of useless functions enacted by utter fools. Yet you make everything right by your sanctioning orders.

Our hopes, our prayers, our sincerest desires are simply to please Your Divine Grace. May we always be firmly fixed in your instructions.

Thank you very much. Hare Kṛṣṇa, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Your servants at the Festival of India, Sandy Ridge, North Carolina.  
(written by Phāṇi Bhūṣaṇa dāsa)

## Food for Life (Brazil)

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

On this all-auspicious day, even the demigods are making arrangements to pay homage to you.

Mankind is in such a degraded state, with rampant moral corruption and violence against one another and animals. The list of sins is infinite. Nevertheless, despite these conditions you remained undisturbed and preached Kṛṣṇa consciousness, following your spiritual master’s order.

Therefore we want to express our thanks to you for your determined devotion in accepting the mission of saving this helpless civilization of Kali-yuga.

Your mission, which is now also our mission, has had great success, rescuing many fallen souls like me. Only a pure devote of Lord Kṛṣṇa could show such mercy, which is so essential to the world.

*śamo damas tapaḥ śaucaṁ kṣāntir ārjavam eva ca*  
*jñānaṁ vijñānam āstikyaṁ brahma-karma svabhāva-jam*

**“Peacefulness, self-control, austerity, purity, tolerance, honesty, knowledge, wisdom and religiousness—these are the natural qualities by which the brāhmaṇas work.” (*****Bhagavad-gītā*** **18.42)**

Doubtless you applied all these qualities and many others in the service of your Guru Mahārāja and Śrī Kṛṣṇa.

*Jaya* Śrīla Prabhupāda! All glories to you!

Your servants at the Food for Life offices in Brazil.  
(written by Mathurā Nātha dāsa)

## Food for Life (Global)

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

On behalf of all your *prasādam* distributors worldwide, I beg you to please accept my humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to *śrī-kṛṣṇa-saṅkīrtana*!

As I write this offering, NATO continues to bomb Serbia, while hundreds of thousands of ethnic Albanians struggle to survive somewhere between Kosovo and Macedonia. These people have practically lost everything, even minimal human amenities, as they huddle together in makeshift shelters. For them it is the worst nightmare. I have witnessed this scene before in Sarajevo, Grozny, and Sukhumi—human beings reduced to living like animals.

In Macedonia, where hundreds of thousands of refugees gather, political “red tape” has stifled most of the humanitarian efforts streaming in from abroad. Everybody is frustrated. Meanwhile, back here in America the media goes out of its way to demonize the Serbian dictator, thus deflecting whatever negativity there may be about the US’s military strikes.

On the other side of the fence, Serb television tells a different story, accusing the US for all the suffering and painting NATO as the aggressors. Russia warns the US to stop bombing, or else...but then balks, knowing that the survival of their economy is in the hands of the U.S. All the while, India and Pakistan continue testing their nuclear warheads. And so, in its usual complex, interdependently paradoxical manner, the material world goes on!

In the midst of all this, we receive letters from devotees from both sides of the conflict in Serbia telling of their plans to help the distressed with *kṛṣṇa-prasādam*! It is refreshing to know that amongst the bickering of these “demon-crazy” nations, Kṛṣṇa consciousness is still very much alive! Ironically, it was the ISKCON temple in Serbia that assisted in providing supplies to the Food for Life program in Sarajevo during the previous conflict! Your devotees are always transcendental to these mundane politics.

I quote parts of those letters here. Megha-rūpa dāsī and Bhaktin Gogana from Belgrade write:

I am in Belgrade, Serbia, former Yugoslavia. Most probably you have heard about the situation here in our country now. NATO forces are bombing our country. In this situation we would like to start some kind of Food for Life program. We need your advice from where to start? For now we do not have any sources for bhoga for prasadam. Should we approach some humanitarian organization?  
Presently there are no refugees inside the country, and people are working at their job, but nobody knows if they will get any money for that. For now it is not so bad, but soon it could be if they continue fighting. There are many people in the Center of the town and we would like to give them some prasadam before they lose their bodies....

Mukunda dāsa from Slovenia writes, “Is FFL Global planning any kind of action regarding refuges from Kosovo. In Slovenia we are planning to start a small help program. I hope it will be successful.”

These letters are encouraging and they illustrate just how compassionate and thoughtful your devotees are. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have blessed your devotees with the gift of *kṛṣṇa-prasādam* and instilled within them a sense of responsibility to help those in distress. A Vaiṣṇava is certainly an ocean of mercy and the savior of fallen, and what better way to demonstrate that than by giving *kṛṣṇa-prasādam* in time of war?

Śrīla Prabhupāda, destiny will have it that I am a little overwhelmed with *gṛhastha* responsibilities at the moment, and as a result I am not able to give the time I would like to my Food for Life service. Nonetheless, you can be sure that my commitment to this mission will never end, because there will always be suffering people in this material world who will need *kṛṣṇa-prasādam*.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, my gut feeling is that what we are seeing now in Serbia is the beginning of something devastating—a massive world calamity! My words will bare truth in time. I am no psychic. I speak from common sense. The earth is aching—wracked with pain from the continuous abuse of its resources and inhabitants. Mother Nature must strike back. It seems inevitable. But how? Only Kṛṣṇa knows. But she must strike back.

In these unstable times, we have no other shelter than Lord Kṛṣṇa and your divine teachings. And what did you teach us? Chant Hare Kṛṣṇa and distribute *kṛṣṇa-prasādam*! Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

May we die on the battlefield fulfilling your order!

Your prasādam distributors at Food for Life (Global).  
(written by Priyavrata dāsa)

## Hamburg

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

Among all German cities, Hamburg is the most blessed because you stayed here for two and a half weeks in the summer of 1969. In fact, the then Hamburg center, although small, was the first European Hare Kṛṣṇa temple.

After your departure from the tiny storefront, driven by your desire to expand the movement, the devotees soon found a bigger place, and the Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa Deities installed there a little later were the first ones on the continent. By and by, all of today’s senior German preachers joined here in Hamburg. Please forgive us our pride in presenting to you that we are maintaining the location of the movement’s early days as a preaching center for our Sunday programs. The place is in the same condition as in the late sixties and early seventies, and by remembering your presence here the atmosphere gets surcharged with the ecstasy of the holy name, and thus we are allowed to continue and increase our devotional service.

Thank you for this, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Lord Kṛṣṇa, His paraphernalia, and His devotees are eternal and full of bliss and knowledge, and so is the chanting of the holy name. By following the instructions emanating from your lotus mouth, we gradually attain perfection. We do not need more than this.

Your servants in Hamburg, Germany.

## Hare Krishna World

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

I am honored to be writing an offering on behalf of *Hare Krishna World,* which has been an ongoing inspiration over the years. Even though I was residing in a remote corner of the world, *Hare Krishna World* made me feel I was an integral part of Śrīla Prabhupāda’s movement.

How blessed is this day! when you took birth to give us life, thousands of us fallen souls. We celebrate this blessed day! when you set forth upon your long and mythic journey to our midst. How wondrous are your magic ways! that you appear and wake us from our ancient sleep. I celebrate this blessed day!

Room, 518 Frederick Street, San Francisco, January 30, 1967:

“Oh, so it is you who have made all this money?”

“...uhh, yes...uh Swami, I, well, all of us...”

“No, how much you have made last night?”

“...uhh, I’m not really sure, I think about $2,000...”

“OOOHH! \[eyebrows arched, mock-huge eyes\] Do you know book-keeping, how to keep the books?”

“...no, not really, I...”

“Then I shall teach you. One who makes money should know how it is spent. Mukunda, bring little paper...Here, you just come here, sit next to me, just here:” new this scent of mustard-oil and sandal—new, sublime this touch of soft gold and saffron-colored person brushing my left elbow, electricity coursing, thinking:

“At this moment I am born: I’ve found the Magic Man!”

Oh, we were a restless bunch, Śrīla Prabhupāda, my generation, remember? We wouldn’t hear philosophy, so many philosophies were there, yogi-this and Tibetan-that, just so many words—we wanted magic, music and Magic...The doors of perception had recently parted, and we had glimpsed for moments some higher, more wonderful place—Seekers were we then for the Magic Man, someone with extraordinary powers, the Ringmaster, someone to lift the veil and lead us into that higher realm of being—let the door slam shut behind us!

...Your bedroom-slippered feet glide by, not inches from my prostrate head...that same faint trail of mustard and sandal (roses too?)—we rise to see your lustrous face, laughing and victorious—(or dark with purpose, eyes grave and hooded, as if tired, as if all this were some distraction from a greater...) You take a moment to sit down on your dais, put on those glasses that from my angle expand your eyes to impossible size, mutter something—“krssna”—deeply in your throat, tap the mike a time or two—and BEGIN:

You sing and dance—you tell of heroes, adventure, daring, and risk—of perfect comfort and the place this exists, the easy way to get there—that bliss is NOW! the place is HERE! Chant Hare Kṛṣṇa and SEE!

And we see YOU: the effulgent example, the effortless laughing perfect man, free from doubt and flaw and error, the consummate actor, the great trickster, the grandfather and child of our concern—the mystic sage, next-door neighbor, the seer into our deepest thoughts: You, the Swami, Swamiji, weaving us deeper, deeper, and deeper, into your magic spell...

“Just understand little this Kṛṣṇa consciousness life, take it up, it is so nice, you will feel—then you go out and spread...”

You weave our weak threads into the unbroken cord of the *sampradāya,* bound us in the fabric of *sanātana- dharma*—then bid us go forth...Kṛṣṇa consciousness, you explain, has throughout time been an evangelistic MOVEMENT, to be spread vigorously: “Our purpose is to spread Kṛṣṇa all over the planet,” you repeatedly say. Spread, spread, spread, you say, and spread becomes our mission: move, preach, risk, and dare...

“When Kṛṣṇa sees His devotee risk little suffering for Him, He must immediately Himself come...Kṛṣṇa will help you, you will see.”

As you coddled us, you threw us from the nest—as you sang to us, you scolded us, your callow godling kids; and as we haltingly made our ways around the globe, you guided us and protected us at every step:

(Ring, Ring, Ring) “Śyāmasundar? Bhaktivedanta Swami here. I have not heard from you in some time, so I was wondering...”

In phone calls, telegrams, frequent letters you advise on every point:

Letter, ACBS to Śyāmasundara dāsa, 10/10/68:

“Since I received your letter dated September 26th \[two weeks!\] I have not heard anything from you, so I am very much anxious to know about the London affairs.”

Then:

(1) Goads me with news that “Sivananda has already opened our ISKCON Temple in Berlin;”

(2) Hints at how to find a house to rent in London;

(3) Instructs me to transfer “$1,655” to a bank account (full details given) in NYC;

(4) Advises on ways to earn an income to maintain our *yātrā*;

(5) Encloses drawings for Yamunā as models for setting up “certificates”;

(6) Prescribes Sloan’s Liniment for Mālātī’s rheumatism.

Then:

“Kindly let me know replies for all the points raised herein...”

Always our parent and CEO, instigator of our transcendental rivalries—our defender, friend, and confidant, sole source of our enjoyment...

People often ask me now, How did you do it? Dare to take on the world like that, accomplish such big things? I answer: “We had the best example! Here’s this seventy-year-old person, our spiritual master, who’s not afraid of anything, no risk too great for him. We saw he dared the impossible, we saw Kṛṣṇa at his side and how empowered he thus became—we simply followed that precedent. He did it, we did it, YOU can do it too! What’s holding you back? You are young and energetic: dare the moon, go for broke, take a leap for Kṛṣṇa—He’ll catch you in His arms!”

“So the conductor, out of joke, asked him \[two year-old boy\], ‘Give me your fare.’ So he first of all said like this: ‘I have no money.’ So the conductor said, ‘Then you get down.’ He immediately said, ‘Oh, here is my father’” (makes some gesture) (laughter)...

Of all your aspects, Prabhupāda, your laughing mood outshines them all: Chuckles mostly, making a point, chuckle by chuckle as we talk, building our soft inner glee—Suddenly the guffaw, the quick delight—and sometimes, surprised by the overtly absurd: a full-rounded belly-laugh trembles your body for minutes on end, tears streaming from tight-squinted eyes, your mouth—a mile of your teeth—wide-open frozen in a rictus of hilarity!

“You are searching out friends to talk with, to joke with, to love. Kṛṣṇa is sitting there for that purpose.”

In *The Nectar of Devotion* you inform us that “sharing laughter” is compatible with and a symptom of the transcendental ecstasies: fraternal, conjugal, parental, chivalrous, devotional states of bliss...

“If we are sitting together, the oneness is the process of service, that’s all. But there are joking. They are cutting jokes. There are some varieties of food. That is *ānanda,* that is bliss...”

Just punch in “laughter” on the Vedabase for your lectures: In 1975 alone, “laughter” was captured 736 times on tapes of your lectures. When I browse back, Śrīla Prabhupāda, through the philosophy discussions we had in the early ’70’s, I sound like a straight man feeding you material for your hilarious one-liners!

Oh, thank you for the laughter, for my life, Śrīla Prabhupāda! How blessed is this day! Thank you for ten thousand brothers and sisters, sweet offspring of your love!

Single-handedly you have breathed life, too, into the gasping desiccated shell of Vedic culture and civilization. You have revived the eternal roots of humankind. Just see: Schoolchildren throughout the world now take karma and the cycle of birth, death, and rebirth as given facts in their lives; the accepted history of human presence on this planet has been widely called into question; you led the wave of Indian migration to the West, opening the door for millions of your countrymen to follow you and be at home; I’ve seen your potency in Russia, where from a tiny seed, one copy of your *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is,* thousands of devotional creepers now flourish; in India the “Krishna” name is now plastered all over the place: every billboard, shop-sign, pop-song, and product...One glance at the *Hare Krishna World* newspaper tells it all: Your influence has reached incalculable levels—I’m not sure but that your books now outnumber the volumes of the Bible and Shakespeare!

Wondrous activities of the Magic Man. I guess you could say someone like Einstein was a magician of sorts, revealing some fragments of life’s great mysteries—but you are The Magician without equal, the One, the Only, step right up folks and see him: change the nature of life itself...

“I am not a magician. My only credit is that I am presenting Kṛṣṇa as He is. The magic will act as soon as you become a pure devotee of Kṛṣṇa. The magic will be done by Kṛṣṇa, not by me or you.”

My one great concern for your Society as it becomes Corporate ISKCON is that we may forget that you are in center stage, our Ringleader and Chief Magician, and this is how you smell, and that is how you gesture, and these are the tears of ecstasy we see you shed (eyes closed) as your voice turns to gravel and slowly chokes to silence—your wisdom, sure, but as well your moods, emotions hard and soft, your compassion and sensitivity, your grasp of the absurd and an ever-ready wit and humor, even unto wild abandon...We need always to remember your practical and elastic sense of dealing with ever-changing circumstances—and, most important, that you love all of Kṛṣṇa’s creatures, but in particular you love us, each and every one of your aspiring servants...All the printed pages cannot outshine the glories of your personality!

tap...(bird singing)...tap tap

“What is this flower?...What is this garbage?...What is this ‘light-speed, speed of lightning?’”

(tap with cane or point with bag-draped finger)...tap..tap..(over the shoulder or straight-ahead, with fresh-morning voice:)

“I want that you shall all go back with me, back to Godhead”...tap..tap..“Let us go back now...”

tap..tap..tap

How blessed is this day!
