Śrīla Prabhupāda’s servants at Hare Krishna World newspaper.  
(written by Śyāmasundara dāsa Adhikārī)

## Institute for Applied Spiritual Technology

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

You are our beloved grandfather. We honor, give thanks, and glorify you on this, your Vyāsa-pūjā. You are a grandfather who gave us everything to reach the ultimate goal—*kṛṣṇa-prema,* love of Kṛṣṇa. You gave us a house to live in—ISKCON, a house that the whole world can live in. You gave us a spiritual father who, by dedication, love, and all his service to you, lovingly encourages us to offer to you, through him, our services. You gave us the highest spiritual gift by diligently translating dozens of volumes of the Vedic literature into English for our purification. You, our beloved grandfather, have given us the house we live in, a spiritual father, and transcendental literatures to eternally guide us. You gave us everything we need to end our material suffering. We thank you with all our hearts. Yet for one who gave so much, the words ‘“Thank you” just don’t seem enough.

We, the members of the Institute for Applied Spiritual Technology and the Gītā-nāgarī farm in Port Royal, Pennsylvania, remember your desire to travel to Gītā-nāgarī even though you were in poor health. You wanted to establish a community where your disciples and their children and generations to come could live, give service to Kṛṣṇa, and take the *mahā-mantra* to every town and village. At the farm we have a sign posted that reads “Śrīla Prabhupāda is coming!” Through this consciousness, we are endeavoring to say “Thank you,” O grandfather Śrīla Prabhupāda. We are endeavoring to fulfill your desire for the Gītā-nāgarī farm. We beg for your mercy so that we may become worthy to continue your mission and pray that we will please you.

Yours in service to Kṛṣṇa,  
The devotees at the Institute for Applied Spiritual Technology in Washington, D.C., and Port Royal, Pennsylvania, USA.

## The International Society for Cow Protection

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

It happened so quickly!  
So cold and icy, everything so white.  
The cow’s hoofs crunching the ice  
As they carry their heavy bodies to the water trough.  
Their thick furry winter coats insulating them.  
Their wet noses feeling the sting of the icy winds.

It happened so quickly!  
Warmth, spring rains, birds softly chirping.  
Brown hills turning luscious green.  
The stream heard from the hillside,  
The warm sunlight crystallizing its bubbling movement.  
The cows grazing the short new grasses.

It happened so quickly!  
The time to plant, the time to sow.  
Are we ready?  
Are the oxen ready to plow?  
Are they strong and healthy?  
Do they know their commands?

It happened so quickly!  
Spring is here!  
Are the teamsters ready?  
Are they trained?  
Are the heritage seeds gathered?  
Is the ox equipment repaired?

It happened so quickly!  
Or were we not watching carefully?  
Not watching the phases of the moon?  
Not watching the signs of changing seasons?  
Were we too absorbed in our own plans, fears, and desires?  
Were we thinking of our desire and not Śrīla Prabhupāda’s desire?

Next time it may happen too quickly!  
Unable to see the signs of the changing seasons,  
We may not be ready.  
Too old, too tired, too absorbed in other pursuits.  
A movement unable to present its spiritual master’s vision.  
A vision of harmony with the land and cows.

Next time it won’t happen too quickly!  
We will be ready!  
We’ll see the signs, we’ll hear the waterfall beneath the thin ice.  
We’ll see the first bird, we’ll smell the sweet warm breeze.  
The oxen and the teamsters will be ready to cultivate the thawed earth.  
We’ll be aware of Śrīla Prabhupāda’s desire.

Which shall it be?

Śrīla Prabhupāda, please bless us with your mercy.  
Let us always be aware of you and your desires.  
Aware that service to the land and cows is service to you.  
And, as long as we are engaged in your service,  
Nothing will happen too quickly.  
By your mercy, we will always be ready!

Your servants in the service of Lord Kṛṣṇa’s cows, ISCOWP (The International Society for Cow Protection), and the ISKCON Ministry for Cow Protection and Agriculture.

## ISKCON Moscow Press-center

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances into the dust of your holy lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

There is no way to repay our debt to you—this is the main realization we were granted since the year that has passed since your last Vyāsa-pūjā. In this *duḥkhālayam aśāśvatam* universe, only you can give true happiness and unshakable shelter. Over the past year your ISKCON Moscow Press-center has been maturing visibly. Some media people were turned from rascals threatening your movement into well-wishers praising it. But the process might be much faster and more widespread if we could manage to present you to the public as you are, as someone deserving everybody’s love and adoration. This is our biggest unfinished project, and we pray at your lotus feet to please give us the spiritual strength and intelligence to perform this herculean task.

Your ISKCON Moscow Press-center kids.  
(written by Vipina-purandara dāsa)

## ISKCON School

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

Thank you for the great privilege of engaging us in the service of the exalted souls who grow up in the society you have founded. You wanted your followers to establish educational institutions all over the world to train children in a life of pure devotional service to Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa. Please bless us that we have the determination, purity, and spirit of cooperation needed to assist you in the mission of true education. Please keep us safe under the shelter of your instructions and the shade of your lotus feet.

The staff at ISKCON School, Hillsborough, North Carolina, USA.

## ISKCON Television (ITV)

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

“When a man comes into good consciousness and accepts a saintly person as a spiritual master, he hears many Vedic instructions in the form of philosophy, stories, narrations about great devotees, and transactions between God and His devotees. In this way a man becomes refreshed in mind, exactly like a person who smears scented sandalwood pulp all over his body and decorates himself with ornaments. These decorations may be compared to knowledge of religion and the self. Through such knowledge one becomes detached from a materialistic way of life and engages himself in always hearing *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam, Bhagavad-gītā,* and other Vedic literatures. The word *sādhv-alaṅkṛta* used in this verse indicates that one must be absorbed in knowledge gathered from the instructions of saintly persons. Just as King Purañjana began to search out his better half, the Queen, one who is decorated with knowledge and instructions from saintly persons should try to search out his original consciousness, Kṛṣṇa consciousness. One cannot return to Kṛṣṇa consciousness unless he is favored by the instructions of a saintly person. Therefore Śrīla Narottama dāsa Ṭhākura sings: *sādhu-śāstra-guru-vākya, cittete kariyā aikya*. If we want to become saintly persons, or if we want to return to our original Kṛṣṇa consciousness, we must associate with *sādhu* (a saintly person), *śāstra* (authoritative Vedic literature), and guru (a bona fide spiritual master). This is the process.” (*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 4.26.12)

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are that bona fide guru who has opened the secret storehouse of transcendental pastimes that refresh the mind, “exactly like a person who smears scented sandalwood pulp all over his body and decorates himself with ornaments.” Moreover, you have tilled a bountiful harvest of preachers who have been favored and empowered by you to distribute these teachings to the most fallen souls, who are suffering the terrible anxieties of materialism.

You have kindly blessed the devotees at ISKCON Television (ITV) with the opportunity for glorifying the achievements of your *sādhakas* who have tirelessly struggled to broadcast the sublime message of your beloved Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu. Thus we are able, by your causeless mercy, to offer you the completed production of *The New Hare Kṛṣṇa World*. We had hoped to honor your ISKCON legacy in a half-hour format, but no matter how hard we tried to edit the show, we couldn’t bring it down to that length. There was just too much to present about the many aspects of your inspiration: book and *prasādam* distribution, Deity worship, temple construction, farm communities and *varṇāśrama, gurukulas*, Vedic science, etc. Our video could have lasted for countless hours of documenting spiritual activities and personal realizations simply, and all due to one person—you.

You are the *bīja,* the seed, from which all the Vaiṣṇava interest has sprung all over the world. So no matter where the chanting of Hare Kṛṣṇa is being sung, by whatever group or organization, you are always the original voice that once chanted softly on beads on the streets of New York City, and then louder still within the walls of a small Second Avenue storefront, and then ever more resoundingly in Tompkins Sq. Park, and then at the Ratha-yātrās that sprung forth in San Francisco, Montreal, New York, and now around the world.

Your voice, your sound vibration, your adherence to the principles of pure devotion are what drive forward the inconceivable plan of the Lord for the propagation of His holy names. So wherever there is the chanting of the holy names outside of India, we know for sure that you are the resonance in whatever pure chanting is going on. Therefore, we can always remember you simply by hearing “Hare Kṛṣṇa.”

All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda! We beg you to show us some mercy so we can continue our devotional work at ITV. And we beg to remain

Your servants at ISKCON Television.  
(written by Nṛsiṁhānanda dāsa)

## Ministry of Educational Development

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to you, Your Divine Grace!

Most powerful preacher, you are the best friend of the conditioned souls. You are saving this world from complete ruination.

You always walked with your head held high, always looking up toward Kṛṣṇa in the spiritual sky.

While on this earth planet you were spiritually youthful, never old. Your preaching, as you traveled the world twelve times, was better than gold.

You were so humble that you glorified your neophyte devotees, referring to them as pure, but you always denied or played down your own greatness.

Perfect spiritual master, true representative of Kṛṣṇa, you give complete shelter to fallen souls like us.

You smashed the Māyāvādīs, enlightened the nondevotees, and showed us the importance of practicing Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Your preaching will continue for the next ten thousand years. More people will read your books, and your glories will unlimitedly spread.

Thank you for saving us. Where would our sinful activities have taken us by now? We would be suffering in hell, struggling in a nondevotee school. Instead we are truly happy in this *gurukula* founded by you. Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Sometimes you encourage us and sometimes you chastise, us but we always feel your presence in our lives.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, in your hand you hold the lamp of knowledge, with which you put out the darkness of *māyā*. Your impart knowledge to us because you have seen the truth.

O Śrīla Prabhupāda, please bless us that we can remain in your service always.

Your servants at the Ministry of Educational Development, Vṛndāvana, India.  
(written by students from Vṛndāvana Gurukula, age 13–15)

## Padayātrā Worldwide

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

On the occasion of your Vyāsa-pūjā, I would like to re-enter your association by reminiscing about the very rare moments you spent with us in Vṛndāvana. I was in your personal quarters on September 12, 1976, just prior to leaving on the very first day of Padayātrā. The conversation that I had with you that day has been providing me great inspiration and guidelines for Padayātrā around the world.

By that day everything had been organized, and the party was ready to go on the road. You were now visiting Vṛndāvana, and I and the other members of the party held a ceremony to inaugurate the Bullock Cart Saṅkīrtana party. We held *kīrtana* and broke a coconut, and then we went into your quarters on the ground floor to seek your blessings. You were on your *āsana* behind the desk, and we sat in front of it, around you. I offered you a plate of broken coconut and some *gaur*. You honored some and distributed the rest.

You spoke at length with us, encouraging us all in our new venture. You began by speaking of Gandhi, who also wanted to inspire people to stay on in the villages.

“I have seen,” you said, “Gandhi wanted to organize a program for constructing toilets in the villages. They have spent so much, did you know that? The first business was toilets, to restrict them from passing stool here and there.”

You revealed the mistake in Gandhi’s thinking: “Because they had no engagement there, why should they remain in the village? There are so many attractions in the city, and they get money. The factory-*wallas,* they’re inviting, ‘Come here! You shall get twenty rupees per day.’ So why shall he remain in the village?”

“One must have some attachment.” You explained, “In the city there are so many artificial attachments, so on what grounds will they remain in the village? Always remember this: Unless you have got some attachment, there is no possibility.”

This was a simple lesson from the *Bhagavad-gītā* that you were applying to the problem. There must be some higher taste, some better, more attractive alternative. Others, like Gandhi, had seen the problem of the modern cities, but you saw this through the eyes of scripture, *śāstra-cakṣuḥ*.

You gave Gandhi a bit of credit: “Gandhi’s program was very nice: organize the village so that they may not come to the city and help the capitalists. Remain satisfied in the village. But where is that satisfaction? That is the failure.”

You paused for a moment, and I proposed the solution, “If they add the holy name to their lives, they will...”

“Yes,” Prabhupāda jumped in, “if they could understand that. Therefore Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s statement will never be false: *ceto-darpaṇa-mārjanam*. If they chant the Hare Kṛṣṇa mantra, then gradually the dirty things accumulated within the heart will be cleansed. But they are trained up to enjoy the so-called facilities of the city. They are not going to village. But if we can introduce this *hari-saṅkīrtana,* and if they have little taste, that is success. It’s a great program. And that taste will come—*ceto-darpaṇa-mārjanam*.

“If he simply becomes little sober that ‘I want to eat, I have to sleep, I want some sense enjoyment and defense. So if I can get easily in the village and have satisfaction of mind, why shall I go three hundred miles away to the city?’ That understanding they can have very easily.” So this is a very simple and practical method you were applying to one big Kali-yuga problem. “They have gone to the city for twenty rupees daily to manufacture Goodyear tire. Now I have seen, in Delhi, the government is advertising, ‘Go back to the village.’ Rascal! You are manufacturing wine and then keeping him engaged in work whole day, so after being tired, he requires some wine. Now they’re inviting ‘Go back to the village.’ Why shall they go?”

“That’s just propaganda,” I said.

“No,” you replied, “they’re thinking, ‘So many people are coming to the cities, how we shall provide for them?’ That’s a problem. After all, the government has to supply them rations. Where is so much foodstuff? And if they are not supplied, then there will be resentment, that’s a fact.

“What are they doing in the city? So many scooters, *putputputputput,* going here and there, and the girls loitering in the street. It will become more and more of a problem. So the solution is that they must go back to the village. Just keep in mind the psychology. That simple life will be possible if one is *bhakta: bhaktiḥ pareśānubhavo viraktir anyatra ca*. Only by devotion, not otherwise. Not by manufacturing toilets. Only *bhakti:* If they get a little attachment for Kṛṣṇa, the questions will be solved automatically, and they will be happy! Undoubtedly. That is Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s first instruction: *ceto-darpaṇa-mārjanam*.”

I told you, “I come from a village, and I’m happy now that I have taken to the holy name and *prasādam*. Half of these boys are from villages.”

“Yes, they are villagers,” you nodded, smiling.

I continued, “They’re chanting, they’re happy, and they’re going back to the villages.”

“Very good life,” you were shaking your head, “the village life is a very good life, provided he has got proper engagement. Otherwise his mind will be disturbed, and he’ll seek after wine, after women, after this, after that: the Devil’s workshop.”

Ravi asked how to keep the village people engaged.

You pointed at me, “That he’ll guide you. You do not know. He is from village, he knows.” Then he added, “Village or no village, wherever you chant, people will come. That is a fact. Either you chant in Calcutta Maidan or in the village, they will be attracted. India is still alive in that sense. We have seen it, we have experience: wherever we have chanted, people have come.

“But give the pure thing: Kṛṣṇa is the Supreme Personality of Godhead. Try to give Him to the people. And how to take it? ‘Hare Kṛṣṇa.’ He hasn’t got to do anything. God has given him the tongue. Induce him: chant Hare Kṛṣṇa. That is the beginning, *sevonmukhe hi jihvādau,* it begins from the *jihvā*.”

You quoted the entire *ceto-darpaṇa-mārjanam śloka* and gave your purport: “Chant as much possible and eat *prasādam*. No education required, no philosophy required. But follow Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s instruction and become guru:

*yāre dekha, tāre kaha ‘kṛṣṇa’-upadeśa*  
*āmāra ājñāya guru hañā tāra’ ei deśa*

**“If they have got little brain, then** ***‘kṛṣṇa’-upadeśa*****. Otherwise, Hare Kṛṣṇa** ***upadeśa,*** **that’s all. Chant Hare Kṛṣṇa. The preacher does not require to be very highly educated. If he simply follows Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s instruction,** ***yāre dekha, tāre kaha ‘kṛṣṇa’-upadeśa,*** **then he’s successful Make your effort sincerely. The basic principle is this: Induce them to chant and take** ***prasādam*****. You’ll be successful.”**

I promised that we would write you regularly, that we would keep you informed of our efforts.

You said, “Yes. Go village to village. It is very noble attempt. And if you sincerely preach, Kṛṣṇa will give you intelligence. *Buddhi-yogaṁ dadāmi taṁ yena mām upayanti te*. There is no doubt.”

Śrīla Prabhupāda, please bless us to have great determination and enthusiasm to always continue Padayātrā Worldwide until the holy name reaches every town and village on this planet.

Thank you for engaging us in your service.

Your servants at Padayātrā Worldwide.  
(written by Lokanāth Swami)

## The Prabhupāda Trust

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances in the dust beneath your divine lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

Śrīla Prabhupāda, as the months and years go by, your singularly unique position as the fully empowered and brilliantly effulgent world *ācārya* becomes more and more evident to everyone, and we grow ever increasingly humbled and astounded by what you accomplished on behalf of the Supreme Lord. Who but the Lord’s most confidential servant and *śaktyāveśa-avatāra* could have done what you have done, while simultaneously and unceasingly exhibiting all of the jewellike qualities of the pure devotee? The list of your accomplishments and divine personal qualities are simply too vast to imagine, what to speak of describe. And even now, from your ever-transcendent position, you personally and lovingly guide each of those who try to sincerely serve the Lord through your transcendental instructions. All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Hopefully, in some lifetime, if we ever become intelligent enough (which will surely only happen through your mercy), that day will come when the awareness and understanding of who you really are, Śrīla Prabhupāda, will finally help to bring us to the point of true humility. Humility is the fragrant garland that adorns the true Vaiṣṇava. You displayed this most important quality to perfection and with such warmth and genuineness. In fact, that is one of the main reasons we love and respect you so much. May I say that again? Your many-jeweled display of wonderful humility was one of the main reasons we grew to love and respect you so much, and it will be one of the main reasons that we will continue to love and respect you for the rest of our lives, and through all of eternity. Surely most, if not all, of us can benefit by further developing this special “blade of grass” humility that Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu stresses so eloquently (*tṛṇād api su-nīcena...*). Pure and profound humility shone like sweet morning sunlight through everything you said, wrote, and did, Śrīla Prabhupāda. By closely studying your example, we may someday develop this quality enough to enable us to maturely work together to much more effectively and lovingly spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness. And, as you said, this will be the way to show you that we really love you.

However infinitesimal is our contribution, we deeply thank you for the inspiration to serve your desires through transcendental book printing and distribution. Through your mercy, this has been increasing further the past year through The Saraswati Charitable Trust. This foundation is dedicated to discovering those developing and pioneering areas of the world where there is little, if any, support and then donating your transcendental literatures to them. Especially to those places of the world in which you expressed a personal interest. Please pour down your mercy, Śrīla Prabhupāda, so that this work may increase more and more for both your pleasure and the benefit of all.

All glories, all glories, all glories to wonderful Śrīla Prabhupāda!!

Your servants at The Prabhupāda Trust and The Saraswati Charitable Trust.  
(written by Satyanārāyaṇa dāsa)

## Prabhupāda Village

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to your lotus feet.

What you have given us is beyond belief,  
A scientific process to bring the world relief.  
No one is barred for caste or creed;  
A little sincerity is all we need.

A true alchemist you have shown yourself to be,  
Awakening the dormant Kṛṣṇa consciousness only you could see.  
You take monkeylike men who are useless to all  
And change them into devotees who will not fall.

Now I can hear some say, “This is not true;  
Look all around you, there are only a few.”  
But the process does work for all those who follow;  
Austerity and discipline are often hard to swallow.

Your ISKCON movement you made so sweet—  
Chanting, dancing, and hearing are such a treat.  
Now how could we ever leave you, O divine Gurudeva?  
Your only desire is the whole world to save.

With our heart and soul let us continue to serve you.  
Little soldiers in your army, we will remain true.  
You are the general Mahāprabhu sent here.  
Anyone following you need never know fear.

This is a war of the greatest kind;  
Casualties are not hard to find.  
But brothers and sisters, today I beg with all my might,  
For our most beloved Śrīla Prabhupāda, let us keep up the fight!

evaṁ janaṁ nipatitaṁ prabhavāhi-kūpe  
kāmābhikāmam anu yaḥ prapatan prasaṅgāt  
kṛtvātmasāt surarṣiṇā bhagavan gṛhītaḥ  
so ‘haṁ kathaṁ nu visṛje tava bhṛtya-sevām

“My dear Lord, O Supreme Personality of Godhead, because of my association with material desires, one after another, I was gradually falling into a blind well full of snakes, following the general populace. But Your servant Nārada Muni kindly accepted me as his disciple and instructed me how to achieve this transcendental position. Therefore, my first duty is to serve him. How could I leave his service?”

Dearest devotees, on this most auspicious day of the anniversary of the appearance of our most beloved Śrīla Prabhupāda, let us renew our vows to chant our sixteen rounds with renewed enthusiasm, take time to read Śrīla Prabhupāda’s books, do a little service at our local temple every week, attend some temple program, and tell others about Kṛṣṇa whenever we can. This movement is made up of all of us, and not only will focusing again on our real duty in this human form enliven us, but Śrīla Prabhupāda’s ISKCON movement will reach new heights. What could be more pleasing to His Divine Grace?

Śrīla Prabhupāda’s loving servants at Prabhupāda Village, Sandy Ridge, North Carolina, USA.  
(written by Rucirā-devī dāsī)

## Radio Krishna Centrale

Most beloved Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble and respectful obeisances. I bow down at your divine lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace, *mahā-patita-pāvaṇa*! All glories to Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi!

I beg Your Divine Grace to allow me to express myself. You saved me; therefore my life is yours. I’m so happy. Not so much because I’ve been saved, but because I belong to you. You are the most intimate associate of the Lord. Why such good fortune is upon me that I can be counted as one of your servants—that I do not know. You are Lord Kṛṣṇa’s mercy and I’m made of your mercy. Whatever I see, I see through your eyes. Whatever I hear, I hear through your ears. Whatever I think, I think through your mind. If you accept something, I accept it too. If you reject something, I reject it. Only what is important to you is important to me. Your words and instructions are the only ones that I want to hear, repeat, remember, and act upon. My only hope is to be able to please you, one way or another. You are not just a master: you are an extraordinary master. An extraordinary jewel in the wonderful *paramparā* golden chain. You spread your extraordinary mercy to every nook and corner, dragging everyone to the devotional service of the Supreme Lord Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi. It is only due to your causeless mercy that Vaiṣṇavas residing in holy places like Śrī Vṛndāvana-dhāma and Śrī Navadvīpa-dhāma have been forcefully pushed out of their local preaching area and dragged into a worldwide preaching arena, as desired by Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu and your beloved spiritual master, Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura. Every sincere soul should understand this obvious fact and adore Your Divine Grace from the core of his or her heart, with words and actions.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, I have a prayer: Please keep all of us, your humble servants, united under your perfect guidance. Do not allow us to scatter like a cloud scattered by strong winds. Give us the intelligence to see how small and fragile we are.

You are so great and still so humble. Once in Rome’s temple kitchen, while completely absorbed in cooking for their Lordships Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi, you looked up and saw all the devotees crowded outside the door and windows, watching Your Divine Grace, totally fascinated. Opening wide your beautiful lotus eyes, you said with a surprised innocent look: “Oh, they are all here to look at me??”

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we are praying to our beloved Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa to please bestow Their mercy upon us, to let us always remember and never forget your pastimes, words, and instructions so that we can forever be with Your Divine Grace.

Your menial servants at Radio Krishna Centrale, in Terni and Florence, Italy.  
(written by Tridaṇḍī dāsa)

## Rūpānuga Vedic College

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances on this most auspicious day of your appearance. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

The whole planet is absorbed in the intrigues of the mind and senses, spiraling down to hell. We find in your *Bhagavad-gītā:*

**Arjuna:** *Cañcalaṁ hi manaḥ kṛṣṇa pramāthi balavad dṛḍham / tasyāhaṁ nigrahaṁ manye vāyor iva su-duṣkaram*. For the mind is restless, turbulent, obstinate and very strong, O Kṛṣṇa, and to subdue it, I think, is more difficult than controlling the wind.

**Kṛṣṇa:** *Asaṁśayaṁ mahā-bāho mano durnigrahaṁ calam / abhyāsena tu kaunteya vairāgyeṇa ca gṛhyate*. O mighty-armed son of Kuntī, it is undoubtedly very difficult to curb the restless mind, but it is possible by suitable practice and by detachment.

Hundreds of versions of the *Bhagavad-gītā*...no one figured out what the “suitable practice” is. Only you established that. Let us take a look at what you gave us.

**Rising early:** The most wonderful experience. Rising before the sun. The sacred *brāhma-muhūrta*. Out of ignorance and darkness into clarity and knowledge.

**Maṅgala-ārati:** The Lord’s transcendental form fills our vision. The vibration of the holy names surround us. Meeting like-minded souls and dancing in ecstasy. Real transcendental bliss.

**Japa:** Sixteen rounds. Time seems irrelevant. Chanting and hearing fill the ether. The spiritual realm becomes a reality. The sleeping world allows the devotees to peacefully taste the nectarean holy name.

**Deity greeting:** The conchshell sounds, the devotees assemble in eager anticipation, the curtains open, and the Lord appears amidst billowing incense, flowing *cāmaras,* and Brahmā’s prayers. What a festival for the mind, senses, and heart!

**Guru-pūjā:** Glorify the Lord’s pure devotee. Bow down, offer flowers. O master, please give us the shade of your lotus feet! Our only desire is to have our consciousness purified by the words emanating from your lotus mouth.

**Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam:** Are we ready? The channel to the heart is open. Transcendental nectar flows through the disciplic succession and is poured into the eager ears of submissive devotees. Imperceptibly the spiritual realm becomes visible. The nightmare of material existence fades into insignificance.

**Prasādam meditation:** Prepared with love and devotion and offered to the lord. The incarnation of Kṛṣṇa’s mercy most appreciated by the devotees. The *piṝce de rīsistance.* Yes... “I am Kṛṣṇa’s servant.”.

**Devotional service:** What to do first? A festival. Who said spiritual life is boring? Book distribution, *harināma,* cooking, cleaning, preaching, painting, building, printing, organizing, driving—everything for Kṛṣṇa. The spiritual world.

**Gaura-ārati:** Lord Caitanya and his associates glance mercifully upon the devotees. Whatever is done is done. Safe again. All wounds are soothed by the *kīrtana*. The Lord is the controller. Again surrender. Lord Brahmā, Śiva, Nārada Muni, Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura, and others are also there.

**Bhagavad-gītā class:** The jolting and jarring Kali-yuga experience takes its toll. Arjuna speaks for us. Kṛṣṇa speaks to us. Recalibration is a daily necessity.

**Rest:** A little snap. Not exactly sleeping. Just resting, enough to regain strength for another day in Kṛṣṇa’s service.

**Study:** Your books, conversations, letters, morning walks, lectures...Enough nectar for many lifetimes. You once said, “I shall never die; I shall live forever in my books.”

You once said,

As there are different sections of educational institutions, there must be one institution how to train up perfect *brāhmaṇas* with ideal characters as above mentioned in the *Bhagavad-gītā*. If there is a section of people of ideal character, say 5 percent, the other 95 percent, by seeing their example, will follow. In other words, a section of the society must be of ideal character. That is essential.

We at the Rūpānuga Vedic College are trying to fulfill your desire for such an educational institution. There are so many impediments, but you showed by your life example that whatever the difficulties, one should never give up. We thank you for giving us the suitable process, and we pray that you will allow us to help you give it to others. Please bless us with the determination to remain fixed in following the program you gave us. We are convinced that if we can do that then all perfection will be achieved.

Your servants at the Rūpānuga Vedic College.

## Vaiṣṇava Academy for Girls

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

There’s an ancient tale of a pied piper  
Who played a tune on his flute.  
He came dancing into the village,  
Beckoning to all of the youth.

His sinister spirit was hidden;  
Deluded by his music, they danced,  
Thinking the sound to be nectar,  
The spell of the poison enhanced.

Some were encouraged by their peers,  
And some took the lure on their own.  
When the elders tried to dissuade them,  
Their cries trailed off into a drone.

A hundred years later his songs  
Are re-mixed and mass-produced.  
Hollywood makes it all glitter;  
Advertisements give it a boost.

The fears of the parents still linger,  
The dangers are still the same.  
The youth are still being misguided  
By pipers with a sinister aim.

Those looking for something honest  
That will take them to Kṛṣṇa one day,  
Something to give them protection  
And keep them from going astray,

Find Prabhupāda, who gave us a shelter,  
Protecting us from MTV.  
You exposed Māyā’s illusion  
And showed us the way to be free.

Prabhupāda, your words are the nectar  
That keeps us from Māyā’s tight grip.  
They prevent us from falling a victim  
To the Pied Piper’s unlimited tricks.

We’re lucky you’re bold and determined,  
Ignoring our sirenlike pleas.  
You lead us to real enjoyment  
By giving us the knowledge supreme

Of the eternal land of Braja,  
Where a blue boy plays tunes on His flute,  
Awakening each town and village  
To the glories of the eternal truth.

Your servants at the Vaiṣṇava Academy for Girls, Alachua, Florida, USA.

## Vṛndāvana Institute for Higher Education (VIHE)

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāminn iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

My Simple Request

I came to Kṛṣṇa consciousness by meeting your devotees, reading your books, visiting your temples. I’m indebted to you for giving me the unique opportunity to practice Kṛṣṇa consciousness. I want to express my indebtedness to you through service and following your instructions. One of my services is to arrange for others to hear and discuss your books. Those who do so inevitably become happy, satisfied, and enlivened in Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

I and your other servants at the VIHE are satisfied to see your devotees satisfied in this way. Your books are a bottomless well of nectar waiting to be drawn by the thirsty devotees. When devotees drink from this well, in the association of your devotees, in the sanctity of Kṛṣṇa’s holy *dhāma,* our thirst for real life increases and our desire for material life shrivels.

Please give me this benediction: to be able to continue serving you by serving your devotees in this way. That is my simple request.

The devotees of the Vṛndāvana Institute for Higher Education (VIHE).  
(written by Braja Bihārī dāsa)

## Śrī Gurv-aṣṭaka

*by Śrīla Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura*

*(1) saṁsāra-dāvānala-līḍha-loka*  
*trāṇāya kāruṇya-ghanāghanatvam*  
*prāptasya kalyāṇa guṇārṇavasya*  
*vande guroḥ śrī-caraṇāravindam*

**The spiritual master is receiving benediction from the ocean of mercy. Just as a cloud pours water on a forest fire to extinguish it, so the spiritual master delivers the materially afflicted world by extinguishing the blazing fire of material existence. I offer my respectful obeisances unto the lotus feet of such a spiritual master, who is an ocean of auspicious qualities.**

*(2) mahāprabhoḥ kīrtana-nṛtya-gīta-*  
*vāditra-mādyan-manaso rasena*  
*romāñca-kampāśru-taraṅga-bhājo*  
*vande guroḥ śrī-caraṇāravindam*

**Chanting the holy name, dancing in ecstasy, singing, and playing musical instruments, the spiritual master is always gladdened by the saṅkīrtana movement of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu. Because he is relishing the mellows of pure devotion within his mind, sometimes his bodily hairs stand on end, he feels quivering in his body, and tears flow from his eyes like waves. I offer my respectful obeisances unto the lotus feet of such a spiritual master.**

*(3) śrī-vigrahārādhana-nitya-nānā-*  
*śṛṅgāra-tan-mandira-mārjanādau*  
*yuktasya bhaktāṁś ca niyuñjato ’pi*  
*vande guroḥ śrī-caraṇāravindam*

**The spiritual master is always engaged in the temple worship of Śrī Śrī Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa. He also engages his disciples in such worship. They dress the Deities in beautiful clothes and ornaments, clean Their temple, and perform other, similar worship of the Lord. I offer my respectful obeisances unto the lotus feet of such a spiritual master.**

*(4) catur-vidha-śrī-bhagavat-prasāda-*  
*svādv-anna-tṛptān hari-bhakta-saṅghān*  
*kṛtvaiva tṛptiṁ bhajataḥ sadaiva*  
*vande guroḥ śrī-caraṇāravindam*

**The spiritual master is always offering Kṛṣṇa four kinds of delicious food \[analyzed as that which is licked, chewed, drunk and sucked\]. When the spiritual master sees that the devotees are satisfied by eating bhagavat-prasādam, he is satisfied. I offer my respectful obeisances unto the lotus feet of such a spiritual master.**

*(5) śrī-rādhikā-mādhavayor apāra-*  
*mādhurya-līlā-guṇa-rūpa-nāmnām*  
*prati-kṣaṇāsvādana-lolupasya*  
*vande guroḥ śrī-caraṇāravindam*

**The spiritual master is always eager to hear and chant about the unlimited conjugal pastimes of Śrī Śrī Rādhikā and Mādhava, and about Their qualities, names and forms. The spiritual master aspires to relish these at every moment. I offer my respectful obeisances unto the lotus feet of such a spiritual master.**

*(6) nikuñja-yūno rati-keli-siddhyai*  
*yā yālibhir yuktir apekṣaṇīyā*  
*tatrāti-dākṣyād ati-vallabhasya*  
*vande guroḥ śrī-caraṇāravindam*

**The spiritual master is very dear, because he is expert in assisting the gopīs, who at different times make different tasteful arrangements for the perfection of Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa’s conjugal loving affairs within the groves of Vṛndāvana. I offer my most humble obeisances unto the lotus feet of such a spiritual master.**

*(7) sākṣād-dharitvena samasta-śāstrair*  
*uktas tathā bhāvyata eva sadbhiḥ*  
*kintu prabhor yaḥ priya eva tasya*  
*vande guroḥ śrī-caraṇāravindam*

**The spiritual master is to be honored as much as the Supreme Lord because he is the most confidential servitor of the Lord. This is acknowledged in all revealed scriptures and followed by all authorities. Therefore I offer my respectful obeisances unto the lotus feet of such a spiritual master, who is a bona fide representative of Śrī Hari \[Kṛṣṇa\].**

*(8) yasya prasādād bhagavat-prasādo*  
*yasyāprasādān na gatiḥ kuto ’pi*  
*dhyāyan stuvaṁs tasya yaśas tri-sandhyaṁ*  
*vande guroḥ śrī-caraṇāravindam*

**By the mercy of the spiritual master one receives the benediction of Kṛṣṇa. Without the grace of the spiritual master, one cannot make any advancement. Therefore, I should always remember and praise the spiritual master. At least three times a day I should offer my respectful obeisances unto the lotus feet of my spiritual master.**
